# NLightning.Application — agent guide

Orchestration layer. It sits between the peer/transport layer (Infrastructure) and the domain plus crypto (Domain, Infrastructure.Bitcoin), and holds:
- PeerManager: the peer table, connect/accept, routing channel messages. Every inbound peer is saved (NL-497): one from a loopback address (`PeerManager.IsLoopback`: `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`) as `PeerModel.IsInboundOnly` with no address (empty host, port 0: the loopback host is not the peer's), unless Tor is on and its `node_announcement` has a dialable address (an onion service: Tor delivers our onion service's connections from loopback), which is saved and dialed back instead (NL-579); a saved dialable row survives every inbound connection, only its last-seen time moves (NL-514), and a peer without one is saved at its `node_announcement`'s address when the graph has one (NL-514), else at the host it connected from with the default port; `StartAsync` registers the channels of every peer, inbound-only ones included, but dials only the others (in parallel, waiting at most `StartupDialWait` = `Node:NetworkTimeout`; slower dials continue in the background, NL-576), and `ReconnectIfNeeded` dials an inbound-only session's peer only at the dialable row saved before (`PeerSession.DialablePeer`, set from `SaveInboundPeerAsync`), never at its loopback address. The static channel backup (`ChannelBackupService.CreateEntry`) and the restore's address list (`ChannelRestoreService.GetAddressesAsync`) skip an inbound-only peer row (NL-497 review).
- ChannelManager: dispatches channel messages and reacts to blockchain events.
- The BOLT 2 v1 channel-open handlers and the BOLT 2 normal-operation receive handlers (update_add/fulfill/fail/fail_malformed_htlc, commitment_signed, revoke_and_ack, update_fee; BOLT2 plan N6-T1).
- MessageFactory: builds outbound protocol messages (Infrastructure's `PeerCommunicationService` still constructs error/warning messages directly).
- The send side (BOLT2 plan N6-T2): `IChannelOperations` (`ChannelOperationsService`), the commit scheduler and the `LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch`, which fails every incoming HTLC back once it is locked in (the default until `AddHtlcSwitchServices()` replaces it).
- The HTLC switch (ABCD W2-B): `Payments/Switch/HtlcSwitch` receives (final hop, invoices), forwards, propagates fulfills and failures upstream and replays after a restart. See "Payments" below.
- channel_reestablish (BOLT2 plan N7, ABCD W2-A): `Channels/Reestablish/` and `ChannelReestablishMessageHandler` (see below).
- Cooperative close (BOLT2 plan N10, legacy `closing_signed`): `Channels/Close/` and the `shutdown`/`closing_signed` handlers (see "Cooperative close" below).

- Channel safety (BOLT2 plan N9-T2/T4, ABCD W3): `Channels/Safety/` (HTLC deadline monitor, fail-the-channel broadcast). See "Channel safety" below.

- BOLT 5 wiring (ABCD W5): `Onchain/` (funding-spend watcher, resolution executor; see "BOLT 5 wiring" below) and the resolvers in `Onchain/Resolvers/`.

- `option_simple_close` (BOLT2 plan N11): `Channels/Close/Simple/` and `Channels/Close/Handlers/` (see "option_simple_close" below).

- BOLT 5 mempool reaction (plan O8, NL-098): `Onchain/Mempool/` (see "BOLT 5 mempool reaction" below).

- Chain-processing halt gate (NL-216): see "Chain-processing halt" below.

- BOLT 5 anchor CPFP (plan O7-T2): `Onchain/Anchors/` (see "BOLT 5 anchor CPFP" below).

Not implemented yet: enabling `option_anchors` (O7-T4; NL-094). O7-T1 wallet signing is in Infrastructure.Bitcoin; O7-T3 (anchors HTLC transactions with wallet fee inputs, their RBF and conflict rebuild, penalty isolation) is in `Onchain/Resolvers/`.

The payment core (ABCD W1-B) lives in `Payments/`: incoming onion processing, final hop, forwarding policy, route and onion building for our payments, and invoices. `Payments/Switch/` (W2-B) is the switch that calls it; the send path (`Payments/Send/PaymentService`, ABCD W2-C) uses it and offers our HTLCs through `IChannelOperations`. See "Payments" below.

## Layout
- `DependencyInjection.cs`: `AddApplicationServices()`. Registers the Domain services that have no DI of their own (IChannelOpenValidator, IChannelFactory, ICommitmentTransactionModelFactory, IFundingTransactionModelFactory; they need the host's IFeeService and AddBitcoinInfrastructure's ILightningSigner) and IChannelLockProvider, `ChannelManager` (built by a factory lambda, then exposed as both `IChannelManager` and `IChannelMessagePublisher`: one instance), IMessageFactory and IPeerManager as singletons, plus `AddCommitmentEngineServices()`, `AddChannelStateTransitionServices()` and `AddChannelOperationsServices()` (all in `Channels/Services/CommitmentEngineServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`), `Channels/Close/CloseServiceCollectionExtensions.AddChannelCloseServices()`, `Gossip.AddGossipServices()` and `Payments.AddPaymentsServices()`. An explicit list in `AddChannelMessageHandlers` registers every `IChannelMessageHandler<>` as Scoped (NL-055: trim/AOT-safe, no assembly scan; the guard test `ChannelMessageHandlerRegistrationTests` fails when a handler implementation in the assembly is not on the list). `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler` is registered as a concrete Scoped type. It is called from `src/NLightning.Daemon/Extensions/NodeServiceExtensions.cs`.
- `Channels/Handlers/Interfaces/IChannelMessageHandler.cs`: `Task<IReadOnlyList<IChannelMessage>> HandleAsync(msg, ChannelState currentState, FeatureOptions negotiatedFeatures, CompactPubKey peerPubKey)`. The returned messages are sent back to the same peer in list order (e.g. revoke_and_ack before commitment_signed); return `[]` for none.
- `Channels/Services/ChannelLockProvider.cs`: `IChannelLockProvider` (Domain) singleton, one non-reentrant `SemaphoreSlim` per channel id, dropped when unused. `AcquireAsync` / blocking `Acquire` return an `IDisposable`.
- `Channels/Handlers/`:
  - `OpenChannel1MessageHandler`: we are the non-initiator. Receives open_channel, replies accept_channel with **our** values (`ChannelParams.Local`, never the opener's; NL-194) and the opener's `channel_type` echoed byte for byte (BOLT 2, NL-218).
  - `AcceptChannel1MessageHandler`: we are the initiator. Receives accept_channel, fails it if `channel_type` differs from the one we sent (`ChannelParams.ToChannelType()`), a reserve is below the other side's dust limit or the peer's in-flight limit is below the validator's floor (`CheckMaxHtlcValueInFlight`, NL-552; `DualFundedOpenService` applies it in both roles too), stores the peer's values with `ChannelModel.UpdateRemoteParams` (our own, incl. `to_self_delay`, never change), builds the funding tx, replies funding_created. The funding is sized with a P2WPKH probe change address first; a change address is reserved from the wallet (`GetUnusedAddressAsync`, NL-280) only when the funding has a change output, and replaces the probe. The channel is not persisted until funding_signed (BOLT 2: a funder that has not broadcast SHOULD NOT remember the channel).
  - `FundingCreatedMessageHandler`: non-initiator. Replies funding_signed, persists the channel and watches the funding tx.
  - `FundingSignedMessageHandler`: initiator. Signs the funding tx, then saves in **one** save the channel as V1FundingSigned, the funding watch, the signed funding tx as a pending `BroadcastTransactionModel` (purpose `Funding`) and the funding output's `WatchedOutpointModel` (BOLT 5 plan O0-T1/T2), tracks both watches in the monitor and publishes through `IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync` (the monitor); a refused publish is only logged, because the monitor sends every stored pending tx again after each block and at startup (NL-258). `IChannelMemoryRepository.UpdateChannel` (the open subscription's V1FundingSigned event) is raised only after the publish. Sends no reply. If anything before that save fails (bad signature, rebuilt funding mismatch, signing), the wallet outputs locked to the channel are released (`ReturnUtxosNotSpentOnChannel`) before the exception goes on (NL-259); at the next start `ChannelManager` persists the V1FundingCreated channel Stale (no funding watch), abandons any pending `Funding`/`Unspecified` broadcast row of it in that same save and releases its UTXO locks. A funding bitcoind refuses for good is abandoned by the chain monitor (NL-294, `Infrastructure.Bitcoin/Wallet/BroadcastRefusalRules`), which releases the locks too; the channel itself stays V1FundingSigned in memory (forgetting it is a follow-up).
  - `ChannelReadyMessageHandler`: on the **first** channel_ready (the remote key set still at the first index) it builds the channel's first commitment snapshot (`ChannelStateTransitionService.CreateInitialCommitments`: balances, feerate, both commitment numbers, the peer's signature of our commitment 0, the peer's current point from the key set and the next point from the message) before the key set's point is overwritten (NL-232), and saves it with `IChannelStateDbRepository.InitializeAsync` in the same save as the channel; the model gets it only after the save. If the snapshot can't be built it logs and the channel works without HTLCs. Channels that received channel_ready before this existed have lost the peer's commitment-0 point and never get a snapshot (logged at startup; their HTLC messages get a warning).
  - Normal operation (N6-T1), all through the scoped `Services/ChannelStateTransitionService`: `UpdateAddHtlcMessageHandler`, `UpdateFulfillHtlcMessageHandler`, `UpdateFailHtlcMessageHandler`, `UpdateFailMalformedHtlcMessageHandler` (a failure_code without BADONION: warning + close, checked first), `UpdateFeeMessageHandler` (feerate bounds `MinAcceptableFeeratePerKw` 253 / `MaxAcceptableFeeratePerKw` 250,000), `CommitmentSignedMessageHandler` (replies revoke_and_ack, then our commitment_signed when changes are pending for the peer) and `RevokeAndAckMessageHandler` (inserts the secret into a fresh copy of the peer's shachain loaded from `IRemoteShachainDbRepository`, saves it with the transition as `ChannelStateExtras.RemoteShachain`, then signs if changes are pending). Each: guard (`GetUpdatableChannel`), engine call, `CommitAsync` (`ApplyAsync` + one `SaveChangesAsync`, then `ChannelModel.UpdateCommitments` + memory update, then the transition's events are queued), then the replies. Engine violations (`CommitmentViolationException`) become `ChannelWarningException { CloseConnection = true }` (BOLT 2's warning-and-close option; the engine state is unchanged and reestablish recovers), except `MustFailChannel` ones (bad revocation secret, B2-RAA-R01) which become `ChannelFailedException`.
  - `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler`: not an IChannelMessageHandler. It is triggered by a funding confirmation and emits channel_ready through its `OnMessageReady` event.
- `Channels/Services/CommitmentSigningService.cs` (BOLT2 plan N3-T5): a concrete service (no interface) with `SignRemoteCommitment(channel, CommitmentTxSpec, remoteNumber, remotePoint)` and `VerifyLocalCommitment(channel, CommitmentTxSpec, localNumber, sig, htlcSigs)` (throws `SignerException`), both returning `CommitmentTxSignatures(txid, sig, htlcSigs)`. It runs factory (spec-driven overload) -> `BuildWithOutputMap` -> `HtlcTransactionModelFactory` -> `IHtlcTransactionBuilder.Build` -> `ILightningSigner` (`SignChannelTransaction` + `SignRemoteHtlcTransactions`, or `ValidateSignature` + `ValidateLocalHtlcSignatures`), so HTLC signatures are always in commitment output order. Vectors: `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/BOLT3/CommitmentSigningServiceVectorTests` (Appendix C/F: as node B the signatures equal `remote_signature`/`remote_htlc_signature`; as node A the vector signatures verify).
- Engine ports (NL-230), same folder: `EngineCommitmentSignerPort : ICommitmentSigner` and `EngineCommitmentVerifierPort : ICommitmentVerifier` (the Domain engine ports in `Channels/Commitments/Interfaces`) look the channel up in `IChannelMemoryRepository` by id (static data only), adapt the engine's `CommitmentSpec` with `CommitmentTxSpec.FromCommitmentSpec`, and call `CommitmentSigningService`; they reject a spec of the wrong holder (`ArgumentException`) and an unknown channel (`InvalidOperationException`). The verifier maps `SignerException` to `false` (the engine then raises B2-CS-R01) and logs it; other exceptions propagate. `EngineRevocationVerifierPort : IRevocationVerifier` delegates to `IPerCommitmentSecretVerifier.Verify`. All four are singletons registered by `CommitmentEngineServiceCollectionExtensions.AddCommitmentEngineServices()` (called from `AddApplicationServices`). Proof: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Services/EngineCommitmentPortsTwoNodeTests` drives two engines with real `LocalLightningSigner`s built from the production DI (`RealSigningCommitmentPair`) through add/CS/RAA/fulfill/fail/update_fee, with and without anchors, and checks that the txid each side signed equals the txid the other verified; tampered commitment/HTLC signatures fail B2-CS-R01 and a wrong secret B2-RAA-R01. The same harness records each node's domain events (`RealSigningNode.Events`, N4-T4) and checks lock-in once on revoke_and_ack and fail/settle only when irrevocable. The N6-T1 receive handlers drive the engine through `ChannelStateTransitionService`. `ChannelManager` hands the `IHtlcSwitch` (Domain, `Channels/Interfaces`; `LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch` by default) the events of each persisted transition, and replays `ChannelDomainEvents.DerivePending` at startup.
- `Channels/Services/ChannelStateTransitionService.cs` (scoped, N6-T1): `GetUpdatableChannel(id, state, name)` is the state guard (HTLCs disabled by `NodeOptions.HtlcsEnabled` → the old "not supported yet" warning; channel not in memory or without a snapshot → warning; `Failed` → `error` again; not `Open` → warning + close, B2-NO-02); `CommitAsync(channel, result, extras)` persists one transition (D3/I1/I2); `CreateRevokeAndAck` calls `ILightningSigner.AdvanceLocalCommitment` then `RevealPerCommitmentSecret` (only after the save, I3); `SignIfPendingAsync` signs the peer's next commitment when `ChannelCommitments.CanSendCommit` (never after data loss) and saves it with `ChannelStateExtras.SentCommitDiff` (our pending updates rebuilt from the snapshot — adds by id, removals by id, fee — then the commitment_signed, in `SentCommitDiffCodec` format: u16 big-endian length + serialized message each) and `LastSent`; `ToWireMessage` maps engine outbound messages to wire messages (`OutboundStartBatch` → `start_batch`, a member's `FundingTxId` else the channel's funding txid); `SignPendingAsync` is the list form of the signature (one `commitment_signed`, or with pending splices `start_batch` + one per active funding, the whole batch in the sent diff) and `SignIfPendingAsync` its single-message form, which with a pending splice signs nothing and schedules the `ICommitScheduler` (optional constructor argument) instead; `ReceiveCommitmentSignedBatchAsync(channel, CommitmentSignedBatch)` (needs the optional `ICommitmentVerifier` argument) is the batch receive (SP-OP-05/06/07: engine checks, one save, then the one revoke_and_ack's secret, then our own signature via `SignPendingAsync`), called by `ChannelManager.HandleCommitmentSignedBatchAsync` under one acquisition of the channel lock with the gates of a single `commitment_signed`, and refused (warning + close) unless `option_splice` (experimental) was negotiated, because the per-funding signatures, pending fundings and revoked fundings are not persisted before lane SP1-C (SP1-C-T4; SP-I3 holds only then); `ChannelManager.Publish` drops a `start_batch` with the members it announces when the channel is not reestablished on the current connection (B2-RE-07); the close coordinator refuses `closechannel` and defers our `shutdown` while `ChannelCommitments.PendingFundings` is not empty (BOLT 2: no shutdown before the splice locks) and signs pending updates with the list form `SignPendingAsync`; `LoadRemoteShachainAsync` gives a fresh store loaded from the database. `ChannelDomainEventQueue` (scoped) collects the events of the persisted transitions. `SecretStorageServiceFactory` (`ISecretStorageServiceFactory`, `TryAddSingleton`) makes `SecretStorageService` instances. All registered by `AddChannelStateTransitionServices()` (in `CommitmentEngineServiceCollectionExtensions`, called from `AddApplicationServices`).
- Channels without a snapshot (channel_ready handled before ABCD wave 1, NL-246) cannot be given one: the first snapshot needs the peer's per-commitment point of its current commitment 0, which that channel_ready replaced with the second point, and nothing we store yields it (we keep no remote commitment 0 transaction, and a signature does not reveal the key). `channel_reestablish` is no way back: BOLT 2 now says the receiver MUST ignore `my_current_per_commitment_point` (the sender only has to send a valid point). Such a channel keeps working without HTLCs (HTLC messages get a warning); close and reopen it once N10 exists, and never build a snapshot from an unverified point.
- Send side (N6-T2), `Channels/Interfaces/` and `Channels/Services/`:
  - `ChannelOperationsService : IChannelOperations` (singleton): Offer/Fulfill/Fail/FailMalformed/UpdateFee each take the channel's lock, check the preconditions (`NodeOptions.HtlcsEnabled`, `Open`, not `Failed`, no data loss, a snapshot, and for **every** operation the channel's link is up: `IPeerLivenessProbe.IsAliveAsync(channelId, peer)`), run the engine (its sender rules throw `CommitmentRefusedException`), persist with `ChannelStateTransitionService.CommitAsync` (one save; its optional `stageWithTransition` callback stages more writes on the same unit of work before that save: the offer's `HtlcOrigin`, and the caller's writes of the `FulfillHtlcAsync` overload with `stageWithFulfill`), publish the wire message through `IChannelMessagePublisher` under the lock, then `ICommitScheduler.Schedule` after the lock. Nothing is persisted or sent on a refusal; an unknown channel throws `KeyNotFoundException`. A removal or fee update for a peer that is away (or on a connection the channel was not opened/reestablished on) is refused like an offer: `PeerManager` drops messages for a peer that is not connected and nothing retransmits before N7, so a persisted-but-unsent update would later be covered by a `commitment_signed` the peer can't verify. The HTLC stays locked in and the switch acts again on the replay (startup, and with `AddHtlcSwitchServices` every `MarkLinkUp`, see the HTLC switch section; N7 must call `MarkLinkUp` after the reestablish). Left for N7: an update enqueued just as its connection closes is lost with that connection (the link is then down for good, so nothing signs over it; N7's reestablish must forget/retransmit it). `RecordOnionSecretAsync` stores the incoming HTLC's Sphinx secret (`IChannelStateDbRepository.SetOnionSharedSecretAsync`, one save). The `HtlcOrigin` of an offer is validated and staged with `IChannelStateDbRepository.SetHtlcOriginAsync` in the add's save (NL-250), so a restart routes the outgoing HTLC's resolution back to its circuit or payment. "channel_reestablish processed" is approximated by the pinned link (N7 must call `MarkLinkUp`).
  - `CommitScheduler : ICommitScheduler` (singleton, `CommitSchedulerOptions.Debounce`, default 10 ms): one waiting round per channel, run on the thread pool without the caller's execution context (so its message goes to the peer's current connection); `SignNowAsync` asks `IPeerLivenessProbe` (ping-before-commit) before the lock and again under it (a reconnection meanwhile must not get a commitment_signed before channel_reestablish), then under the lock `ChannelStateTransitionService.SignPendingAsync` (never while `RemoteNextCommit` exists, D7; persisted with the diff; a batch while splices are pending) and publishes every message in one `Publish`. `WhenIdleAsync` for tests. A failed signature is logged; the changes stay pending. The receive handlers still sign on their own after commitment_signed/revoke_and_ack.
  - `IPeerLivenessProbe` → `ConnectedPeerLivenessProbe` (singleton): channel-scoped. `MarkLinkUp(channelId, peer)` pins the peer's current connection (the `PeerModel` instance `IPeerManager.GetPeer` returns; one per connection); `IsAliveAsync(channelId, peer)` is true only while `GetPeer` still returns that same instance, so after a reconnection the channel is down until marked again. `ChannelManager` marks a channel when it turns `Open` (channel_ready handled, or our funding confirmation); a channel loaded at startup or reconnected is marked after its channel_reestablish (N7), and the production registration gates the probe with the `ReestablishTracker` (`ReestablishGatedLivenessProbe`). Without a peer manager (in-process tests) a marked channel is up. The peer manager is resolved lazily. The BOLT 2 ping before `commitment_signed` is not the probe's job: `CommitScheduler.SignNowAsync` asks `IPingBeforeCommit` (`PingBeforeCommit`, singleton, `TryAdd`ed by `AddChannelOperationsServices`) after the cheap link check and before taking the lock; it pings through `IPeerService.PingAsync` when `IPeerService.LastMessageReceivedAt` is older than `CommitSchedulerOptions.PingWhenQuietFor` (30 s) and waits `PongTimeout` (10 s). No pong: nothing is signed and the connection is closed (BOLT 1 MAY), so the next connection's reestablish signs the pending changes; without a peer manager (in-process harnesses) every peer counts as responsive (NL-251).
  - `IChannelMessagePublisher`: implemented by `ChannelManager.Publish` (raises `OnResponseMessageReady`); call it under the channel's lock. It drops normal-operation messages (128-135) of a channel that is not reestablished on the peer's current connection (the connection can be replaced during the caller's save; the reestablish retransmits what was persisted), checked and raised under the same gate as `OnPeerConnectionChanged`, which `PeerManager` calls before it swaps the session, so nothing precedes our channel_reestablish on a new connection (B2-RE-07).
  - `Channels/Switch/LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch : IHtlcSwitch` (`TryAddSingleton`; replace it with `services.Replace(...)`): on `IncomingHtlcLockedIn` it skips an HTLC that is no longer in `RcvdAddAckRevocation` (idempotent replay), fails a blinded HTLC with `invalid_onion_blinding` (malformed), peels with `ISphinxService.PeelAsLocalNode(packet, payment_hash)` (BADONION → `update_fail_malformed_htlc` with the exception's sha256_of_onion; `invalid_onion_payload` with a secret → encrypted), records the shared secret, then fails with `incorrect_or_unknown_payment_details(htlc_msat, IBlockchainMonitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight)` when final and the height is known, or `temporary_node_failure` when it would forward or no block was processed yet (height 0 would mislead the payer); no BOLT 4 final-node checks yet (`final_incorrect_cltv_expiry`/`final_incorrect_htlc_amount`, W2-B), encrypted by `IFailureOnionService.CreateErrorPacket`. No replay-cache entry. Outgoing fulfilled/failed are logged; `OutgoingHtlcSettled` prunes the archived row (`PruneSettledHtlcsAsync`, one save, NL-243). A `CommitmentRefusedException`/`KeyNotFoundException` is logged, not thrown.
- `Channels/Managers/ChannelManager.cs`: the `switch (message.Type)` in `DispatchChannelMessageAsync`. `HandleChannelMessageAsync` creates the message scope **before** taking the lock; after the lock is released it drains the scope's `ChannelDomainEventQueue` into the registered `IHtlcSwitch` (a failing switch is logged, the events are re-derivable), also when the handler threw after a persisted transition. A `ChannelFailedException` from a handler is persisted first (under the lock): `ChannelState.Failed` and the serialized `error` (`ChannelModel.MarkErrorSent`) through `ChannelDbRepository.UpdateAsync` + save (skipped when the channel is already Failed with a stored error), then rethrown (PeerManager sends the error and keeps the connection). funding_created runs under its temporary id's lock **and** the real channel id's lock (computed with `IChannelIdFactory`, temporary first; NL-235): the only place two locks are held. `RegisterExistingChannelAsync` first resumes the stored state (N7-T5): Closed/Stale and pre-funding states (None, V1Opening, V2Opening) are not registered; a funder's `V1FundingCreated` (only from builds before BOLT 5 plan O0, which saved the channel before the publish and V1FundingSigned after it; the handler now saves V1FundingSigned with the funding tx in one save) becomes V1FundingSigned when a watched transaction with its funding txid and channel id is stored (the watch is saved before the publish, so the tx may be out) and is persisted Stale and not registered otherwise (BOLT 2: a funder that has not broadcast SHOULD NOT remember; NL-048 wontfix, its locked UTXOs are not released); V1FundingSigned, ReadyFor*, ShuttingDown, Negotiating, Closing (see "Cooperative close") and Failed are registered as they are. It also keeps `Failed` channels in memory, and for a channel with a snapshot runs `ChannelCommitments.RevertUncommitted` and persists it when it changed something (before any reestablish); for an `Open` channel it then derives the pending events (`ChannelDomainEvents.DerivePending` over the snapshot and the unpruned settled rows from `IChannelStateDbRepository.LoadAsync`; a failure is logged and skipped) and hands them to the switch **after** releasing the lock (I8). At startup no channel's link is up, so a replayed fail is refused (logged by the switch) and the HTLC stays locked in until the replay after N7's reestablish. `HandleChannelMessageAsync` (a channel that was not `Open` before the message and is after it) and the funding confirmation (after `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler`) call `IPeerLivenessProbe.MarkLinkUp` under the lock. Also handles blockchain events: `HandleNewBlockDetected` (ForgetStaleChannels, ConfirmUnconfirmedChannels) and `HandleFundingConfirmationAsync`.
- `Node/Managers/PeerManager.cs`: `ConcurrentDictionary<CompactPubKey, PeerSession>`; a session is one connection: the `PeerModel`, a bounded inbound queue (1024, the transport read loop waits when full) drained by one loop, a close token, and the connection's `PeerOutbox`. A connection becomes a session only after `IPeerService.WaitForInitAsync` (both inits exchanged; its preferred address and features come from that init): `ConnectToPeerAsync` returns only then and throws `ConnectionException` (after disposing the peer service) if the connection closes first, and an inbound connection is handled on its own task (`HandleNewPeerConnectedAsync`) and dropped with an info log in that case. So neither end ever keeps a connection the other cannot use (NL-240). `StopAsync` sets `_stopping` under the session lock and cancels the pending init waits (`WaitForInitAsync` gets a stopping token; the connection is disconnected and disposed); `TryInstallSession` refuses under the same lock once stopping (`InstallResult.Stopping`), so every installed session is in the disconnect pass, and `StopAsync` also waits (5 s timeout) for the inbound setups still running (`_inboundSetups`, registered before any of the setup runs), so no peer is saved to the database after it returns. Sessions subscribe to the peer service right after that (before any DB work; `PeerService` replays channel messages and a disconnection that happened before), and only then are installed. Closing a session cancels its loop (queued messages are dropped, the one being handled finishes); the next session of the same peer starts its loop only after the previous loop ended, and `StopAsync` waits for all loops. Replies raised while a loop handles a message go to that loop's session (an `AsyncLocal`), so they are dropped rather than sent on a newer connection. A new connection replaces the current one, except within `SimultaneousConnectWindow` (5 s) of an opposite-direction connection, where the connection initiated by the lower pubkey is kept (LND's `shouldDropLocalConnection`). A runtime drop of a peer with active channels in `IChannelMemoryRepository` starts the backoff reconnect loop unless the disconnect was ours (`DisconnectPeer`, `StopAsync`, replacement). It subscribes to `IChannelManager.OnResponseMessageReady` in its constructor and only enqueues there (never throws). Maps exceptions (through the outbox, so they follow the replies queued before them), always scoped to the message's channel id (never an all-zero `error`, BOLT 1): ChannelFailedException sends `error` for the channel and keeps the connection (N7), any other ChannelErrorException sends `error` for the channel and disconnects, ChannelWarningException sends a `warning` and stays connected (or disconnects after it when `CloseConnection` is set), any other exception (our bug) sends a channel `warning` and disconnects. `start_batch` (127) never reaches the channel manager as a message: the inbound loop groups it (SP1-A-T3, BOLT 2 "Batching channel messages", SP-OP-04): `batch_size` <= 1 is ignored with a (non-closing) channel `warning`; > 20 (`MaxBatchSize`) is a `warning` and close; a missing or non-132 `message_type` is ignored and the following messages are handled one by one; otherwise the next `batch_size` messages are collected (per connection, dropped with it) and handed to `IChannelManager.HandleCommitmentSignedBatchAsync` in one call as a `CommitmentSignedBatch`, its failures mapped like a single message's; a grouped message for another channel, or one that is not a `commitment_signed`, is a `warning` and close scoped to the batch's channel. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Node/Managers/PeerManagerBatchTests.cs`.
- Node write fence (NL-1341, Domain `Node/Fencing/INodeWriteFence`; optional, none registered by default): `PeerManager` resolves it lazily and hands it to every `PeerOutbox`, which asks `CheckEffectAsync(PeerSend)` before each message, gossip, warning, error and onion message (not before a disconnect); a refusal sends nothing, closes the outbox and disconnects the peer (`IPeerService.Disconnect` with the refusal). `PeerStorageService` asks it before our `peer_storage` backup (a fenced instance must not replace the peer's copy with an older one) and `InvoiceService` before `Invoice.Encode` signs a BOLT 11 invoice. Sends that bypass the outbox (gossip query replies and sync queries, init/ping/pong) are not fenced (NL-1343).
- `PeerManager.StartAsync` (NL-201): awaits `IChannelManager.RegisterExistingChannelAsync` (takes the channel lock; memory, plus the signer only when no `IChannelSigningInfoSource` is registered: the production signer loads channels from the database itself, NL-343; a source-less signer (in-process tests) is also registered again at the funding confirmation so it knows the real SCID) for every non-Closed/Stale channel of every stored peer before connecting to any of them; a failing channel is logged and skipped. A peer with such channels that can't be reached gets a background reconnect loop (`NodeOptions.ReconnectInitialDelay`, default 5 s, doubling up to `NodeOptions.ReconnectMaxDelay`, default 10 min; config `Node:ReconnectInitialDelay`/`Node:ReconnectMaxDelay`; `PeerManager` takes an optional `IOptions<NodeOptions>` and falls back to those defaults without it) that ends when the peer is connected (either direction) or `StopAsync` cancels it (StopAsync waits for the loops before disconnecting peers). Reestablish deadline (NL-796): after `StartReestablishAsync` each connection arms a timer (`Node:ReestablishTimeout`, default 60 s, zero = off; `TimeProvider` from the service provider, else the system clock) owned by its `PeerSession` and disposed on `Close`; when it fires on the still-current session and `IChannelManager.GetChannelsAwaitingPeerReestablish` lists a channel (ReadyForThem/ReadyForUs/Open/ShuttingDown/Negotiating, our `channel_reestablish` sent on this connection, not reestablished; never V1FundingSigned or Closing), one connection-level `warning` goes out through the outbox and the connection closes without `SuppressReconnect`, so `ReconnectIfNeeded` dials again; the channel stays gated meanwhile.
- `Node/Bootstrap/PeerBootstrapService` (BOLT 10, NL-113, the graph top-up, NL-543, and the peer-count keeper, NL-547; Domain `IPeerBootstrapService`, singleton in `AddApplicationServices`): started by `NltgDaemonService` after `PeerManager.StartAsync` (a background task, never awaited; `StopAsync` cancels the loop and the keeper and waits up to 5 s for both, before the peer manager stops). Does nothing unless `BootstrapOptions.IsEnabledOn(network)` (`Node:Bootstrap:Enabled`; unset = on on mainnet only, owner decision 2026-09-28) (then it warns once if `BootstrapOptions.ObsoleteSeedsIgnored`: an operator-edited obsolete `Node:DnsSeedServers`, which is never used). Before every run of the initial phase (after `StartupDelay`, then every `RetryInterval`, at most `MaxRuns`) it checks the gate. Final reasons end the initial phase: no effective seeds for the network (`BootstrapOptions.GetEffectiveSeeds`: mainnet/testnet defaults; configured seeds ignored with one warning elsewhere unless `AllowSeedsOnThisNetwork`) when no `IGraphStore` is registered either, and `MinPeers` connected. The others skip that run only (logged at Information once, repeats at Debug) and count toward `MaxRuns`: chain processing halted (`IBlockchainMonitor`, optional), a saved dialable peer with an active (not Closed/Stale) channel that is not connected (the peer manager keeps reconnecting those; a channel-less saved peer is dialed once at start and never again, so it does not count; `IUnitOfWork.GetPeersForStartupAsync` in a scope). Graph top-up (NL-543, same switch as the bootstrap, owner choice 2026-09-28): a run first asks the Domain `GraphPeerCandidateSelector` over `IGraphStore.GetSnapshot()` (optional) for announced nodes without unknown even features, not ourselves or connected, with an IPv4/IPv6 address of `AddressFamilies` that passes `SeedAddressFilter` (`AllowNonRoutableAddresses` lifts it; Tor and DNS hostnames skipped) and did not fail within `FailedEndpointTtl`, and at least one active channel (unspent, its own direction's update enabled and at most 14 days old); good ones (announcement at most 14 days old, 2+ active channels) first, random within each tier, one per endpoint, `MaxPeersFromBootstrap x 3` at most. It dials them (seed `GraphPeerCandidateSelector.GraphSource` = `graph` in the dial records) until the peers missing to `MinPeers` connect, with the same `DialAsync` limits; only when the node is still below `MinPeers` does the run ask the seeds, for at most `MaxPeersFromBootstrap` minus the graph's connections. `BootstrapRunRecord.GraphSelected/GraphAttempted/GraphConnected/AskedSeeds` record it. Peer-count keeper (NL-547, same switch, no new key): the initial phase still ends at `MinPeers` or `MaxRuns` (which bounds only that phase), then a second background task (`Keeper`) runs for the process lifetime unless the phase ended with no seeds and no graph store: every `MaintenanceInterval` (5 min) `MaintainOnceAsync` (internal, tests call it) does nothing at `MinPeers` (and resets the backoff), waits while backing off, skips on the same gate (halted chain, saved channel peers being reconnected; no backoff for those), else runs the same top-up (`RunOnceAsync(..., maintenance: true)`: graph, then seeds, same limits; records `BootstrapRunRecord.Maintenance`). A top-up that leaves the node short sets the next one after `MaintenanceInterval`, doubling per short top-up up to `MaxMaintenanceBackoff` (1 h), reset once `MinPeers` are connected; at zero peers it warns once per backoff step (not again at the cap), else Information/Debug, never Error. Failed endpoints (graph and seeds) are kept with their failure time and skipped for `FailedEndpointTtl` (1 h), at most `PeerBootstrapService.MaxFailedEndpoints` (1,024), the oldest failure forgotten first. `PeerBootstrapStatus.Maintaining`/`NextTopUpAt` report it. Optional `TimeProvider` constructor argument (backoff, TTL, record times). Polling only: no peer-disconnect event is used. The seed part of a run queries the seeds one at a time in random order until `MaxPeersFromBootstrap x 3` candidates, drops connected, saved and our own node ids and endpoints whose dial failed within `FailedEndpointTtl`, keeps one address per node and one node per (address, port), caps each seed at `ceil(MaxPeersFromBootstrap / seeds) + 1` when several answered, and dials in batches of `MaxDialConcurrency` through `IPeerManager.DialPeerAsync` with a token cancelled after `BootstrapOptions.GetEffectiveConnectTimeout(NetworkTimeout)` (`ConnectTimeout`, 30 s, never less than `NetworkTimeout`; a shorter one is a validation error only with an explicit `Enabled = true`) until `MaxPeersFromBootstrap` succeed. `GetStatus()` returns what it did (`PeerBootstrapStatus`: the runs with their skip reasons, every seed query with its outcome, candidates, rejected count, resolver (`UsedFallbackResolver`, `SystemResolverOutcome`) and time, every dial with its outcome (`Connected`, `AlreadyConnected`, `Failed`, `TimedOut` when the connect timeout cancelled it) and time, the loop's end reason; at most 1,000 records per list, memory only); the gossip probe's `--bootstrap` mode reads it. `PeerManager.DialPeerAsync` abandons the TCP connect, handshake or init wait on cancellation and closes what it opened, so a timed-out dial never outlives its slot or becomes a session or a saved peer; once the session is installed it completes (`ConnectToPeerAsync(info)` is `DialPeerAsync(info, None)`). The peer manager saves each connected peer. Routine failures log at Warning/Debug, never Error. NL-541 (BOLT 10 assisted location): `IPeerBootstrapService.LocatePeerAsync(nodeId, ct)` asks the effective seeds the node's virtual host (`IDnsSeedClient.LocateNodeAsync`, Domain `DnsSeedNodeLocation`) and returns the deduped usable endpoints (empty = no seed knows the node; the queries are recorded in `PeerBootstrapStatus.SeedQueries` as run 0; it dials nothing and works with the bootstrap off, but never in Tor-only mode). The initial phase and the keeper run `LocateReconnectingPeersAsync` (internal) when the gate's skip is the reconnecting-peers one (`Gate.LocatePeers`, so a halted chain locates nothing): at most `PeerBootstrapService.MaxLocatedPeersPerCheck` (4) saved channel peers are located and the answers that are neither the saved endpoint (the peer manager is already dialing it) nor an endpoint that failed within `FailedEndpointTtl` are dialed, plus the saved port on each located address when the seed answered only the default port (the live seeds answer the addresses but not the per-node SRV port). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Node/Bootstrap/PeerBootstrapServiceTests` (graph top-up region with real `GraphSnapshot`s; keeper region with a manual clock), Domain `Node/Bootstrap/GraphPeerCandidateSelectorTests`, `Node/Managers/PeerManagerTests` (`Given_ADialWhose*`).
- `Channels/Fees/` (BOLT2 plan N9-T1/N9-T3, ABCD W3-C): `FeeUpdatePolicy` (pure: funder only; target = estimate (times `NonAnchorFeerateMarginPercent`, default 200, without anchors: the BOLT 2 "significant margin") clamped to [max(`Node:FeeUpdates:MinFeeratePerKw`, `Node:MinCommitmentFeeRatePerKw` (275, NL-564)) >= 253, `MaxFeeratePerKw` 50,000 / `MaxAnchorFeeratePerKw` 2,500 with anchors]; moves only when the target differs from `ChannelCommitments.LatestFeeratePerKw` by >= `ThresholdPercent` (20) or the feerate is below the minimum; an increase must pass the engine's `SendFee` (B2-FEE-R03, our reserve) and, without anchors, the dust limit at the new feerate (B2-DUST-05), else the highest sendable feerate in between is used (binary search) or nothing), `DustExposurePolicy` (pure: `Resolve(commitments, nodeLimit)` = the snapshot's `CommitmentParams.MaxDustHtlcExposureMsat` ?? `NodeOptions.MaxDustHtlcExposureMsat` (the fallback covers only the receive and fee checks: the engine's send rules B2-DUST-03/04 read the stored value, so a snapshot without one offers unlimited dust until it is backfilled); `ProspectiveExposureMsat`, `CheckFeeIncrease`, and `CheckLockedInIncoming` = B2-DUST-01/02 for a locked-in incoming HTLC, counting only the outgoing HTLCs and the incoming ones with a lower id, so replays give the same answer), `FeeUpdateScheduler : IFeeUpdateScheduler` (singleton; `StartAsync` runs a round every `Node:FeeUpdates:Interval` (10 min) unless `Enabled` is false, `RunOnceAsync` runs one now and returns a `FeeUpdateOutcome` per channel we fund (`Open`, snapshot, no data loss); estimate from `IFeeService.GetFeeRatePerKwAsync` (0 or an exception: nothing); sends through `IChannelOperations.UpdateFeeAsync`, so the commit scheduler signs it; refusals are logged and retried next round) and `DustExposureHtlcSwitch` (an `IHtlcSwitch` decorator: an `IncomingHtlcLockedIn` over the dust limit is failed with `temporary_channel_failure` (onion peeled with `replayOwner: null` (no replay check) for the shared secret; a malformed onion is left to the switch) before the switch can forward it or reveal the preimage; never when the switch already stored the HTLC's onion secret or a forward circuit; serialized per incoming HTLC). `ChannelFeeServiceCollectionExtensions.AddChannelFeeServices()` registers the scheduler and decorates the registered switch (idempotent; call it after `AddHtlcSwitchServices()`); the host must call `IFeeUpdateScheduler.StartAsync` after `PeerManager.StartAsync` and `StopAsync` on shutdown. The send side of the dust rule (B2-DUST-03/04) is the engine's (`UpdateValidator`, from the snapshot's params); `ChannelReadyMessageHandler` stores `NodeOptions.MaxDustHtlcExposureMsat` with the first snapshot (`ChannelStateTransitionService.CreateInitialCommitments(..., maxDustHtlcExposureMsat)`, NL-254). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Fees/` (policy tables, scheduler with mocks, `FeeUpdateHarnessTests` on `TwoNodeHarness` incl. a crossing add, `DustExposureHtlcSwitchTests`, `FirstSnapshotDustPolicyTests`); Docker `Docker/FeeUpdateFlowTests`.
- `Gossip/` (ABCD W1-E, NL-099 subset: direct `channel_update` exchange on our unannounced channels): `Interfaces/IChannelUpdateService`, `Services/ChannelUpdateService` (singleton), `Events/ChannelUpdateReadyEventArgs`, and `GossipServiceCollectionExtensions.AddGossipServices()` (TryAdd, so idempotent; called from `AddApplicationServices`). `PeerManager` takes it as an optional last constructor parameter: without it nothing is sent or stored. The service subscribes to `IChannelMemoryRepository.OnChannelUpdated`; the first time a channel is seen `Open` with a short channel id it runs `SendChannelUpdateAsync` on another task, which takes the channel's lock first (the event is raised under the lock by the handler that opened the channel, before it queues its own channel_ready, so our update always follows channel_ready in the outbox), builds and signs the update (`must_be_one|dont_forward`, `direction` = 1 when our node id is the greater, the real scid or, for an `option_scid_alias` channel (`ChannelParams.UseScidAlias` > No), the peer's alias `RemoteAlias` (BOLT 2 forbids routing into it by the real scid), fee/CLTV delta from `NodeOptions.Routing`, `htlc_minimum_msat` = max(peer's htlc_minimum, `Routing.HtlcMinimumMsat`), `htlc_maximum_msat` = min(capacity, peer's max_htlc_value_in_flight, `Routing.HtlcMaximumMsat`), timestamp = UNIX time but always above the previous one; no update at all, with a warning log, when the minimum is above that maximum or an alias channel has no `RemoteAlias` yet: `CreateChannelUpdate` throws `InvalidOperationException` there) and raises `OnChannelUpdateReady`, which `PeerManager` turns into `PeerOutbox.TryEnqueueGossip`. `HandleRemoteChannelUpdate(peer, message)` keeps the peer's update only if it is for our chain, names a channel with that peer (real scid, our `LocalAliases` or its `RemoteAlias`), has the peer's direction, verifies with the peer's node id (`ILightningSigner.VerifyNodeMessage`), is newer, is at most `ChannelUpdateService.MaxFutureTimestamp` (14 days) ahead of our clock and has `htlc_maximum_msat` <= capacity; `TryGetRemoteChannelUpdate`/`TryGetLocalChannelUpdate` read them (for route hints and BOLT 4 UPDATE failures). Resend: every session `PeerManager` installs (after init; also after a reconnect or a restart) runs `SendChannelUpdatesToPeerAsync(peer)` on another task: for each `Open` channel with that peer (under its channel lock) the last update again if its policy is still current (same timestamp, as LND does on reconnect), else a new one. LND ignores a same-policy "keep-alive" update younger than 24 h, so only a policy change (e.g. new `Routing` options after a restart) shows up there. The peer's update is kept in memory only (forgotten on restart until it sends a new one). Note LND never puts an unannounced node in `addinvoice --private` hints (it requires `IsPublicNode`), so that needs `node_announcement` (NL-099). Docker proof: `Docker/ChannelUpdateExchangeTests` (LND `GetChanInfo` shows our policy; we stored LND's). Offline peers (NL-349, BOLT 7 plan G1-T5): with an `IServiceProvider` (DI passes it; tests pass one holding an `IPeerLivenessProbe`) and `GossipOptions.DisableAfter` > 0 (`Gossip:DisableAfter`, 20 min, zero turns it off) the constructor starts a timer on the system clock (the offline time is measured with the injected `TimeProvider`, so a test clock's timers stay the test's own) every `GetOfflineCheckInterval` (a quarter of `DisableAfter`, 1 s to 1 min) that runs `internal CheckOfflinePeersAsync` (`LastOfflineCheck` for tests): an open announced channel (`IsPublic`) whose link (`IPeerLivenessProbe.IsAliveAsync`, resolved lazily) stays down for `DisableAfter`, counted from the first check that found it down, gets one disabled update under its lock, to the relay only (the offline start and the probe are checked again under the lock, so a reconnection racing the check disables nothing); it is enabled again with a newer update (to the peer and the relay) only by a check that finds the link up (after the reestablish): the peer's next connection (`SendChannelUpdatesToPeerAsync`) gets the disabled update as is and restarts the offline time, so a failed reestablish never advertises the channel enabled. Memory only.
- `Gossip/Announcements/` (BOLT 7 plan G1, gossip wave G-B lane B1; registered by `AnnouncementServiceCollectionExtensions.AddGossipAnnouncementServices()`, called from `AddGossipServices`): `ChannelAnnouncementBuilder` (static: the unsigned `channel_announcement` both ends build (empty features, no trailing data, `node_id_1` the lesser compressed key, `bitcoin_key_N` its funding key), the checks of the peer's half, `Assemble` (each half in its node's slot) and the four checks of an assembled one), `IChannelAnnouncementService` → `ChannelAnnouncementService` (singleton; call it under the channel lock; persists nothing): `CanSendAnnouncementSignatures` (public, `Open` (both channel_ready), no shutdown script on either side, no data loss, SCID known and `GossipOptions.GetAnnouncementDepth` confirmations at `IBlockchainMonitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight`: 6, only regtest may configure less), `CreateAnnouncementSignatures` (signed by `ILightningSigner.SignChannelAnnouncement`), `VerifyRemoteSignatures`, the per-connection record (`WasSentOnConnection`/`MarkSentOnConnection`/`OnPeerConnectionChanged`, reset by `ChannelManager.OnPeerConnectedAsync` and `OnPeerConnectionChanged`: our half is sent at most once per connection, so two nodes never ping-pong), `TryAssembleAnnouncement` (only when we sent ours and hold theirs, at depth; re-signs ours (RFC 6979, deterministic) and verifies all four signatures, so a half stored for another SCID gives null) and `OnChannelAnnounced` (hands a new announcement to the Domain `IOwnGossipSink` with the capacity, once per announcement). `NullOwnGossipSink` is the `TryAdd` default until the graph store (G2-T4) registers the real sink. `CanSendAnnouncementSignatures` is also false on mainnet unless `Gossip:AllowPublicChannelsOnMainnet` (`GossipOptions.ArePublicChannelsAllowed`, plan D12; a peer's public `open_channel` is refused there, so no channel waits for a half we never send). A stored peer half that does not verify for the channel's current SCID (stored before our funding confirmation, or before a reorg) is forgotten by `TryAssembleAnnouncement` (only the peer's half: our sent time is kept), so `IsAnnounced`/`ChannelUpdateService.IsPublic` turn false and ours is due again on the next connection; `CompleteAnnouncementAsync` and the handler persist that. `OnShortChannelIdChanged(channelId)` (NL-350) forgets the per-connection record and the completed announcement of a channel whose SCID a reorg moved, so ours is due again at the new depth on the current connection.
  - G1-T4 (gossip wave G-B step 2): `PrepareOwnAnnouncementSignaturesAsync(channel, peer, unitOfWork)` returns our half when it is due on the peer's current connection (may be sent now, not sent on this connection, and the two halves not both exchanged before: sent once at the depth, again on every reconnection while the peer's half is missing), with the sent time saved and the per-connection record set; `CompleteAnnouncementAsync(channel, unitOfWork)` assembles and hands on a channel whose halves are both in (`static IsAnnounced(channel)`: public, SCID, both halves, Open/ShuttingDown/Negotiating) once per process (`IsAnnouncementComplete`). `ChannelManager` drives it: after a processed `channel_reestablish` (when the tracker marked it reestablished, or without a tracker) and when a message turned the channel Open, our half is appended to the replies and the announcement completed after they are raised; on every new block (`HandleNewBlockDetected` → `ScheduleAnnouncementRound`, `internal Task AnnouncementRound` for tests) every Open public channel not complete in this process is handled under its lock (skipped while the channel is not reestablished on its peer's current connection: the reestablish sends it) and our `node_announcement` refresh is requested when a channel is complete. `OnChannelAnnounced` hands the 256 to `OwnGossipPublisher` (graph sink + relay), then `IChannelUpdateService.OnChannelAnnounced` and `INodeAnnouncementService.RequestAnnouncement`.
  - `OwnGossipPublisher` (singleton): our 256/258/257 to `IOwnGossipSink` and `IGossipRelayScheduler`.
  - `INodeAnnouncementService` → `NodeAnnouncementService` (G1-T6, singleton): only with an announced channel (`IsAnnounced`); features `NodeOptions.Features.GetNodeFeatures(FeatureContext.NodeAnnouncement).GetWireBytes()`, `Node:Alias` (`EncodeAlias`), `Node:Color` (`GetColorBytes`), `GossipOptions.GetAnnounceAddressDescriptors()` (none by default, D11) merged with every `IAnnouncedAddressSource` (our Tor onion service, NL-569; `MergeAddresses`: once each, type order, never a second DNS name; a source's `AnnouncedAddressesChanged` requests a new announcement); timestamp = max(now, stored + 1, last + 1) where the stored one is our node's `GraphNodes` row (`IUnitOfWork.GraphDbRepository`), written (with the raw announcement) in its own scope and save **before** the announcement is published; a current one with the same fields younger than `Gossip:NodeAnnouncementRefreshInterval` (13 days) is only published again. `RequestAnnouncement` runs it on the thread pool (`internal Task LastRequest`). A failed save publishes nothing.
- `Gossip/Relay/` (G1-T7 own gossip, G3-T3 others' gossip; `RelayServiceCollectionExtensions.AddGossipRelayServices()`, called by `AddGossipAnnouncementServices`, registers the scheduler, `GossipOriginTracker`, `IGossipPeerSender` and the directory; the daemon also calls `AddGossipRelayOriginTracking()` after `AddGossipGraphServices` and binds `GossipRelayOptions` from `Gossip`): `IGossipRelayScheduler` → `GossipRelayScheduler` (singleton, partial: `GossipRelayScheduler.cs` own path, `GossipRelayScheduler.Relay.cs` others). **Own path:** our own 256/258/257 queued (the latest per SCID, SCID+direction and node; an older 258/257 is ignored), flushed every `Gossip:OwnGossipFlushInterval` (60 s; the timer starts with the first message; `FlushAsync` flushes now) to every connected peer whose `init` networks include our chain (or name none), regardless of filters (B7-Q-05), in the order 256 → 258 → 257, a 256 only once an update for its SCID is queued, our 257 only once one of our 256 went out on that connection (earlier or in the same flush; BOLT 7: a node_announcement for a node without a known channel is ignored), each message once per connection (tracked per `IPeerService`, weakly: a new connection gets everything again). **Others' gossip** (only with an `IGraphStore`, an `IGossipSyncManager` and `GossipRelayOptions.IsRelayEnabledFor` (`Gossip:RelayEnabled` unset = on everywhere, mainnet included since the NL-417 proof); `IsRelayingOthers`): a timer every `RelayTickInterval` (1 s, started by the first `FilterReceived`) runs `RelayTickAsync` (internal, tests call it with a manual clock): every `RelayCollectInterval` (10 s) the graph snapshot is diffed against the versions seen before (256 by presence, 258 per direction and 257 by timestamp; the first scan is only a baseline, so a restart does not re-relay the stored graph) and what changed goes into the pending set (newest per key) of every connection with a filter; a 256 counts as seen only once its channel has a relayable update (a 256 flushed alone is dropped, so it goes out with the update that arrives in a later window); spent, `Unverified`, `dont_forward` and our own messages (our node id from the optional `ISecureKeyManager`: our 256, our direction's 258 and our 257 stay with the own path) are never relayed. Each connection flushes every `RelayFlushInterval` (60 s) at its own phase (`GetRelayPhase(nodeId)`, from the node id: staggered): only inside its `gossip_timestamp_filter` (`GossipTimestampFilter.Includes`, `first <= ts < first + range`; a 256 takes its forwardable updates' timestamps and never goes out without one; a 257 only for a node that still has a channel), 256s then 258s then 257s, never to a connection without a filter (B7-RL-01) or off our chain, never a version the peer sent us (origin suppression: `OriginTrackingGossipIngress`, the peer services' `IGossipIngress` decorator over `GossipIngress`, records every 256/257/258 a peer hands over in `GossipOriginTracker`, keyed by type + channel/direction/node + timestamp, bounded by `MaxTrackedOrigins` 100,000, oldest forgotten first). A new filter (`IGossipSyncManager.FilterReceived`) replaces the pending set with a backlog: one lazy pass over the snapshot taken at the next tick (per channel by SCID its 256 then its updates inside the filter, then the node announcements), paced at `BacklogMessagesPerSecond` (1,000) per tick-second. **Send path (NL-351):** everything goes through `IGossipPeerSender` → `PeerGossipSender` (`SendAsync(peer, message, size)` → `GossipEnqueueResult`, `GetDepth(peer)`): `IPeerGossipOutbox.EnqueueGossip(connection, message, size)` (Domain port; resolved lazily from the provider) when one is registered, so gossip is FIFO with the peer's `PeerOutbox`; else `IPeerService.SendGossipMessageAsync` awaited (the pre-G3 behavior). The port is meant for `PeerManager` (the current session's outbox when the given connection is still the current one); `PeerManager` implements it (only the peer's current connection; a replaced one gets false) and `AddApplicationServices` registers it as the peer manager, so the direct send is only the fallback without a registration (tests); each connection's share of a relay tick (backlog, flush) runs on its own task and the tick waits at most `GossipRelayOptions.RelaySendWait` (2 s) for them: a connection whose run is still sending (a stalled transport write on the direct path) is skipped by later ticks until the run ends, so one slow peer never stops the relay to the others (the own path still sends peer by peer); `Gone` or a throwing send ends that connection's flush. **Backpressure (NL-360):** `Full` pauses the connection instead: the refused backlog message is held (`RelayPeerState.HeldBacklogItem`) and sent first on resume, the rest of an interrupted flush goes back into the pending set ahead of what was collected since (`PutBack`: older, so the `MaxRelayPendingPerPeer` bound evicts it first, a 256 with its 258s) and the flush continues at the next tick (`NextFlushAt` is not advanced); while paused no backlog or flush runs, and each tick resumes it once `GetDepth` shows at most `GossipRelayOptions.RelayResumePercent` (50) of the caps (`GossipOutboxDepth.IsAtOrBelow`); a paused connection whose `SentMessages` do not move for `RelayStallTimeout` (10 min, 0 = off) stalls once (`Stall`: backlog ended, which releases its snapshot, pending dropped as `relay_stalled`, logged at Information; the connection is kept); NL-548: no flush runs while a backlog does (what was collected waits), after a stall a later update of a channel past the backlog's position (`RelayPeerState.TruncatedAt`) goes out after its announcement and a node announcement only for a node with a channel the peer got, and node announcements (backlog and flush) go only for nodes with a relayable channel; our own gossip stops at the first refusal and the rest goes at the next own flush. `GetStatus()` (`GossipRelayStatus`: relay on, pending, paused connections, the connected peers' outbox messages and bytes) feeds `describegraph` (keys 29-34); counters `relay.paused`, `relay.stalled`, gauge `relay.paused_connections`. Tests: `GossipRelayBackpressureTests` (a `CappedOutboxSender` fake whose peer reads on demand, plus the production `PeerOutbox`). `IGossipPeerDirectory` → `PeerManagerGossipPeerDirectory` (`IPeerManager.ListPeers`, resolved lazily: the peer manager depends on the relay through the channel update service). Tests: `Gossip/Relay/GossipRelaySchedulerTests` (own path), `GossipRelayOthersTests` (`SyncTestGraph` store, a mocked sync manager for the filters, `RelayTestClock` whose timers never fire: no relay before a filter, order, `>=`/`<` boundaries, the nothing filter, origin suppression (and a newer version to the origin), newest-only, baseline, spent/unverified/dont_forward, other chain, staggered phases, the paced backlog, our own channel, the outbox path and a closed outbox, relay off), `GossipOriginTrackerTests`.
- Gossip DoS limits and metrics (BOLT7 plan §3.8, G5-T1/G5-T2 and the metrics half of G5-T4, gossip wave G-D lane D1). All in `GossipIngress` with the `GossipGraphOptions` keys (`Gossip:*`): per-peer queue `MaxQueuedPerPeer` (2,000) and global `MaxQueued` (20,000), excess dropped and handed to the sync as missed SCIDs; `GossipRateLimiter` (wall clock, only accepted messages spend: checked after the signature, spent right before the apply): `channel_update` per (scid, direction) a token bucket of `ChannelUpdateBurst` (4) refilled one per `ChannelUpdateRateInterval` (60 s), `node_announcement` one per node per `NodeAnnouncementRateInterval` (10 min); the newest validly signed message the rate refused is kept per channel direction / node (at most `MaxRateLimited`, 10,000; beyond it counted as `rate_limited_full`) and queued again by the write-behind loop once a token is back (`ReplayRateLimited`, queue `rate_limited`), so the last policy of a burst is not lost; a keep-alive `channel_update` (`GraphPolicy.HasSameFieldsAs` the stored one) only when more than `KeepAliveMinInterval` (24 h) newer; timestamps more than `MaxFutureTimestamp` (14 days, `ChannelUpdateService.MaxFutureTimestamp`) ahead dropped (257 in the ingress, 258 through the validator's `MaxFutureSkew`); `MaxChannels` (200,000) / `MaxNodes` (100,000) from `IGraphStore.ChannelCount`/`NodeCount`: a new channel (before its signatures and chain lookup) or a new node's announcement beyond them is refused (logged at the first and every 1,000th); our own gossip (`ApplyOwnAsync`) bypasses every limit. `GossipMisbehaviourTracker` (keyed by the peer's node id, so it survives reconnections): every `warning` outcome (invalid signatures, bad encodings) counts one; a funding output that contradicts its announcement (`ScriptMismatch`, `AmountMismatch`, `TransactionIndexOutOfRange`; like `OutputSpentOrMissing` or an unreadable block) proves the announcement false but never scores anybody, since honest peers relay unchecked announcements too (NL-371); `MisbehaviourThreshold` (5) inside `MisbehaviourWindow` (10 min) bans the peer for `MisbehaviourBanDuration` (1 h): an in-memory peer ban (at most `MaxMisbehaviourBans`, 10,000, the one ending first makes room; ended ones pruned by the write-behind loop) and, only when the peer is a node of the graph, `IGraphStore.Ban` (persisted in `GraphBannedNodes`; the node's own gossip is then ignored too, as for B7-CA-04), so throwaway node ids cost neither rows nor unbounded memory; the offence that reaches the threshold disconnects with one `warning` carrying its own text and "Too much invalid gossip" (scored before the warning goes out: a connection closes only with its first `Disconnect`); for the rest of the ban (a restart keeps only the persisted node ban) its queued messages are dropped by the workers and its new ones by `TryEnqueue`, without validation and without disconnecting the new connection (it may carry our channels); a banned peer's messages that were already past the worker's ban check are never scored again, so the ban is neither renewed nor sent a second `Disconnect` (NL-746); a node blacklisted by B7-CA-04 still relays other nodes' gossip. `GossipIngressResult.LimitReason` (a `Metrics/GossipMetricReasons` value) says why the ingress itself refused a message; `MetricReason` is it or the validator's reason in snake_case (`GossipMetrics.TagValue`, also used for the chain lookup `status`: every tag value is snake_case). `Metrics/GossipMetrics` (singleton, `AddGossipMetrics()`, called by `AddGossipGraphServices` and `AddGossipRelayServices`; the ingress, the relay scheduler and the sync manager take it as an optional last constructor argument): `Meter("NLightning.Gossip")` with `nlightning.gossip.messages.{received,accepted,rejected,orphaned,dropped,relayed}` (tags `type`, `reason`, `path` own/others), `nlightning.gossip.chain.lookups` (`status`), `nlightning.gossip.peers.banned`, `nlightning.gossip.sync.duration` (s, `outcome` completed/failed), `nlightning.gossip.store.duration` (s, `operation` load/flush, `outcome`; recorded by `GraphStore`, which takes the meter as an optional last constructor argument) and the observable `nlightning.gossip.queue.depth` (`queue`: ingress, orphans, retries, rate_limited, relay_pending, graph_write_behind; `RegisterQueue`). Relay bound: each connection keeps at most `GossipRelayOptions.MaxRelayPendingPerPeer` (5,000, `Gossip:MaxRelayPendingPerPeer`) messages waiting for its flush; beyond it the oldest `node_announcement` goes first, then the oldest channel message (insertion order; a replaced key counts as new), a `channel_announcement` together with its waiting updates, counted as `relay_backlog_full`; a dropped 256's SCID is remembered per connection and a later 258 of it is flushed after the 256 again (from the graph), so a peer that stops reading gets at most that many queued on its `PeerOutbox` per flush; since NL-360 the outbox itself is capped (`Gossip:MaxOutboxGossipPerPeer` 10,000 / `MaxOutboxGossipBytesPerPeer` 4 MiB, about 9 MB of heap when full) and the relay pauses a full connection, backlog included (see `Gossip/Relay/` above; `GossipRelayOptions.RelayResumePercent`, `RelayStallTimeout`). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Gossip/Graph/GossipIngressLimitsTests` (each limit, the caps, the score and the ban), `GossipFloodTests` (the G5-T2 proof: a seeded fuzz flood of invalid signatures through the real queues and two workers bans the flooder while another peer's valid gossip is all applied), `GossipRateLimiterTests`, `Gossip/Relay/GossipRelayBacklogBoundTests`, `Gossip/Metrics/GossipMetricsTests` (`GossipMetricsRecorder`: a `MeterListener` on one instance's meter only, since parallel tests create meters of the same name).
- `Gossip/Sync/` (BOLT7 plan G3-T1/G3-T2 and the responder half of G3-T4, gossip wave G-C lane C1; `SyncServiceCollectionExtensions.AddGossipSyncServices()`, called by the daemon's `AddNltgNodeServices` next to the graph, which also binds and validates `GossipSyncOptions` from the `Gossip` section): `GossipSyncManager` (singleton; itself, `Interfaces/IGossipSyncManager` and the Domain `IGossipSyncService` the peer services get from `PeerServiceFactory`). One session per connection (keyed by the `IPeerService` instance, dropped on its `OnDisconnect`) with two loops that start only after `OnPeerInitialized` (BOLT 1: init first): the responder answers the peer's 263/261 in arrival order through `QueryResponder` (all replies of one query back to back, never at the relay flush; more than `MaxQueuedQueriesPerPeer` (4) waiting → `warning`, dropped; a malformed query → `warning`, the next is still answered), the querier runs our work one item at a time (B7-Q-01/Q-03: never a second query before the first is answered): at init a `gossip_queries` peer (negotiated: both offer it) becomes a sync peer while fewer than `SyncPeers` (3) are connected: `query_channel_range(0, tip + 1)` (tip from `IBlockchainMonitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight`, the whole u32 range without one; `query_option` timestamps when `gossip_queries_ex` is negotiated), replies checked by `RangeReplyCollector` (B7-Q-04: chain, first reply starts the range, non-decreasing `first_blocknum`, final covers the query (capped at 0xFFFFFFFF as LND), fields decode; a violation → `warning`, sync ended, nothing queried), diff against the graph (unknown SCIDs; with timestamps also known ones whose update is newer, as `query_flags` bits; spent ones never), `query_short_channel_ids` batches of `MaxScidsPerQuery` (8,000, fewer with flags so they fit 65,535 bytes), then once per connection `gossip_timestamp_filter(start - SyncFilterBacklog (2 weeks), 0xFFFFFFFF)` (with `gossip_queries_ex` timestamps: `start - TimestampSyncFilterMarginSeconds` (600), since the sync already asked for every newer update; `start` = when the range query went out) and `HasCompletedInitialSync` (our `full_information` = 1 from then on); other `gossip_queries` peers get `(now, 0xFFFFFFFF)`, a peer without it `(0xFFFFFFFF, 0)` (B7-Q-06). This replaces wave G-B's bootstrap `(0, 0xFFFFFFFF)`. A reply that does not come within `SyncReplyTimeout` (2 min) ends the sync without a warning, and a reply that breaks the rules ends it with one; either way the connection's query slot is poisoned (`PeerSession.IsQuerySlotPoisoned`): the peer may still be answering, and BOLT 7 forbids a second query before the end marker (a late `reply_short_channel_ids_end` would complete the next query), so nothing more is queried on that connection (queued work is dropped, `QueryScidAsync`, the missed-SCID retry and the rotation skip it); a sync peer whose range sync failed gets `gossip_timestamp_filter(now, 0xFFFFFFFF)` after the warning (LND/CLN relay nothing before a filter). Replies nobody waits for are ignored. Backpressure (NL-353): `AddGossipSyncServices` hands the manager `GossipIngress.QueuedCount` and min(`MaxQueuedPerPeer`, `MaxQueued`); each `query_short_channel_ids` then asks for at most capacity / 2 / 5 channels (a channel brings up to 5 messages; 200 by default) and waits (polling every 100 ms) until the queried peer's own ingress queue (`GossipIngress.QueuedCountOf`, NL-412) is at most half full, without a time limit, so a sync's answers fit in what the ingress keeps and other peers' gossip never holds a query back (without the per-peer depth: the whole queue, at most `SyncReplyTimeout`, then it sends anyway). The missed-SCID retry also asks again for stored channels that lack a policy in one direction (a dropped `channel_update`, NL-410). NL-415 (D12 lane Z3): the per-batch re-diff also skips an unknown channel whose announcement is already on its way: claimed by another session's batch (`Sync/QueriedChannelTracker`, shared by all sessions: a batch claims its unknown channels atomically when it is built, the claim is renewed at the peer's `reply_short_channel_ids_end` and lasts `Gossip:QueriedChannelTtl` (10 min, 0 = off) from then, a failed or unanswered query releases it at once, an end with `full_information` = 0 ends it at once (BOLT 7: the peer lacks up-to-date channel information); a range-sync claim is recyclable: when it ends, `RetryMissedShortChannelIds` puts its channel into the missed backlog if it is still not in the graph, so a peer that answers without sending an announcement delays the channel by at most the TTL plus one retry interval (the retry's own claims are not recycled, so a channel no peer delivers is asked for again once); the first diff sees only the graph, so a released channel is still asked for by a later batch) or pending in a Domain `IGossipPendingChannels` (`AddGossipSyncServices` passes every registered one plus the ingress and the funding output lookup when they implement it: `FundingOutputLookup` does (lookups waiting or running, mempool-spent answers until the next block) and `GossipIngress` does since NL-420 (a channel counts as pending while a message of it sits in a worker queue or is being processed, or its announcement waits in the pending index)). The missed-SCID retry skips (and keeps in its backlog) channels a pending source holds, so a channel whose funding output is spent in the mempool is asked for again only after the next block (NL-414). A source that throws counts as not pending. Timers (started with the first peer, stopped by `Dispose`): every `SyncRotationInterval` (20 min) `RotateSyncPeer()` runs the range query on the connected `gossip_queries` peer synced longest ago; every `MissedScidRetryInterval` (1 min) `RetryMissedShortChannelIds()` asks an idle peer again for the SCIDs `GossipIngress.TakeMissedShortChannelIds()` handed over that are still not in the graph (NL-353; kept in a backlog while no peer can be asked). NL-718: the wait for the late reply of a query we gave up on (NL-365) is bounded by another `SyncReplyTimeout`; then the connection's query slot is poisoned (nothing more asked on it), instead of the next query waiting forever. NL-407: the querier keeps `Gossip:MinQueryInterval` (250 ms, 0 = off) between two of its queries (`query_channel_range` and `query_short_channel_ids`) on one connection (`PaceQueryAsync`, `PeerSession.LastQueryAt`, on the manager's `TimeProvider`): Eclair answers at most 5 gossip queries per second per connection and silently drops the rest (no `reply_short_channel_ids_end`), which poisoned the query slot after four quick 200-channel batches; the sync tests set it to zero unless they test the pacing. `QueryScidAsync(scid)` (for G3-T5) queues a one-SCID query on the least busy `gossip_queries` peer and completes true on its `reply_short_channel_ids_end` (false: no peer, a spent channel, the connection or the wait ended). `TryGetPeerFilter(peer)` / `FilterReceived` expose the peer's `gossip_timestamp_filter` (our chain only) for the relay (G3-T3, `Gossip/Relay/`). Nothing is queried or filtered while `IsSyncEnabled` is false (`Gossip:SyncEnabled` unset = on everywhere, mainnet included since wave d12 (plan D12), and only with the graph ingress enabled); queries are always answered. Query replies, queries and filters go through `IPeerService.SendGossipMessageAsync` (not the `PeerOutbox`; NL-351 covers own and relayed gossip, see `Gossip/Relay/`). `QueryResponder`: serves channels that are announced (raw bytes), unspent and not `Unverified`; a range reply lists only those with at least one update, ascending, chunked by `MaxScidsPerReply` (8,000) and by 65,535 bytes (24 bytes per SCID with timestamps and checksums), first reply at the query's `first_blocknum`, each reply up to its last channel's block, the next after it (or on the same block when a block is split), the last to the query's end with `sync_complete`; `query_option` bit 0 → `timestamps_tlv`, bit 1 → `checksums_tlv` (`Domain/Gossip/Queries/ChannelUpdateChecksum`); a scid query gets, per known channel in query order, what `query_flags` asks (no flags: 256, both 258, both 257), each 257 once per reply, then the end with our chain check. The sorted served-channel list is cached per graph snapshot. For the channel of Core Lightning's captured `reply_channel_range` (with its two captured updates) our reply equals CLN's field for field, `timestamps_tlv` and `checksums_tlv` included (`QueryResponderTests`, `Tests.Utils/Vectors/Bolt7QueryVectors`; G3-T4), so `FeatureOptions.ExpandedGossipQueries` is Optional. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Gossip/Sync/` (`SyncTestGraph` fills a `GraphStore` directly, `FakeGossipPeer` records what we send; `QueryResponderTests`, `RangeReplyCollectorTests`, `GossipSyncManagerTests`: the state machine against the fake peer incl. bad replies → warning, one outstanding query, ex flags, timeouts, rotation, NL-353).
- Taproot gossip in the graph, sync and relay (NL-878 T7 lane G; BOLTs PR #1059 draft `4eef3dfa`; only while we advertise `option_gossip_v2`, experimental, `GossipIngress.IsGossipV2Enabled`, else 267/269/271 are dropped silently): Domain `GraphChannel`/`GraphPolicy`/`GraphNode` carry `Versions` (`GraphGossipVersions` V1/V2), `RawAnnouncement2`, the v2 policy slots `Policy1V2`/`Policy2V2` (`GraphPolicy.GossipVersion` 2, `Timestamp` = block height, `DisableFlags`, inbound fees; `FromChannelUpdate2` applies the half-capacity `htlc_maximum_msat` default) next to the v1 ones (`Policy1`/`Policy2`/`GetPolicy(direction)` stay BOLT 7 only), and `GetRoutingPolicy` (v2 first) for routing (`GraphAdjacency`); nullable bitcoin keys (3-key proof). `GossipIngress.V2.cs`: `channel_announcement_2` checked by `Domain/Gossip/Validation/GossipV2Validator`, then (4-key form) its MuSig2 signature without the output (`IGossipV2SignatureVerifier.CheckChannelSignature`, a forgery warns and closes before any lookup, NL-1140), then `IFundingOutputLookup.LookupAsync` (P2WSH or P2TR, outpoint = scid output, amount >= capacity, depth) and `IGossipV2SignatureVerifier.CheckChannelProof`, stored at once with the announced capacity or added to a BOLT 7 channel of the same nodes (`IGraphStore.TryAddAnnouncementVersion`; a v1 announcement is added to a v2-only channel the same way, `AddV1AnnouncementToV2ChannelAsync`); `channel_update_2` (BIP 340 by the sciddir direction's node, block height within [tip − 2016, tip + 1] and not below the funding block, newer wins, own rate-limit bucket `direction + 2`, orphans in `OrphanUpdateCache.AddUpdate2`); `node_announcement_2` (node with a channel of either protocol, newer block height, no rate limit), merged with the node's v1 announcement (`GraphStore.MergeNodes`, v2 fields win); a keyless (3-key) `channel_announcement_2` of a new channel waits without a lookup in a second `PendingAnnouncementIndex` (`PendingAnnouncement.IsV2`, the v1 bounds `MaxPendingAnnouncements`/`PendingAnnouncementTtl`, gauge `queue=pending_announcements_v2`, `PendingAnnouncement2Count`, `IsPending`) until its first valid `channel_update_2` promotes it (`PromoteV2Async`: candidate whose node signed the update, then lookup and proof; a failed proof drops the candidate without blaming the update's sender; NL-1140); a BOLT 7 update of a v2-only channel waits as an orphan. Tip from the optional `IBlockchainMonitor`. Own v2 gossip: the `IOwnGossipSink` `*2` methods queue into the own loop (`ApplyOwnV2Async`, `Own`; our node in memory in the graph, its row saved by `NodeAnnouncementService` before it publishes, NL-1142). The announcement of our public taproot channels (`Gossip/Announcements/ChannelAnnouncement2Service`, `AnnouncedChannels2`, `Channels/Handlers/AnnouncementSignatures2MessageHandler`): one 4-of-4 MuSig2 session per connection, nonces in a re-sent `channel_ready` at the announcement depth or in `channel_reestablish` TLV 7 (bit 1 while not announced in this process; `ReestablishService.CreateOwnAsync` asks the service for the TLVs, `ChannelManager.AppendAnnouncement2Messages` routes the peer's), nothing persisted (a restart re-signs); sessions are per funding: a splice's nonces ride `splice_locked` (`CreateSpliceLockedNonces`/`OnSpliceLockedNonces` from `SpliceService`, the signer accepts a registered pending splice for nonces only) and `SpliceService.AfterLockAsync` calls `OnSpliceLocked`, which drops the old announcement and signs the splice's session when it is 6 deep (NL-1131); a spliced channel (`LocalFundingKeyIndex != 0`) never re-sends `channel_ready` nonces; published with our `channel_update_2` (`ChannelUpdateService.OnChannelAnnounced2`, also re-issued whenever `CreateChannelUpdate` runs for a v2-announced channel) and `node_announcement_2`; `ChannelAnnouncementService` never signs v1 for a taproot channel. Persistence: migration `AddGossipV2` (version columns default 1, policy key (scid, direction, version), inbound fees, nullable bitcoin keys). Pathfinding honours inbound fees (`GraphPathfinder`: the forwarder's inbound fee on the incoming edge is charged on what it forwards; `PathHop.InboundFee*`, folded into `GraphPath.ToRoutingInfos`, so `HintRouteBuilder.BuildAlong` rebuilds the same amounts) and v2 staleness by block height (`PathfindingRequest.CurrentBlockHeight`, `GraphPathSource` from the chain monitor; `GraphPruner` keeps a channel while either protocol is fresh). Sync: `gossip_timestamp_filter` TLV 2 `block_height_range` (`Domain/Gossip/Queries/GossipBlockHeightRange`, wire `RawKnown(2)`); `GossipSyncManager` keeps a v2 peer's range (`TryGetPeerBlockHeightRange`), sends one with every filter to a v2 peer (`ToBlockHeightRange`: ten-minute blocks below the tip), compares `timestamps_tlv` entries below 500,000,000 as block heights against the v2 policies; `QueryResponder` lists and serves v2 channels (block heights, `Checksum2`) only to a v2 requester. Relay: `GossipRelayScheduler.RelayV2.cs` (others' v2 to v2 connections with a range, 267 → 271 → 269, an update brings its announcement, origin suppression, bounded pending, an unpaced backlog NL-1141) and the own path's 267/271/269 to every v2 peer. Tests: `Gossip/Graph/GossipIngressV2Tests` (real MuSig2 and BIP 340 via `Tests.Utils/Gossip/GossipV2TestSigner`), `Gossip/Sync/QueryResponderV2Tests`, `GossipSyncManagerTests` (v2 cases), `Gossip/Relay/GossipRelayV2Tests`, `GraphPrunerTests`, `Payments/Routing/GraphRoutePlannerTests` (inbound fee); Domain `Gossip/GossipV2ValidatorTests`, `GossipV2GraphModelTests`, `Routing/GraphPathfinderV2Tests`; SQLite `Integration.Tests/Persistence/GossipV2SchemaRoundTrip`.
- Peers that ignore `query_short_channel_ids` (NL-722, lane b10-ldk): rust-lightning 0.3 answers `query_channel_range` but leaves `query_short_channel_ids` unimplemented ("Not implemented", ignored), and LDK streams its graph only to a `gossip_timestamp_filter` whose `first_timestamp` is more than 6 h old. When a range sync's scid query goes unanswered (`PeerSession.ScidQueryUnanswered`), the failed sync's filter is the backlog filter of a sync without timestamps (`PeerSession.FallbackFilterStart` = start - `Gossip:SyncFilterBacklog`) instead of the live filter from now, so an LDK peer's existing gossip reaches us. `QueryScidAsync` still cannot use such a peer (its next query waits for the end that never comes). Test: `GossipSyncManagerTests.Given_APeerThatIgnoresScidQueries_*`; Docker `Interop/Ldk/LdkGossipTests` (LDK's `node_announcement` from its full sync).
