Only a peer that never answered a `query_short_channel_ids` on the connection gets that backlog filter (`PeerSession.ScidQueryAnswered`, set by an answer in time or a late end consumed before the next query; NL-744): a peer that answered an earlier batch was only slow and gets the live filter. Left as is: a peer whose very first scid query times out still gets the backlog filter. Test: `GossipSyncManagerTests.Given_APeerThatAnsweredAScidQuery_*`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Gossip/Announcements/` (`AnnouncementTestPair`: both ends of a public channel with real `LocalLightningSigner`s from a seed (node key included), the real verifier, a recording sink and relay (`RecordingRelayScheduler`); `ChannelAnnouncementServiceTests`; `NodeAnnouncementServiceTests`; `AnnouncementHarnessTests`: the G1-T4 proof on `TwoNodeHarness(announceChannel: true)` (a public channel at `TwoNodeHarness.ShortChannelId`, `HarnessNode.RaiseBlockAsync`/`SetTip` drive the node's `ChainMonitor`, the harness's secure key manager has the node key): identical 256 bytes whose four signatures verify, retransmission after a link drop, nothing redundant once both halves are in, the reestablish before our half on a new connection, nothing below 6 confirmations or after a shutdown, public `channel_update` and `node_announcement` in relay order, both nodes restarted between the halves (NL-355: `TwoNodeHarness.RestartNodeAsync` is a clean restart, link down; the harness store saves the channel row's announcement fields staged by `ChannelDbRepository.UpdateAsync` (`InMemoryChannelStateStore.CommittedAnnouncement`, in backups too) and a restart puts them back on the rebuilt model)), `Gossip/Relay/GossipRelaySchedulerTests`.
- `Channels/Handlers/AnnouncementSignaturesMessageHandler` (G1-T3, NL-342; `ChannelManager` case `MessageTypes.AnnouncementSignatures` after `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync`): not loaded → ignored; another peer's channel → `error`; ShuttingDown/closing or a shutdown script set → ignored; private channel → `warning`; SCID not the channel's (before our funding confirmation: only the output index is checked) → `warning`; a bad node or bitcoin signature → `warning` + close (plan D10: never fails the channel); otherwise the peer's half is stored (`ChannelModel.SetRemoteAnnouncementSignatures`), and when `CanSendAnnouncementSignatures` and ours did not go out on this connection ours is the reply (`MarkAnnouncementSignaturesSent`), both in one save before the reply; a repeat of the stored half saves nothing; then `TryAssembleAnnouncement` → `OnChannelAnnounced`. Before we sent channel_ready the half is only stored (BOLT 7 defer). Tests: `Channels/Handlers/AnnouncementSignaturesMessageHandlerTests` (both ends assemble identical bytes whose four signatures verify; mismatch, bad signature, below depth, early, a deferred half for another SCID, replay, reconnection, private, shutdown).
- `Channels/Handlers/StfuMessageHandler` (splicing plan Q1-T1, NL-019; `ChannelManager` case `MessageTypes.Stfu` after `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync` and `ThrowIfNotReestablished`, B2-RE-07): thin, under the channel's lock: not loaded → channel `warning` (connection kept); another peer's channel → `error`; no `IQuiescenceService` registered (optional ctor argument) → `warning` + close (the pre-quiescence behavior); otherwise `IQuiescenceService.OnStfuReceived(channel, payload, negotiatedFeatures.GetNodeFeatures())` and its reply `stfu`, if any, is the handler's reply; the service's `ChannelWarningException`s (Q-S-01/Q-S-03, built with `QuiescenceRules.CreateWarning`) propagate. Tests: `Channels/Managers/ChannelManagerStfuRoutingTests` (reaches the service with the channel lock held, reply raised under the lock, no reply, second stfu, no service, other peer, stfu before channel_reestablish refused by the B2-RE-07 gate with a warning + close and after it delivered, and exactly one `IChannelMessageHandler<StfuMessage>` registered by `AddApplicationServices`). This is the only stfu handler: the quiescence service (plan Q1-T3, `Channels/Quiescence/`) plugs in through `IQuiescenceService`; never add a second `IChannelMessageHandler<StfuMessage>` (the reflection registration would silently keep the last one).
- NL-379: `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` (optional `IAnchorReserveService` argument, registered by `AddBitcoinInfrastructure`) refuses an `option_anchors` channel (from `ChannelParams.OptionAnchorOutputs` of the channel the factory built, before it is stored) with an `error` when `EnsureCanAcceptAnchorsChannelAsync(channel)` throws `AnchorReserveException` (the confirmed available wallet balance does not cover `Node:Anchors` reserve with the new channel); an admitted channel counts toward the reserve while it is being opened (NL-379 review: concurrent opens can not all pass against the reserve of one). Tests: `Channels/Handlers/OpenChannel1AnchorReserveTests`.
- `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` refuses an `open_channel` with `announce_channel` when `GossipOptions.AcceptPublicChannels` is false (`error`, BOLT 2 MAY; default true, optional `IOptions<GossipOptions>` argument) or when `GossipOptions.ArePublicChannelsAllowed(network)` is false (mainnet without `Gossip:AllowPublicChannelsOnMainnet`: BOLT 7 would oblige us to send `announcement_signatures`, which D12 forbids there; optional `IOptions<NodeOptions>` argument, mainnet when absent); the Domain `ChannelFactory` stores the opener's flag as `ChannelParams.AnnounceChannel` (NL-341), and as initiator the request's `IsPublic` (a public channel's type leaves out `option_scid_alias`, `UseScidAlias` Optional when negotiated; public + zero-conf is refused).
- `Gossip/Graph/` (BOLT7 plan G2-T4, wave G-B; registered by `GossipGraphServiceCollectionExtensions.AddGossipGraphServices()`, which the daemon's `AddNltgNodeServices` calls and binds `GossipGraphOptions` from the `Gossip` section): `GraphStore : IGraphStore` (singleton; the in-memory graph of Domain `GraphChannel`/`GraphNode`/`GraphPolicy` behind one writer lock, never a channel lock; `GetSnapshot()` returns an immutable `GraphSnapshot` rebuilt only after a change; persisted write-behind (plan D2): changes mark keys dirty and `FlushAsync` writes their current values (or deletes) through `IUnitOfWork.GraphDbRepository` in one save of its own scope, a failed save keeps them pending; `LoadAsync` reads everything once at startup, changes made before it win; funding txids are saved with their channel (`GraphChannels.FundingTxId`, migration `AddGraphFundingTxId`, NL-352): the chain check or our own channel sets them at the add, and `TrySetFundingTxId` (the pruner's lookup of a row saved without one) marks the channel dirty, so after a restart only rows without one are looked up) and `GossipIngress` (singleton; `IGossipIngress` for `PeerService`, and the Domain `IOwnGossipSink` of lane B1 (`AddOwnChannelAnnouncement(payload, capacity)`, `AddOwnChannelUpdate`, `AddOwnNodeAnnouncement`: void, idempotent, never blocking, since callers may hold a channel lock) for our own 256/258/257; `AddGossipGraphServices` `Replace`s whatever sink was registered before (B1's `NullOwnGossipSink` default), `Daemon.Tests` `NodeServiceExtensionsTests` checks the sink is the ingress). Pipeline per message: exact-duplicate filter (`RecentMessageCache`, SHA-256 of type + payload, only for settled messages), Domain `GossipValidator`, then `IGossipSignatureVerifier` (4 signatures for a 256, the direction's node for a 258), then for a 256 `IFundingOutputLookup.VerifyAsync` (Found with at least `GossipGraphOptions.GetAnnouncementDepth(network)` confirmations (`Gossip:AnnouncementDepth`, shared with B1's send depth and read the same way: only regtest may go below 6): the amount is the capacity; fewer confirmations or a transient status (`IsTransient`) is `Deferred` and retried by the workers after `RetryDelay` up to `MaxRetries`; `BlockUnavailable` is ignored, or kept `Unverified` without capacity with `FundingValidation = SkipUnavailable`; any other status is ignored). Outcomes (`GossipIngressResult`): bad signatures get a `warning` and the connection closed (B7-CA-03/CU-02/NA-03, `IPeerService.Disconnect(WarningException)`), an invalid key/unordered node ids/malformed addrlen a `warning` only, BOLT 7 "ignore" cases nothing; a 258 of an unknown channel (unless `dont_forward`: a private channel's update is never graph data) and a 257 of a node without channels wait in `OrphanUpdateCache` (newest per direction/node, `MaxOrphans` 10,000, `OrphanTtl` 10 min) and are replayed through the whole pipeline when the channel is added (check and add under one gate, so no orphan is lost to the race). B7-CA-04: a validly signed announcement of the same funding output (same bitcoin keys) for other node ids bans all four nodes for `ConflictBanDuration` (persisted in `GraphBannedNodes`) and forgets every channel with a banned end (BOLT 7 "blacklist ... AND forget any channels connected to them"); never our node (the optional `ISecureKeyManager`) or an `Own` channel; different bitcoin keys never ban anyone. Queues: `MaxQueuedPerPeer` 2,000 and `MaxQueued` 20,000 (excess dropped: `PeerService` hands gossip over from the transport's synchronous read event, so it cannot wait), `Workers` (default half the processors). Known gap until G3: the only sync is the bootstrap `gossip_timestamp_filter` dump, so a large graph (signet/testnet) can overflow the queues or exhaust `MaxRetries` x `RetryDelay` (5 min) on transient chain answers; nothing re-requests those messages yet. `DroppedCount` counts them (a warning at the first and every 1,000th) and `TakeMissedShortChannelIds()` hands the SCIDs of dropped/given-up 256/258s (at most `MaxMissedShortChannelIds` 100,000, removed when the channel is stored) to the G3 sync for `query_short_channel_ids`. `StartAsync` (or the first queued message) loads the store and starts the workers and the flush loop (`FlushInterval` 5 s, or `FlushBatchSize` 1,000 pending changes); `StopAsync` stops them and flushes (the daemon's `GossipGraphHostedService` starts and stops it; the Docker `NLightningTestNode` does not yet). Own gossip: the sink methods only queue (`OwnGossipItem`, unbounded, dropped while the graph is disabled) and start the ingress; one loop applies them in order after the load (`ApplyOwnAsync`, internal, tests call it directly; `WhenOwnGossipAppliedAsync` for tests): a 256 is stored as `Own` with the given capacity and, when our channel is loaded (`IChannelMemoryRepository`), its funding txid (no chain lookup), a 258 is applied or waits for its 256, a 257 goes into memory only through `IGraphStore.TryApplyOwnNode`: our node's `GraphNodes` row has one writer, B1's `NodeAnnouncementService`, which saves it before it publishes (an older pending write of that row is dropped, so the write-behind never overwrites it). `StartCoreAsync` yields first, so neither a peer's read loop nor a sink caller ever runs the load. `Gossip:Enabled` unset means on everywhere, mainnet included since wave d12 (plan D12, `GossipGraphOptions.IsEnabledFor`; `false` turns it off). `GraphPruner` (G2-T5, singleton from `AddGossipGraphServices`; `Start()` before the chain monitor starts, `StopAsync()` on shutdown, both by the daemon's `GossipGraphHostedService`; nothing while the graph is disabled): subscribes to `IBlockchainMonitor.OnBlockInputs` (every processed block's spent outpoints, plan D4) and `OnBlockDisconnected`, which only enqueue; one loop loads the store, looks up (at once when the monitor already has a height, else at the first block, since the monitor loads its height only in its own `StartAsync`, after the pruner starts: a spend found at height 0 would skip the 72-block delay) the funding txid of every channel without one (`IFundingOutputLookup.LookupAsync`: Found records it with `IGraphStore.TrySetFundingTxId`, `OutputSpentOrMissing` marks the channel spent at the last processed block, a transient status is retried at the next block, any other is not asked again and left to the stale rule), then per block: a spent funding outpoint (`IGraphStore.TryGetChannelByFundingOutpoint`) sets `SpentAtHeight` (idempotent on a replayed block), a channel is removed once the block at `SpentAtHeight + SpentChannelRetentionBlocks` (72) is processed, a channel whose newest update of each direction (else its announcement's arrival, `TryGetChannelReceivedAt`) is older than `DeleteStaleAfter` (28 days, `TimeProvider`) is removed unless it is ours (`Own`, or our node id as an end), and every node announcement left without a channel is removed (never ours); the spends of the last `RecentSpendBlocks` (6) blocks are matched again at every block, so a channel the ingress stored just after the block that spent it (its chain check ran at the previous tip) is still marked at that block; a reorg clears the spends above the fork (`ClearSpentAbove`, and drops those blocks from the recent spends), the new branch marks them again, and every channel whose funding block is above the fork is looked up again at the next blocks (`RecheckReorgedFundingAsync`: the same txid at its SCID keeps it, another txid, no output there or a spent one marks it spent at that block, so it is forgotten 72 blocks later (BOLT 7 "spent or reorganized out"), a transient answer asks again at the next block, any other drops it from the check). The store's pending changes are flushed after each block that changed them. Routing exclusion of stale (14 days, `GraphChannel.IsStale`) and spent channels is the pathfinder's job (G4). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Gossip/Graph/` (`GraphTestKit`: store over `InMemoryGraphDbRepository`, real verifier, mocked lookup, `TestGossipKey` signing; `GossipIngressVectorTests` over the LND/CLN captures), `Integration.Tests/Persistence/GossipGraphReloadTests` (SQLite restart; the pruner's spend and removal across two restarts). `GraphPrunerTests`: fake clock and blocks (spent + 72, replay, spend reorg, stale both directions, a node with only stale channels, ours kept, startup lookups, a monitor without height at start, a channel stored after its spending block, a funding block reorged out, the monitor events, mainnet off).
- Graph persistence performance and `describegraph` (BOLT 7 plan G5-T3, G5-T4 IPC half; gossip wave G-D lane D2): `GraphStore.FlushAsync` cuts the pending work into batches of `WriteBatchSize` rows (default 5,000; deletions, channels, policies, nodes, bans in that order, so a policy's channel is saved first), each in a unit of work of its own through the repository's bulk members (one read per 500 keys instead of one per row: 1.6 s against 3.3 s for 20,000 channels on SQLite); a failed batch and the ones after it stay pending, the earlier ones are written (a crash loses only unsaved batches, which peers send again, plan D2). `LoadAsync` streams the three tables (`IGraphDbRepository.Stream*Async`) and applies them in batches of `LoadBatchSize` (10,000) under the writer lock (changes made before or during the load still win, `GraphStorePersistenceTests` changes the store between batches). `IGraphStore.GetMemoryEstimate()` → `GraphMemoryEstimate` (channels, policies, nodes, variable bytes, `StoreBytes`, `SnapshotBytes`, `TotalMegabytes`), O(1): every change keeps `PolicyCount` and the variable-byte sum up to date; the fixed costs per channel/policy/node/snapshot entry are `GraphMemoryAccounting`'s constants, calibrated on the synthetic SQLite load (G5-T1 budgets with it; `Gossip:MaxMemoryMb` is D1's). Measured (2026-09-26, Apple M3 Ultra, .NET 10.0.3 Release, SQLite file, `Integration.Tests/Persistence/GraphStoreLoadPerformanceTests`, `Explicit`, `Category=Long`, run with `-- xUnit.Explicit=only`): 200,000 channels + 400,000 policies + 50,000 nodes load in 1.55 s (target 10 s), the batched flush of the whole graph takes 19-29 s, the first snapshot 70-105 ms; retained managed heap 475 MiB for the store (2.5 KB per channel; node ids are not interned yet, G5-T1) plus 39 MiB per snapshot, estimate 468.5 + 39.1 MiB; the process working set grew 336-368 MiB during the load. A mainnet-sized graph (about 50,000 channels, 15,000 nodes) estimates at about 130 MiB. No `AddGossipIndexes` migration: the load is full scans, sync queries and the relay read the in-memory snapshot, and the pruner's `SpentAtHeight` read already has its index. `GossipGraphDescriber` (singleton, registered by the daemon) reads one snapshot's counts, the store's pending writes and estimate, `GossipIngress` (queued, `DroppedCount`, `Orphans.Count`) and `GossipSyncManager.GetPeerStates()` (`GossipSyncPeerState` per connection: init done, negotiated queries, sync peer, range sync running, last completed range sync, the peer's filter and the last one we sent (`PeerSession.SentFilter`), stopped querying, queued work) for the daemon's `describegraph` (ClientCommand 20). Tests: `Gossip/Graph/GraphStorePersistenceTests` (batches, a failed second batch, one-row load batches, the estimate against a recomputation after every kind of change), `Gossip/Graph/GossipGraphDescriberTests`; SQLite: `Integration.Tests/Persistence/GraphStoreLoadPerformanceTests` (5,000 channels by default: the bulk-loaded graph equals the incrementally built one, estimates equal) and `GraphDbRepositoryBulkTests`.
- Gossip memory budget (NL-373, BOLT 7 plan G5-T1, wave D12 lane Z2; kept simple by owner decision): `Gossip:MaxMemoryMb` (`GossipGraphOptions.MaxMemoryMb`, default `DefaultMaxMemoryMb` = 1,024 on every network, also written by the daemon's config template; 0 turns it off), `MemoryResumePercent` (90) and `MemorySampleInterval` (1 s). `Gossip/Graph/GossipMemoryBudget` (singleton from `AddGossipGraphServices`, with `IProcessMemoryReader` → `ProcessMemoryReader`: `Process.WorkingSet64` (RSS; a reading of 0 throws, since `Environment.WorkingSet` returns 0 on Unix when it fails) and the GC's committed bytes (`GCMemoryInfo.TotalCommittedBytes`, or `GC.GetTotalMemory(false)` when larger)) reads the process lazily, at most once per interval; above the budget (strictly) it refuses every **new** channel (a 256 whose SCID the store lacks, checked right after the `MaxChannels` cap, before the signatures and the chain lookup) and every **new** node (a 257 without a stored announcement, after `MaxNodes`) through one guarded call each in `GossipIngress` (since D12 Z1 also at the promotion of a pending announcement by its first update, which keeps it pending; `RefuseNew`, `GossipIngressResult.Limited` with `GossipMetricReasons.MemoryBudget` = `memory_budget`, counted as rejected; not handed to the missed-SCID re-query: the next range sync asks again), while known channels and nodes keep updating and our own gossip (`ApplyOwnAsync`) is always applied; it accepts new ones again below `MemoryResumePercent` of the budget (hysteresis). Each crossing logs one warning (and one information on the way back) and counts `nlightning.gossip.memory.budget.exceeded`; the observable `nlightning.gossip.memory.working_set` (By) reads the process too (rate-limited), so it moves while only updates arrive. A failed read or a reading of 0 keeps the last decision. `describegraph` shows it (`DescribeGraphClientHandler` takes the budget as an optional constructor argument; IPC keys 23-27: budget, RSS, GC committed bytes (printed as managed heap), over-budget flag, refused count; the client prints a `Memory (process)` line). Not done (follow-ups): interned node ids and bitcoin keys, a streaming `getblock` reader (NL-416); the budget covers the whole process, so sync garbage counts until the GC gives it back. Tests: `Gossip/Graph/GossipMemoryBudgetTests` (fake reader and clock: crossing logged and counted once, strict boundary, hysteresis, sample interval, off, failed read, state and gauge, option validation, and through `GraphTestKit(memoryBudget:)` a refused new channel without a chain lookup, known channel and node updates applied while a new node is refused, resume below 90 %).
- Mainnet gossip probe fixes (integrated on `wip/fafo` from the probe branches, `docs/agents/MAINNET_GOSSIP_PROBE.md`, harness `tools/NLightning.GossipProbe`): `Gossip:AssumeChannelValid` (default false; `GossipGraphOptions.GetAssumeChannelValidErrors` refuses it on mainnet while HTLCs are enabled or public channels are allowed, checked by the daemon's options validation) stores a validly signed `channel_announcement` without the funding output lookup as `GraphChannelVerification.Assumed` (no capacity, no funding txid: the pruner never looks it up, a close is left to the stale rule; `GraphChannel.IsChainChecked` is false, so it is never relayed or served; pathfinding uses `GraphChannel.EstimatedCapacityMsat`, the larger `htlc_maximum_msat`, as the capacity); `RangeReplyCollector` accepts CLN's wrapping reply sequence (NL-400); every `query_short_channel_ids` batch of a range sync is diffed against the graph right before it goes out (NL-402), and with timestamps an unknown channel whose updates are both older than `Gossip:SkipChannelsStaleFor` (14 days) is not asked for (NL-404); `OriginTrackingGossipIngress` records nothing while the relay is off (NL-405). Infrastructure's `MessageService` ignores a malformed 256/257/258 with one warning per connection instead of closing it (NL-401).
- Graph hygiene (wave d12 lane Z1, NL-406 and NL-408; `Gossip/Graph/`): a validly signed `channel_announcement` from a peer is **not stored on arrival**. It waits in `PendingAnnouncementIndex` (outside the graph: not in `IGraphView`, so never routed over, served by `QueryResponder` or relayed; no chain lookup; only the wire bytes, the sender's node id and the time are kept, about 430 bytes each; several different announcements per scid may wait as candidates (at most `PendingAnnouncementIndex.MaxCandidatesPerChannel` = 4, one per sending peer: a peer's newer one replaces its own, a different one beyond 4 is refused as dropped `pending_candidates_full`, so a later forgery never displaces the real one); bounded by `Gossip:MaxPendingAnnouncements` (50,000 candidates; when full the oldest entry of the peer holding the most makes room, so one peer's flood of forgeries evicts its own entries, counted as dropped `pending_full`) and `Gossip:PendingAnnouncementTtl` (14 days from when it was kept, an identical re-send does not renew it; pruned by the write-behind loop, `GossipIngress.PrunePendingAnnouncements`, counted as dropped `pending_expired`); gauge `queue=pending_announcements`; `describegraph` shows `PendingAnnouncements` (IPC response key 28); not persisted: peers send them again after a restart) until its first valid `channel_update`, which promotes it (`GossipIngress.PromoteAsync`): the update is validated (fields) and promotes the candidate whose node signed it, the funding output is looked up (`CheckFundingAsync`, the old announcement-time rules: 6 confirmations, `SkipUnavailable`, `AssumeChannelValid`; a transient answer or too few confirmations defers the update, which the worker retries), then the channel enters the graph (`AddChannelAndReplayAsync`, which also drops the pending entry and replays orphans) and the update is applied as for any stored channel. Results: `GossipIngressOutcome.Pending` for a kept announcement (or `Accepted` when an orphaned update promoted it at once); the `accepted` counter of `channel_announcement` counts promotions. A permanent chain failure drops the pending entry and remembers the announcement as a duplicate; a contradiction (NL-371) refuses the announcement without scoring or disconnecting anybody. An update whose signature matches none of the candidates is kept as an orphan without a warning (none was checked on chain, so they may all be forgeries) and its scid is handed to the sync (`TakeMissedShortChannelIds`) so the real announcement is asked for again; a later announcement for that scid replays the orphan. A permanent chain failure drops only the failing candidate. Former known limit, closed by NL-418: four sybil peers announcing forgeries of one scid before the real one used to keep it out until their entries expired; now, once an orphaned `channel_update`'s signature has matched none of them, the next announcement for the scid evicts them (dropped `pending_candidates_evicted`) and the waiting update promotes the real one. An identical pending announcement again is `Ignored`/`AlreadyKnown` without a signature check; our own announcements (`ApplyOwnAsync`) are stored at once and remove a pending entry. `IsPendingAnnouncement(scid)`/`PendingAnnouncementCount` are public. A node_announcement of a node whose only channels are pending is an orphan (10 min TTL) until the promotion replays it. NL-408: `GossipIngress` has one unbounded queue per worker (`Gossip:Workers`, `PartitionCount`), chosen by `PartitionOf(message, n)` (short channel id for 256/258, node id for 257, through splitmix64's finalizer), so a channel's announcement and updates are handled in arrival order by one worker and the initial sync no longer orphans updates (the synthetic two-peer sync of 150 channels on 4 workers: 0 orphans, 146 with round-robin queues); `MaxQueued` bounds all queues together (`QueuedCount`), `MaxQueuedPerPeer` each peer's share; retries and rate-limited replays go back to their channel's queue. Tests: `Gossip/Graph/PendingAnnouncementTests` (pending, not served, not relayed, promoted, orphan-first, TTL, cap, forgery first and forgery after the real one, a flooding peer evicting its own entries, chain contradiction by another peer's update, own channel, stale first update), `PendingAnnouncementIndexTests`, `GossipIngressPartitionTests`; `GraphTestKit.AnnounceAsync` sends an announcement followed by node_1's update (`TestGossipKey.Of(pubKey)` finds any test key) for tests that need a channel in the graph through the ingress.
- `Node/Services/PeerOutbox.cs`: the only send path for a peer's channel messages: a FIFO (`System.Threading.Channels`) drained by one loop that awaits each `IPeerService` send. `TryEnqueue`, `TryEnqueueGossip` (our `channel_update`, sent with `IPeerService.SendGossipMessageAsync`, never refused for the cap), `EnqueueGossip(message, size)` (NL-360, the bounded form for own and relayed gossip: `GossipEnqueueResult.Full` when it would take the gossip share (`QueuedGossipCount`/`QueuedGossipBytes`, queued and not taken by the loop) over the constructor's `maxQueuedGossip` messages or `maxQueuedGossipBytes` bytes (an empty share always takes one message, so a large `node_announcement` never blocks it), calling `onGossipRefused`; `Gone` when the outbox is closed; `TryEnqueueGossip(message, capped: true)` is its bool form; `GossipDepth` = queued messages and bytes, the caps and `SentGossipCount`, the connection's progress; channel messages, warnings, errors, the disconnect and the uncapped `channel_update` are never refused for it, and FIFO order holds; `PeerManager` builds every outbox with `Gossip:MaxOutboxGossipPerPeer` (10,000) and `Gossip:MaxOutboxGossipBytesPerPeer` (4 MiB) (`GossipSyncOptions`, read once from the provider, the defaults without a registration) and implements `IPeerGossipOutbox.EnqueueGossip`/`GetGossipDepth` over the current session's outbox (a replaced connection is `Gone`/null); a refusal is `nlightning.gossip.outbox.refused` and `PeerManager.RefusedOutboxGossipCount`, `QueuedOutboxGossipCount` is the `outbox_gossip` queue gauge and `QueuedOutboxGossipBytes` the `nlightning.gossip.outbox.bytes` gauge), `TryEnqueueWarning`, `TryEnqueueError` (an `error` without disconnecting, `IPeerService.SendErrorAsync`), and `TryEnqueueDisconnect` (terminal: sent after everything queued before it, later items are refused). Nothing else calls `IPeerService.SendMessageAsync` for channel messages: the Daemon's `OpenChannelClientHandler` calls `IChannelManager.StartOpeningChannelAsync`, which stores the temporary channel and raises open_channel through `OnResponseMessageReady` under the temporary channel's lock.
- `Protocol/Factories/MessageFactory.cs`: implements Domain `IMessageFactory`. Thin payload+TLV builders with no validation.
- `Channels/Reestablish/` (N7, registered by `ReestablishServiceCollectionExtensions.AddReestablishServices()`, called from `AddApplicationServices` **before** `AddChannelOperationsServices`, whose probe registration is a `TryAdd`):
  - `ReestablishTracker` (singleton, also the Domain `IReestablishTracker` that `listchannels` reads for `IsReestablished`): per channel `Awaiting` (default) / `Sent` (our channel_reestablish went out on the current connection) / `Reestablished` (both exchanged), plus a separate "opened here" flag. `TryMarkReestablished` is a compare-and-set from `Sent`, and `ResetPeer` runs synchronously when the peer's connection changes, so a reestablish that finishes on a replaced connection never counts for the new one. `MarkOpened` for a channel that turned Open on the current connection (channel_ready starts normal operation) sets only the flag: `IsReestablished` is true for `Reestablished` or opened here, while `GetStatus` keeps the exchange status, so a peer's channel_reestablish arriving after channel_ready is still answered (LND sends one for a channel that was pending at connect, and waits for ours forever). Memory only: every restart starts with nothing reestablished.
  - `ReestablishGatedLivenessProbe`: the production `IPeerLivenessProbe` = `ConnectedPeerLivenessProbe` AND the tracker, so every `IChannelOperations` call and every scheduler signature waits for the reestablish (B2-RE-07).
  - `ReestablishService` (scoped; optional `IServiceProvider` for the scope's unit of work, the splice state port, the announcement service, the key manager and the peer manager): `GetLocalState(channel)` (from the snapshot, or the channel's numbers when there is none), `GetLocalStateAsync(channel, negotiatedFeatures?)` (plus, for a V2 channel or with `option_splice`, the latest constructed, not aborted `InteractiveTxSessions` row as `ReestablishInteractiveTxState` (IT-SIG-01 order from our node id), and with `option_splice` the funding-lock facts `ReestablishSpliceState` from `ISpliceStatePort.GetFundings`: the last pending splice we sent `splice_locked` for, else a current funding of kind splice; channel_ready sent = ReadyForUs..Closing; announcement halves held (the channel's `RemoteAnnouncementSignatures` counts only for the original funding: for a locked splice the funding's `AnnouncementSignaturesReceived`, from the port or its `ChannelFundings` row, since the lock does not reset the stored half) and readiness per active funding through `IChannelAnnouncementService.IsReadyForAnnouncementSignatures`, falling back to `CanSendAnnouncementSignatures` for the current funding until lane SP2-B implements it; the features are the given ones, else the peer's connection, else our own options (harnesses)), `CreateOwnAsync(channel, negotiatedFeatures?)` (next_commitment_number L+1, next_revocation_number R, the peer's secret R-1 from its persisted shachain or zeroes, our point L, and the planner's `next_funding` (TLV 1) and `my_current_funding_locked` (TLV 5, `option_splice` only)) and `IsOurSecret(channel, n, s)` (checked against our point n through `IRevocationVerifier`, so no secret is revealed).
  - The rules are the pure Domain `Channels/Reestablish/ReestablishPlanner` (see the Domain CLAUDE.md).
- `Channels/Handlers/ChannelReestablishMessageHandler`: Failed channel → `ChannelFailedException` (error re-sent); not in memory or another peer's → `ChannelErrorException`; a reestablish after we answered one on the same connection (`Reestablished`) is ignored; ours goes first if it was not sent on this connection yet (`Awaiting`: also for a channel that turned Open on this connection); then the planner: `DataLoss` persists `DataLossDetected` (own save) and throws `ChannelFailedException` without broadcast (B2-RE-23); `Fail` throws `ChannelFailedException` (`MustBroadcast` for next_commitment_number 0); `Resume` first processes `ReestablishPlan.PeerSpliceLocked` (SP-RE-04: `ISpliceService.HandlePeerFundingLockedAsync`; the fallback to a synthesized `splice_locked` was removed in wave SPR, NL-488), then returns tx_abort (a `next_funding` that is not our latest interactive transaction; through `IInteractiveTxDriver.AbortQuiescence` so the echo is not answered again; when the driver returns none (it holds a negotiation or already waits for its echo) nothing is sent, and a raw `tx_abort` only when no driver is registered), our splice `commitment_signed` again (`NextFundingCommitmentSigned`: rebuilt from the signatures `IChannelFundingDbRepository.GetRemoteCommitmentAsync` stored with the peer's commitment on that funding, byte-identical; a dual-funded open's through `DualFundReestablish`) and our `tx_signatures` (`NextFundingTxSignatures`: `IInteractiveTxDriver.CreateTxSignaturesRetransmission`, else, when the driver no longer holds the negotiation (a restart), `ReestablishService.CreateStoredTxSignaturesAsync` rebuilds them from the stored `InteractiveTxSessions` row (our witnesses and `shared_input_signature`, saved before the first send; nothing when the row says they were never sent)), channel_ready (not when a splice TLV is in either message, SP-RE-05), channel_ready (only for ReadyForUs/Open, point L+1, first local alias or the real scid), the regenerated revoke_and_ack (`CreateRevokeAndAck(L-1, L+1)`), the stored `SentCommitDiff` decoded with `SentCommitDiffCodec` (re-serialized byte-identical), in `LastSentCommitmentMessage` order, then `PendingLocalUpdates` with their original ids. Last, `AnnouncementSignatures` (the peer's TLV 5 bit 0 on our current funding, ready and not sent on this connection: created, `MarkAnnouncementSignaturesSent` saved, `MarkSentOnConnection`, so `ChannelManager`'s own append does not send it twice). A dual-funded open waiting for its funding is loaded first (`DualFundReestablish.EnsureLoadedAsync`) when it has an interactive-tx row. Proofs: `Channels/Splicing/SpliceConformanceTests` (SP-T-03..11 on `SpliceHarness(realEngine: true)`), `Channels/Reestablish/ReestablishFundingLockedTests`, the Domain `ReestablishPlannerSpliceTests`.
- Reestablish lifecycle (`IChannelManager`, `PeerManager`): `PeerManager` calls `OnPeerConnectionChanged(peer)` synchronously when a session is installed (before it becomes `GetPeer`-visible) and when the current session drops; the new session's inbound loop first awaits `OnPeerConnectedAsync(peer)` (reverts the peer's unsigned updates, raises our channel_reestablish for every `V1FundingSigned`/`ReadyForThem`/`ReadyForUs`/`Open`/`ShuttingDown`/`Negotiating`/`Closing` channel with the peer (BOLT 2 MUST; it retransmits a channel_ready lost with the previous connection), returns the stored `error` of each `Failed` channel, which `PeerOutbox.TryEnqueueError` sends without disconnecting), and the loop's `finally` awaits `OnPeerDisconnectedAsync(peer)` (per-channel `RevertUncommitted` + save). The next connection's loop starts only after that. After the handler, `ChannelManager` (under the lock) marks the channel reestablished, calls `IPeerLivenessProbe.MarkLinkUp` and queues the channel's pending HTLC events (NL-252), and after the lock asks `ICommitScheduler.Schedule` when changes are pending for the peer. Normal-operation messages (128-135) on an `Open` channel that is not reestablished get `ChannelWarningException { CloseConnection = true }` (no gating without a registered `ReestablishTracker`, e.g. in older unit tests). Any message on a `Failed` channel throws `ChannelFailedException` carrying the stored error's text (nothing is persisted again); `PeerManager` turns every `ChannelFailedException` into an `error` **without** disconnecting (BOLT 1 MAY; the peer's other channels go on). `IChannelManager.HandleChannelMessageAsync` returns a plain `Task` (NL-234): replies go out only through `OnResponseMessageReady`.

## Quiescence (BOLT 2 "Channel Quiescence", splicing plan wave Q lane Q-B: Q1-T3..T6; `Channels/Quiescence/`)
- `QuiescenceServiceCollectionExtensions.AddQuiescenceServices()` (TryAdd, idempotent; the integrator calls it from `AddApplicationServices`): `QuiescenceService` as itself, `IQuiescenceService` (Domain) and `IStfuReleaseScheduler` (one instance), `QuiescenceTimeoutMonitor`, `IQuiescencePeerDisconnector` → `PeerServiceQuiescenceDisconnector`. Nothing for the host to start (the service starts the monitor with the first quiescence). Options `Node:Quiescence` (`NodeOptions.Quiescence`, Domain `QuiescenceOptions` in `NodeOptions.cs`: `Timeout` 60 s, at most 60 s (BOLT 2 Q-R-03), `IdleTimeout` 5 min, at least `Timeout`; both in the daemon template).
- `QuiescenceService` (singleton, memory only, plan D1): per channel an entry (`QuiescenceState` + peer, connection, start time, the waiter of `RequestAsync`), changed under the channel lock. An entry belongs to the `PeerModel` instance `IPeerManager.GetPeer` returned when it started: it ends when that connection's `IPeerService.OnDisconnect` fires or another connection replaces it (checked by `GetState`), and on `OnPeerDisconnected` (Q-R-04; the only way in the in-process harnesses, which have no peer manager). `RequestAsync` (takes the lock): `Open`, `option_quiesce` negotiated (the connection's negotiated `FeatureOptions`; without a peer manager, our own `Node:Features:OptionQuiesce`), nothing in progress; queues the request, sends `stfu(1)` at once when nothing of ours is pending, schedules a commit (our unsigned updates must drain) and completes with the initiator when quiescent (`Remote` when the peer's `stfu` came first, Q-R-05 funder rule when both sent 1); cancelled before our `stfu` → withdrawn (`OperationCanceledException`); faults with `InvalidOperationException` when the quiescence ends first. `OnStfuReceived` (for lane Q-A's `StfuMessageHandler`, under the lock): no `option_quiesce` (Q-S-01), a second `stfu` (Q-S-03), a `stfu` with `initiator` = 0 when we sent none (Q-S-03, as Q-A's `QuiescenceRules.Receive` `UnsolicitedReply`; nothing is recorded) or a channel not `Open` → `ChannelWarningException { CloseConnection = true }`; ignored (null) on a timed-out entry; quiescent when we had sent ours (Q-R-01), else our reply `stfu(0)` at once or later (Q-R-02). `TryReleaseStfu`: sends the owed `stfu` when `option_quiesce` is negotiated, the channel is `Open`, reestablished on this connection (`ReestablishTracker`, when registered) and `HasPendingLocalUpdates` is false (Q-S-02: our adds 10-13, our removals 35-38, our unfinished fee update; the peer's updates never block us). `Terminate` (under the lock): removes the entry, faults a waiting request, and (except for `Disconnected`) replays the channel's pending HTLC events into the switch (`LinkUpEventReplayer`, the registered one or its own) and schedules a commit, so a fulfill or fail refused meanwhile is sent now. `Timeout` is different: the peer considers the channel quiescent until the disconnection (Q-R-04), so the entry is kept and marked terminated (`QuiescenceSnapshot.TerminatedAt`): `GetState` unchanged (updates still refused), the waiter faulted, no `stfu`, no resume, a cancellation or another end reason ignored; only the disconnection removes it, and the reestablish on the next connection replays the pending events. The BOLT 2 rules come from the Domain `QuiescenceRules` (wave qit integration: `IsNegotiated`, `Receive`/`CreateWarning`, `InitiatorFlagToSend`, `HasPendingLocalUpdates`, `ResolveInitiator`). A `Probe` we initiated has no dependent protocol: once quiescent with us as initiator, a background round under the channel lock calls `IInteractiveTxDriver.AbortQuiescence` and publishes our `tx_abort` (plan D2, SP-Q-01); the driver terminates the quiescence (`TxAbort`) and takes the peer's echo as the echo; without a driver the probe ends only with the disconnection.
- Owed `stfu` after a transition (`IStfuReleaseScheduler.ScheduleRelease`, called by `ChannelStateTransitionService.CommitAsync` after every persisted transition): runs on the thread pool, takes the channel lock once the handler's replies were raised, checks the link (`IPeerLivenessProbe`), calls `TryReleaseStfu` and publishes. It is not published inside the handler because the `revoke_and_ack` the `commitment_signed` handler returns must go out first (a `stfu` ahead of it would announce our changes committed while, for the peer, they are still pending). `WhenIdleAsync` waits for the rounds and replays (tests).
- Gate (Q1-T4): `ChannelOperationsService` (optional `IQuiescenceService` argument) refuses every update while `BlocksNewLocalUpdates` (a queued request, a `stfu` sent or received, quiescent) with `ChannelQuiescentException` (`Q-S-04` once we sent `stfu`, else `Q-R-02`), nothing persisted or sent. `CommitScheduler` is unchanged: pending changes are still signed and revoked (the drain). `HtlcSwitch` is unchanged: it logs the refusal like a link that is down, and the replay at the end of the quiescence (or the reestablish after a disconnect) sends the fulfill (final hop: the set is committed with the preimage on the record and the invoice settled, as for NL-322; forward: the circuit keeps the outgoing record). A forward whose outgoing channel is quiescent is failed back with `temporary_channel_failure` as before.
- `QuiescenceTimeoutMonitor` (Q1-T5): a timer on the injected `TimeProvider` (every tenth of the shorter limit, 1 to 5 s) runs `CheckAsync`: a channel quiescing or quiescent (counted from our request or the first `stfu`) with HTLCs in its snapshot for `Timeout`, or at all for `IdleTimeout`, is marked timed out under its lock (`Terminate(Timeout)`, re-checked there) and its connection closed with a channel `warning` after the lock (`IPeerService.Disconnect`, which keeps our reconnect loop; `IPeerManager.DisconnectPeer` would stop it); while that connection stays open the close is repeated every `Timeout`.
- Wired at the wave qit integration: `ChannelManager` routes `stfu` to `StfuMessageHandler` (Q-A) and, before the update handlers, turns a peer's `update_*` after its `stfu` into `QuiescenceRules.CreateWarning` (Q-S-04 on receive, warning + close; `ThrowIfUpdateAfterPeerStfu`); `AddApplicationServices` calls `AddQuiescenceServices()`; the probe exit above. Since wave sp1 (NL-470): `ChannelManager.OnPeerDisconnectedAsync` calls `IQuiescenceService.OnPeerDisconnected` first (Q-R-04, also for a quiescence not bound to a connection: requested while the peer was away, or in a harness without a peer manager), and `CompleteReestablishAsync` calls `IStfuReleaseScheduler.ScheduleRelease` after a channel is reestablished, so a `stfu` owed from before the reestablish goes out then (after the reestablish's replies) instead of waiting for a commitment transition. `QuiescenceService.QuiescenceEnded` (event, once per quiescence, with the `QuiescenceEndReason`; raised under the channel lock for `Terminate`, without it on a disconnection) is how a dependent protocol learns its quiescence is over (the splice).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Quiescence/` (`QuiescenceTestPair`: `TwoNodeHarness` with the production services, a manual clock, a recording disconnector and a fulfilling switch; `stfu` is still routed by the kit straight to the receiver's service under its lock, in wire order, by reflection over `HarnessNode._outbox`, although `ChannelManager` has the case since the wave qit integration (NL-470); `QuiescenceHarnessTests` (Proof Q1-T6: simultaneous `stfu` with the funder as initiator, HTLCs in flight both ways with each `stfu` after its sender's last commitment message, a fulfill refused while quiescing and sent once after the end, disconnect, the 60 s and idle timeouts, a stuck request timed out, a second `stfu`), `QuiescenceServiceTests` (initiator table, the pending rule, the peer's `stfu` over our queued request, cancel, refusals, connection binding), `QuiescenceGateTests`, `QuiescenceSwitchTests` (the production `HtlcSwitch` on `ThreeNodeHarness`: final hop and forward fulfills held by a quiescent channel are sent once after `Terminate`)).

## Splicing (BOLT 2 "Channel Splicing", splicing plan wave SP1 lane SP1-D; `Channels/Splicing/`)
- `SpliceServiceCollectionExtensions.AddSpliceServices()` (TryAdd, idempotent; called by `AddApplicationServices` after `AddInteractiveTxServices`): `SpliceService` as itself, `ISpliceService` (Domain) and `ISpliceCommitmentReceiver` (one instance), `SpliceDepthWatcher` (subscribes to `IBlockchainMonitor.OnTransactionConfirmed` in its constructor; `SpliceService` resolves it in its own constructor and the watcher resolves the service lazily, so it runs from the first splice message or operator splice of the process on (found missing by Proof SP1: nothing resolved it); the host resolves it and awaits `CatchUpAsync()` at startup, after the chain monitor and `PeerManager.StartAsync`: every pending splice of a loaded channel without our `splice_locked` whose `WatchedTransactions` row already completed is handed to `OnSpliceDepthReachedAsync` (a confirmation raised while nothing listened)), `ISpliceOutDestination` → `WalletSpliceOutDestination` (the operator's address on our network, else a new P2WPKH wallet address) and `ISpliceStatePort` → `EngineSpliceStatePort` (the seam over lanes SP1-B/SP1-C: the engine's `SignSpliceCommitment`/`ReceiveSpliceCommitment`/`LockFunding`/`DiscardPendingFundings`, the signer's `RegisterFunding`/`MarkSpliceCommitmentPersisted`/`LockFunding`, the `IChannelFundingDbRepository` rows; the engine decides which fundings are active (the peer's verified splice `commitment_signed` makes the funding pending before `tx_signatures`), the port overlays the `splice_locked` flags and confirmation height; `Stage*`/`Sign*`/`Receive*` stage on the caller's unit of work, `On*Saved`/`ApplyFundings` apply after the save; a `tx_abort` after the commitment step discards the pending funding through `Discard` + `StageFundingsAsync`). Options `Splice` (`SpliceOptions`: `RotateFundingKey` (D5, default true: `ILightningSigner.GetFundingPubKey` with the next key index), `MinFeeratePerKw` 253 / `MaxFeeratePerKw` 250,000 (a peer's `splice_init` outside, or its `tx_init_rbf` above: `tx_abort`), `MaxRbfFeeShareSatoshis` (50,000), `RequireConfirmedInputs`). The handlers `Handlers/Splice{Init,Ack,Locked}MessageHandler` are on the explicit handler list in `DependencyInjection.cs` (NL-055); `ChannelManager` routes 80/81/77 after `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync` and the B2-RE-07 gate, and in the `commitment_signed` case first asks `ISpliceCommitmentReceiver.IsSpliceCommitmentSigned` (the CS for a splice's new funding is not a commitment update: same number, no RAA, SP-CS-01/02).
- `SpliceService` (singleton, negotiations in memory keyed by channel id, changed under the channel lock): `StartAsync` (the operator; takes the lock itself) checks `SpliceRules.CheckSendInit` without quiescence, reserves the splice-in wallet inputs (`IInteractiveTxContributor`, weight of the common fields + shared input + funding output), `IQuiescenceService.RequestAsync(Splice)` (a `Remote` initiator ends it: the peer's splice goes first), then under the lock checks again with the real quiescence, sends `splice_init` and waits for the negotiation's end (`SpliceResult`). A splice-out's contribution is `-(amount + fee)`, the fee of the common fields, the shared input and output and the splice-out output at the feerate (D16). `HandleSpliceInitAsync` (SP-R-01 via `SpliceRules.CheckReceiveInit`; warning + close or `tx_abort` through `IInteractiveTxDriver.AbortQuiescence`, which also ends the quiescence) accepts with contribution 0 (D10), starts the driver as non-initiator and answers `splice_ack`; `HandleSpliceAckAsync` (SP-R-02) starts the driver as initiator with our contribution. The host `SpliceNegotiationHost : IInteractiveTxHost` (purpose `Splice`, one per negotiation) supplies the `SharedFundingSpec` (the current funding as shared input with `SpliceFundingScripts.SharedInputWeight`, the new P2WSH 2-of-2 output of both new keys, shares = the gross balances before and after the contributions), runs `SpliceRules.CheckTxComplete` on the constructed transaction, then `ISpliceStatePort.SignSpliceCommitmentAsync`; the peer's splice CS (`HandleSpliceCommitmentSignedAsync`) is verified and staged by the port and saved in its own save before `ISpliceStatePort.OnSpliceCommitmentSaved` (SP-I1 in the signer) and `IInteractiveTxDriver.OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync` (our `tx_signatures` when we sign first); an invalid one ends the negotiation with our `tx_abort`. `shared_input_signature` comes from `ILightningSigner.SignSpliceSharedInput`, the peer's is checked with `ValidateSpliceSharedInputSignature` when the witness is built (invalid: `ChannelFailedException` `SP-SIG-01`, `MustBroadcast`), the witness is `SpliceFundingScripts.BuildWitness` (BCL-only DER and witness serialization, keys in lexicographic order). The completion stages, in the driver's save, the pending funding (`ISpliceStatePort.AddPending` + `StageFundingsAsync`), the splice transaction as a `BroadcastTransactions` row (purpose `Funding`) and a `WatchedTransactions` row at `max(1, minimum_depth)` (D8); it is applied to memory (port, monitor tracking and publish, the waiter) when the driver ends the quiescence right after that save (`QuiescenceService.QuiescenceEnded` with `TxSignaturesExchanged`, under the lock). Any other end of the quiescence while a completion is staged (a disconnection, raised without the lock, possibly during the driver's save), or a completion staged when no quiescence is left to end, is applied only by a background round that takes the channel lock (so the driver's save is over) and finds the negotiation's `InteractiveTxSessions` row `Signed`; a failed save applies, tracks and publishes nothing (persist, then memory, then send). Any other end of the quiescence (disconnection, `tx_abort`, timeout) ends a negotiation not remembered yet (its wallet reservation released); from our CS on it is kept for the reconnection (wave SP2). A `tx_abort` after the commitment step (before our `tx_signatures`) also takes the new funding out of the pending set when the peer's splice `commitment_signed` already put it there (`ISpliceStatePort.Discard` + `StageFundingsAsync`, own save), and otherwise marks the `ChannelFundings` row our `commitment_signed`'s save wrote `Discarded` (own save), so a restart does not load it as a pending splice. After a restart the reestablish handler calls `SpliceService.EnsureLoadedAsync(channel, uow)` when the latest interactive tx is a splice without the peer's `tx_signatures`: the negotiation is rebuilt from the latest non-aborted, not `Signed` `InteractiveTxSessions` row of purpose `Splice` and its `Pending` `ChannelFundings` row (contributions from the funding's deltas, the shared funding recomputed) and resumed in the driver (`ResumeAsync`), so the peer's retransmitted splice `commitment_signed` and `tx_signatures` complete it (wave sp2 integration; `SpliceConformanceTests.Given_BothCommitSigsLostAndTheInitiatorRestarted_*`). The peer's splice `commitment_signed` is saved in the engine (SP-I2) one save before the driver's step records it in the session row (and signs when we sign first); a crash between the two (NL-698) is completed on reconnection: `ReestablishService` counts the commitment as received once the restored local commitment holds the peer's signatures on that funding (no `next_funding` bit 0, so the peer's retransmission is never refused as SP-OP-05), and `EnsureLoadedAsync` replays `IInteractiveTxDriver.OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync` after `ResumeAsync`, so the `NextFundingTxSignatures` step finds our `tx_signatures` (proof: `SpliceConformanceTests.Given_ACrashAtEverySpliceSave_*`, a crash at every save of both sides; the SP-T-04/05/08 restart variants are there too, NL-496).
- `splice_locked` (wave SP2 lane SP2-B, BOLT 2 "Splice Completion"): `SpliceDepthWatcher` hands a confirmation of a pending splice to `SpliceService.OnSpliceDepthReachedAsync` (idempotent: nothing when ours was sent; save `SpliceLockedSent` + the confirmation height and real SCID, then publish `splice_locked` followed by whatever the lock returns); `HandleSpliceLockedAsync` (SP-LK-02: not a pending splice → warning + close; the current funding's txid or a duplicate → ignored) and `ISpliceService.HandlePeerFundingLockedAsync(channel, txid, uow)` (SP-RE-04, for lane SP2-A's reestablish: a pending splice whose `splice_locked` we have not received is processed as one, anything else ignored without a warning) save `SpliceLockedReceived`; the lock happens only when both flags are on the **same** txid (SP-LK-03: `ISpliceStatePort.Lock` discards the RBF siblings and ancestors); a peer's `splice_locked` for another RBF candidate than ours is remembered and logged, never failed (D11). Before the lock the current funding gets the channel's SCID when its row lacked it, so the `Replaced` row keeps it (the retired map is rebuilt from it). The lock's save also stores a `FundingOutput` `WatchedOutpoints` row for the new funding outpoint (when missing), tracked after the save; since lane SP2-C the new funding outpoint is watched from the save before our splice `commitment_signed` (`Onchain/SpliceFundingWatch`, staged in `CreateSpliceCommitmentSignedAsync` and tracked after that save), so the lock finds the row and adds none. After the lock's save (`AfterLockAsync`, still under the lock): the replaced SCID is retired in `IRetiredScidMap` (`RetiredScidMap`, D12: retired at the splice's confirmation height, resolves until + `RetiredShortChannelId.RetentionBlocks` (72); in memory, pruned on every `OnNewBlockDetected` and refused by `TryResolve` at the monitor's tip from its expiry on; `LoadAsync(height)` rebuilds it from the `ChannelFundings` rows of the non-Closed/Stale channels: each `Replaced` funding with a SCID retires at the next locked funding's `ConfirmedHeight`; `HtlcSwitch.ResolveOutgoingChannel` falls back to it for an `Open` channel; a channel whose `option_scid_alias` is Compulsory never retires its real SCID, neither at the lock nor in `LoadAsync` (BOLT 2: no HTLC by the real SCID, NL-348); registered by `AddSpliceServices`; the host must await `IRetiredScidMap.LoadAsync(monitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight)` before `PeerManager.StartAsync`), and for a public channel the announcement restarts on the splice (SP-G-01): both halves of the replaced funding's `announcement_signatures` were already reset in the lock's own save (`StageAnnouncementResetAsync` stages the channel row through `ChannelDbRepository.UpdateAsync` before it, which keeps the lock's funding columns and SCID; a failed save puts the halves back on the shared model), then `IChannelAnnouncementService.OnShortChannelIdChanged`, a peer half deferred before our `splice_locked` is taken (`ProcessDeferredRemoteAnnouncementSignaturesAsync`), otherwise ours is returned when the splice already has the announcement depth (`PrepareOwnAnnouncementSignaturesAsync`), then `CompleteAnnouncementAsync`; later the channel manager's block-driven announcement round sends ours at the splice's 6th confirmation, as for an open. Our `channel_update` follows the new SCID through `ChannelUpdateService` (below). Pending splices are restored into the engine snapshot after a restart: `ChannelStateDbRepository.LoadAsync` passes every `Pending` `ChannelFundings` row whose local slot carries the peer's signatures at our current number (and the unacked remote slot's signatures) as `pendingFundings`; a row with only our `commitment_signed` stays out, and stored signatures that do not fit load the channel on its current funding (wave sp2 integration). Splice RBF: see "Splice RBF" below. On the lock `EngineSpliceStatePort.ApplyFundings` moves `ChannelModel.ShortChannelId` to the splice's and calls the signer's 3-argument `LockFunding`.
- Splice RBF (wave SPR lane SPR-A, NL-489, NL-481; `SpliceService.Rbf.cs`): any quiescence initiator may RBF the pending splice (BOLT 2: not only the splice initiator); the `tx_init_rbf` sender is the interactive-tx initiator of the new attempt (shared input, funding output, common fields). `BumpAsync(SpliceBumpRequest)` (the contract's `BumpAsync(channelId, feerate)` forwards to it; takes the lock itself like `StartAsync`): `SpliceRules.CheckSendRbf` without quiescence, `MaxFeeSatoshis` against our fee share, `IQuiescenceService.RequestAsync(SpliceRbf)`, the rules again quiescent, then `IInteractiveTxDriver.RequestRbfAsync` and our `tx_init_rbf` with the **signed** contribution (the driver's message is rewritten by `WithContribution`, negative for a splice-out, NL-481; a splice RBF's `tx_init_rbf`/`tx_ack_rbf` always carries the TLV, 0 included, since CLN v26.06.8 fails an RBF without it, NL-503; the dual-funded RBF still omits a 0). The peer's `tx_init_rbf`/`tx_ack_rbf` go through `TxInitRbfMessageHandler`/`TxAckRbfMessageHandler` to `HandleTxInitRbfAsync`/`HandleTxAckRbfAsync` when `HandlesRbf` (Open/ShuttingDown/Negotiating and `option_splice` negotiated; else the driver as before, a dual-funded open): `SpliceRules.CheckReceiveRbf` (warning + close for a broken MUST: not quiescent, not the quiescence initiator, the sender's `splice_locked` (SP-LK-04, NL-489), zeroconf, a splice-out above its balance; else `tx_abort`: feerate floor, "created recently" (NL-520: until `Splice:MinRbfBlocks` (1) new blocks since the latest attempt, whose creation height is its broadcast row's `FirstBroadcastHeight`; an explicit `Splice:MinRbfInterval` replaces it with a wall clock), > 10 attempts below the next-block estimate, nothing pending, a negotiation running, a batch above 20) / `CheckReceiveAckRbf`, then the RBF negotiation (funding keys of the pending splice: `tx_init_rbf` carries none) and the driver; a `tx_abort` the driver answers also ends the quiescence. Our contribution is rebuilt from the latest attempt's stored `InteractiveTxSessions` row (`PlanRbfContribution`: the same wallet inputs and reservation paying the new fee from the change, or the same splice-out output with the fee from our balance; as initiator without either, the shared fee from our balance; a peer's RBF we cannot pay into gets contribution 0). The driver learns the signed attempts from their rows (`ResumeAsync`) and the channel's one `SpliceNegotiationHost` is pointed at the new negotiation (`_hosts`). Before our `commitment_signed` the attempt is checked (`CheckRbfAttempt`: the shared input is the current funding output, `FundingSet.AddRbfSibling`: replaces the latest attempt, beats its feerate, batch <= 20) and SP-TX-05 with the previous attempt's fee; the funding is a `SpliceRbf` with `RbfOf`. The completion stores the broadcast with `ReplacesTransactionId` and leaves the attempt it bumps `Pending` (wave SPR review: the chain monitor keeps sending every attempt and the mempool keeps the one it accepts; a replacement a bitcoind refuses under BIP 125 rule 4, or one whose peer input was double-spent, never leaves the splice without a broadcast attempt); the lock of any attempt discards the others in its save (`FundingSet.Lock`) and marks their broadcasts `Abandoned`. Their wallet reservations are released by the executor's `DiscardedSpliceReservations` (lane SPR-E) once the lock is irrevocable (NL-492), never at the lock. A peer's `tx_init_rbf` above `Splice:MaxFeeratePerKw` gets `tx_abort` (in a peer's RBF we pay our share of the fee at its feerate), and our share above `Splice:MaxRbfFeeShareSatoshis` (50,000 sat, null = off) or above half of what our contribution moves makes us contribute nothing to that attempt (`GetRbfFeeShareRefusal`). An RBF attempt that took over the channel's host (`SpliceNegotiation.HostPredecessor`) gives it back when it ends without completing (`RestoreHost`, from `End`), and the lock removes the channel's `_hosts` entry. Harness restart (NL-508): `SpliceHarness.RestartAsync` registers the saved fundings with the restarted node's signer the way production loads them through `ChannelSigningInfoDbRepository` (every funding row except the current one, plus the SP-I1 marks of the pending fundings whose local commitment the snapshot holds with the peer's signatures), so a restarted node signs batches and splice shared inputs without a re-registration. Tests: `Channels/Splicing/{SpliceRbfHarnessTests,SpliceRbfContributionTests}`, Domain `Channels/Splicing/{SpliceRbfRulesTests,FundingSetRbfTests}`.
- Gossip across a splice (lane SP2-B, NL-478): `ChannelAnnouncementBuilder.BuildUnsigned`/`GetRemoteSignatureChecks` name `ChannelModel.LocalFundingPubKey`/`RemoteFundingPubKey` (the current funding's, rotated per splice), never the key sets' original funding keys. `ChannelAnnouncementService` (optional `ISpliceStatePort` constructor argument for the pending fundings and their flags; the engine's fundings without it): `IsReadyForAnnouncementSignatures(channel, txid)` is true only for the current funding when `CanSendAnnouncementSignatures` (a pending or replaced funding never), `ShouldDeferRemoteAnnouncementSignatures(channel, scid)` is true for a SCID that is not the channel's and names a pending splice we have not sent `splice_locked` for (its known SCID, else its funding output index), and `AnnouncementSignaturesMessageHandler` then keeps the half in memory (`DeferRemoteAnnouncementSignatures`, the latest per channel) instead of the SCID warning; `ProcessDeferredRemoteAnnouncementSignaturesAsync` stores it when it signs the channel's current announcement (saved), answers with ours when due, and hands on the assembled announcement. `ChannelUpdateService` remembers each open channel's SCID from `OnChannelUpdated`; when it moves after our first update (a splice lock, also a reorg, NL-350) a new update for the new SCID goes to the peer once the lock is released (private, `dont_forward`, until the splice is announced; the old SCID's public update is neither disabled nor withdrawn: the network forgets it 72 blocks after the spend, and the retired map forwards it meanwhile). Invoices' `r` hints read `ChannelModel.ShortChannelId`, so they follow the lock. Tests: `Channels/Splicing/{SpliceLockRulesTests,RetiredScidMapTests,RetiredScidForwardTests}` (the forward over the old SCID in `ThreeNodeHarness`, lock reproduced at the switch boundary), `Gossip/Announcements/{ChannelAnnouncementSpliceTests,SpliceAnnouncementHarnessTests}` (a real splice of an announced channel on `SpliceHarness(realEngine: true, announceChannel: true, configureServices)` re-announced at 6 confirmations with the new SCID and rotated keys), `Gossip/ChannelUpdateServiceSpliceTests`.
- Splice auto-bump (wave SPR, SPR-T3, lane SPR-B; `Onchain/Fees/SpliceAutoBumper : ISpliceAutoBumper`, registered by `Onchain/Fees/SpliceAutoBumperServiceCollectionExtensions.AddSpliceAutoBumper()`, TryAdd, as itself and the interface; the host calls `Start()` after the chain monitor and `PeerManager.StartAsync`, `StopAsync()` before the chain monitor stops). Off unless `Splice:AutoBumpAfterBlocks` is set above 0 (`Start` then subscribes to `OnNewBlockDetected`; rounds coalesce like `OnionReplayBlockPruner`'s). `BumpStaleSplicesAsync(height)` (idempotent per height) bumps an `Open` channel's latest pending attempt (`ISpliceStatePort.GetFundings(channel).LatestAttempt`) when: no attempt confirmed or `splice_locked` sent, no splice negotiation running (`GetNegotiation` not Signed/Aborted), ours (non-zero `LocalBalanceDeltaMsat`, or our last negotiation named an attempt), fewer than `Splice:MaxRbfAttempts` RBF attempts, its `BroadcastTransactions` row still `Pending`, no bump of ours running, and `AutoBumpAfterBlocks` blocks since its `FirstBroadcastHeight` (no row: the height the bumper first saw it) and since our last try on that attempt (memory), so at most once per interval. Feerate max(fee estimate, `InteractiveTxRbfRules.GetMinimumNextFeerate`) clamped to `Splice:AutoBumpMaxFeeratePerKw` (default 25,000 sat/kw; the operator's limit, not the acceptor bound `MaxFeeratePerKw`); a splice whose RBF minimum is above that ceiling is skipped and counted as a try (one warning per interval). `ISpliceService.BumpAsync(new SpliceBumpRequest(channel, feerate, Splice:AutoBumpMaxFeeSat))` (default fee cap 100,000 sat, null = none); a throw becomes an `Aborted` result with its reason (retried one interval later). A round waits for each bump at most `Splice:AutoBumpMaxWait` (120 s); a slower negotiation goes on, is reported with its current state, keeps its channel skipped until it ends, and `StopAsync` cancels and awaits it. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Fees/SpliceAutoBumperTests`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Splicing/` (`SpliceHarness` = `TwoNodeHarness` with the production splice, quiescence and interactive-tx services, every message through the receiver's `ChannelManager`, the real `InteractiveTxBuilder`, `FakeSpliceStatePort` and `SpliceSigningProxy` standing in for lanes SP1-B/SP1-C, `FakeInteractiveTxContributor` wallets; `SpliceHarnessTests`: SP-T-01 message sequence, a `tx_abort` after the commitment step, splice-in and splice-out each side initiating, updates while pending, lock at depth, concurrent `splice_locked`, the rejections, SP-SIG-01, and the NL-470 seams; `SpliceNode.WatchedOutpoints` and `SpliceNode.FailSave` (a save that throws with nothing committed) record and break the node's saves; `SpliceEngineHarnessTests` on `SpliceHarness(realEngine: true)` (the production `EngineSpliceStatePort`, the real signer splice members, an in-memory `InMemoryChannelFundingRepository`, `start_batch` groups delivered through `HandleCommitmentSignedBatchAsync` as `PeerManager` groups them): SP-T-01 with `start_batch(2)` + one `commitment_signed` per funding both ways and a single one on the new funding after the lock, splice-in and splice-out each side initiating with a payment each way while pending, a lock that waits for the other side, SP-T-02's crossing `splice_locked` (the obsolete batch member ignored), the discard after a `tx_abort`; `SpliceFundingScriptsTests` against NBitcoin (a 2-of-2 spend verified by the interpreter); `SpliceRegistrationTests`).

## Simple taproot channels: open, operation, reestablish (NL-877 T3/T5, taproot wave t02 lane OPS; `Channels/Taproot/`)
- **Opens (v1 and dual-funded):** `openchannel --channel-type taproot` (IPC key 11 `ChannelType`, `OpenChannelClientRequest.IsSimpleTaproot`) needs our `Features:OptionSimpleTaproot` advertised (experimental: `AllowExperimentalFeatures`), the peer's 80/81 and `option_simple_close`, and a private channel (`OpenChannelClientHandler.CheckSimpleTaprootPeer`/`CheckSimpleTaprootRequest`; `--request-inbound` buys liquidity over the P2TR funding script since t03 lane RBF, NL-971); it opens v2 by the NL-551 rules (`DualFundedOpenRequest.SimpleTaproot`, lane V2 below) and v1 with `--v1` or a push. On the peer side the validator accepts the type only where `ChannelOpenMandatoryValidationParameters.AllowSimpleTaproot` is set (the v1 payloads and both dual-funded checks). Channel type {80} (+46/+50), never 12/22; `announce_channel` with it fails the open. `open_channel`/`accept_channel` carry our commitment 0 verification nonce (`GetLocalVerificationNonce(keyIndex, null, 0)`); the peer's is required, checked as two points (`TaprootChannelNonces.IsValidPublicNonce`) and kept in memory as `ChannelModel.RemoteOpeningNonce`. `funding_created`/`funding_signed` carry a zero signature and `partial_signature_with_nonce` (required; verified for local commitment 0, ours signed against the peer's opening nonce); the peer's is `ChannelModel.LastReceivedPartialSignature` (persisted). The funding output is the MuSig2 P2TR one (`FundingOutputInfo.IsSimpleTaproot`, set by `ChannelModel` from `ChannelParams.OptionSimpleTaproot`; `FundingTransactionBuilder.BuildFundingTxOut`).
- **Nonce numbering** (`TaprootChannelNonces`, counter-derived by the signer, never stored): `channel_ready` = our nonce for local commitment 1 (`FundingConfirmedMessageHandler`, reestablish retransmission); `revoke_and_ack` = per active funding, the nonce for `OutboundRevokeAndAck.NextCommitmentNumber` (`ChannelStateTransitionService.CreateRevokeAndAck`, also the reestablish retransmission); `channel_reestablish` = the nonce for our `next_commitment_number` (`ReestablishService.CreateOwnAsync`). The peer's `channel_ready` nonce is required (`TAPROOT-CR-R01`) and feeds the first snapshot; a re-sent `channel_ready` (LND after reestablish) is taken only while no commitment was signed since the open, else ignored.
- **Normal operation:** `ToWireMessage` sends the taproot `commitment_signed` overload whenever the engine's signatures carry a `PartialSignature`; `ToReceivedSignatures` refuses a non-zero `signature` (`TAPROOT-CS-R00`) and hands the TLV to the engine (absent: `TAPROOT-CS-R01`); `RevokeAndAckMessageHandler` hands `next_local_nonces` to `ReceiveRevoke` (absent or missing a funding: `TAPROOT-NONCE-R01`).
- **Reestablish** (`TaprootReestablish`): the peer's map is required (`TAPROOT-RE-R01`) and replaces the engine's nonces (one save) before any retransmission; our unacked `commitment_signed` is never replayed from `SentCommitDiff`: the diff's updates go out as stored, then `ResignRemoteNextCommit` signs again (fresh JIT nonce, the new signatures and diff saved first).
- **Proofs:** `Channels/Taproot/TaprootHarnessTests` (two-node harness `simpleTaproot: true`: link drops at every boundary and a crash at every save without a signing nonce sent twice), `TaprootOpenHarness`(Tests) (SQLite v1 open, payments, restarts, broadcast script verification, open refusals), `TaprootSqliteCrashTests` (D-T4 on SQLite), `TaprootForwardTests` (`ThreeNodeHarness simpleTaproot: true`).
- **Splices of simple taproot channels** (NL-965, taproot wave t03 lane SPL, BOLTs PR #1324 as Eclair 0.14.3 speaks it): in, out and RBF in both roles, through the same `SpliceService`/driver path as any splice (`SpliceNegotiation.IsSimpleTaproot`). A taproot splice always rotates the funding key (`GetNewFundingKey`, whatever `Splice:RotateFundingKey`; the signer refuses a `Splice`/`SpliceRbf` pending funding on key 0: key 0's commitment 0 nonce of a v1 open has no txid, NL-972). Shared input and new funding are MuSig2 P2TR (`SpliceFundingScripts.CreateScriptPubKey`, `TaprootSharedInputWeight` 230 wu; `SharedFundingInput.IsTaproot`). Every `tx_complete` we send carries `commit_nonces` (our verification nonces of our current local commitment and the next one on the transaction negotiated so far, `ILightningSigner.GetLocalVerificationNonce(channelId, fundingKeyIndex, txid, n)`, always txid-bound) and `funding_nonce` (one JIT signing nonce per attempt, `CreateSpliceFundingNonce`, secret in signer memory); the peer's last ones are required (`MissingCommitNonce`/`MissingFundingNonce` → our `tx_abort`). Our splice `commitment_signed` is a partial signature against the peer's current commit nonce (`ChannelCommitments.SignSpliceCommitment(funding, signer, remoteNonce)`); the peer's next commit nonce becomes `RemoteNextNonces[splice]` with its splice `commitment_signed` (`ReceiveSpliceCommitment(..., remoteNextNonce)`). The shared input is signed at the commitment step (`IInteractiveTxHost.SignSharedInputPartialAsync` → `SignSpliceSharedInputPartial`, BIP 341 over every spent output; the nonce is consumed) and the partial signature is stored with the session row before our `commitment_signed` (`InteractiveTxSessionModel.OurSharedInputPartialSignature`; both sides' ride in the witness blobs as format version 2, no column); it leaves only in `tx_signatures` type 2 after the peer's `commitment_signed` (IT-SIG-03, the release half of SP-I1 for taproot). The peer's type 2 is required and checked when the witness is built (`AggregateSpliceSharedInputSignature`; missing or invalid → `ChannelFailedException` SP-SIG-01, `MustBroadcast`). Reestablish: our `channel_reestablish` names the splice in negotiation in `next_local_nonces` (our nonce for `next_commitment_number`) and sends `current_commit_nonce` while the peer's splice `commitment_signed` is missing; `SpliceService.ReceiveReestablishNonces` takes the peer's (after `EnsureLoadedAsync`), and our splice `commitment_signed` is re-signed against its type 24 (`ResignSpliceCommitmentAsync`), never replayed. Liquidity ads with a taproot splice are refused both ways (NL-971). A force close on a pending or locked splice funding aggregates the MuSig2 key path (`ChannelFailureService.BuildOnFundingAsync`, `LocalCommitmentBroadcastBuilder`). Tests: `Channels/Splicing/SpliceTaprootHarnessTests` (in, out, RBF both roles, lock, payments, force closes, shared input script-executed, nonce and signature refusals), the taproot cases of `SpliceConformanceTests` (reestablish flows and a crash at every save), Infrastructure.Bitcoin `Taproot/SimpleTaprootSpliceSigningTests`, Domain `SimpleTaprootCommitmentsTests`/`InteractiveTxSessionTests`, Integration `InteractiveTxSessionPersistenceTests`.

## Cooperative close (BOLT2 plan N10: N10-T1..T3)
- States: `Open` → `ShuttingDown` (23, a `shutdown` sent or received; persisted with the script) → `Negotiating` (25, both scripts known and `ChannelCommitments.IsCleared`: no HTLC, every fee update final, no revoke_and_ack owed) → `Closing` (30, the agreed fully signed closing tx persisted in `ChannelModel.ClosingTransaction` before it is sent or broadcast) → `Closed` (40, when the watched closing tx reaches `ChannelCloseOptions.ConfirmationDepth`, default 6; `ChannelManager.CompleteCloseAsync` persists it and removes the channel from memory). Scripts and the closing tx are columns of `Channels` (migration `AddShutdownState`).
- `Channels/Close/ChannelCloseCoordinator` (scoped, runs under the channel's lock, returns the messages to send in wire order): `InitiateAsync` (IPC: signs our pending updates first with `ChannelStateTransitionService.SignIfPendingAsync`, B2-SHUT-S03; refuses when they wait for a revoke_and_ack; only Open channels; once only), `ReceiveShutdownAsync` (script form per `Domain/Channels/Closing/ShutdownScriptValidator`, anysegwit/simple_close from the negotiated features: warning, state unchanged, B2-SHUT-R02; the peer's upfront script when `option_upfront_shutdown_script` was negotiated: warning + close, B2-SHUT-R05; a second shutdown with another script: warning + close; before channel_ready: warning (the MAY of B2-SHUT-R03 is not implemented); in Closing: ignored, except that a funder on the legacy close answers the peer's `shutdown` after a reconnection with the agreed `closing_signed` (its fee and our signature of the stored transaction, `fee_range` [fee, fee] when we send ranges), once per connection (NL-725: CLN restarts closingd on reconnection and waits for the funder; the peer's answer at the agreed fee is then ignored); replies with ours once none of our updates is unsigned, signing them first), `AdvanceAsync` (our deferred shutdown, the move to Negotiating, and, for the funder, the opening `closing_signed` once both shutdowns went over the **current** connection), `ReceiveClosingSignedAsync` (fee above the funder's balance or an inverted range: warning + close; the signature must verify for one variant (outputs trimmed by the peer's dust limit, the same without the peer's output, or trimmed by ours; B2-CLS-R01); any output below its script's BOLT 3 dust threshold fails the channel (B2-CLS-R10); then the pure `LegacyClosingNegotiator` decides: propose, agree (sign the variant the peer signed, persist Closing, echo when due, with the closing tx's `WatchedTransactionModel` staged in the **same** save as Closing (then `IBlockchainMonitor.TrackWatchedTransaction` + `PublishTransactionAsync`; a failed broadcast is logged), warn or fail), in Closing a peer's `closing_signed` (it restarted the negotiation after our final one was lost) is answered with our signature of the stored tx at its fee (a different fee: always; the same fee: once per connection, `ClosingNegotiationRegistry.Entry.AgreedClosingSignedSentOnConnection`, so two Closing nodes never echo forever), and `CreateShutdownResend` (B2-RE-28). From the first shutdown on, every proposal we sign can be broadcast, so the funding outpoint is watched for a spend (`IBlockchainMonitor.WatchOutpointSpend`; ChannelManager re-registers it at startup for ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing). Fee: the IPC request's feerate, else the estimate of `ClosingFeeEstimator` (singleton, `AddChannelCloseServices`; one `IFeeService.GetFeeRatePerKwAsync` fetch at a time, the last good value kept for `Node:Close:FeeEstimateMaxAge` (10 min), a failed fetch (exception or 0) not retried before `FeeEstimateRetryAfter` (1 min)) read once per negotiation (per connection) into `ClosingNegotiationRegistry.Entry.EstimateFeeratePerKw` (the host's fee service is a transient typed HttpClient, so the cached value alone was always 0 and every close proposed the floor, W4-E). Under the channel lock only the first message of a negotiation waits for a fetch, at most `FeeEstimateWaitUnderLock` (2 s; `Entry.EstimateAttempted`), later ones take what the estimator has; the IPC close prefetches before taking the lock and a received `shutdown` starts a fetch without waiting (W4-E review F3); without an estimate the fee service's cached value, then the floor. Floor 253 sat/kw, × `ClosingFeeCalculator.EstimateWeight`; the funder accepts up to `ChannelCloseOptions.MaxFeeMultiplier` (3) × its estimate, the non-funder anything from the relay floor up to the funder's balance. `fee_range` is sent unless `ChannelCloseOptions.SendFeeRange` (`Node:Close:SendFeeRange`) or the IPC request turns it off; a peer that sends none is answered without one too.
- `ClosingNegotiationRegistry` (singleton): per channel, what happened on the current connection (shutdown sent/received), the running `ClosingNegotiation` (memory only; `ChannelManager.OnPeerConnectedAsync` resets it: BOLT 2 restarts the negotiation on reconnection, B2-RE-29), the IPC request, the waiters for the closing tx and the two closing deadlines (`ReplyDueAt`, dropped by a new connection; `FeeRangeDueAt`, kept across reconnections).
- Closing timeouts (NL-284, ABCD W4-E): `ClosingTimeoutMonitor` (singleton, `AddChannelCloseServices`, optional `TimeProvider`) runs one timer per channel. The coordinator arms `ReplyDueAt` = now + `Node:Close:ClosingSignedReplyTimeout` (5 min) whenever it sends a `closing_signed` that needs an answer (the funder's opening one, every proposal) and clears it on any `closing_signed` received, on agreement, on a new connection and when the channel fails out of the negotiation (B2-CLS-R10 or a negotiator failure, which also clear `FeeRangeDueAt`); the reply deadline only counts while the peer is on the connection the channel's link is pinned to (`IPeerLivenessProbe`, checked when the timer fires): a peer that dropped after our `closing_signed` is not failed for it (W4-E review F1; checked before the lock, so a drop in the instant between the check and the lock is not covered); a `fee_range` without overlap (the R04 warning) arms `FeeRangeDueAt` = first such range + `FeeRangeTimeout` (10 min), cleared by any non-warning outcome (an overlapping range, a closing_signed without range, agreement). When one passes, the monitor calls `IChannelFailureService.FailChannelAsync` (broadcast; `B2-CLS-03` / `B2-CLS-R04`) with a `StillApplies` precondition (still `Negotiating` and still past the deadline, checked under the channel lock). Zero or negative timeouts turn them off. Memory only: a restart restarts the negotiation.
- `ChannelCloseService : IChannelCloseService` (Domain; singleton, IPC `closechannel`): lock, link check (`IPeerLivenessProbe`), `InitiateAsync` + `AdvanceAsync`, publish, optional wait for the closing tx. A ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing channel only reports.
