Our upfront script (NL-045): `UpfrontShutdownScriptSource` (singleton, `AddChannelCloseServices`) sets, when both nodes advertised `option_upfront_shutdown_script` (`FeatureOptions.UpfrontShutdownScript` negotiated > No; the feature still defaults to No), a fresh P2WPKH wallet address reserved in the wallet (`IBitcoinWalletService.ReserveUnusedAddressAsync`, own scope and save; `WalletAddresses.IsReserved`, never handed out by `GetUnusedAddressAsync` again) as `ChannelParams.Local.UpfrontShutdownScript` (`ChannelModel.SetLocalUpfrontShutdownScript`, only in V1Opening), persisted with the channel config; `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` does it after the channel is admitted (anchors reserve checked) and the Daemon's `OpenChannelClientHandler` after the funding UTXOs are locked, both before open/accept is built (optional constructor argument; without it, or without the feature, a zero-length script is sent as before). The Domain `ChannelFactory` no longer refuses a peer that requires the feature (a zero-length script is allowed). `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` assigns the script after the temporary channel is stored (and removes it again when the assignment throws) and passes the peer: the source remembers each fundee script with its temporary channel and hands it to a later fundee open once that temporary channel is gone while its model is still `V1Opening` (abandoned before funding_created, nothing persisted with it), so a peer that opens and walks away does not grow the wallet (needs `IChannelMemoryRepository`, an optional constructor argument; memory only). A channel past the open keeps its script; our own opens always reserve a fresh address. `ShutdownScriptProvider` (scoped): our upfront script if we sent one (B2-SHUT-S09), else a fresh P2WPKH wallet address (`IBitcoinWalletService`), which it hands to `IBlockchainMonitor.WatchBitcoinAddress` again; the monitor keeps watching every wallet address after a deposit, so the closing output is found as a deposit. The wallet returns its first address without a UTXO to every caller (NL-280), so an address that is already the shutdown script of another loaded channel in ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing, or that another close of this process claimed first (`ClosingNegotiationRegistry.TryReserveShutdownScript`, atomic across channel locks, released by `Remove` when the channel closes), is swapped for the first unused change address (W4-E, reviews F3/F4); a third concurrent close can still collide (logged), and that change address is also the next funding transaction's change output, until the wallet reserves addresses (NL-280).
- Wiring: `ChannelManager` dispatches `shutdown`/`closing_signed` (gated like normal-operation messages until the channel is reestablished) and calls `AdvanceAsync` after every handled message of a ShuttingDown/Negotiating channel (a failure there is logged, never sent to the peer). ShuttingDown carries updates like Open (`ChannelStateTransitionService.CarriesUpdates`: receive handlers, `SignIfPendingAsync`, `ChannelOperationsService`), except: our `update_add_htlc` is refused (B2-ADD-S13), our `update_fee` too once no HTLC is left (B2-SHUT-S07), and the peer's add after its own shutdown gets warning + close (B2-SHUT-R06). The reestablish handler answers ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing channels, retransmits channel_ready for them when both next_commitment_numbers are 1 (B2-RE-15; a closing channel was Open) and appends our shutdown; a mismatch on a Closing channel is only logged (failing it would broadcast a commitment against the agreed close), and `ChannelManager` never turns a Closing channel into Failed. The channel_ready handler ignores a retransmitted channel_ready in those states. `OnPeerConnectedAsync` sends channel_reestablish for them; startup registers them (Closing: a completed watch closes the channel at once, a missing watch (an older build saved it after Closing) is created again, else the stored tx is rebroadcast through `IBitcoinChainService`). Every new block retries the end of a Closing channel whose watch already completed (`CompleteConfirmedCloses`). `ChannelManager.HandleFundingSpentAsync` (on `IBlockchainMonitor.OnWatchedOutpointSpent`): a spend of a ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing channel's funding output by a mutual close we did not record (one input = the funding outpoint, final sequence, lock time 0, every output to one of the two shutdown scripts: the peer broadcast a proposal we signed before its closing_signed reached us, or the other dust variant) becomes the closing tx: Closing + its watch (first seen at that block) in one save, then Closed at depth as usual; any other funding spend goes to the on-chain watcher (BOLT 5 wiring, ABCD W5) and a spend of a resolution output to the executor. The HTLC switch and payments only use `Open` channels outgoing. HTLCs the peer adds after our shutdown (NL-279, B2-SHUT-S08, "SHOULD fail to route"; the peer may not have seen it yet, so the add itself is legal): `SendShutdownAsync` records the peer's next HTLC id (`ChannelCommitments.RemoteNextHtlcId`, or the channel's without a snapshot) as `ChannelModel.FirstRemoteHtlcIdAfterLocalShutdown` in the same save as our script (column `Channels.FirstRemoteHtlcIdAfterLocalShutdown`, migration `AddShutdownHtlcBoundaryAndAddressReservation`); `HtlcSwitch.HandleLockedInAsync` fails every locked-in incoming HTLC with an id at or above it (`ChannelModel.IsRemoteHtlcAddedAfterLocalShutdown`) back with `temporary_node_failure` once its onion peeled as a forward or a final hop (secret recorded first; a malformed or failed onion keeps its own failure; a circuit is handled first as before), so it is neither forwarded nor accepted as final hop (a shutdown means no new HTLC is accepted, BOLT 2 rationale). Never on a channel closing on chain (`Failed`/`OnchainResolving`): no failure can be sent there, so a forward is left to time out and a final-hop HTLC of ours is accepted and claimed on chain (NL-316) like any other. HTLCs added before our shutdown but locked in after it are routed as usual. A shutdown persisted by an older build has no boundary (null) and keeps the old behavior. When a reconnection drops the peer's uncommitted adds (`ChannelManager.RevertUncommittedAsync`, at startup, connect and disconnect), the boundary is lowered to the new `RemoteNextHtlcId` (`ChannelModel.LowerFirstRemoteHtlcIdAfterLocalShutdown`) and saved with the revert (`ChannelDbRepository.UpdateAsync` in the same save): the peer reuses those ids after our retransmitted shutdown. Proof: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/Switch/ShutdownFailBackTests` on `ThreeNodeHarness` with the close services (forward and final hop failed back, the close goes on to Negotiating, an HTLC locked in before the shutdown still forwarded, the boundary back from SQLite after Bob's restart, a post-shutdown final HTLC on a channel gone on chain claimed, not failed back) and `Channels/Harness/ShutdownBoundaryRevertTests` (the boundary lowered and saved at a link drop).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Close/` (`CloseHarness` = `TwoNodeHarness` with the close services and a recording monitor; `CooperativeCloseHarnessTests`: funder/non-funder close, no fee_range, HTLC in flight, shutdown after the covering commitment_signed, reconnect in ShuttingDown and in Negotiating, script forms, bad signature, the other variant, refusals; idle channel reconnected in ShuttingDown (channel_ready + shutdown retransmitted), Closing with the final echo lost; `ChannelCloseCoordinatorTests`: upfront script, pre-open, dust output, watch staged in the Closing save before the broadcast, the agreed closing_signed re-sent in Closing; `ClosingLifecycleTests`: Closed on confirmation, startup rebroadcast, a missing or completed watch at startup, the per-block retry, funding spends by an unrecorded mutual close or by another tx, and reestablish). Proof against LND (4 tests, green): `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/CooperativeCloseFlowTests` (a payment each way, then we close or LND closes, with and without fee_range). LND (0.20, 0.21) never sends fee_range and lowers its fee by 10 % per closing_signed from its own estimate, so with our default estimate it takes about 19 rounds to reach our funder limit (`Node:Close:MaxFeeMultiplier` x our estimate), which we hold (B2-CLS-R09). Holding is a deviation from BOLT 2's MUST (propose strictly between): `LegacyClosingNegotiator` holds only against a peer that already sent a fee before and moved strictly towards ours (R07), else warning + close; a strict peer may fail the channel over a repeated fee (LND accepts it). The fee_range receive path (B2-CLS-R03..R06) and the reestablish in ShuttingDown/Negotiating/Closing are proven in-process only (`CloseHarness`: both ends are our code); the Docker proof runs the legacy negotiation in all four cases. Since lane b10-close-interop (NL-286) the reestablish in those states is also proven in Docker: `Docker/Interop/Cln/ClnCloseRestartTests` (CLN's `shutdown` or `closing_signed` lost with the link, then a reconnection or a restart; a restart in Closing before and while the closing tx confirms) and `Docker/CloseRestartFlowTests` (LND: a restart while ShuttingDown with a held HTLC, new HTLCs refused both ways; a restart in Closing); the fee_range receive path against CLN in `ClnCloseTests`.

## option_simple_close (BOLT2 plan N11: N11-T1, N11-T2; `Channels/Close/Simple/`, `Channels/Close/Handlers/`)
- Negotiated per connection: `ClosingNegotiationRegistry.Entry.SimpleClose` is set from the negotiated features of the peer's `shutdown` (`ChannelCloseCoordinator.ReceiveShutdownAsync`), `closing_complete` or `closing_sig`, and reset by a new connection. With it, `AdvanceAsync` never opens a legacy negotiation: once Negotiating (both shutdowns, `IsCleared`) and both shutdowns went over the current connection, `SimpleCloseCoordinator.ProposeIfDueAsync` sends our own `closing_complete` once per connection (both sides do, funder or not); a peer's `closing_signed` gets a `warning` (message ignored). Without it the legacy close is unchanged (the fallback for a peer without bits 60/61; `FeatureOptions.OptionSimpleClose` is Optional by default since taproot plan D-T1, 2026-10-03, so two default nodes, Eclair 0.14.3 and LND with `--protocol.rbf-coop-close` close with simple close; `CloseHarness` pins it off unless `simpleClose: true`).
- `Simple/SimpleClosingTerms` (pure: closer/closee balances in msat, both scripts, `fee_satoshis`; `CloserAmountSat` = balance rounded down minus the fee, 0 for OP_RETURN; `CloseeAmountSat`; per-script dust via `ShutdownScriptValidator.GetDustThresholdSat`, OP_RETURN never dust; `Build(ClosingSigKind)`) and `Simple/SimpleCloseRules` (pure: `SelectCloserKinds` = B2-SC-C01/C02/C06/C07 (lesser side: `closee_output_only` when its own output is dust, else `closer_and_closee_outputs`; otherwise `closer_output_only` alone when the closee is dust, else both 1 and 3; refusals carry the requirement id), `SelectCloseeKind` = B2-SC-E06, `ChooseFee` = feerate (floor 253) x `ClosingFeeCalculator.EstimateWeight` of both outputs, capped at our balance and, when we are not the lesser side, so our own output stays at its dust threshold; null (no proposal) below the floor fee). We never use an OP_RETURN script or the "uneconomical" MAY rows.
- `Simple/SimpleCloseCoordinator` (scoped, under the channel lock): `ProposeIfDueAsync` (lock time = `IBlockchainMonitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight`; every selected variant built with `IClosingTransactionBuilder.BuildSimple` and signed; the outstanding `SimpleCloseProposal` kept in the registry entry, memory only), `BumpAsync` (RBF: a new `closing_complete` at a feerate; refused while ours is unanswered, B2-SC-C09), `ReceiveClosingCompleteAsync` (E01 fee above the closer's balance, E02 closee script not ours, E03 closer script not a valid shutdown form or not the peer's upfront script: `warning` + close; the signature BOLT 2 selects missing (E07) or invalid (E08, the signer's low-S check included): `warning` + close; then sign, B2-SC-E10 `ChannelModel.ReplaceRemoteShutdownScript(closer_scriptpubkey)`, persist, broadcast, `closing_sig` in the same TLV field), `ReceiveClosingSigAsync` (G01 fields differ from ours, G02 not exactly one signature, G03 a field we did not send, G04 invalid: `warning` + close; otherwise the fully signed variant is persisted and broadcast). Every fully signed transaction (ours completed by a `closing_sig`, or the peer's we signed) becomes `ChannelModel.ClosingTransaction` (the last one wins) with `ChannelState.Closing` and its `WatchedTransactionModel` (when none exists for the txid) in one save, before the reply or the broadcast; a failed broadcast (e.g. a conflicting transaction with a higher fee) is logged. NL-983: once the funding output's watch records a spend in a processed block (`IWatchedOutpointDbRepository.GetAsync`, `IsSpent`), a later fully signed transaction is neither stored nor broadcast (the `closing_sig` reply to a `closing_complete` still goes out); a mempool spend does not stop it (RBF). `ChannelManager` also adopts the funding watch's spender when it is a mutual close other than the stored one (`TryAdoptFundingSpendAsync`, the block read through `IBitcoinChainService`): at its watch's depth and at startup in Closing, so a channel stuck by the old replacement closes. A Closing channel still signs the peer's new `closing_complete` and bumps ours. Every protocol failure is `warning` + close (never fail the channel: BOLT 2 allows either, and failing would broadcast our commitment while a mutual close is possible). No timeout for a missing `closing_sig` yet (BOLT 2 SHOULD close the connection): the only disconnect API, `IPeerManager.DisconnectPeer`, also suppresses our reconnect loop, so it waits for a `PeerManager` disconnect that keeps reconnecting; until then an unanswered proposal blocks RBF (B2-SC-C09) until the next connection.
- Whichever transaction confirms closes the channel: `ChannelManager.IsMutualCloseOf` accepts the simple-close shape (sequence 0xFFFFFFFD, any lock time; one or two outputs, at most one of them not to our script) as well as the legacy one (0xFFFFFFFF, lock time 0, every output to one of the two current scripts), so a spend by a transaction other than the stored one becomes the closing transaction (the existing funding-spend path) and reaches Closed at depth. The peer's output is not checked against its current script because the peer may have replaced it since (E10, or its `shutdown` after a reconnection): an earlier transaction we signed (its proposal we answered, ours it completed, or our unanswered one it holds our signatures for) pays the old one. That is safe because the funding output needs our signature and we sign that sequence only on simple closing transactions. Watches of the replaced transactions stay pending forever (no delete API).
- `ChannelCloseCoordinator.ReceiveClosingCompleteAsync`/`ReceiveClosingSigAsync` (called by `Close/Handlers/ClosingCompleteMessageHandler`/`ClosingSigMessageHandler`; the first moves a cleared ShuttingDown channel to Negotiating) and `BumpSimpleCloseAsync`; `ChannelCloseService`: a request with a feerate on a Negotiating/Closing channel with simple close negotiated on this connection sends a new `closing_complete` (RBF) instead of only reporting; one it can't send (a Closing channel without the flag on this connection, or a Negotiating one whose flag was set on an earlier connection, `Entry.SimpleCloseSeen`) throws `InvalidOperationException` instead of being dropped. A changed peer script in `shutdown` is accepted with simple close (after the peer changed it in `closing_complete`), else still warning + close.
- Simple taproot close and force close (NL-877 T4/T5, taproot wave t02 lane CLOSE): a taproot channel closes cooperatively with `option_simple_close` only. Every `shutdown` of ours carries a fresh closee nonce (`Close/Simple/TaprootCloseNonces.CreateShutdown`: `ILightningSigner.ForgetClosingNonces` then `CreateClosingNonce`; used by `SendShutdownAsync`, `CreateShutdownResend` and the reestablish handler's re-send), kept in `ClosingNegotiationRegistry.Entry.LocalCloseeNonce`; the peer's `shutdown_nonce` (and each later `next_closee_nonce`) is `Entry.RemoteCloseeNonce`, replaced by a re-sent shutdown. Memory only: a new connection resets both and `ChannelManager.OnPeerConnectedAsync` forgets the signer's secrets. A taproot `shutdown` without (or with an invalid) nonce fails the channel (`ChannelFailedException`, `MustBroadcast`, as Eclair's MissingClosingNonce); one on a connection without simple close gets warning + close; a `closing_signed` on a taproot channel gets a warning (never an error, which would make the peer broadcast), and `AdvanceAsync` never opens a legacy negotiation; `closechannel` is refused when the peer's connection did not negotiate simple close (`ChannelCloseService.ThrowIfTaprootWithoutSimpleClose`, NL-967). `SimpleCloseCoordinator`: as closer each variant is signed with `SignClosingAsCloser` against the peer's closee nonce (then cleared) and sent in TLVs 5/6/7 (98 bytes), the nonce kept in `SimpleCloseProposal.RemoteCloseeNonce`; the peer's `closing_sig` (one 32-byte partial signature) is aggregated with `AggregateClosingSignature` (its failure is B2-SC-G04) and its `next_closee_nonce` is the peer's next closee nonce (none: no bump of ours until it re-sends shutdown). As closee the variant BOLT 2 selects (`SimpleCloseRules.SelectCloseeKind(terms, bool)`) is checked and signed by `SignClosingAsClosee` with our closee nonce (a bad signature is B2-SC-E08 and keeps the nonce), aggregated, persisted, broadcast, and our `closing_sig` carries a fresh `next_closee_nonce`. Proofs: `Channels/Close/SimpleTaprootCloseHarnessTests` (two real signers, both roles, RBF in both directions, reconnection, every transaction executed against the P2TR funding output). Force close: `LocalCommitmentBroadcastBuilder` signs our latest commitment with `SignLocalCommitmentForBroadcast(channelId, null, number, tx, partial)` from the stored peer partial signature (`ChannelFailureServiceTests.Given_TaprootChannelWithHtlcs_*`). On chain (T4 safety floor): `CommitmentOutputMapper` maps taproot commitments with each output's tapscript leaf and control block (`CommitmentOutputDescriptor.TaprootControlBlock`/`IsSimpleTaproot`, persisted in `OutputDescriptorData` flag 0x10), `FindSimpleTaprootPaymentToRemote` serves the data-loss path; our `to_local`, our `to_remote` and the revoked `to_local` are swept by script path (`SweepInput.TaprootControlBlock`/`SpentScriptPubKey`, `SweepSigningContext.TaprootSpentOutputs`, BIP 340 over every spent output; `SweepScheduler` re-signs them). Our own commitment is resolved in full (taproot wave t03 lane T4L, NL-966 (1, 5, 6), NL-904 item 4): `LocalCommitResolver` builds our zero-fee HTLC-timeout/success through the anchors path (`EstimateAnchorBaseWeight` → `IAnchorFeeInputProvider.SelectAsync` → `AddFeeInputs`, whose taproot result carries the leaf, control block and `HtlcTransactionBuildResult.FeeInputSpentOutputs` → `SignLocalHtlcTransaction`, BIP 340 `SIGHASH_DEFAULT` over every spent output (`TaprootSignatures.ComputeHtlcSigHash(..., allowFeeInputs)`, only for our own signature) → `AddWitness` → the wallet signs its inputs with the P2TR HTLC output as `htlcInput`), RBF-maintained as anchors; the second-level row is the P2TR `TaprootHtlcResolutionOutput` (delay leaf + control block in `OutputDescriptorData`), swept by script path (`SweepInputFactory.SecondLevelOutput(..., taprootControlBlock, spentScriptPubKey)`) and re-signed by `SweepScheduler`; upstream outcomes as anchors (`OnchainTimeout`). `AnchorCpfpService` (`AnchorCpfpService.Taproot.cs`) finds our P2TR anchor (our commitment: our `local_delayedpubkey` at its number through the optional `ICommitmentKeyDerivationService`; the peer's: our payment basepoint), signs it by key path (`ILightningSigner.SignTaprootAnchorInput`) and sweeps any taproot anchor by its `OP_16 OP_CSV` leaf (`AnchorOutpoint.IsTaproot`). Proofs: `Onchain/Resolvers/Local/LocalTaprootHtlcResolutionTests` (both directions, P2WPKH and P2TR fee inputs, a restart mid-resolution, RBF), `Onchain/Anchors/AnchorCpfpServiceTests`/`AnchorPeerCpfpTests` taproot cases, `SweepSchedulerTests.Given_OurTaprootSecondLevelSweepUnconfirmed_*`. The peer's commitments (wave t03 lane T4R, NL-966): the mapper records with each HTLC output of a peer commitment the leaf we spend it by (our offered HTLC: the accepted output's timeout leaf; the peer's: the offered output's success leaf; a revoked one: the timeout leaf, whose control block gives the merkle root), so `RemoteCommitResolver` claims our offered HTLCs after `cltv_expiry` (`<sig> <leaf> <cb>`, nLockTime cltv, nSequence 1) and the peer's with an allowed preimage (`<sig> <preimage> <leaf> <cb>`, final-hop and relay claims included), and `RevokedCommitResolver` penalizes revoked HTLC outputs and the peer's second-level outputs (`TaprootHtlcResolutionOutput`) by the revocation **key path** (`SweepInput.TaprootMerkleRoot`/`IsTaprootKeyPath`, `SweepSigningContext.TaprootMerkleRoot`, the signer tweaks the revocation key with the root and checks the output key; `SweepScheduler` re-signs a 1-item witness by key path, the root recomputed from the row's leaf and control block by `Domain/Onchain/Taproot/TapscriptMerkleRoot`), also before the commitment confirms (mempool penalties). `HtlcWitnessParser` reads the taproot witness shapes, so a preimage the peer reveals on either commitment (direct claim or HTLC-success, confirmed or in the mempool) fulfills upstream as on anchors. A taproot HTLC row written before t03 (no control block) takes the rebuilt commitment's leaf first. Splicing a taproot channel works since taproot wave t03 (NL-965, "Splices of simple taproot channels" below). Static channel backups holding a taproot channel are plaintext version 2 (flag bit 4); peer storage entries carry flag bit 2.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Close/Simple/SimpleCloseRulesTests` (requirement table), `Close/SimpleCloseHarnessTests` (`CloseHarness(simpleClose: true)` sets `HarnessNode.NegotiatedFeatures`: both proposals signed and broadcast, lesser-side dust, the fee cap, RBF, C09, reconnection in Closing then RBF, E01-E03/E07/E08/E09/E10 and G01-G04/G06 with crafted messages signed by the peer's real key, not negotiated, closing_signed refused). Docker proof against LND 0.21 with `--protocol.rbf-coop-close` on alice: `Docker/CooperativeCloseFlowTests` (`Given_SimpleCloseNegotiated_*`, `Given_SimpleClose_When_BothSidesBumpTheFee_*`).

## Channel safety (BOLT2 plan N9-T2, N9-T4; `Channels/Safety/`)
Registered by `ChannelSafetyServiceCollectionExtensions.AddChannelSafetyServices()` (TryAdd singletons, idempotent). `AddApplicationServices` calls it (since 983b2b4), and `NltgDaemonService` (and the Docker `NLightningTestNode`) call `IChannelFailureService.Start()` and `IHtlcExpiryMonitor.Start()` after `PeerManager.StartAsync` and the payment reconcile, and `IHtlcExpiryMonitor.StopAsync()` / `IChannelFailureService.Stop()` before the chain monitor stops; the daemon binds `ChannelSafetyOptions` from `Node:Safety` (`GraceBlocks` 2, `FulfillSafetyBlocks` 18, `FailBackBlocks` null = `Routing.CltvExpiryDelta`, `CommitmentConfirmationDepth` 1). The Docker proof (`Docker/ChannelSafetyFlowTests`) resolves them from the node's DI graph.
- Rules: the pure Domain `Channels/Policies/HtlcDeadlinePolicy` (see the Domain guide). "Past a deadline" is `height >= deadline`.
- `HtlcExpiryMonitor : IHtlcExpiryMonitor`: on `IBlockchainMonitor.OnNewBlockDetected` (rounds on the thread pool, never overlapping, coalesced to the latest height; `CheckAsync(height)` runs one round; `WhenIdleAsync` for tests) it evaluates every HTLC of every loaded `Open`/`Failed` channel with a snapshot, outside every lock. A `FailChannel` decision calls `IChannelFailureService.FailChannelAsync` (broadcast) once per channel per process (again every block while it returns `PublishFailed`; the service also retries on its own); `FailBackUpstream` (only on `Open`) calls `IChannelOperations.FailHtlcAsync` with `temporary_node_failure` encrypted with the stored onion secret (`IChannelStateDbRepository.GetOnionSharedSecretAsync`, else a re-peel through `IncomingOnionProcessor` with `replayOwner: null`, whose malformed result becomes `FailMalformedHtlcAsync`); a refusal is retried next block. The incoming resolution: circuit `Fulfilled` or an outgoing HTLC (by circuit or `FindHtlcsByOriginAsync(Forwarded(in))`) with `KnownPreimage` → `PreimageKnown`; circuit `Failed` → `Unresolved`; a forward (circuit not `Pending`) whose every outgoing HTLC is on a channel no longer loaded and `Closed` in the database with no `KnownPreimage` on its stored record (`Onchain/ClosedChannelHtlcs.FindAsync`) → `Unresolved` (NL-320: failed back at the fail-back deadline even when the upstream link stays up and the switch never replays the lock-in; a stored preimage → `PreimageKnown`); any other circuit or outgoing HTLC → `AwaitingDownstream` (never failed upstream); no forward → `PreimageKnown` only when the switch committed the HTLC to its set (`Onchain/Resolvers/FinalHopClaims.GetAcceptedPreimageAsync`: the record's `KnownPreimage` hashes to it, no fail removal, the invoice `Settled` with that preimage; the NL-323 commit point, the same test as the on-chain claim, NL-337), else `UnresolvedFinalHop` (failed back at the fulfillment deadline, not at the forwarding distance): a duplicate HTLC for a Settled invoice whose 0x400F could not be sent, or a part marked by a set that never settled, is failed back, never the channel failed.
- `ChannelFailureService : IChannelFailureService` (the **only** broadcast path, D10): under the channel lock, a `ChannelFailureRequest.StillApplies` precondition (optional; false → `NotApplicable`, nothing done, and an earlier failure's pending publish retry is kept; a later request without broadcast keeps it too, only data loss or no broadcastable commitment drops it, W4-E review F2), then (unless data loss or `Broadcast = false`) build and sign the latest local commitment with `LocalCommitmentBroadcastBuilder` (snapshot `LocalCommit` + its `RemoteSignatures`, or commitment 0 + `LastReceivedSignature` without a snapshot; built exactly as `VerifyLocalCommitment` built it, signed by `ILightningSigner.SignLocalCommitmentForBroadcast`, which refuses a revoked number and anything after data loss), then persist `Failed` + the serialized `error` (kept when already stored) **and** the commitment's `BroadcastTransactionModel` (purpose `LocalCommitment`, with its `CommitmentNumber`; when no row for that txid exists) in one save (NL-271, ABCD W5: no crash can lose the intent to broadcast; the chain monitor sends pending rows again after every block and at startup, and the channel manager restores the signer's S1 mark from the row at registration, NL-297); after the lock, `IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync(row)` (the monitor), whose false (refused) is `PublishFailed`; a row that is already `Confirmed` is `Rebroadcast` without a send, an `Abandoned` one (the peer's commitment won) is `Superseded`. The channel manager's `MustBroadcast` path uses `PrepareFailureUnderLockAsync` (everything up to the save, under the manager's lock) + `CompleteFailureAsync` (publish, after the lock), so a handler's failure is never persisted Failed without its row. Txids are logged in display order (NL-275). A send counts as done only when it succeeds or the node already has the tx (RPC -27, `txn-already-in-mempool`/`txn-already-known`/`txn-same-nonwitness-data-in-mempool`, or `getrawtransaction` finds it); any other refusal (node down, fee too low, a conflict with the peer's commitment) is `PublishFailed`, not remembered as published, and retried on every new block (`RetryPendingPublishesAsync`, without re-sending the error) until it goes through; then the `error` through `IChannelErrorSender` (`PeerChannelErrorSender`: `IPeerService.SendErrorAsync` on the current connection, bypassing the `PeerOutbox`; seam for an outbox-ordered API). Data loss: `ILightningSigner.MarkDataLoss` and `RefusedDataLoss`, nothing broadcast (I12). Statuses: `Broadcast`, `Rebroadcast`, `FailedWithoutBroadcast`, `RefusedDataLoss`, `NoBroadcastableCommitment`, `PublishFailed`, `NotApplicable` (Closing/Closed/Stale/OnchainResolving/pre-funding). `Start()` subscribes to `OnNewBlockDetected` (the retries) and resumes in the background (`ResumeInterruptedBroadcastsAsync`, `WhenResumedAsync` for tests) the broadcast of every loaded `Failed` channel without data loss that an older build failed (an unconfirmed non-funding watch and no `LocalCommitment` row): the row is written then. The service no longer closes a channel when its commitment confirms: the funding spend moves it to `OnchainResolving` through the on-chain watcher (see "BOLT 5 wiring"). A `Closing`, `Closed`, `Stale` or `OnchainResolving` channel is `NotApplicable`: a Closing channel is never turned Failed (its agreed mutual close would leave it Failed for good once confirmed; also for `forceclosechannel`, W5 review). `ChannelSafetyOptions.CommitmentConfirmationDepth` is unused (kept for configuration files).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Safety/` (`HtlcDeadlinePolicyTests` deadline table at N-1/N/N+G, `ChannelFailureServiceTests` over `RealSigningCommitmentPair` (real txs: valid 2-of-2 witness, latest txid only, anchors and not, revoked snapshot refused, data loss, commitment 0 without a snapshot, the row with its commitment number in the Failed save, rebroadcast, confirmed/abandoned rows, a refused send is `PublishFailed` and retried per block, the older build's watch-only resume, prepare under the lock + complete after, no Closed on confirmation), `HtlcExpiryMonitorTests`).

## BOLT 5 wiring (ABCD W5, plan O2-T5, O6-T2; `Onchain/`)
Registered by `OnchainServiceCollectionExtensions.AddOnchainServices()` (TryAdd singletons, from `AddApplicationServices` after `AddChannelSafetyServices`); `OnchainOptions` from `Node:Onchain` (`ReasonableDepth` 6 for the resolvers, `IrrevocableDepth` 100). Nothing to start: `ChannelManager` routes every `IOutpointWatcher.OnWatchedOutpointSpent` (a funding-outpoint spend that is not an unrecorded mutual close of a closing channel → `IOnchainChannelWatcher.HandleFundingSpentAsync`; a spend of any other watched outpoint of the channel → `IOnchainResolutionExecutor.HandleOutputSpentAsync`) and every new block (`ScheduleRound(height)`, background, coalesced to the latest height; `WhenIdleAsync` for tests), always after releasing the channel lock.
- `OnchainChannelWatcher` (NL-272): under the channel's lock, idempotent (a spend already recorded at the same height and block is `Replayed`, nothing written; the same tx at another height or block (a reorg) is `Replayed` with only the close's `SpentAtHeight`/`BlockHash` updated, so the irrevocable depth counts from the new block; a different recorded spend is overwritten, NL-292). Builds the `FundingSpendContext` (our local candidate from the `LocalCommitment` broadcast row, else the rebuilt latest local commitment; the peer's current commitment from the snapshot, or without one only while `RemoteKeySet` still holds its point; the unacked next one; the closing txid; both shutdown scripts), classifies with `FundingSpendClassifier` (`NotFundingSpend` and `Mutual` are left alone) and maps with `ICommitmentOutputMapper` (local, remote, remote-next; revoked: point = `secret*G` from the shachain (`ISecretStorageServiceFactory` + `RemoteShachainDbRepository`), spec from the revocation log, else a stand-in spec without HTLCs mapped by script; future and anything unmappable: `FindPaymentToRemote`). One save: `ChannelCloseModel`, one `OutputResolutionModel` (Pending, `OutputDescriptorData` blob, HTLC direction/id) and one `ResolutionOutput` watch per output that `IsOurs`, our pending `LocalCommitment` rows abandoned when another tx spent the funding output, a stored `error` ("channel closed on chain") when the channel had none, and `OnchainResolving`. After the lock: `TrackWatchedOutpoint`, the error through `IChannelErrorSender` (B5-GEN-04), critical alerts (B5-GEN-06 unknown, B5-RMT-03 future, B5-GEN-06 for vouts the mapper could not identify (`CommitmentOutputMap.UnmappedVouts`, with amounts; "predates the revocation log" for a revoked number below `GetLogStartAsync`)), `IOnchainResolutionExecutor.CatchUpSpendsAsync(new watches, spend height)` (the monitor keeps processing blocks while the spend is classified: an output spent in the commitment's block or a block processed before the watch was tracked is found by scanning those blocks), then `ResolveChannelAsync(spend height)`. HTLCs without an output (`HtlcsWithoutOutput`) are not persisted: the resolvers re-derive them from the snapshot every round. Unknown spends also go to `OnchainResolving` with no output here (the plan said Failed); since NL-320 the remote resolver gives every output of the spend a watched row as for data loss (below) and fails our offered HTLCs upstream once expired, and the channel closes when those rows are irrevocable or ignored. Known limit: the model is mutated before the save; a failed save leaves the in-memory channel at 37 with its error marked (the replayed block records it; the error then goes out only on reconnection, like NL-282).
- `OnchainResolutionExecutor`: per channel in `OnchainResolving`, under its lock, one unit of work: a spent output → `Resolved` at the spend height (unless already Irrevocable/Ignored or resolved at that height) and `IOutputResolver.OnOutputSpentAsync`; every `Resolved` output 100 deep → `Irrevocable`; `ResolveAsync(close, outputs, height)` of the first resolver (`GetServices<IOutputResolver>()` from the round's scope) whose `CanResolve(kind)`; when the funding spend and every output are irrevocable (or `Ignored`) → `Closed` + the revocation log deleted, in the same save (Closed is staged on the database's copy from `ChannelDbRepository.GetByIdAsync`; the shared model turns Closed and leaves memory only after the save, so a failed save is retried next round), and the channel is removed from memory. After the save: new watches tracked, then caught up (`CatchUpSpendsAsync`: blocks from the watch's parent height (commitment height, the handled spend's height, our broadcast's `ConfirmedHeight`, for a pending broadcast the monitor's `LastProcessedBlockHeight` read before the round) to bitcoind's tip through `IBitcoinChainService` when registered; a spend found runs like a monitor event and is recorded on the watch with `MarkSpentAsync` in that round's save; NL-311: in the first round of the process, after every channel's resolution (the time-critical part comes first), one block scan (`CatchUpSavedWatchesAsync`) does the same for the saved watch of every `Pending`/`Waiting`/`Broadcast` row of every channel not caught up yet, from its parent height or from the spend the monitor recorded on it, so a crash between a save and `TrackWatchedOutpoint`, or between the monitor's block save and the spend's handling, misses nothing; a scan stopped by a chain read error resumes in the next round at the first block it did not scan (memory only; a restart scans from the parent height again)), new or still pending broadcast rows published (`IChainBroadcaster`), `IChannelDomainEvent`s to the scope's `IHtlcSwitch`, alerts logged. A close kind without a resolver is logged once and never closes (its outputs stay Pending). The resolvers (`Onchain/Resolvers/`, below) are registered right after `AddOnchainServices()` in `AddApplicationServices` (`AddLocalCommitResolutionServices`, `AddRemoteCommitResolutionServices`, `AddRevokedCommitResolver`); exactly one handles each commitment kind (Local; Remote/RemoteNext/Future/Unknown; Revoked), none handles Mutual (`Daemon.Tests` `OnchainClientHandlerTests` checks the composed graph). The daemon binds `LocalCommitResolverOptions`, `RemoteResolutionOptions` and `RevokedCommitResolverOptions` from `Node:Onchain` too.
- Fee bumping (O6-T1, NL-317): `Onchain/Fees/SweepScheduler : ISweepScheduler` (singleton from `AddOnchainServices`, with the shared `SweepFeePolicy` built from `OnchainOptions.FeePolicy` = `Node:Onchain:FeePolicy`, which the resolvers use too) runs in every block round of the executor after the resolver (never on a spend event): each `Pending` broadcast of purpose `Sweep`/`HtlcClaim`/`Penalty` whose `SweepFeePolicy.ShouldBump(FirstBroadcastHeight, tip, deadline)` holds (deadline = the earliest `DeadlineHeight` of the rows it spends; without one, `SweepConfTarget` blocks) is re-signed with the same inputs, sequences, lock time and destination at `SweepFeePolicy.DecideReplacement` (BIP 125 minimum or the estimate for the deadline's target, `FeeEstimates.GetForTargetAsync`, NL-296). Every input must spend one of the channel's rows: the key comes from the row's descriptor (`DelayedToLocal` → delayed key at its point, `PaymentToRemote` → payment basepoint, the peer's HTLC outputs → HTLC key at its point, `Revoked*` → revocation key with the peer's secret of `close.CommitmentNumber` from the shachain), amount and witness script from its `OutputDescriptorData`; only witness item 0 (the signature) changes. The replacement row (`ReplacesTransactionId`, `FirstBroadcastHeight` = tip), the rows moved to it and the old row `Replaced` go into the round's save; the executor publishes after it. It also retires what can no longer confirm (NL-294 partial): a pending one with an input spent on chain by another transaction is `Abandoned`, a pending one no row names that a later row replaces (a split penalty batch) is `Replaced`. A transaction it cannot re-sign or outbid within the cap is kept and logged once. Pre-signed non-anchor HTLC txs and our commitment are never bumped here: anchors HTLC txs are RBF-bumped by `LocalCommitResolver` and the anchors commitment through its CPFP child by `AnchorCpfpService` (NL-387: the class remark is stale).
- Reorgs (O6-T3, NL-292): in every block round the executor first unresolves rows whose watched spend the chain monitor rolled back (`Resolved` with an unspent watch → `Broadcast` when `ResolvingTransactionId` is set (that broadcast made pending again, `MarkPendingAsync`, and published after the round's save, since the resolvers never re-emit a broadcast for a row that names its resolving transaction), else `Pending`; a spend found at another height moves `ResolvedHeight`), then checks that `close.BlockHash` is still bitcoind's block at `SpentAtHeight` (skipped when the hash is empty or there is no `IBitcoinChainService`). If not, the channel is paused: no aging, resolver, bumping or Closed, state untouched (never back to Open), the funding outpoint re-tracked once; our own commitment (`LocalCommitment` close) is made pending again and published at once; for the peer's (or any other) commitment, after `OnchainOptions.ReorgGraceBlocks` (`Node:Onchain:ReorgGraceBlocks`, 6) rounds our latest commitment is broadcast once (its stored `LocalCommitment` row made pending, else signed by `LocalCommitmentBroadcastBuilder` and stored with its number; never after data loss). Pause and grace are process memory: after a restart the check pauses the channel again and the grace restarts. When the commitment confirms again the watcher records its new block (same tx) or the new close (another tx: `RetireReplacedCloseAsync` ignores every row of the old close and abandons the channel's other pending broadcasts in the same save). `Onchain/Reorg/FundingReconfirmationHandler` (created and subscribed to `IBlockchainMonitor.OnTransactionConfirmed` by the executor's constructor when its `IOutpointWatcher` is the monitor): a funding confirmation of a channel that already has a short channel id at another position (the monitor re-raises it after a reorg) moves the SCID and `FundingCreatedAtBlockHeight`, staged on the database copy, saved, then applied to the shared model; the same save resets the channel's announcement state (`ChannelModel.ResetAnnouncementSignatures`, applied to the shared model after the save; NL-350), then `IChannelAnnouncementService.OnShortChannelIdChanged` and, for a signer without an `IChannelSigningInfoSource`, a new `RegisterChannel` with the new SCID; the channel is private again (dont_forward) until both halves for the new SCID are exchanged at its depth (the block round sends ours); after the lock it sends a new `channel_update` (`IChannelUpdateService.SendChannelUpdateAsync`, when registered). The update and route-hint caches are keyed by channel id and read the SCID from the channel; invoices issued before the move keep the old SCID, and a funding tx that never confirms again leaves the old SCID (NL-292 partial). The upstream fails a replaced close already made at reasonable depth are not re-checked against the new close's HTLC outputs: `RetireReplacedCloseAsync` logs a critical `[B5-GEN-06]` naming every HTLC row of the old close that was Resolved (ledger follow-up).
- `OnchainResolving` channels: `OnPeerConnectedAsync` re-sends their stored error, every message gets it again (`DispatchChannelMessageAsync`), operations are refused (`CarriesUpdates`), the failure service and the HTLC expiry monitor skip them, and registration only logs them.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/` (`OnchainChannelWatcherTests` over real commitments: local, remote, remote-next, revoked, future, unknown, replay, failed save, already failed; `OnchainResolutionExecutorTests`: one save then publish/track/raise, repeated broadcast, spends, depth 99/100 and Closed, a failed save at depth 100 retried, no resolver, failed save, spends mined before the watch (same block, next block, a resolver's new watch) over `FakeBitcoinChain`, saved watches caught up in the first round after a restart (NL-311), scheduling; `OnchainRestartCatchUpTests`: the NL-311 restart proof on a real SQLite database (save without tracking, spend mined meanwhile, the new process resolves it; bitcoind unreachable in the first round); `ChannelManagerOnchainTests`: S1 restored at registration (NL-297) and the routing; `OnchainTestStore` is the in-memory database with per-save write lists and rollback of a failed save). Docker Proof O2: `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/Onchain/OnchainO2Tests` (we force-close / LND force-closes; `scripts/run-cluster.sh -n 1 --suite onchain`, the cluster only since NL-820). O6: `Onchain/Fees/SweepSchedulerTests` (over `LocalCommitResolutionHarness` with `HoldMempool`: replaced after the sweep target with a BIP 125 fee and script-valid, the estimate paid, the replacement abandoned when the original is mined, kept when no replacement fits the cap), `OnchainReorgTests` (fake chain: funding spend reorged out → state not lowered, outpoint re-watched, nothing aged; peer's commitment gone past the grace → our commitment broadcast; ours re-published at once; a rolled-back spend unresolved; an abandoned sweep revived and published when its rival spend is reorged out; SCID moved by `FundingReconfirmationHandler` and a channel_update sent), `OnchainChannelWatcherTests.Given_AnotherSpendAfterAReorg_...`; Docker Proof O6 in `OnchainO6Tests`.

## BOLT 5 across fundings (splicing plan §3.6, wave sp2 lane SP2-C, NL-479)
- `Onchain/OnchainFundings` (internal static): `GetAllAsync(uow, channel)` = every funding of the channel (the current one first, from its `ChannelFundings` row or `ChannelModel.FundingOutput`, then pending, replaced and discarded ones in creation order, plus pending ones only the engine holds; a unit of work without funding rows gives the current one), `IsCurrent`, `FindSpent(fundings, tx)` (the funding whose outpoint an input spends), `SpliceTxIdsFor` and `Map(mapper, modelFactory, channel, funding, spec, case, number, point, onChain)`: a commitment on another funding than the current one is rebuilt with `CommitmentSigningService.WithFunding` (its outpoint, capacity and funding keys, which key the anchors), exactly as it was signed.
- `OnchainChannelWatcher` handles a spend of any funding (the spent outpoint must be one of them): one `FundingSpendContext` per funding (`BuildContextsAsync`; commitment numbers are the channel's for every funding (SP-I3); our commitment from its `LocalCommitment` row spending that funding, else rebuilt; a pending splice's commitments from the engine (`SpecFor` its deltas) or, after a restart, its stored slots at the channel's numbers; a retired funding's peer commitment only while the engine still lists it in `RemoteCommit.SignedOnFundings`, else by number with a txid that matches nothing, mapped by script), `FundingSpendClassifier.ClassifyAny`. `Splice`: no close (logged); when the channel is `Failed` the pending splice funding is handed to `ISpliceCommitmentBroadcaster` after the lock. A `Discarded` splice that confirms next to a recorded close means that close was reorged out (the close's save discarded it): in one save the close is retired (`RetireReplacedCloseAsync`: its rows `Ignored`, its pending broadcasts abandoned), its `ChannelCloses` row deleted (so the executor stops reviving our commitment on the spent funding; it logs "resolving on chain without a recorded funding spend" until the next close) and the splice set back to `Pending`, then the broadcaster is asked; a close next to a still `Pending` splice spent another funding and stands. `ChannelManager` hands a splice spend of a `Failed` or `OnchainResolving` channel to the watcher (an `Open` one is still swallowed: the lock moves it). A commitment close of the current funding also discards every pending splice (funding row `Discarded`, its `Funding` broadcast abandoned) in the close's save. Revoked: the entry of the funding it spends (`GetRevokedAsync`: `GetAsync(channelId, fundingTxId, n)`; without one, any funding's entry for that number rebased on the spent funding's deltas (the HTLC set and scripts are the same on every funding; after a restart the engine lost `SignedOnFundings`, so the revocation was logged on the current funding only), else the stand-in spec at that funding's capacity). `ClassifyAsync` (mempool) classifies across fundings too.
- The channel manager hands a spend of a watched outpoint that is not the channel's current funding to the executor; `OnchainResolutionExecutor.HandleOutputSpentAsync` passes it to the watcher when the watch's purpose is `FundingOutput` (a pending splice's or a retired funding's). Every block round also runs `Reorg/SpliceReorgMonitor` (`SpliceReorgs`): a `Replaced` funding whose watch no longer records a spend (its splice's block was disconnected) is logged CRITICAL `[SP2-C-T4]` once until it is spent again when this process saw it happen (the watch was spent in an earlier round); a reorg that predates the process is derived from the persisted watch (the same unspent condition on the first round of a process: the lock needs the splice's confirmations, so its spend was recorded and has been cleared) and is logged once as information, never the CRITICAL again (NL-493); a `Replaced` funding without a watch row is logged once as a warning (`IsUnwatched`); the channel keeps operating. Wave spr lane SPR-E: each block round first runs, once per process, `Reorg/RecordedFundingSpendReplay` (NL-493: a funding watch that records a spend by a transaction that is not a funding of the channel, and not the recorded close's (or, for a Failed/OnchainResolving channel, by a pending or discarded splice), is handed to `IOnchainChannelWatcher.HandleFundingSpentAsync` again, the transaction read from its block through `IBitcoinChainService`; covers a crash between the chain monitor's block save and the watcher's save; a spend whose block cannot be read (error or pruned, logged) or whose hand-over threw is retried next round, the ones handed over are not handed again, and after `RecordedFundingSpendReplay.MaxRounds` (144) failing rounds it gives up with a CRITICAL log; a record whose transaction is no longer in its block is skipped; `FundingSpendReplay` for tests), then `Reorg/DiscardedSpliceReservations` (NL-492: for every loaded channel, an unresolved `InteractiveTxSessions` row whose constructed transaction is a `Discarded` funding with a wallet input of ours is settled once the transaction that spent the funding it spends (its shared input's watch; not the splice itself) is `IrrevocableDepth` deep: `IInteractiveTxContributor.ReleaseDiscardedAsync(tx, kept)` with `kept` = the inputs of the winning splice when it is one of ours, then, only once no `IFeeInputSelector` reservation holds our other inputs of it, `ResolvedAt` saved under the channel lock; while the contributor keeps a reservation the session stays unresolved and the release is tried again every block (the startup sweep never releases a reservation an unresolved negotiation holds); nothing is released earlier, so a reorg never has a release to roll back; a crash between the release and the save repeats the idempotent release). The "resolving on chain without a recorded funding spend" warning is logged once per channel and process (a close retired for its splice waits for our commitment on the splice funding).
- Resolvers: `LocalCommitResolver` (optional `ICommitmentTransactionModelFactory`) rebuilds a close that is not on the current funding from the stored local slot of another funding (`FindOnOtherFundingAsync`, same number) and signs its HTLC transactions with that slot's peer signatures (`LocalCommitContext.OtherFundingLocalCommit`); `RemoteCommitResolver` (same optional factory) tries the snapshot's commitment moved to each pending funding (`FindOnPendingFundingAsync`) before mapping the transaction on chain by script; `RevokedCommitDataSource` sets `RevokedCommitContext.Funding` (from the commitment's input) and reads that funding's log entry, and `RevokedCommitResolver` maps on it (optional factory).
- Force close with a pending splice (SP2-C-T2): `ChannelFailureService` implements `Onchain/Interfaces/ISpliceCommitmentBroadcaster` (registered by `AddOnchainServices` over the failure service): `BroadcastOnSpliceAsync(channelId, spliceTxId)` (Failed or OnchainResolving, no data loss, a pending funding) signs our latest local commitment on the splice funding (engine spec + `LocalCommit.SignaturesFor`, else the stored slot at the same number; `ILightningSigner.SignLocalCommitmentForBroadcast(channelId, fundingTxId, ...)`, SP-I4), stores its `LocalCommitment` row, abandons the pending one on the funding the splice spent, then publishes (and schedules the anchor CPFP round). `CheckConfirmedSplicesAsync` runs on every block: a failed channel (or an `OnchainResolving` one without a recorded close, i.e. the watcher retired it for the splice) whose pending splice's `WatchedTransactions` row has a block gets it once per process. `BroadcastOnSpliceAsync` refuses (`NotApplicable`) an `OnchainResolving` channel that has a recorded close. Our commitment on the splice funding needs the stored slot at the channel's current number (or the engine's pending funding): after a restart with a splice pending the engine has no pending fundings and the slot may lag (`NoBroadcastableCommitment`) until the SP2 restore of pending fundings lands. The failure service takes the model factory and commitment builder as optional constructor arguments.
- Tests: `Application.Tests/Onchain/OnchainSpliceWatcherTests` (real commitments: splice not a close, commitments on the pending funding local and remote, the discard, a revoked commitment of the replaced funding with its own log entry, a failed channel's splice), `OnchainSpliceForceCloseTests` (on `SpliceHarness(realEngine: true)`: our commitment on the splice funding script-verified against the splice output), `OnchainSpliceExecutorTests` (routing, the reorg monitor), `Resolvers/Revoked/RevokedSpliceFundingTests`; Docker `Integration.Tests/Docker/Onchain/OnchainSpliceTests` (Proof SP2 (d)/(e), two NLightning nodes).

## BOLT 5 local commitment resolution (plan O3-T3/T4; `Onchain/Resolvers/`)
- `LocalCommitResolver : IOutputResolver` (Domain port, W5-A; `CanResolve(LocalCommitment)`) resolves **our** commitment on chain. Stateless: each `ResolveAsync(close, rows, height)` / `OnOutputSpentAsync(close, row, spender, height)` opens a scope, reloads the channel with its snapshot (`ChannelDbRepository.GetByIdAsync`), rebuilds our commitment with `ICommitmentOutputMapper` (the rebuilt txid must equal `ChannelCloseModel.CommitmentTransactionId`, and the close's number our latest local number, else it logs and does nothing), reads each output's spend from `WatchedOutpointDbRepository` (spender txid/height; "by us" = the row's `ResolvingTransactionId` or a `BroadcastTransactions` row of the channel) and runs the pure `OutputResolutionPlanner`. It only returns actions (the executor stages them in one save, then publishes, watches, raises, alerts): `UpsertOutputAction` (rows for our outputs the watcher did not record, `Waiting` + `WaitUntilHeight`, `Broadcast` + `ResolvingTransactionId`, `Ignored` for an expired peer HTLC we may not claim or a dust sweep), `BroadcastAction` (HTLC-timeout/success purpose `HtlcTransaction`: the peer's stored HTLC signature from `LocalCommit.RemoteSignatures` by the HTLC's rank among the HTLC outputs in vout order + `ILightningSigner.SignLocalHtlcTransaction` + `IHtlcTransactionBuilder.AddWitness`; sweeps purpose `Sweep`: `SweepInputFactory` + `SweepTransactionBuilder.BuildWithFee` at `SweepFeePolicy.Decide(IFeeService estimate)` + `Sign`), `WatchOutpointAction`, `StageWriteAction`, `RaiseChannelEventAction`, `AlertAction`. A row that names its resolving transaction is never built again (the monitor rebroadcasts, O6 bumps).
- Rows: one per output of ours on the commitment (`DelayedToLocal`, `LocalOfferedHtlc`, `LocalReceivedHtlc`), plus, when our HTLC-timeout/success confirms, one for its vout 0 (`DelayedToLocal` with the HTLC's direction/id, `OutputDescriptorData` with the `HtlcResolutionOutput` witness script, watched). The second-level sweep is decided by the parent HTLC row's plan. Anchor channels (O7-T3, NL-314): the zero-fee HTLC-timeout/success is combined with wallet inputs before our signature (`IHtlcTransactionBuilder.EstimateAnchorBaseWeight` with a 34-byte change script → `IAnchorFeeInputProvider.SelectAsync(AnchorFeeInputOwner(channel, commitment txid, vout), baseWeight, feerate)` → `AddFeeInputs` (HTLC pair at index 0, fee inputs after it, change output 1) → `SignLocalHtlcTransaction` over the combined tx → `AddWitness` → `IAnchorFeeInputProvider.SignAsync` for the wallet inputs; the feerate is the estimate for the HTLC's deadline (`cltv_expiry` for a success), floored by `SweepFeePolicy`, the floor when one input's fee would exceed the HTLC's value). The port's contract (see its remarks): reservations are persistent (written before `SelectAsync` returns, surviving restarts), keyed by the owner, replaced by the owner's next selection (so a round whose save fails leaks nothing, and an RBF replacement may reuse them), confirmed outputs only, ended by `ReleaseAsync(owner)` (the resolver calls it when nothing was built and once per process when the HTLC output is spent). No selection, or any failure, broadcasts nothing and the next block tries again; a shortage (or no provider: the default `UnavailableAnchorFeeInputProvider` logs nothing) is a warning once per output and process, debug afterwards. While the transaction is pending (row `Broadcast`, output unspent) the resolver maintains it itself (`MaintainAnchorHtlcTransactionAsync`; the `SweepScheduler` never bumps `HtlcTransaction`): a wallet input no longer confirmed-unspent (`IBitcoinChainService.GetConfirmedUnspentOutputAsync`) while the HTLC input is → the row is `Abandoned` and rebuilt at once with new inputs; otherwise on `SweepFeePolicy.ShouldBump` it is replaced by RBF (fee at least `GetReplacementFee` over the old fee, bounded from its stored feerate; not above the HTLC's value; `ReplacesTransactionId`, old row `Replaced` in the same save). Since the HTLC output stays at vout 0, the second-level row and sweep are unchanged. Seam (NL-314 stays partial): `LocalLightningSigner` still refuses an HTLC transaction with more than one input, so in production every attempt fails and is retried; the tests sign combined transactions through the test decorator `CombinedHtlcSigningProxy` (`test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Resolvers/Local/AnchorTestWallet.cs`, with the stand-in wallet `AnchorTestWallet`; tests `LocalAnchorHtlcResolutionTests`, `LocalAnchorHtlcMaintenanceTests`, `Revoked/RevokedAnchorResolutionTests`).
- Preimage rules (B5-LCL-RO-02): an incoming HTLC is claimed only with our own fulfill in its record (`Removal` Fulfill), with its own `KnownPreimage` (set only by the switch when it accepted it as our final hop, checked against the hash, used only while the record has no fail removal and the invoice of the hash is `Settled` with that preimage: the settle is the commit point of a set; `Resolvers/FinalHopClaims.GetAcceptedPreimageAsync`, ABCD W7-B, NL-316/NL-322/NL-323) or with the `KnownPreimage` of the outgoing HTLC that forwards it (`FindHtlcsByOriginAsync(Forwarded(channel, id))`, live or archived, checked against the hash) — the switch's upstream fulfill is refused once the channel is closed. A preimage seen on chain (the peer's direct claim of our offered HTLC, `HtlcWitnessParser`) is staged into the HTLC record's `KnownPreimage` (`IChannelStateDbRepository.ApplyAsync` with a one-record transition over `ChannelCommitments.Restore`, also put into the loaded model) in the same round as `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` (I10), so the switch's own replays derive it too. When that stage is lost (no record, a failed save, a spend never reported) and a `LocalOfferedHtlc` output was spent by someone else, the per-block round fetches the spender through the optional `IBitcoinChainService` (from the block at the watch's recorded spend height first, so no `txindex` is needed, then `getrawtransaction`; NL-315) and reads its witness again (preimage → staged + fulfilled); only a witness that proves a path without the preimage (HTLC-timeout, revocation) may lead to the upstream fail. An unreadable spender (pruned block and no txindex) never fails upstream: it is logged every round and alerted (`B5-LCL-LO-03`) once per process and HTLC, in the first round at or past the reasonable depth (so also after the node was offline over that block). `StageKnownPreimageAsync` logs an error when the record is missing.
- Sweeps: an output is `Ignored` only when it cannot pay the floor fee plus a dust output (`ShutdownScriptValidator.GetDustThresholdSat` of the destination); when the capped fee (half the value) would leave less than dust, the fee is lowered to leave exactly dust. A builder refusal after that check is a bug: logged as an error, the row is left as it was and retried next block.
- Upstream events are raised again every round until the row is `Irrevocable` (the switch is idempotent; a refused upstream removal is retried each block). After that the switch covers it: the replayed upstream lock-in of a `Failed` circuit whose outgoing record derives no resolution (an `OnchainTimeout` is never persisted, or the outgoing channel is no longer loaded) fails the upstream HTLC with `permanent_channel_failure` (`HtlcSwitch.ResumeCircuitAsync`); so does, since NL-320, an `Offered` circuit whose outgoing channel is `Closed` in the database (not loaded) and whose stored record (live or archived) derives no resolution, once the tip is the circuit's outgoing `cltv_expiry + OnchainOptions.ReasonableDepth` (`Node:Onchain:ReasonableDepth`, the resolvers' value; a lost resolver event, or a channel closed before the resolvers failed such HTLCs); a record with a preimage fulfills instead. Fail = `OutgoingHtlcFailed` with `HtlcRemoval.OnchainTimeout()` (`HtlcRemovalKind.OnchainTimeout = 4`, never persisted) once our HTLC-timeout is `ReasonableDepth` deep (`LocalCommitResolverOptions`, default 6), or, for an outgoing HTLC without an output in our commitment (trimmed, not yet in it, removed), once the commitment is that deep, at once when neither the peer's current nor next commitment has an output for it; a failure the peer had sent keeps its reason. `HtlcSwitch` turns `OnchainTimeout` into our own `permanent_channel_failure` for a forward, and `PaymentService.InterpretFailure` reports `PermanentChannelFailure` (no source index) for our own payment. Alerts come only from the spending witness (`OnOutputSpentAsync`).
- `Local/`: `LocalCommitResolverOptions`, `ISweepDestinationProvider` → `WalletSweepDestinationProvider` (an unused P2WPKH wallet address per sweep, handed to the monitor again), and `LocalCommitResolutionServiceCollectionExtensions.AddLocalCommitResolutionServices()` (TryAdd; the resolver as itself and as an `IOutputResolver` enumerable entry; `UnavailableAnchorFeeInputProvider` as the `IAnchorFeeInputProvider` unless the host registered the wallet's adapter first: it selects nothing). The signer must keep the channel registered while it is `OnchainResolving`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Resolvers/Local/` (`LocalCommitResolutionHarness`: Alice's real commitment of a `RealSigningCommitmentPair`, in-memory rows/watches/broadcasts and a fake executor that mines blocks; every built transaction is script-executed against the output it spends), `Payments/Switch/OnchainEventsTests`; Docker Proof O3 in `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/Onchain/OnchainO3Tests` (4/4 green at the wave 5 integration).

## Revoked commitment on chain: penalties (BOLT 5 plan O5-T2/O5-T3, ABCD W5-D; `Onchain/Resolvers/`)
- `RevokedCommitResolver : IOutputResolver` (Domain port of W5-A; `CanResolve` only `ChannelCloseKind.RevokedCommitment`), registered by `Onchain/Resolvers/Revoked/RevokedResolverServiceCollectionExtensions.AddRevokedCommitResolver()` (singleton, also as `IOutputResolver` through `TryAddEnumerable`, idempotent; needs `AddOnchainBitcoinServices`, the signer, key derivation, chain/wallet/fee services and `ISecretStorageServiceFactory`). It never locks, saves or publishes: the W5-A executor applies its actions in one save, then publishes and raises.
- Each round (`ResolveAsync`): `IRevokedCommitDataSource.LoadAsync` (`RevokedCommitDataSource`: channel from memory, else the database; one penalty destination per channel (the script of a stored `Penalty` row of the channel, else one reserved wallet address, cached for the process), so a rebuilt penalty keeps its txid; the peer's secret n from the persisted shachain, point = secret·G; the revocation log entry and log start; the commitment tx from the block at `SpentAtHeight`, no txindex needed) → `ICommitmentOutputMapper.Map(..., CommitmentCase.Revoked, ...)` (from the log's spec; without an entry a no-HTLC spec with both balances at half the capacity, mapped by script; unmapped vouts → `AlertAction`, "pre-O1" when the number predates the log) → per output (`RevokedToLocal`, `RevokedHtlc`, `PaymentToRemote`) the row (created with `OutputDescriptorData` + a watch when missing), its recorded spend (the watched outpoint's spend first, `RevokedOutputSpend` with the spender's txid and the tx from its block or null when it cannot be fetched; the tx seen in `OnOutputSpentAsync` only while the row still says it was spent at that height, so a reorged-out spend is dropped; path and preimage from `HtlcWitnessParser`; ByUs = a stored broadcast of ours or the row's `ResolvingTransactionId`) and `OutputResolutionPlanner.Plan`. A spend that cannot be read is never guessed: ours is known from its txid, any other holds the output (a `B5-GEN-06` alert; its txid still invalidates a batch that spent it, and a second-level row already recorded for that txid is still penalized). Sweeps become `PenaltyNeed`s, `RaiseFulfilled`/`RaiseFailed` become `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled`/`OutgoingHtlcFailed` (removal `OnchainHtlcRemovals.OnchainTimeout()`, kind 4 = W5-B's `HtlcRemovalKind.OnchainTimeout`), raised **every round** the planner decides them (a failed executor save applies nothing; the switch is idempotent), one per HTLC per round, and never a fail after a fulfill was asked in this process; `AlertAction`s are returned once per process. The on-chain preimage is not persisted by the resolver (no repository API for it yet): after a restart the fulfill is rebuilt from the recorded spend. B5-REV-RES-03 applies only when every output of the revoked commitment is mapped: our offered HTLCs in the snapshot without an output then go through `PlanHtlcWithoutOutput` (fail at reasonable depth of the commitment, fulfill with a known preimage). While some output is unmapped (pre-log commitment, missing log entry, differing rebuild) any of them may be our offered HTLC, which the cheater can still claim with the preimage: the unmapped outputs are watched, a preimage in their spends fulfills at once, and the failure waits until every unmapped output is spent, reasonably deep, by a readable transaction without the preimage.
- The cheater's HTLC-timeout/success on a revoked HTLC output (`OnOutputSpentAsync`, or found again by `ResolveAsync`): a `RevokedSecondLevel` row for its output (vout = the spending input's index) with the `to_local`-form script (revocation key and the cheater's delayed key at the revoked point, `ChannelParams.Local.ToSelfDelay`; checked against the output's scriptPubKey, else an alert), a watch, deadline = its height + that delay, and at once an `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` when its witness reveals our offered HTLC's preimage (B5-REV-07, B5-REV-RES-01).
- `Revoked/PenaltyTransactionComposer`: outputs without a live transaction share one `PenaltyTransactionBuilder.BuildBatched` penalty (with our `to_remote`), except those within `security_delay` (18) of their deadline (to_local and second level: confirmation + CSV; HTLC outputs: `cltv_expiry`), which get `BuildSingle`, as does from the first round, on an option_anchors channel, a revoked HTLC we offered (`PenaltyNeed.Isolate`, O7-T3: the cheater's HTLC-success may already spend it and, with `SINGLE|ANYONECANPAY`, be pinned, so it never shares a batch with `to_local`); a still-unconfirmed batch is split once any member comes within 18 blocks (each output alone, `ReplacesTransactionId` = the batch, the most urgent paying at least `SweepFeePolicy.GetReplacementFee` of the batch's fee); a batch that lost an input to the cheater (spent by another tx) is rebuilt for what it still has (B5-REV-09). The split is skipped (batch kept, `B5-REV-08` alert) when the batch's fee is unknown or the most urgent output cannot outbid it alone, since every single would then be refused as a replacement; the urgent single is emitted first, so the others no longer conflict once it replaced the batch. Fees: `IFeeService` estimate through `SweepFeePolicy.Decide` (floor 253, 50 % cap, 100 % for a penalty near its deadline; a single penalty whose fee would leave less than dust pays everything above dust instead); only an output that cannot pay its own fee at the floor becomes `Ignored` with an alert, any other builder refusal is alerted and retried next block. Signed with `ISweepTransactionBuilder.Sign` (revocation key: `SweepKeyKind.Revocation`), `BroadcastTransactionModel` purpose `Penalty` (`Sweep` for a lone `to_remote`), rows `Broadcast` with `ResolvingTransactionId`. RBF of a single penalty or batch, the per-target estimate (the earliest deadline among the needs sets the target, NL-296) and retiring a split batch's row are done by the O6 `SweepScheduler` (above).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Resolvers/Revoked/` (`RevokedBreachKit`: `RealSigningCommitmentPair` cheater/victim with real signers, the revoked commitment rebuilt as the victim signed it, the cheater's HTLC-timeout/success, a driver standing in for the executor, every penalty input executed by NBitcoin against the output it spends; `RevokedResolutionTests`: batch, idempotence, second level after their HTLC-timeout, preimage from their HTLC-success fulfilled at once, our offered HTLC failed from depth 6 (every round), committed HTLC without output, split at deadline − 18, urgent outputs alone, no log entry, pre-log alert, fee spike (the small HTLC penalized down to dust), a lost fulfill asked again, unmapped offered HTLC (pre-log or missing entry) never failed while unspent / fulfilled from its HTLC-success / failed once spent 6 deep without the preimage, an unreadable spender after a restart (no guess, batch rebuilt with the recorded second level), a reorged-out spend ignored, a batch with an unknown fee kept; `PenaltyTransactionComposerTests` (mocked builders: builder refusal alerted and retried, dust abandoned, split skipped when the batch fee is unknown or not outbiddable); `RevokedCommitDataSourceTests`).

## BOLT 5 mempool reaction (plan O8, NL-098; `Onchain/Mempool/`)
- `MempoolReactor : IMempoolReactor` (singleton, `MempoolServiceCollectionExtensions.AddOnchainMempoolServices()`, called by the daemon's `AddNltgNodeServices`, not by `AddApplicationServices`; the daemon and the Docker `NLightningTestNode` call `Start()` before the chain monitor starts and `StopAsync()` before it stops; `Node:Onchain:Mempool:Enabled` (default true) turns it off). `Start()` subscribes to `IBlockchainMonitor.OnWatchedOutpointSpentInMempool` and `OnNewBlockDetected`, which only enqueue; one background loop handles the items one at a time (`WhenIdleAsync` for tests) and first re-follows the penalties prepared before a restart (`RestorePreparedAsync`: pending `Penalty`/`Sweep`/`HtlcClaim` rows of channels without a `ChannelCloses` row).
- `HandleSpendAsync(args)` (internal): **preimages** first: every witness item of the unconfirmed transaction that is the preimage of one of our offered HTLCs of the channel (`HtlcWitnessParser.Parse`, checked against the payment hash; incoming HTLCs are never matched) is staged into the record (`KnownPreimage`, one `IChannelStateDbRepository.ApplyAsync` + save under the channel lock, then `ChannelModel.UpdateCommitments`), then `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` goes to the scope's `IHtlcSwitch` (upstream fulfill, or our payment). Skipped when the record already has that preimage or a fulfill removal, so it acts once. Then, for a spend of the channel's **funding output**: skipped when a close is recorded (the block came first); else classified with `OnchainChannelWatcher.ClassifyAsync` (internal; the confirmed classification without recording anything); a `Revoked` commitment gets `RevokedCommitResolver.PrepareUnconfirmedPenaltiesAsync` (below), its `BroadcastAction`s stored as pending `BroadcastTransactions` rows in one save under the lock, then published through the monitor (`IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync`) and followed by commitment txid; a pending prepared row that already spends that commitment is only published again, and an `Abandoned` one (evicted earlier; a penalty rebuilt for the same commitment has the same txid: lock time 0, the channel's one penalty address from `RevokedCommitDataSource.GetDestinationScriptAsync`, RFC 6979, often the same fee) is made pending again (`MarkPendingAsync` + `MarkPending`, one save) and published. The peer's current or next commitment of an anchor channel is handed to the optional `IAnchorCpfpService.OnPeerCommitmentInMempool` (NL-381; the CPFP bumps it through our anchor on it, see "BOLT 5 anchor CPFP"). Every other kind is only logged: nothing (close, rows, state) is recorded before the block.
- `CheckPreparedAsync` (every block): a prepared commitment whose channel now has a close is forgotten (the watcher settled its penalties); one bitcoind no longer knows (`IBitcoinChainService.GetTransactionAsync` null; an RPC error counts as known) for `EvictionGraceBlocks` (3) blocks in a row, counted only while the monitor is at bitcoind's tip and not halted (without txindex a confirmed transaction out of the mempool is "unknown" too, so a block during catch-up never counts), has its penalties `Abandoned` (under the channel lock, re-checking the close), so the monitor stops resending them.
- `RevokedCommitResolver.PrepareUnconfirmedPenaltiesAsync(channel, commitment, number, tip)`: loads the context through the new `IRevokedCommitDataSource.LoadAsync(close, commitment, ct)` overload (default interface method; `RevokedCommitDataSource` uses the given transaction instead of reading a block), maps the commitment, builds one need per `RevokedToLocal`/`RevokedHtlc`/`PaymentToRemote` output (deadlines counted from tip + 1) and runs the same `PenaltyTransactionComposer` as `ResolveAsync`; returns only the `BroadcastAction`s and alerts.
- `OnchainChannelWatcher`, when it records a funding spend (`PersistAsync`), settles the prepared transactions in the same save (`SettlePreparedAsync`): a pending `Penalty`/`Sweep`/`HtlcClaim` row whose inputs spend the confirmed spend becomes the `ResolvingTransactionId` of the rows of the outputs it spends (`Broadcast`, with the revoked deadline), so the resolver never builds a second penalty; one that spends another transaction (a revoked commitment that was replaced) is abandoned; an `Abandoned` prepared row that spends the confirmed spend (and no output a live one spends) is made pending again, linked the same way and published after the lock (through the `IOutpointWatcher` when it is an `IChainBroadcaster`, the monitor). The executor also revives an `Abandoned` row whose txid a resolver's `BroadcastAction` produces again (pending in the round's save, then published). `RetireReplacedCloseAsync` (reorg) keeps a pending row that spends the new spend.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Mempool/MempoolReactorTests` (real commitments of a `RealSigningCommitmentPair`, the watcher and the penalty resolver with a stub data source over `OnchainTestStore`: preimage persisted before the switch and acted on once, no match, the revoked commitment's penalty stored then published with nothing else recorded, seen again, linked at confirmation, abandoned when another commitment confirms, abandoned after the eviction grace (a reappearance restarts the count), restored after a restart, a recorded close, our own commitment, the loop, disabled), `Onchain/Resolvers/Revoked/RevokedMempoolPenaltyTests` (script-valid penalty over every output of the unconfirmed commitment, no row). Docker proof: `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/Onchain/OnchainMempoolTests`.

## BOLT 5 anchor CPFP (plan O7-T2, B5-FAIL-06; `Onchain/Anchors/`)
- `AnchorCpfpService : IAnchorCpfpService` (singleton, `AnchorCpfpServiceCollectionExtensions.AddAnchorCpfpServices()`: TryAdd of `IAnchorChildTransactionBuilder` (Infrastructure.Bitcoin), `AnchorCpfpPolicy`, the service as itself and as the interface, and `AnchorCpfpOptions` (`Node:Onchain:Anchors`: `Enabled`, `NoDeadlineConfTarget` 36, `MaxFeePerMilleOfStake` 500, `MinFeeCapSat` 20,000, `SweepAnchors`); call it after `AddOnchainServices` (the shared `SweepFeePolicy`) and `AddLocalCommitResolutionServices` (`ISweepDestinationProvider`, the change and sweep script). Not called by `AddApplicationServices` yet: the integrator adds the line). `ChannelFailureService` takes it as an optional last constructor argument: its `Start`/`Stop` start and stop it, and `CompleteFailureAsync` calls `ScheduleCommitmentRound` for an anchor channel after the publish (outside the lock; it only schedules the round in the background with the service's stopping token and returns: on the `MustBroadcast` path `ChannelManager` sends the error only after `CompleteFailureAsync` returns, on the peer's inbound loop, which must not wait for a block round; `WhenIdleAsync` waits for scheduled rounds too). `IBitcoinChainService` is an optional constructor argument (`gettxout` for the end and the sweep, below). Nothing for the host to start.
- Rounds: one per new block (`OnNewBlockDetected`, background, never overlapping, coalesced to the latest height; `RunOnceAsync(height)` runs one, `WhenIdleAsync` for tests) over every loaded anchor channel (`ChannelParams.OptionAnchorOutputs`) that is `Failed` or `OnchainResolving`, under that channel's lock, one save per channel; publish (`IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync`), release and sweep after the lock. Reads the channel's `LocalCommitment` row and its `AnchorCpfp` rows (`BroadcastPurpose.AnchorCpfp = 8`).
- Pending commitment: deadline = the earliest `cltv_expiry` of the HTLCs untrimmed on the snapshot's local commitment (`CommitmentFeeCalculator.IsHtlcTrimmed` with our dust limit; none: `NoDeadlineConfTarget`), stake = our `to_local` + those HTLCs; without a deadline no child when the stake is 0 or the child's floor fee is above the cap, estimate for its target through `FeeEstimates` (floored at 253). No child while the commitment alone pays the estimate; else `AnchorCpfpPolicy.DecideChild` (package = commitment + child at the estimate, rounded up; at least the child's own floor fee; capped at `AnchorCpfpPolicy.GetFeeCap(stake, hasDeadline)` = stake x `MaxFeePerMilleOfStake`, raised to `MinFeeCapSat` only with a deadline). Wallet inputs come from the Domain-less port `IAnchorFeeInputSource` (in this folder; reserve per channel, `GetReservedAsync`, `ReleaseAsync`; reservations must be durable (persisted, restored at startup), the service never re-reserves after a restart; the O7-T1 fee-input selector backs it through an adapter; optional: without it no child, a warning at `Start`), re-deciding the fee for their weight until they cover fee + a change above dust. Child (`IAnchorChildTransactionBuilder.BuildChild`): version 2, input 0 our anchor then the wallet inputs, all `nSequence` 0xFFFFFFFD, one change output to `ISweepDestinationProvider`'s script. Signing: `ILightningSigner.SignAnchorInput` (funding key over the anchor script built by the signer, 330 sat only) and `SignWalletTransaction` (must sign only the wallet inputs; P2TR inputs need the anchor prevout, see `IAnchorFeeInputSource`), then every input is script-checked (`Transaction.CreateValidator`) before the row is stored; a signing failure (e.g. `NotImplementedException` before O7-T1) drops the child and releases the fresh reservation (logged once); so does a failed save of a first child (released before the exception ends the round). Row: `AnchorCpfp`, `FeeratePerKw` = the child's own feerate over its signed weight.
- RBF: when `SweepFeePolicy.ShouldBump(child.FirstBroadcastHeight, tip, deadline)` (past the deadline it keeps bumping every `RbfIntervalBlocks`) and (with a deadline, always, as `SweepScheduler` bumps claims; without one, only while) the package pays less than the estimate, `AnchorCpfpPolicy.DecideReplacement` (BIP 125 minimum over the old fee's upper bound `(rate + 1) * weight / 1000 + 1`, or the estimate, capped; none when the minimum is above the cap): same anchor (the conflict), the channel's reserved inputs plus more when needed, the old change script; the new row (`ReplacesTransactionId`, `FirstBroadcastHeight` = tip) and the old row `Replaced` in one save.
- End: commitment `Abandoned` or `Replaced` → pending children `Abandoned` (never rebroadcast) and the reservation released at once (no child can confirm).
