Commitment `Confirmed` with a pending child: the child may still confirm (it spends a confirmed output), so it stays pending (rebroadcast by the monitor, never bumped) and the reservation is kept (a funding tx taking its inputs would conflict with it under BIP 125 and be refused forever, NL-294) until our anchor is spent in the active chain (`IBitcoinChainService.GetConfirmedUnspentOutputAsync`; every child spends it) and a later round runs at or above the bitcoind tip read when that was seen (the monitor processed the spending block, so a winning child is `Confirmed`), or `ConfirmedCommitmentChildWaitBlocks` (2016) passed since the confirmation; then the still-pending children are `Abandoned` and the reservation released (once per process). Anchor sweep: once the confirmed commitment's next block is 16 deep and no child is pending, the anchors still unspent (`IBitcoinChainService.GetUnspentOutputAsync`, mempool included; a chain error skips the round and retries at the next block; without a chain service ours only when no child was ever made) are swept with empty signatures (`BuildAnchorSweep`, `nSequence` 16) to a wallet script when `AnchorCpfpPolicy.DecideAnchorSweep` says it pays for itself (output >= dust and fee <= `SweepMaxFeePerMille` of 330 x n: two anchors only below about 2.3 sat/vB, one never), else skipped (logged); planned once per commitment and process, stored as an `AnchorSweep` `BroadcastTransactions` row (`BroadcastPurpose.AnchorSweep` = 12) with its fee in the round's save and published through `IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync` (NL-611; anyone may take them first: a refused send marks the row `Abandoned`, never retried, and the chain monitor may abandon it after permanent refusals, `BroadcastRefusalRules.MayAbandon`; never bumped).
- Package relay (NL-380, wave O7b): a commitment below bitcoind's mempool minimum is refused alone and its child as an orphan. When the monitor's `PublishAsync` refuses a new child, and every round in which the newest pending child was not replaced and bitcoind does not have it (`IBitcoinChainService.GetTransactionAsync`: `getrawtransaction` finds mempool transactions without txindex), the round sends the commitment row and that child row together through `IBitcoinChainService.SubmitPackageAsync` (Bitcoin Core 28+ 1p1c; after the lock, in `CompleteAsync`). The rows are the persisted `BroadcastTransactions`, so the first round after a restart sends the pair as a package again (the monitor keeps sending both one by one). A package refused for its fee (`PackageSubmitResult.IsFeeRefusal`) marks the child in memory, and the next round replaces it without waiting for `RbfIntervalBlocks` and even when, without a deadline, it pays the estimate (the estimate is below what the mempool takes); each replacement adds at least the BIP 125 increment, up to the cap. No `submitpackage` (older node) or no chain service: logged once, the pair is only sent one by one. Tests: the package cases of `AnchorCpfpServiceTests` (`FakeAnchorChain.Mempool`/`Packages`/`PackageAnswer`).
- The peer's commitment (NL-381, wave O7b; `AnchorCpfpService.Peer.cs`): a child belongs to the commitment its input 0 spends (a `LocalCommitment` row's txid: ours; anything else: the peer's), so no new purpose or column. Each round of a channel runs the peer's part first: found in bitcoind's mempool when the O8 `MempoolReactor` handed it over (`IAnchorCpfpService.OnPeerCommitmentInMempool(channel, tx, isNext)` for a `RemoteCommit`/`RemoteNextCommit` of an anchor channel; memory only; the channel then joins the rounds even while `Open`), or, for every round channel, by `getrawtransaction` of the peer's current and next commitment txids (rebuilt with the optional `ICommitmentOutputMapper`) and of the parents of our pending children of it (so after a restart, and when our own commitment is refused because the peer's holds the funding output while an HTLC deadline approached and the monitor failed the channel); it must spend the funding outpoint. A child only while it carries untrimmed HTLCs (the peer's dust limit): deadline = their earliest `cltv_expiry`, stake = our `to_remote` + those HTLCs; then the same `PlanChildAsync` as ours (first child, RBF every `RbfIntervalBlocks`, BIP 125 increment, cap), published one by one (no package: its parent is in the mempool). While it is there our own commitment gets no child and no package. The reservation is shared by both commitments' children (only one commitment can confirm): each part reports None/Active/Done and the inputs go back once neither is Active (a failed first child releases only when the other part has no pending child). End: a recorded close (`IOnchainResolutionDbRepository.GetCloseAsync`) of another transaction abandons the peer's children; the peer's confirmed commitment keeps its pending child with its inputs as ours does (anchor seen spent, or `ConfirmedCommitmentChildWaitBlocks`); a peer commitment bitcoind no longer has for `PeerCommitmentMissingBlocks` (6) blocks in a row (counted once per height, only with the monitor at bitcoind's tip, not halted) has its pending children abandoned. Sweep: a close of kind remote, remote-next, future or revoked, 16 blocks deep, read from the hand-over or from the block at `SpentAtHeight` (`GetBlockAsync`), both anchors swept like ours (same `PlanAnchorSweepAsync`, once per commitment and process). The hand-over is also stored as a pending `PeerCommitment` row (NL-390) and handed to the monitor with `IBlockchainMonitor.TrackPendingBroadcast`, so the block that holds it confirms it; nothing is stored once a funding spend is recorded for the channel (NL-779: such rows were sent after every block forever). Tests: `Onchain/Anchors/AnchorPeerCpfpTests` (Bob's real signed commitment: script-valid child from the hand-over and from the probe, ours not bumped meanwhile, RBF, no child without an untrimmed HTLC, inputs kept until the anchor is spent, released after the child confirmed, abandoned when ours confirmed or the peer's left the mempool, anchors swept at +16) and `Onchain/Mempool/MempoolPeerCommitmentTests` (the hand-over, anchors only, never our own commitment).
- Mempool minimum (wave O7b review): the estimate a child targets (ours and the peer's) is floored at bitcoind's current mempool minimum (`IBitcoinChainService.GetMempoolMinFeeRatePerKwAsync`, `getmempoolinfo` `mempoolminfee` in sat/kw rounded up; null/error: the estimate alone), so a commitment that pays the estimate but not the mempool minimum gets a child and is packaged, and a replacement after a fee refusal targets at least that minimum.
- Restart of an `Open` channel whose peer force-closed (wave O7b review): the mempool hand-over is memory only and the monitor does not report a mempool transaction again, so the first block round of the process loads the channels with a pending child of the peer's commitment (input 0 spends no `LocalCommitment` row of the channel; `GetPendingAsync` + `GetByChannelIdAsync`, retried next round on a database error) and keeps them in the rounds (RBF, abandonment, release) while their peer part is Active. A recorded close with no child row but a reservation this process has not released (a crash between the reservation's save and the first child's) reports Done, so the reservation goes back.
- Not covered: the peer's commitment below the mempool minimum is not in our mempool, so it is never seen (no package of the peer's commitment with our child).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Anchors/` (`AnchorCpfpPolicyTests`; `AnchorCpfpServiceTests` over a real anchor commitment of `RealSigningCommitmentPair` failed with an HTLC in flight, `FakeAnchorWallet` (P2WPKH wallet + port) and `WalletSigningProxy` (a `DispatchProxy` over the real signer that replaces `SignWalletTransaction`): child script-valid at the package rate, none when the commitment pays, RBF after 2 blocks with a BIP 125 fee and the old row replaced (also past the deadline), kept when the package pays, release once the child confirmed, a confirmed commitment's pending child kept with its inputs until its anchor is seen spent and the monitor caught up, or until the wait passed, abandon + release on loss, a restart reusing the durable reservation for the replacement, a failed first save released, trimmed HTLCs setting no deadline, spent anchors never swept and a chain error retried, wallet signing missing, empty wallet, no anchors, the hook, anchors swept at +16 only at low fees; `AnchorCpfpHookTests`: `ChannelFailureService` schedules the round for anchor channels only, starts/stops it, survives its failure).
- Wallet adapters (wave O7 integration, `Onchain/Wallet/`, `AddAnchorWalletServices()` in `AddApplicationServices` before `AddLocalCommitResolutionServices`; `AddAnchorCpfpServices()` follows the resolvers): `WalletAnchorFeeInputSource : IAnchorFeeInputSource` (CPFP; reservations of `IFeeInputSelector` with purpose `anchor-cpfp:<channel id>`, found again by purpose after a restart) and `WalletAnchorFeeInputProvider : IAnchorFeeInputProvider` (anchors HTLC transactions; purpose `anchor-htlc:<commitment txid>:<vout>`, a new selection releases the owner's earlier one, change from the reservation or `ISweepDestinationProvider`, `SignAsync` signs the reservation that holds every fee input with `SignWalletTransaction(tx, reservationId, [htlcInput])`). Both TryAdds fall back (null source, `UnavailableAnchorFeeInputProvider`) when no `IFeeInputSelector` is registered. The CPFP child passes the anchor's prevout to `SignWalletTransaction` so P2TR fee inputs can sign (BIP 341); `IAnchorFeeInputProvider.SignAsync` takes the HTLC output (`SpentOutput`) for the same reason. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Wallet/AnchorWalletAdapterTests`.

## BOLT 1 peer storage (wave rf1, NL-010; `Node/PeerStorage/`)
- `PeerStorageServiceCollectionExtensions.AddPeerStorageServices()` (TryAdd singletons; called by the daemon's `AddNltgNodeServices`, after `AddChannelBackupNodeServices`, which binds `PeerStorageOptions` from `Node:PeerStorage`; not by `AddApplicationServices`): `PeerStorageService` (as itself and as the Domain `IPeerStorageService` that `PeerServiceFactory` hands every `PeerService`), Infrastructure's `PeerStorageCipher` and the default blob provider `ChannelListPeerBackupBlobProvider`. A host that registers its own `IPeerBackupBlobProvider` first (the static channel backup lane) keeps it. `FeatureOptions.OptionProvideStorage` is Optional and not experimental (a host that advertises it without `AddPeerStorageServices()` drops every `peer_storage` against the BOLT 1 MUST; `PeerServiceFactory` logs that misconfiguration once). The host (`NltgDaemonService`, `NLightningTestNode`) calls `IPeerStorageService.StopAsync()` after `PeerManager.StopAsync` and before the container is disposed: it stops the round and writes the delayed blobs (disposal alone can no longer open a scope, so `Dispose` is only a fallback).
- Provider: a `peer_storage` is kept only while we offer the feature and only from a peer with a channel in `IChannelMemoryRepository` (not Closed/Stale) or whose blob we already keep (`PeerStorageOptions.StoreWithoutChannel` lifts that); a blob from a peer without a channel yet (peers send theirs right after init, before our open_channel) is held in memory (latest per peer, at most `MaxPendingWithoutChannel` = 64 peers, forgotten after `PendingWithoutChannelLifetime` = 30 min) and adopted by the next round once a channel with that peer exists. The latest blob per peer is in memory (the table `PeerStorageBlobs` is loaded once, blocking, at the first connection or message) and written in its own scope at most once per `MinStoreInterval` (1 min; later ones wait for the round, every `BackupInterval`, or the disposal; the same bytes again write nothing); blobs are never deleted. `OnPeerInitialized` enqueues `peer_storage_retrieval` on the connection before it returns, and `PeerService` calls it before `WaitForInitAsync` completes, so it precedes `channel_reestablish`.
- Client: a peer that negotiated `option_provide_storage` gets our blob at its first connection of the process, when the round finds the blob's `Fingerprint` changed (a new channel, a closed one), and again when its `peer_storage_retrieval` holds another backup than the last we sent (by fingerprint, so a re-encryption of the same backup from before a restart is not re-sent; a retrieval handled before our init hook registered the connection forgets the last sent, so the next connection or round sends it). At the first connection of the process our backup waits for the peer's retrieval (at most `PeerStorageOptions.RetrievalWait`, 30 s; zero sends at once), and once a retrieval names a channel we do not know no backup goes to any peer until the restart (`BackupsHeldForDataLoss`): the peer's copy is the evidence and survives our restarts on the peer. Retrievals are recorded only from peers with a channel, peers we sent a backup to, or when the blob is ours. A retrieval is read with `IPeerBackupBlobProvider.TryReadBlobAsync`; channels it names that `IChannelDbRepository.ExistsAsync` does not know are logged as possible data loss and kept in `GetRetrievals()` (`PeerBackupRetrieval.UnknownChannels`, this process only). NL-432: every recorded retrieval is also written, before any backup goes back to that peer, to `PeerStorageRetrievals` through `IUnitOfWork.PeerStorageRetrievalDbRepository` (one row per peer, the latest: raw blob, `ReceivedAt`, `MatchesLastSent`, the channel ids unknown at receipt; migration `AddPeerStorageRetrievals`); a failed write stays in memory and is retried by every round and by `StopAsync`. `ListRetrievalsAsync` reads the rows (plus unwritten ones, `Persisted = false`), decodes each blob again with the blob provider and checks every named channel with `ExistsAsync` now (`PeerBackupChannelStatus.UnknownWhenReceived`/`KnownNow`, `PeerStorageRetrievalReport.StillUnknown`); `ListStoredBlobsAsync` is the provider side (latest blob per peer, delayed writes included). Both back the daemon's `listpeerstorage` (ClientCommand 32). `IUnitOfWork.PeerStorageRetrievalDbRepository` has a throwing default for test doubles.
- `ChannelListPeerBackupBlobProvider`: plaintext `"NLPB"`, version 2 (lane SP2-E, NL-478), u64 creation time, u16 count, (channel id 32 || peer node id 33 || flags 1 || funding txid 32 || u16 output index || u32 our funding key index) per channel past funding and not Closed/Stale (at most `MaxChannels` = 629; flags bit 0: the funding outpoint is set, bit 1: the key index is known: the stored funding row's (`IChannelFundingDbRepository.GetFundingSetAsync` through the optional `IServiceScopeFactory`), else the engine's current splice funding, else 0 when the funding key is the key set's), zero padding to `IPeerStorageCipher.MaxPlaintextLength`, so every blob is exactly 65531 bytes (BOLT 1 SHOULD pad); the fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the entries, so a splice lock or a dual-funded RBF changes it and the blob is sent again. Version 1 blobs (channel id and peer only, `PeerBackupChannel` funding fields null) are still read; an older build reads a version 2 blob as not ours. Never write a conditional with a bare `null` into a `TxId?`/`ShortChannelId?` (the implicit `byte[]` operators turn it into a throw, NL-443).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Node/PeerStorage/` (`PeerStorageTestContext`: production service, cipher and provider over in-memory `PeerStorageBlobs` and `PeerStorageRetrievals` tables, a manual clock and a controllable channel list; `PeerStorageRetrievalPersistenceTests` for NL-432); SQLite: `Integration.Tests/Persistence/PeerStoragePersistenceTests` (both migrations) and `ListPeerStorageIpcRoundTripTests` (a data-loss retrieval written by the production service and unit of work, listed over the IPC envelope after a restart and printed); Docker proof `Integration.Tests/Docker/Interop/Cln/ClnPeerStorageTests` (CLN v26.06.8 keeps our blob in its datastore `chanbackup/peers/<our id>`, hands it back after its disconnect and after our restart; we keep CLN's and CLN receives our retrieval; the retrieval is listed from `PeerStorageRetrievals` before and after our restart).

## Onion message transport and rate limit (BOLT 4 onion messages, wave M6 lane C: OM2-T1, OM2-T2)
- `PeerOutbox.TryEnqueueOnionMessage(OnionMessageMessage)`: onion messages are a separate, lower-priority class on their own queue. The send loop (one `SemaphoreSlim` released per write to either queue and once on close) takes the main FIFO first (channel messages, gossip, warnings, errors, the disconnect) and an onion message when the main queue is empty or, while gossip waits, in place of the gossip at the head after `PeerOutbox.GossipSendsPerOnionMessage` (8) gossip sends since the last onion message (a long gossip stream cannot starve them); a channel message, warning, error or disconnect at the head always goes first, so an onion message never delays one beyond the one send already on the wire; it goes out with `IPeerService.SendOnionMessageAsync` (a failure is logged at debug and the loop goes on). The queue is bounded by the constructor's `maxQueuedOnionMessages` (default `PeerOutbox.DefaultMaxQueuedOnionMessages` = 64, BOLT12 plan `OnionMessages:MaxOutboxPerPeer`; below 1 means 1): one more is refused, counted (`DroppedOnionMessageCount`) and reported through `onOnionMessageDropped`. `TryEnqueueDisconnect` drops the onion messages still waiting; `Complete` still sends them after the rest. `QueuedOnionMessageCount` is the waiting count.
- `PeerManager` implements the Domain port `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox` (`CanSendOnionMessage(nodeId)`, `TryEnqueueOnionMessage(nodeId, message)`): only the peer's current, not disconnected connection whose negotiated `Features.OptionOnionMessages` is not `No`; never connects (plan D6); false when the outbox is full or closed. `PeerManager.MaxOutboxOnionMessagesPerPeer` (public, default 64) is read when a connection's outbox is built: `AddApplicationServices` builds `PeerManager` through a factory that sets it from `OnionMessages:MaxOutboxPerPeer`. `QueuedOutboxOnionMessageCount` sums the current outboxes; every outbox's refusal increments the node-wide `DroppedOutboxOnionMessageCount` (the `dropped{reason=outbox_full}` source for M6-D's meter; the per-connection count is `PeerOutbox.DroppedOnionMessageCount`). When the peer's current session disconnects, `PeerManager` calls the optional `IOnionMessageRateLimiter.RemovePeer(nodeId)` (resolved lazily from the provider). `AddApplicationServices` registers `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox` as the `IPeerManager` singleton, like `IPeerGossipOutbox`.
- `OnionMessages/OnionMessageRateLimiter : IOnionMessageRateLimiter` (OM-R-01, MAY rate-limit by dropping): four token buckets, per peer and node-wide, in bytes and in messages; a message is admitted only if all four hold its tokens and then takes from all four (a drop takes nothing). `OnionMessageRateLimits` (same file) holds the limits; defaults per peer 64 KiB/s with a 256 KiB burst and 20 messages/s with a burst of 20, node-wide 640 KiB/s with a 2,560 KiB burst and 200 messages/s with a burst of 200; a rate or burst <= 0 turns that bucket off. Buckets start full and refill with `TimeProvider.GetTimestamp` (the constructor takes an optional `TimeProvider`, default `System`). A sweep every `SweepInterval` (256) calls forgets every peer whose buckets are full again, connected or not (a full bucket equals a fresh one), so memory stays bounded even when `RemovePeer` is never called or a late message re-adds a removed peer; `RemovePeer` forgets a full peer at once; a peer in debt is kept until refilled, so reconnecting never refills a peer early. `AdmittedCount`/`DroppedCount` for metrics. `AddOnionMessageServices` registers it (TryAdd) with the limits of `OnionMessageOptions` (`Peer*`/`Global*`) and the optional `TimeProvider`. The service (M6-D) calls `TryAdmit(peer, packetLength)` first in `HandleIncoming`.
- Tests: `Node/Services/PeerOutboxOnionMessageTests` (priority, gossip interleave, cap, disconnect, complete, failing send), `Node/Managers/PeerManagerOnionMessageTests` (port, negotiation gate, no connect, cap and node-wide drop count, `RemovePeer` on disconnect, none when a newer connection replaced the old one), `OnionMessages/OnionMessageRateLimiterTests` (manual clock: burst, refill, byte bucket, per-peer isolation, global caps, drops take nothing, disconnect/reconnect, sweep, concurrency).

## BOLT 4 onion messages (wave M6, NL-079, plan `docs/agents/BOLT12_PLAN.md` OM2-T3..T5, OM3; `OnionMessages/`)
- `OnionMessageServiceCollectionExtensions.AddOnionMessageServices()` (called by `AddApplicationServices`; TryAdd, idempotent): `OnionMessageService` (as itself and `IOnionMessageService`) and `OnionMessageMetrics` (`Meter("NLightning.OnionMessages")`: received, forwarded, delivered[kind], dropped[reason] (`OnionMessageDropReasons`), sent; also readable in memory for tests). Built by a factory lambda so the optional collaborators are taken only when registered: `IOnionMessagePacketBuilder` (lane M6-B; without it the service stays off and logs a warning), `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox` (the send path, `PeerManager`; without it the service stays off and logs a warning), `IOnionMessageRateLimiter` (lane M6-C; without it nothing is limited), `IGraphStore`, every `IOnionMessageHandler` (singletons; no two claim one type, types from 64), `TimeProvider`. `OnionMessageOptions` binds from `OnionMessages` (the daemon's `NodeServiceExtensions` must `Configure` it; the template in `NodeConfigurationExtensions` writes every default); invalid options (`GetValidationErrors`, e.g. `ConnectToReply` true, plan D6) are logged as an error and keep the service off: the constructor never throws, because `PeerServiceFactory` resolves the service while it builds every connection. `DefaultReplyTimeout` (`ReplyTimeout`) is used by the concrete `SendAndWaitForReplyAsync` overload without a timeout. Integrator lines: `services.Configure<OnionMessageOptions>(configuration.GetSection(OnionMessageOptions.SectionName))` in `AddNltgNodeServices`; the limiter built from the same options (`PeerBytesPerSecond`, `PeerBurstBytes`, `PeerMessagesPerSecond`, `PeerBurstMessages`, `Global*` map one to one onto `OnionMessageRateLimits`); `PeerManager.MaxOutboxOnionMessagesPerPeer = MaxOutboxPerPeer`.
- On only when we advertise `option_onion_messages` (Optional by default since Proof M6, plan D9) and a builder is registered: otherwise `HandleIncoming` drops (`not_available`) and every send returns `NotAvailable`.
- Receive: `HandleIncoming` (the peer read loop) only rate-limits and queues (bounded `MaxQueuedMessages`); one worker hands the message to the Domain `IOnionMessageUnwrapper` (Infrastructure.Bitcoin's `OnionMessageUnwrapper`, the node's only reader since NL-442: peel with empty associated data and the message's path_key, the strict Domain `OnionMessageTlvsCodec`, unblind, and every reader rule: any `allowed_features` bit, `payment_relay` or `payment_constraints`, a non-final hop with more than field 4, a `path_id` or no next hop, a final hop with more than one payload field → `Ignored`, whose `IgnoreKind` becomes the `dropped` tag through `OnionMessageService.ToDropReason`; a throw (no key manager) is `peel`). Non-final (`Forward`): next node by `next_node_id` or by SCID (`OnionMessagePathFinder.ResolveOurChannel`: our channel's real SCID or local alias, then a graph channel with us at one end); never forwarded to us, back to the sender, or to a peer that is not connected or did not negotiate onion messages (`IPeerOnionMessageOutbox.CanSendOnionMessage`); queued with `BlindedHopUnblinding.NextPathKey` (the override when present) by `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox.TryEnqueueOnionMessage`, never awaited, so a peer that stops reading only fills its own capped outbox (a refusal is `dropped{outbox_full}`) and never stalls the single worker. Final (`Deliver`): a `path_id` of our reply paths (`PendingReplyRegistry.IsOurs`, a per-process HMAC tag) goes only to its waiting caller when the type is expected, else it is ignored; otherwise the handler of the payload type runs on a second bounded queue (`MaxQueuedHandlerWork`), off the peer loop; no payload field counts as `delivered{empty}`.
- Send: `SendAsync` sends once. A node id: `OnionMessagePathFinder.FindPrefix` (a connected onion-message peer, else a BFS over the graph through nodes advertising bit 38/39, at most `MaxPathHops`) and a path we blind ourselves over every hop (the Domain `IBlindedMessagePathBuilder.CreateMessagePath`, Infrastructure.Bitcoin's vector-proven `BlindedMessagePathBuilder`: next_node_id per hop, equal-length padding, refuses payment_relay/constraints; `MessagePathFactory` was removed, NL-442; `ReplyPathFactory` and the offers' `OfferService` use the same builder). The service takes the unwrapper and the path builder as required constructor arguments (`AddOnionMessageCryptoServices`, part of `AddBitcoinInfrastructure`). A blinded path: the introduction node resolved (`sciddir_or_pubkey` through our channels or the graph), our own hops unblinded while the introduction node is us, then the unblinded prefix to it (the builder ends it with `next_path_key_override = first_path_key`). Contents over 32768 bytes or a builder `ArgumentException` → `TooLarge`; a first-hop outbox refusal → `Dropped` (and `dropped{outbox_full}`); sends are queued, never awaited. `SendAndWaitForReplyAsync` registers a `PendingReplyRegistry` wait (`MaxPendingReplies`), builds a reply path with `ReplyPathFactory` (introduction = a connected onion-message peer, one with an open channel first, else us; plan D7) and waits with `Task.WaitAsync(timeout, TimeProvider)`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/OnionMessages/` (`Harness/OnionMessageTestNode`: the production service from `AddOnionMessageServices` over `AddBitcoinInfrastructure` with its production packet builder, path builder and unwrapper (`PacketBuilder`, `PathBuilder`; NL-442), `RawOnionMessageWriter` (`Raw`: paths and packets the production writers refuse, for the reader-rule tests), `LinkedPeerService` connections (each with a bounded outbox pumped into the remote service; `Stalled` blocks its writes, `RefuseSends` refuses) with the node itself as `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox`, and a channel list; `OnionMessageHarnessTests` is Proof OM2-T5: forward through Bob as introduction node, reply over Alice's reply path, 32 KiB, rate limit, dispatch by type, introduction node = us, graph paths; `OnionMessageServiceTests` the reader rules; `OnionMessageChannelTests` SCID/alias forwarding, sciddir through our channel, reply-path introduction choice; `OnionMessageVectorTests` runs the BOLT 4 `blinded-onion-message-onion-test.json` through four nodes, every forward byte-exact).

## Static channel backups (SCB, wave rf1 lane R1 step 1; `Channels/Backup/`)
- Like LND's `exportchanbackup`/`verifychanbackup`. `ChannelBackupCodec` (plaintext v1, big-endian, one length-prefixed record per channel: channel id, peer node id and last known addresses, funding outpoint, capacity, funding height, SCID, flags (initiator, anchors, announced, inferred params), version, scid alias support, minimum depth, channel_type wire bytes, our key index with our funding key and payment basepoint (to check the derivation), the peer's funding key and four basepoints, both `ChannelParty`s; a reader skips unknown trailing record bytes and refuses anything else malformed) and `ChannelBackupCipher` (file = `"NLSCB"` + version 1 + 24-byte random nonce + XChaCha20-Poly1305 over the plaintext with the 6-byte header as AAD; key = HKDF-SHA256(node private key, salt `"NLightning static channel backup"`, info `"nltg-scb-v1"`)). Never private keys.
- `IChannelBackupService` → `ChannelBackupService` (singleton, `AddChannelBackupServices()`, not called by `AddApplicationServices`: the daemon's `AddChannelBackupNodeServices` does): `CreateSnapshotAsync`/`ExportAsync` read the database (`ChannelDbRepository.GetAllAsync` + the peer row); a channel is backed up (`IsBackedUp`) when its funding outpoint and the peer's key set are known and it is not None/Closed/Stale (OnchainResolving stays in: a restore still sweeps its `to_remote`). `VerifyAsync` decrypts with our key, checks chain and node id, and re-derives each channel's funding key and payment basepoint from its key index (`ILightningSigner.GetChannelBasepoints`); a bad backup is a result, not an exception. `WriteFileAsync` writes `ChannelBackupOptions.FilePath` atomically (`ChannelBackupFile`: owner-only temp file in the same directory, fsync, rename, then an fsync of the directory on Linux/macOS, best effort) only when the channels differ from the file (creation time ignored), never replaces a file holding channels while the database has no channel row at all (`KeptExisting`: a wiped database, the file is what the restore needs), moves a file that holds a channel the database has no row for at all (lost, e.g. a new channel opened on a wiped database before the restore) aside as `<file>.<time>.superseded` before writing the new one (rf1 review), and moves a file that does not decrypt with our key aside (`<file>.<time>.unreadable`); `ChannelBackupFile.MoveAside` never overwrites (`-2`, `-3`, ... on a name clash).
- `ChannelBackupMonitor` (singleton; the host calls `Start()`/`StopAsync()`): requests a write at start, on `IChannelMemoryRepository.OnChannelUpgraded`, on `OnChannelUpdated` only when a channel's backed-up flag or SCID changed (HTLC updates are filtered), and every `RefreshInterval` (10 min; catches channels that left memory without an event); requests coalesce, one write at a time after `WriteDelay` (1 s). Options `Node:Backup` (`FilePath`, `Enabled`, `WriteDelay`, `RefreshInterval`).
- Restore (step 2, like LND's `restorechanbackup`): `IChannelRestoreService` → `ChannelRestoreService` (singleton, `AddChannelBackupServices`; needs `IChannelManager`, `IPeerManager`, the chain monitor's `IOutpointWatcher`, `IMessageFactory`, `IMessageSerializer`, `ISha256`). `RestoreAsync(backup)`: decrypt, refuse another chain or node (`ChannelRestorePlanner.Validate`, `ChannelBackupException`), then the pure `ChannelRestorePlanner.Plan` per channel: `Restore`, `AlreadyExists` (any row with that id, even unreadable: never touched), `KeysMismatch` (the key index does not re-derive the recorded funding key and payment basepoint), `Duplicate`. Before anything is stored the key manager's channel index is advanced to at least the highest restored `KeyIndex` (`IChannelKeyIndexReserver` → `SecureKeyManagerKeyIndexReserver`: `GetNextChannelKey` until there, then an awaited key file write when the manager is the file-backed `SecureKeyManager`; `ISecureKeyManager` has no set-and-persist API, seam for the key-file owner), so a stale key file never gives a new channel a restored channel's keys. Per restored channel one save: the peer row (when missing: the backup's first address, else empty host/port 0), the recovery channel (`RecoveryChannels.Create`) with its serialized `error` (`RecoveryChannels.PeerErrorMessage`) and the `FundingOutput` watch; then `IChannelManager.RegisterExistingChannelAsync` and `TrackWatchedOutpoint`. Then `IFundingSpendLocator` (`ChainFundingSpendLocator` over `IBitcoinChainService`, TryAdd'ed; `NullFundingSpendLocator` without a chain service) checks the funding output: `gettxout` without the mempool, and when it is spent the blocks from the tip down to max(floor, tip - `Node:Backup:RestoreSpendSearchDepth` + 1) (4032) for its spend, `Node:Backup:RestoreSpendSearchBatchSize` (8) blocks read at once. The floor is the SCID height, else the backup's `FundingHeight` (a backup written before the funding confirmed; the search then also stops at the block holding the funding transaction, and none found means `NotConfirmed`); neither: `NotConfirmed` without reading the chain. A spend found is recorded on the funding watch (`MarkSpentAsync`, own save) and handed to `IOnchainChannelWatcher.HandleFundingSpentAsync` as the monitor would have (the peer force-closed on our wiped node's `error` before the restore). A spend older than the window (`FundingSpendLocation.HasOlderBlocksToSearch`, NL-430) is searched in the background by `IFundingSpendLocator.RescanAsync(entry, belowHeight)` down to the floor and handed over the same way once found; the channel's `Detail` starts with `FundingSpendRescan:`. Only a spend with nothing left to search (or no on-chain watcher) still reports `FundingAlreadySpent: ... rescan from height N`. The background search lives in memory: `IChannelRestoreService.ResumeSpendSearches()` (called by the daemon's `ChannelBackupHostedService` at every start; returns at once) looks the spend up again in the background for every recovery channel still waiting (`IsRecoveryChannel`, from `ChannelDbRepository.GetAllAsync`, entry rebuilt with `ChannelBackupService.CreateEntry`), each once the start-up registration has loaded it (the on-chain watcher ignores a channel that is not loaded; `ResumeRegistrationTimeout` 5 min, then skipped with a warning), so an interrupted search starts again (an unspent output costs one `gettxout`). A block the bitcoin node pruned stops the search with `FundingSpendStatus.BlocksPruned` (bitcoind's "pruned data" error; the blocks above it are searched first): the detail starts `FundingSpendPruned:` and says to sweep by hand, never to retry. The background search logs its progress every `ChainFundingSpendLocator.ProgressLogBlocks` (1,000) blocks and hashes the block transactions only for an entry without a short channel id (with one, the floor already is the funding block). Running `restorechanbackup` again re-checks every channel that is already a recovery channel still Failed (`AlreadyExists` with a "recovery channel waiting" detail): the spend lookup runs again and its peer is connected when it is not. Peers (NL-431): every known address is tried in order, graph `node_announcement` addresses first (`IGraphStore`, IPv4/IPv6/DNS via `ChannelBackupService.ConnectableAddresses`), then the peer row, then every backup address (the backup itself carries the peer row plus the announced addresses, at most `ChannelBackupService.MaxAddressesPerChannel` 8). The restore (which holds its lock) tries the first address and the next ones only within `ChannelRestoreService.ConnectBudget` (20 s); the addresses left are tried at once by the background loop, before its first backoff wait. A connected peer of a new recovery channel is disconnected first and waited for, up to `DisconnectTimeout` 10 s, so the new connection runs `OnPeerConnectedAsync` (still connected after it: `Connected` false with the reason). When no address answers, a background loop retries them (graph re-read each round) with `Node:ReconnectInitialDelay`/`ReconnectMaxDelay` backoff until the peer is connected, no recovery channel with it is still waiting (`IChannelMemoryRepository`), or the service is disposed. One restore at a time; the service is `IDisposable` (cancels the background work; `WaitForBackgroundWorkAsync` is internal for tests).
- Recovery channels (`RecoveryChannels`, no schema change): `ChannelState.Failed` + `DataLossDetected` + no snapshot + no signature either way (`IsRecoveryChannel`; a channel failed after funding always has the peer's signature). Our keys come from the signer (`GetChannelBasepoints(KeyIndex)`), the peer's basepoints, parameters, outpoint, SCID and the obscuring factor from the backup; balances unknown (all shown on the peer's side), feerate a 253 sat/kw placeholder; the peer's key set carries `UnknownPerCommitmentIndex` (0, commitment 2^48 - 1) so the on-chain watcher never takes the placeholder point for the peer's current commitment: the peer's commitment is then `FutureCommitment` (data loss) and `RemoteCommitResolver.AddDataLossRowsAsync` sweeps our `to_remote` (P2WPKH, or anchors' CSV-1 P2WSH). Nothing ever signs or broadcasts a commitment for it (signer data-loss mark from the database, the failure service and the executor's reorg grace skip data-loss channels). `ChannelManager.OnPeerConnectedAsync` (the only hook) raises for it, before its stored error, the BOLT 2 data-loss `channel_reestablish` (`CreateDataLossReestablish`: `next_commitment_number` 0, `next_revocation_number` 0, zero secret, our point of commitment 0 from the signer or our payment basepoint): the receiver MUST fail the channel and broadcast its latest commitment (B2-RE-14). A funding output the peer spent before the restore is found by the restore itself: within `RestoreSpendSearchDepth` at once, older in the background down to the funding block (above).
- Restore tests: `Channels/Backup/ChannelRestorePlannerTests`, `ChannelRestoreServiceTests` (mocked repositories/managers: order save → register → connect, existing channel kept, tampered/other node/other chain refused, key mismatch, a connected peer disconnected first, an unreachable peer, a failed save), `RecoveryChannelsTests`, `RecoveryChannelReconnectTests` (the `ChannelManager` hook); SQLite reload in `Integration.Tests/Persistence/RecoveryChannelPersistenceTests`; Docker proof `Integration.Tests/Docker/BackupRestoreFlowTests` (anchors and static_remotekey against LND: export, wipe, restore, LND force-closes, our `to_remote` swept to the new wallet; `scripts/run-cluster.sh -n 1 --suite onchain --class NLightning.Integration.Tests.Docker.BackupRestoreFlowTests`, the cluster only since NL-820).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Backup/` (codec round trip/truncation/forward compatibility, cipher layout/HKDF by hand/every flipped bit refused, service snapshot/verify/write rules with mocked repositories, monitor). Note `.gitignore` ignores `Backup*/` (Visual Studio upgrade folders); a negation keeps `**/Channels/Backup/`.
- Splices and dual-funded opens (wave sp2 lane SP2-E, NL-478): a record's funding fields are the channel's **current** funding (`ChannelModel.FundingOutput`: outpoint, capacity, both funding keys), and the record appends, still codec version 1, `u32 local_funding_key_index | u8 pending_count | pending x (32 txid | u16 index | u64 capacity | u32 key index | 33 local key | 33 remote key)` (`ChannelBackupEntry.LocalFundingKeyIndex`/`PendingFundings`, from `IChannelFundingDbRepository.GetFundingSetAsync`; a record without them reads index 0, no pending). `IChannelFundingKeySource` → `SignerChannelFundingKeySource` (TryAdd'ed by `AddChannelBackupServices`: index 0 from `GetChannelBasepoints`, index i from `LocalLightningSigner.GetFundingPubKey(channelKeyIndex, i)` (`m/0'/i'`), null for another signer) re-derives the rotated key: `VerifyAsync` and `ChannelRestorePlanner.Plan(..., deriveFundingKey)` check the funding key at the entry's index (a reader that predates the field checks index 0 and refuses a spliced channel: `KeysMismatch`, never a restore with the wrong key); a pending splice whose key does not derive is dropped from the entry. `ChannelBackupMonitor` keys each channel by backed-up flag, SCID, funding outpoint, our funding key and pending splice outpoints (`GetBackupKey`), so a splice lock and a dual-funded open or RBF rewrite `<configPath>/channel.backup`. Restore: `ChannelRestoreService.FollowSplicesAsync` locates the backed-up funding and, while its spend is a splice (`IFundingSpendLocator.FollowSpliceAsync`, default null; `ChainFundingSpendLocator` with the pure `SpliceSpendFollower`: not a BOLT 3 commitment shape (locktime 0x20, sequence 0x80), then a pending splice of the backup, else a P2WSH 2-of-2 of our key at the current index .. +`MaxKeyRotationLookahead` (8) with the peer's known keys, else the 2-of-2 script in the witness of that output's spend in the recent window (a peer that rotated its key too)), moves to the splice's output (SCID from its block and index) and locates it, at most `MaxSplices` (64) times; the recovery channel is made at the last funding (`RecoveryChannels.Create(entry, basepoints, derivedFundingKey, sha256)`, the key sets keep the basepoints) and, when it is a splice's or the backup named pending splices, its rows go in the same save (`RecoveryChannels.CreateFundings`: a `Splice` current row with our key index, pending rows with the capacity change on the peer's side) so it reloads with the splice's keys and the signer gets the key index. A stored recovery channel found spliced later (a restore run again, the start-up resume, the background search) is moved (`MoveRecoveryChannelAsync`: `UpsertAsync` pending + `ApplyLockAsync` + a watch of the new outpoint in one save under the channel's `IChannelLockProvider` lock when registered, then `ReplaceFundingOutput` on the memory model, the signer's `RegisterFunding` + `LockFunding` (errors ignored: it loads the channel from the database otherwise) and `TrackWatchedOutpoint`). Result details of a followed channel start `FundingSpliced:`. A splice the chain monitor reports for a recovery channel (it confirmed after the restore) never reaches the on-chain watcher as a close: `AddChannelBackupServices` wraps the registered `IOnchainChannelWatcher` (or, registered later, `OnchainChannelWatcher`) in `SpliceFollowingOnchainChannelWatcher`, which first asks `IChannelRestoreService.TryHandleRecoveryFundingSpendAsync` (recovery channel in memory and a spend that is no commitment: follow and move the channel; a failure there drops the spend with a critical log instead of recording a close); the restore service itself uses the unwrapped watcher (`Inner`). A spend that is no commitment and can't be followed yet (the peer rotated its funding key and the splice's output is unspent) is never handed over as a close (review finding): the channel stays a recovery channel at the spent funding, the detail starts `FundingSplicedUnresolved:`, and the P2WSH outputs of that transaction are checked (`IFundingSpendLocator.CheckSpliceOutputAsync`) at once, after every block (`OnNewBlockDetected` of the outpoint watcher when it is the chain monitor; `internal CheckSpliceWaitsAsync` for tests), at every start and on `restorechanbackup` again, until one is spent with a 2-of-2 witness naming our key (moved there, the peer's commitment handed over) or all of them are spent by something else (then handed over). Simple taproot channels (NL-1059): the follower matches P2TR outputs of `KeyAgg(our candidate key, the peer's known keys)` (`SpliceSpendFollower.Matches(..., simpleTaproot, IMusig2Service)`, the locator takes the optional `IMusig2Service`); past a splice whose peer key the backup does not name, `ChainFundingSpendLocator` walks the P2TR outputs spend by spend (at most `MaxTaprootOutputChecks` = 32 per follow) until the peer's commitment that pays our taproot `to_remote` or anchor (`SpliceSpendFollower.PaysUsOnTaprootCommitment`, keyed to our payment basepoint) or a splice our keys recognize proves the path, and the restore asks the peer: the latest `peer_storage_retrieval` of the channel's peer (`IPeerStorageService.GetRetrievals`, a lazy optional dependency) names the funding outpoint and our key index, and the splice is followed to it (`FollowPeerStorageHintAsync`, also in every splice-wait round); a funding found that way keeps the peer's last known funding key. Dual-funded opens: the backup of a v2 channel without a short channel id names every other stored negotiation for which we sent `tx_signatures` as a pending funding with the channel's keys (`GetDualFundCandidatesAsync`); the restore (and the start-up resume and a second restore, `SelectConfirmedCandidateAsync`) makes the channel at the candidate that confirmed (`FundingRbf:`, moved with kind `Initial`), else keeps the candidates as pending rows. The writer checks every entry's funding key against `IChannelFundingKeySource` (`CheckFundingKey`: the recorded index, else 0..`MaxFundingKeyIndexSearch` (256)); an entry no index derives keeps the file's previous entry (or is left out) and is reported in `ChannelBackupWriteResult.UnverifiedChannels` with an error log. Tests: `Channels/Backup/SplicedChannelBackupTests` (codec, older reader refused, planner, recovery channel, backup of a spliced channel, a dual-funded (V2) channel), `SpliceSpendFollowerTests`, `ChainFundingSpendLocatorSpliceTests`, `ChannelRestoreServiceSpliceTests` and `ChannelRestoreServiceSpliceReviewTests` (the proof: `SpliceBackupKit` = a real `LocalLightningSigner`'s keys, real funding/splice/commitment transactions in blocks, the real `ChainFundingSpendLocator`: pre-splice backup with the peer's commitment on the splice, pre-splice with the splice open, post-splice, a signer without rotated keys refused, a stored recovery channel moved, the background search), `ChannelBackupMonitorSpliceTests`, `ChannelBackupReviewTests`, `SpliceFollowingOnchainChannelWatcherTests`, `Node/PeerStorage/PeerBackupBlobVersion2Tests`; SQLite `Integration.Tests/Persistence/SplicedRecoveryChannelPersistenceTests`; Docker `Docker/Interop/Cln/ClnSpliceBackupRestoreTests` (against CLN, which splices: backups before and after a splice, wipe, restore, CLN force-closes on the splice, our `to_remote` swept).

## Interactive-tx driver (splicing plan wave IT, IT4-T1/T2; `InteractiveTx/`, `Channels/Handlers/Tx*MessageHandler.cs`)
- `InteractiveTxDriver : IInteractiveTxDriver` (singleton, `AddInteractiveTxServices`) runs one negotiation per channel and keeps its completed attempts for RBF, in memory, touched only under the channel's lock. The BOLT 2 rules live in the Domain `InteractiveTxSession` (lane IT-A), reached through the `IInteractiveTxEngine`/`IInteractiveTxNegotiation` seam (`DomainInteractiveTxEngine` in production, the test `ReferenceInteractiveTxEngine` in `Application.Tests/InteractiveTx/TestDoubles/`).
- The dependent protocol (splice, dual-funded open, a test) implements `IInteractiveTxHost` and calls `StartAsync(InteractiveTxTerms, host)` under the lock: the host supplies the `SharedFundingSpec` (`GetSharedFundingAsync`), the commitment step (`CreateCommitmentSignedAsync`: our `commitment_signed` for the new funding, staged on the given unit of work), the shared input's signature/witness, the completion (`OnCompletedAsync` with the finalized transaction; broadcast is the host's) and `OnAbortedAsync`. When the host has verified the peer's `commitment_signed` it calls `OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync` (IT-SIG-03); our `tx_signatures` follow in the IT-SIG-01 order. `RequestRbfAsync` sends `tx_init_rbf` (floor `GetMinimumRbfFeeratePerKw`: max(25/24 x, +25 sat/kw)); a peer's `tx_init_rbf` is checked against the floor before `OnRbfRequestedAsync`.
- Persistence: the session row (`IUnitOfWork.InteractiveTxSessionDbRepository`, lane IT-C) is added in the save that precedes our `commitment_signed` (with the host's staged writes) and updated in one save per later transition (CS received, our/their `tx_signatures`, Signed, Aborted). `OnDisconnectedAsync` (called by `ChannelManager.OnPeerDisconnectedAsync` under each channel's lock for every channel `GetChannels(peer)` lists, wave qit integration) forgets an unstored negotiation (reservation released); a stored one stays for `ResumeAsync` (restart, `next_funding`).
- `tx_abort` rules: with no negotiation, 66-73 get our `tx_abort` and the peer's `tx_abort` is echoed; while our `tx_abort` waits for its echo, stale 66-73 and the echo itself are ignored, and `StartAsync`/`RequestRbfAsync` throw (a new attempt would take the echo as its own and the two drivers would bounce `tx_abort` forever); the wait ends with the echo, `OnDisconnectedAsync` or `InteractiveTxDriver.AbortEchoTimeout` (1 min, the driver's `TimeProvider`), and is not started when the peer already sent `tx_signatures` (it keeps the negotiation and never echoes). A `tx_abort` arriving after we echoed one, with no negotiation, RBF request or new quiescence (`IQuiescenceService.GetState` blocking updates) since, is not echoed again. After our `tx_signatures` we never send `tx_abort` (`AbortAsync` throws) and a peer's `tx_abort` is logged and ignored (the negotiation is kept, IT-ABT-01).
- Persist, then memory, then send: a failed save restores the attempt's negotiation and row in memory (the caller's message can be handled again); a fully signed attempt becomes completed (and the quiescence ends) only after its save. The driver checks IT-RBF-01 itself before the commitment step: an attempt that spends no input of an earlier completed one is aborted, whatever the engine does. `ResumeAsync(..., completedAttempts)` takes the channel's other stored rows (Signed ones become completed attempts; a Signed `model` is only remembered), `CreateTxSignaturesRetransmission(channelId, txid)` rebuilds our stored `tx_signatures` for `channel_reestablish` `next_funding` (the `commitment_signed` retransmission is the host's), and `GetChannels(peer)` lists the channels to pass to `OnDisconnectedAsync` (under each channel's lock) when the peer's connection closes. Wired at the wave qit integration: `AddApplicationServices` calls `AddInteractiveTxServices()` (and `AddInteractiveTxContributorServices()`), and `ChannelManager.OnPeerDisconnectedAsync` calls `OnDisconnectedAsync` for each of `GetChannels(peer)` under that channel's lock (c502fdb6). A peer message the engine rejects or throws on (`ArgumentException`/`InvalidOperationException`/...) is answered with `tx_abort`, never a warning or a channel failure. The wallet reservation is released on abort unless a completed attempt still holds it (an RBF that re-adds its inputs). Every end of a negotiation (abort sent/received, both signatures) calls `IQuiescenceService.Terminate` when one is registered.
- The nine handlers derive from `InteractiveTx/Handlers/InteractiveTxMessageHandler<T>`, resolve the driver per message (`GetService`), and answer `tx_abort` themselves when none is registered; `ChannelManager` dispatches 66-74 through `DispatchInteractiveTxMessageAsync` (unknown channel → `error`, as for every channel message). `open_channel2`/`accept_channel2` go to `Channels/DualFunding/` since wave DF (below).

## Per-channel routing policy (wave sp1 lane SP1-G; `Channels/RoutingPolicies/`)
- Contracts in Domain `Channels/RoutingPolicies` (`ChannelPolicyOverride`: nullable fields = `Node:Routing`; `EffectiveChannelPolicy`; `IChannelPolicyService`) and `Channels/Interfaces/IChannelPolicyDbRepository` (table `ChannelPolicies`, lane SP1-C). `ChannelPolicyServiceCollectionExtensions.AddChannelPolicyServices()` (TryAdd, idempotent; the Daemon's `AddChannelPolicyIpcServices()` calls it): `ChannelPolicyStore` (itself and `IChannelPolicyProvider`, one instance) and `ChannelPolicyService : IChannelPolicyService`. Without the registration every channel uses `Node:Routing` (the consumers take the provider as an optional constructor argument).
- `ChannelPolicyRules` (pure): `Resolve` = override values where set, `Node:Routing` elsewhere, then the announced HTLC range (min = max(peer's `htlc_minimum_msat`, configured), max = min(capacity, peer's `max_htlc_value_in_flight_msat` when set, configured)); `GetValidationErrors` (cltv delta >= 34, above `ExpiryTooSoonBlocks`, <= `MaxCltvExpiryDistance`; `htlc_maximum_msat` positive and <= capacity (BOLT 7 MUST); announced min <= announced max); `Merge` (patch semantics), `IsEmpty`, `HasSameValues`.
- `ChannelPolicyStore`: memory cache for the synchronous readers, loaded once (`LoadAsync`, or the first read/write waits for it), written through `IUnitOfWork.ChannelPolicyDbRepository` in its own scope and save before memory changes. A unit of work whose repository throws `NotSupportedException` (the contract's default, i.e. a build without SP1-C's table) turns it memory-only with one warning (`IsPersistent` false; `setchannelpolicy` then reports `IsPersisted` false and the CLI warns). The host must await `LoadAsync` before `PeerManager.StartAsync` and fail the start if it throws; a synchronous read whose lazy load fails leaves `IsLoaded` false (retried at most every `LoadRetryInterval`, 5 s), and while it is false `HtlcForwardingPolicy` refuses every forward (`temporary_channel_failure`) and `ChannelUpdateService` makes no update (never `Node:Routing` in place of an unread override). Each change keeps the configured values it replaced for `PreviousPolicyGracePeriod` (10 min, memory only; `GetPreviousPolicies`). Rows of closed channels are never deleted.
- `ChannelPolicyService`: under the channel lock (not reentrant: never call it while holding the channel's lock) merge + validate (`ArgumentException`) + save; unknown channel (not in `IChannelMemoryRepository`) → `KeyNotFoundException`. After the lock a changed policy is announced with `IChannelUpdateService.SendChannelUpdateAsync` (fresh signed update, timestamp strictly above our previous one, to the peer and, announced, to the graph + own-gossip relay, which flushes the newest per channel/direction every 60 s); every call first awaits `LoadAsync` (a DB failure throws instead of answering with `Node:Routing`); a change that leaves the announced fee, delta and HTLC range as they were (an unchanged patch, a value equal to `Node:Routing`'s, a maximum the channel's limits cap anyway, a reset without override) is saved but sends nothing (BOLT 7: no redundant `channel_update`); a failed send is logged (the policy applies anyway). No rate limit of our own on the peer path (peers apply theirs; LND 10 per channel and minute).
- Consumers: `ChannelUpdateService` (`TryGetPolicy`/`BuildUnsignedUpdate`/`IsCurrent` use `IChannelPolicyProvider.GetEffectivePolicy`, so a reconnection sends a new update when the policy changed), `Payments/Policy/HtlcForwardingPolicy` (fee, cltv delta, htlc min/max of the **outgoing** channel from `GetConfiguredPolicy`; for 10 minutes after a change the fee (or blinded `payment_relay`) and the cltv delta may satisfy any replaced policy instead, BOLT 7 "SHOULD keep accepting the previous channel parameters for 10 minutes"; a lower max / higher min applies at once; `ExpiryTooSoonBlocks`/`MaxCltvExpiryDistance` stay node-wide), the Daemon's `ListChannelsClientHandler`. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/RoutingPolicies/` (`ChannelPolicyTestKit`, `InMemoryChannelPolicyTable`), `Gossip/ChannelUpdateServiceTests` (policy cases), `Payments/Policy/HtlcForwardingPolicyTests`.

## Chain-processing halt (NL-216)
While `IBlockchainMonitor.IsChainProcessingHalted` (a block that keeps failing, or a reorg deeper than the header ring) the node takes no new risk that only the chain can settle (`Domain.Bitcoin.Constants.ChainProcessingHalt`): `ChannelOperationsService.OfferHtlcAsync` throws `CommitmentRefusedException("NL-216", ...)` (payments avoid the channel; the switch fails a forward back upstream with `temporary_channel_failure`), `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` (optional `IBlockchainMonitor` argument) throws `ChannelErrorException` for the peer's `open_channel`, and `HtlcSwitch` fails a new final-hop HTLC back with `temporary_node_failure` instead of revealing the preimage (a committed set's replay is still fulfilled). Fulfills (forwards, committed sets), fails, fee updates, closes and every broadcast go on. The daemon's IPC side (`chainstatus`, `openchannel`, `payinvoice`) is in the Daemon CLAUDE.md. Tests: `Channels/Services/ChannelOperationsServiceTests` (`Given_ChainProcessingHalted_*`), `Channels/Handlers/OpenChannel1MessageHandlerTests`, `Payments/Switch/ChainHaltSwitchTests` (final hop and forwarder on `ThreeNodeHarness`).


## Graceful shutdown drain (NL-591)
`INodeDrainState` (Domain `Node/Interfaces`, singleton `Node/Services/NodeDrainState`, registered by `AddApplicationServices`) is set by the daemon's `shutdown` command (Daemon CLAUDE.md "Operator commands") once it began and found no HTLC in flight, and stays set until the process stops (a refused shutdown ends it again). While it is set the same choke points as the chain-halt gate refuse new activity (`Domain/Node/Constants/NodeDrain`, requirement id NL-591): `ChannelOperationsService.OfferHtlcAsync` throws `CommitmentRefusedException` (forwards fail back upstream with `temporary_channel_failure`), `HtlcSwitch` fails a new final-hop HTLC back with `temporary_node_failure` (a committed set is still fulfilled), `OpenChannel1MessageHandler` and `DualFundedOpenService` (our open and the peer's `open_channel2`) refuse with an `error`, and `SpliceService` refuses our splice and bump (`InvalidOperationException`) and answers the peer's `splice_init`/`tx_init_rbf` with `tx_abort`, and `DualFundedOpenService.DecideRbfAsync` answers a peer's RBF of a dual-funded open with `tx_abort` (NL-592). Fulfills, fails, fee updates, closes and broadcasts go on. `INodeBusyStateMonitor`/`NodeBusyStateMonitor` (`Node/Services`) snapshots what `shutdown --wait` waits for (NL-592): the HTLCs in flight on every channel that is not Closed/Stale and does not resolve on chain (`ChannelHtlcs.InFlightOffChain`; a Failed or OnchainResolving channel's HTLCs are `HtlcsResolvingOnChain`, never busy, but still give the nearest expiry, NL-1006), a mid-flight negotiation (an interactive-tx attempt in progress or its abort echo/RBF request, any quiescence, an unsigned open) and the nearest `cltv_expiry` with the blocks until our deadline for it. Every gate takes the state as an optional dependency (null = never draining). Tests: `ChannelOperationsServiceTests.Given_TheNodeDraining_*`, `OpenChannel1MessageHandlerTests.Given_TheNodeDraining_*`, `Node/Services/NodeDrainStateTests`.
## Accounting feed: sealer and snapshot (NL-602, plan `docs/agents/ACCOUNTING_PLAN.md` A1-T4/T5; `Accounting/`)
`AccountingServiceCollectionExtensions.AddAccountingServices()` (TryAdd, called by `AddApplicationServices`; the daemon binds `AccountingOptions` from `Accounting`) registers `AccountingEventSealerService` (as itself and the Domain `IAccountingEventSealer`, one instance) and `NodeSnapshotSource : INodeSnapshotSource`. The sealer is the feed's only sealer (writers only `Add` rows on their transition's unit of work): a timer loop modeled on `ExpiredBolt12InvoicePruner` (`Start()` runs a round at once and then every `SealInterval` (5 s; zero or less = the default), `Nudge()` asks for an early round, `StopAsync()` cancels and awaits; `NltgDaemonService` and `NLightningTestNode` start it after the chain monitor and stop it before), each round in batches of `SealBatchSize` (500), one scope and one save per batch (`GetUnsealedAsync` → `GetSealedKeysAsync` → `GetChainTipAsync` → the Domain `AccountingEventSealer.Seal` → `ApplySealsAsync` → `SaveChangesAsync`) until a batch is short or `MaxBatchesPerRound` (100) ran. `SealNowAsync` (the IPC 41 handler calls it before listing) and the loop share one `SemaphoreSlim`, so rounds never overlap and the ledger sequence stays dense; a row that commits after a round read the table is sealed by the next one. A duplicate key is logged as a warning with the key and counted on `Meter("NLightning.Accounting")` `nlightning.accounting.events.duplicate`; sealed rows on `nlightning.accounting.events.sealed`. A failed round is logged and retried; the rows stay unsealed. `NodeSnapshotSource` reads every loaded channel that is not Closed/Stale (gross balances, in-flight HTLCs from the snapshot; zero off-chain amounts once `OnchainResolving`), the unresolved `OutputResolutions` rows that are ours and unspent (Pending/Waiting/Broadcast; HTLC outputs reported apart; only the outputs the books count, `OnchainAccounting.CountsAtClose`, in the pending amounts, the others as `PendingUncountedMsat`, NL-618; channels no longer loaded get a bucket of their own) through a scope of its own, and `IUtxoMemoryRepository`'s confirmed/unconfirmed/locked balances at the chain monitor's height; nothing is persisted (no `Snapshot` event yet). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/` (`AccountingEventSealerServiceTests` on a SQLite file through the production unit of work: rounds, batches, duplicates and metrics, concurrent `SealNowAsync`, the loop and a failing round; `NodeSnapshotSourceTests`), Daemon `Handlers/AccountingClientHandlersTests`, `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingCommandsTests`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingIpcRoundTripTests`.
Backfill (A1-T6, `Accounting/Backfill/`): `AccountingBackfillService` (singleton, as itself and the Domain `IAccountingBackfill`, from `AddAccountingServices`) opens the feed with a **cutover**, not a reconstruction (a spent UTXO row is deleted, so wallet history cannot be rebuilt). `EnsureCutoverAsync` runs in `NltgDaemonService.ExecuteAsync` and `NLightningTestNode.StartAsync` before `PeerManager.StartAsync` and the chain monitor (the database is static then; a daemon failure is logged and the node starts anyway): when the marker `open:cutover` exists it returns after that one indexed lookup; otherwise, reading the database through a scoped unit of work, it stages in one save the `OpeningBalance` events (flag `Backfilled`, `Final`) built by the pure `AccountingCutoverEvents`: `channel:{id}` (our gross local balance of every channel past its funding confirmation that is not Closed/Stale/OnchainResolving; none for a channel awaiting its funding, whose `ChannelFunded` comes later), `wallet` (every UTXO row) and `pending:{id}` per force close being resolved (its unresolved outputs that count as `OnchainResolutionExecutor` counts them) with a synthetic `ChannelForceClosed` of 0 under the live close key whose `countedVouts` make later `OutputResolved` events leave that bucket, then the marker (`cutoverAt`, height, counts). A feed that already has events without a marker gets only the marker (`skippedOpening=true`, warning). A failed cutover holds the singleton `AccountingFeedGate` (Domain, registered by `AddRepositoriesInfrastructureServices` and `AddAccountingServices`, passed to `UnitOfWork`): `AccountingEventDbRepository.Add` drops every event but the `open:` keys and `StartMemoBackfill` does not run, so the next start's cutover writes the opening balances (NL-619); a successful cutover releases it. A channel that is Failed or `OnchainResolving` when its funding confirms gets its `ChannelFunded` (and push) from `ChannelManager.ConfirmFundingAsync` through `ChannelAccountingEvents.RecordLateChannelFundedAsync`, once per funding, own save (NL-617). `StartMemoBackfill` (after the chain monitor; `StopAsync` before it stops) writes the history before the cutover as memo events (`Backfilled`, details `memo=true`) with the live writers' keys and kinds through their helpers (`PaymentAccountingEvents.InvoiceSettled` (channel id now nullable), `PaymentSucceeded`/`PaymentFailed`, `ForwardSettled`, `ChannelAccountingEvents.BuildChannelFundedAsync`/`BuildMutualClose`, the `Stage*` methods now wrap them): settled invoices (`IInvoiceDbRepository.ListSettledAsync`), completed payments, fulfilled forwards, the funding of channels with an opening balance or Closed at the cutover (never one confirmed after it, never a spliced one) and the mutual close of channels Closed at the cutover; pages of 500, one save each, a key already in the feed skipped (resumable), marker `open:memo:complete`. `RunMemoBackfillAsync` runs it inline (tests). Meter `nlightning.accounting.backfill.opening`/`.memo`. Tests: Integration `Persistence/AccountingBackfillTests` (SQLite: fresh node, channels + wallet + resolving close + awaiting funding, idempotency, the dev-node skip, memo keys and no double write, resume after a cancellation), `Persistence/AccountingStartupScaleTests` (the flat-startup proof: `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` of the startup reads on 50,000 rows; `Explicit` `Category=Long` timing on 1,000,000), `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_ACloseFromBeforeTheFeed_*` (the pending opening nets to zero through the real executor).
Force closes before the cutover (NL-624, `Accounting/Backfill/ForceCloseMemoEvents`): the memo pass also writes, for a channel Closed at the cutover with a `ChannelCloses` row, its `ChannelForceClosed` (live key `ChannelForceClosed(channelId, commitment)`; B = the channel row's gross local balance, `balanceSource=latest-local-commitment`; `pendingMsat`/`countedVouts` from the commitment's rows by `OnchainAccounting.CountsAtClose`; `FeeMsat` = our commitment broadcast row's fee when we funded, else 0 with `feeUnknown`; `OccurredAt` = the close row's `CreatedAt`, block = its `SpentAtHeight`), the resolution of every final output (`OutputResolved`/`PenaltyClaimed`/`BreachLoss` or `OutputIgnored` for a counted output given up, under the live keys; resolved by us when one of our confirmed broadcasts spends it, the row's `ResolvingTransactionId` first, with the live `OnchainAccounting.Ours` flows, else `Lost` by the peer) and the `AnchorCpfpFee` of every confirmed `AnchorCpfp` row (the chain monitor's shape); for a channel still resolving at the cutover (its close key holds the synthetic event) only the outputs resolved, and CPFP children confirmed, at or below the cutover marker's block height (never an `Ignored` row: its time is unknown). All `memo=true` (detail `memoSource=records`), so they never post; a key already in the feed is skipped (resumable). The force-close source has its own completion marker `open:memo:complete:forceclose` (`AccountingBackfillService.LaterMemoSources`, NL-682): a node whose `open:memo:complete` predates it runs only that source at its next start; only a close row created at or before the cutover (or resolving at it) is written; its `ChannelForceClosed` carries `capacitySat` and `openedAtHeight` for the channels report (`AccountingReportService` takes them, and `funder` for the initiator, when no `ChannelFunded`/opening balance gives them). Test: Integration `Persistence/AccountingBackfillTests.Given_AForceCloseBeforeTheCutover_*`.

## Accounting books: reports and exports (NL-602 A2 lane B3; `Accounting/Reports/`, `Accounting/Export/`)
`AccountingReportServiceCollectionExtensions.AddAccountingReportServices()` (TryAdd, called by `AddAccountingServices`) registers `AccountingReportService : IAccountingReports` and `AccountingExportService : IAccountingExports` (Domain `Accounting/Books/Reports/` and `Books/Export/`), singletons that read through scopes of their own and take the optional `IAccountingBooks` (lane B2) and `IAccountingEventSealer`. Every call goes through `AccountingBooksAccess.PrepareAsync`: books missing or `IsEnabled` false → `AccountingBooksDisabledException`; else `SealNowAsync` (a failure only logged) then `ProjectNowAsync`. Balance sheet: `GetBalancesAsync` for now, `SumPostingsAsync(null, at)` for a past time; assets debit-positive, liabilities/equity credit-positive, retained earnings = −(income + expense balances), so `IsBalanced` holds (no period close before A3). Income statement and fees from `SumPostingsAsync(since, until)` ([since, until)); fees list every `AccountingAccountCategories.FeeAccounts` account plus the `SweepFeeBump` events (and their reversals) of the period, informational. Channels/peers view from the feed (`IAccountingEventDbRepository.ListAsync` in pages of 500): a first pass of `ChannelFunded`/`SpliceLocked`/close events up to `until` (capacity `capacitySat` × 1000, open/close time, `isInitiator`), a second pass of the period's events: `ForwardSettled` (`incomingChannelId`/`outgoingChannelId` details: fee as routing in and out, amounts forwarded), `PaymentSucceeded` (sent or, with `selfPayment`, rebalanced out and its fee the rebalance cost; its counterparty is the payee, so never the channel's peer), `InvoiceSettled`, pushes, on-chain fees per kind, `lostMsat` of a force close, `ForwardLostOnchain`/`BreachLoss` losses, `Reversal`s by `originalKind` (fees only). Yield = routing out / capacity (the outgoing channel's policy set the fee), annualized over the time open within the period (≥ 1 h). Register = `ListEntriesAsync` paged by ledger sequence. Exports: `AccountingExportFormatter` (pure, `\n` line ends, golden files `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Export/Golden/`; `bean-check` (beancount 3, pip) accepts `books.beancount`; `hledger check --strict` 1.52 accepts `books.journal` since NL-665): hledger (`commodity 1. msat`, the form hledger requires (a decimal mark, zero decimals; NL-665), an `account` directive per role, `; key:`/`; seq:`/`; time:` comments, amounts in msat), beancount (`MSAT`, account names mapped onto `Assets|Liabilities|Equity|Income|Expenses` by the role's category, `open` at the earliest entry of the export, metadata `key`/`seq`/`time`), CSV (one row per posting, RFC 4180 quoting, a leading `= + - @` neutralized). Descriptions = kind + the event's `description` detail with control characters removed. Entries without postings are left out of the journals. `ExportAsync` pages by ledger sequence (≤ 1,000 entries); cursor 0 writes the header (beancount scans the period first for the date and accounts). The daemon never writes a file (plan §11): IPC 44 streams the pages and the client writes them. `Domain/Accounting/Services/AccountingChainVerifier.VerifyAsync` walks `GetSealedRangeAsync` pages recomputing each hash over the previous stored one (IPC 45 verify, books on or off). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Reports/` (`AccountingBooksTestKit`: in-memory books and feed), `Accounting/Export/`, Daemon `Handlers/AccountingBooksClientHandlersTests`, `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingBooksIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingBooksCommandsTests`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingChainVerifyTests` (SQLite: tampered amount, deleted row) and `Persistence/AccountingBooksIpcRoundTripTests` (the production `AccountingBooksService` over SQLite with stand-in posting rules: report and paged export over IPC 43/44).
Reconcile outstanding (NL-621, `Accounting/Books/ClearingOutstandingReader`): the reconcile reads, after the snapshot and in a scope of its own, the clearing balance held by transactions the node knows in flight (pending `BroadcastTransactions`, watched transactions not completed, the pending fundings of the snapshot's loaded channels: a funding or splice below its depth or awaiting `splice_locked`, a mutual close below its depth): per transaction the clearing postings of the books' entries of its facts, by exact key (each input's `WalletOutputSpent` only when its `spentBy` is that transaction, each output's `WalletReceived`, its `ChannelFunded`/`SpliceLocked`/`ChannelClosedMutual`/`WalletSent`/`AnchorCpfpFee`; every confirmation generation `:c{n}` and its reversal), the bytes from the broadcast row or the channel's closing transaction. That amount is the clearing line's `AccountingReconcileLine.OutstandingMsat` (Domain; `DriftMsat` = books − node − outstanding, `IsClean` ignores it), metered on `nlightning.accounting.reconcile.outstanding_msat` and never logged as a warning; only the remainder is drift (`drift_msat`, warning). A failure to read it is logged and reads as none. IPC 45 carries it as key 6 of `AccountingReconcileLineIpcResponse` (0 from an older daemon); the client prints `clean, N msat outstanding (in flight)` and `outstanding N msat` on the line. Tests: `Accounting/AccountingBooksServiceTests.Given_ASpliceBelowItsLock_*` (pending row, reorged change, unexplained drift, lock), Integration `Persistence/AccountingReconcileOutstandingTests` (confirmed row with its watch, then the pending funding, then the lock), Daemon `AccountingBooksIpcHandlerTests`/`AccountingBooksCommandsTests` (`*Outstanding*`).

Reconcile settles in flight (NL-886, `Accounting/Books/HtlcOutstandingReader`): a settle is booked before the commitment dance folds its HTLC into the gross balances (`InvoiceSettled` in our fulfill's save, `PaymentSucceeded`/`ForwardSettled` while the switch handles the peer's fulfill), so the channels line's `OutstandingMsat` is, over the counted channels' HTLCs that are not final (`ChannelBalanceBucket.Htlcs`, `InFlightHtlcBucket`, read by `NodeSnapshotSource` from the same commitment state as the balances), +amount for each incoming HTLC whose `ForwardSettled` (its channel, its id) or `InvoiceSettled` (its hash) is booked and −amount for each outgoing one whose settle is booked by its stored origin (`Forwarded` → the incoming HTLC's `ForwardSettled`, `Local` or none → `PaymentSucceeded`); an HTLC being failed is never counted, and no marker on the HTLC is required. `ReconcileCoreAsync` takes the snapshot first, then seals and projects, then reads the books under the round gate: a settle's event commits before its HTLC can be final, so an HTLC folded in the snapshot has its event booked, and an event committed after the snapshot is matched to the HTLC as snapshotted. Only the remainder is drift (logged, metered). Tests: Integration `Persistence/AccountingReconcileHtlcSettleTests` (invoice, both race orders, split payment, forward per channel, real drift, failing HTLC).
Splice broadcasts (NL-626): `SpliceService` saves a splice (and each RBF attempt) as `BroadcastPurpose.Splice = 11`; rows saved before stay `Funding`. Every rule keyed off `Funding` treats both alike: `BroadcastRefusalRules.MayAbandon`/`IsFunding` (abandonment NL-294, lock release, NL-461 forgetting only a `V1FundingSigned` channel), `OnchainChannelWatcher.DiscardConflictingSplicesAsync`; the rebroadcast, `pendingsweeps` and the backfill never filter on it, and the wallet events' `purpose` detail now says `Splice`. Tests: `SpliceEngineHarnessTests` (both sides' rows), `OnchainSpliceWatcherTests.Given_OurCommitmentOnTheCurrentFunding_*` (both purposes), `BroadcastRefusalRulesTests`, Integration `ChainMonitorAccountingTests.Given_OurSplice_*`.

## Accounting financial profile: schema in place (NL-602 A3-T0)
Migration `AddAccountingFinancial` holds every A3 table and column; the A3 lanes add no migration. The A2 services are unchanged: every `IAccountingBooksDbRepository` member without a book argument (and `AccountingEntryQuery` without `Book`) is the operational book, so reports, exports, reconcile and the operational rebuild never see financial rows, and `ClearAsync()` clears only the operational book. The financial projector (A3-T4) writes `AccountingEntry { Book = Financial }` with the operational entry's `LedgerSeq` (adjustments with `Adjustment` > 0, D-A8), named lines (`AccountingPosting.AccountName`, required there), its cursor through `SetCursorAsync(AccountingBook.Financial, seq)`, and lots/reliefs through `IUnitOfWork.AccountingLotDbRepository`, all in one save. Prices (T2) go through `IUnitOfWork.AccountingPriceDbRepository` and `SetPostingValueAsync`/`ListUnvaluedPostingsAsync`; rules and overrides (T3) through `AccountingRuleDbRepository`/`AccountingOverrideDbRepository`; periods (T5) through `AccountingPeriodDbRepository` plus `MarkEntriesClosedAsync` and `AccountingLotDbRepository.MarkClosedAsync`; labels and tags (T1) are `Label`/`Tags` on `InvoiceModel`, `PaymentModel`, `OfferModel`, `ChannelModel` and `BroadcastTransactionModel`, persisted by their repositories' add. Contracts: Domain CLAUDE.md "Accounting financial books contracts"; tables: Persistence CLAUDE.md.

Live-review fixes (2026-10-02; NL-622, NL-623, NL-625, NL-627): the channels view's first pass also reads `OpeningBalance` events, so a channel the feed only knows from the cutover takes its scid (event or `scid` detail), peer, `capacitySat` and `isInitiator` from its opening balance when no `ChannelFunded`/`SpliceLocked` gives them (a later one replaces the capacity; a splice never sets the initiator). A memo or backfilled `ChannelFunded` (flag `Backfilled` or `memo=true`) and every opening balance only set `AccountingChannelLine.TrackedSince` (the earliest cutover time) and `OpenedAtBlockHeight` (the funding scid's block, else the event's block), never `OpenedAt`; `OpenedAt` is a live `ChannelFunded`'s time or, through the optional Domain `IBlockTimeSource` (Infrastructure.Bitcoin `ChainBlockTimeSource`, passed by the registration with `GetService`), the funding block's header time; a failing source is logged at Debug and the channel shows as open since the feed began. `WasOpenDuring` falls back to `TrackedSince`; with `OpenedAt` unknown a yield is annualized only over a period whose `since` is at or after `TrackedSince`. `YieldOnCapacity`/`AnnualizedYield` stay the routing yield before costs; `NetYieldOnCapacity`/`NetAnnualizedYield` are `NetMsat` over the capacity (negative for a loss), over the same window (IPC keys 32-35 of `AccountingChannelIpcResponse`). A balance sheet at a past time carries `LastLedgerSeqAt`, the highest ledger sequence of the entries it counts (`OccurredAt < at`, paged through `ListEntriesAsync`; 0 when none; IPC key 8 of `AccountingBalanceSheetIpcResponse`), and the client prints it next to the books' tip. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Reports/AccountingReportReviewFixTests` (`AccountingBooksTestKit.AddBuilt` takes a fully built event).

## Accounting financial classification (NL-602 A3-T3; `Accounting/Financial/`)
`AccountingOptions.Profile` (`Accounting:Profile`, `Operational` default / `Financial`) and `FinancialAccountNames` (`GetFinancialChart()`, chart in Domain CLAUDE.md "Accounting financial classification"). `AccountingClassificationService` (singleton, as itself and `IAccountingClassificationAdmin`; `AddAccountingClassificationServices()`, called by `AddAccountingServices`) serves IPC 45 `classify` (`AccountingAdminClientHandler` delegates `AccountingAdminAction.Classify` to it): `rule add` (validated by `AccountingRuleValidator`, created-at from `TimeProvider`, adds serialized so the highest id after the save is the new rule), `rule list` (warns about stored rules that can never match), `rule remove|enable|disable`, `rule test <key>` (the operational entry + its event by ledger sequence, classified with the enabled rules and the override; a candidate rule from the request is matched alone, its target may be empty), `set <key> <account>` (the event must exist; refused when its projected entry has no classifiable line or the target is an asset), `unset`, `list` (overrides by key, `--skip`) and `list --unclassified` (pages of the projected operational entries after `--after`, each classified **now** with the same engine, at most `MaxScanPerCall` 5,000 entries per call; `NextAfter`/`HasMore`). Test and the unclassified listing seal and project first (`AccountingBooksAccess`) and need the books on; rules and overrides are managed whatever the profile (a warning says the financial book is not kept under `Operational`). **For A3-T4:** call `CreateEngineAsync(unitOfWork)` once per batch (rules change without a restart), `GetManyAsync` the batch's overrides, `Classify` + `MapPostings` each entry, set `AccountingEntry.Classification`/`RuleId` and `AccountingEntryFlags.Unclassified` from the answer. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Financial/AccountingClassificationServiceTests` (SQLite, real sealer and operational books), Daemon `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingClassifyIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingClassifyCommandsTests`. **Closed periods (NL-660, D-A8):** `set`/`unset` and every rule add/remove/enable/disable, in the same save and under the sink's `EnterAsync`, stage through `IAccountingAdjustmentSink.StageAdjustmentAsync` (reason `Override`/`RuleChange`, dedupe key `reclass:{n}`, dated now) one adjustment per closed financial entry (its adjustment-0 entry, or its `LateFact` adjustment) that the new classification puts elsewhere: the Domain `AccountingReclassification.PlanMove` moves the classifiable lines (the base entry's plus its `Price` adjustments') from where the book holds them now (after earlier `reclass:` adjustments) to the new accounts, msat and fiat at their original values, so a change back is the exact undo and a repeat posts nothing; no lot moves (lot kinds follow the role, NL-674).
