A rule change classifies with the rules as they will be after the save (an added rule has no id yet: its adjustments carry `RuleId` null) and scans every closed entry with a classifiable line (one read per entry). An open late fact is projected again like an open entry (the cursor goes to its sequence). A base line that was unvalued at a forced close moves with no fiat, and the back-valuation then values the move at its own time (NL-681). Tests: `Accounting/Financial/FinancialReclassificationTests`, Domain `Accounting/Financial/AccountingReclassificationTests`.

## Accounting period close, lock and signed digests (NL-602 A3-T5; `Accounting/Financial/AccountingPeriodService.cs`)
`AddAccountingServices` registers `AccountingPeriodService` (singleton, as itself, the Domain `IAccountingPeriods` and the lock's `IAccountingAdjustmentSink`; the sink with `Replace`, so it wins over A3-T2's `NullAccountingAdjustmentSink` default in whatever order the two registrations end up) and, with TryAdd, the off `NullFinancialBooksProjector` as `IFinancialBooksProjector`: **A3-T4 must register its projector before `AddAccountingServices` runs or with `Replace`**, else closes and `rebuild --book financial` are refused ("financial book is off"). **Close** (`CloseAsync(period, force)`, IPC 45 action 20): `AccountingPeriodRange` parses `YYYY-MM` or `YYYY-MM-DD..YYYY-MM-DD` (last day included, UTC); refused before the period's end, with the books or the financial projector off, or without an `ILightningSigner`; seals and projects the operational book (`IAccountingBooks.ProjectNowAsync`) and the financial one (`IFinancialBooksProjector.ProjectAsync`) first, then inside the projector's `RunExclusiveAsync` and the sink's write lock (`EnterAsync`): closes are **contiguous** (the first may start anywhere no financial entry precedes; each next starts at the last close's `End`), a period closes once; refused unless `--force` while the period has unvalued postings (`CountOpenUnvaluedPostingsAsync`), unclassified entries (flag `Unclassified`) or a book lags (operational cursor < sealed tip, financial cursor < operational); one save writes the `AccountingPeriods` row (`LastLedgerSeq` = financial cursor, `ChainHash` = the feed's stored hash there, `Digest`, `Signature` = `SignNodeMessage(digest)`, `Forced` when a refusal was overridden, `ClosingState`) and marks the period's financial entries (`MarkEntriesClosedAsync`) and the lots/reliefs before its end (`MarkClosedAsync`). The digest (`Domain/Accounting/Financial/AccountingCloseDigest`, D-A13): SHA-256 over the tag `nltg/accounting/period-close/v1`, the header (period, bounds, last seq, chain hash, previous close's digest (chained), node id, forced, closed at), each entry's canonical hash (ledger/adjustment order), each relief of the period, each lot open at the end with its remaining amount then (current remaining + reliefs since the end, `SumReliefsSinceAsync`), the counts and the closing state's text; it leaves out what a later legitimate write changes (an entry's `ClosedPeriodId`, a lot's current `RemainingMsat`, `Account`, `ClosedPeriodId`). `AccountingClosingState` (JSON, canonical) = the financial balances over every closed period (previous close's + the period's postings) and `ReplayAfterLedgerSeq` (the first open entry's sequence − 1, at most the cursor). **Lock** (`IAccountingAdjustmentSink`): every time before the last close's end is locked (`GetLockingPeriodAsync`, cached until the next close/rebuild); a writer holds `EnterAsync` from its check to its save and hands a correction to `StageAdjustmentAsync(unitOfWork, AccountingAdjustment)`, which stages it in the writer's unit of work as a financial entry with the fact's ledger sequence, the next adjustment number (≥ 1), dated now (or the last close's end if the clock is behind), flag `Adjustment`, note `[dedupe] adjusts <period>: <Reason> of <fact time>`; a `LateFact` is skipped when the event key already has a financial entry (the replay of a rebuild), other reasons when an adjustment with the same `DedupeKey` exists; an open-period time is an `ArgumentException`. Contract for the writers: T4's projector posts an operational entry dated in a locked period as a `LateFact` (lots and reliefs dated as the adjustment, `SourceAdjustment`/`Adjustment` = its number); T2's back-valuation never values a closed posting (`SetPostingValueAsync` also returns false for one) and hands it to `AdjustLateValuationAsync(unitOfWork, AccountingLateValuation)` (T2's member of the sink: a `Price` adjustment, one zero-msat line on the posting's account with the fiat value and price id, `DedupeKey` `price:{seq}:{adj}:{index}`; true when staged or already there, false for a posting that is not closed); T3's `classify set` and rule changes post `Override`/`RuleChange`; a lot acquired before the last close's end is never changed again (verify reports it). **Verify** (`VerifyClosesAsync`, added to IPC 45 verify as `PeriodVerifications`): per closed period, the digest recomputed from the stored rows, the signature against our node id, the chain hash against the feed's stored hash at `LastLedgerSeq`, the closing balances against the previous close's plus the period's entries, contiguity, and no financial entry dated in the period outside its close (`StrayEntryCount`: a write past the lock). The closes cache is invalidated under the write lock right after a close or reset commits, and a cache load that raced one is dropped (version counter), so a writer's check under the lock always sees the last close. **Rebuild** (`RebuildFinancialAsync`, `rebuild --book financial`): under the projector's exclusive section and the write lock, `ResetToCloseAsync(Financial, new AccountingBookReset(state.ReplayAfterLedgerSeq, state.Balances))` (one DB transaction: deletes the open adjustment-0 entries and their postings, gives the deleted reliefs' msat back to the lots that stay, deletes the reliefs of the open entries and the lots they opened (imported lots, closed lots and adjustment lots stay), balances = closing + kept adjustments, cursor = the replay point; before any close: cursor 0, no balances), then `IFinancialBooksProjector.ProjectAsync` replays the open period (the closed facts it meets are skipped by the sink). Adjustments survive a rebuild (they are the audit trail, dated when they were made). Meter `nlightning.accounting.periods.closed`, `nlightning.accounting.adjustments`. Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Financial/` (`FinancialCloseTestKit`: SQLite file, real sealer, operational books with stub rules, real `LocalLightningSigner`, a small `TestFinancialProjector` that keeps the contract; `AccountingPeriodServiceTests`: close and signature, list/show, refusals and `--force`, late fact / price / override / rule change as adjustments, the lock's times, tampering with an entry, a lot, a relief, the digest, the signature, the closing state or the feed's hash found by verify, a chained second close, rebuild after a close and before any close equal to the incremental book), Domain `Accounting/Financial/AccountingCloseDigestTests`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingPeriodPersistenceTests` (the repository members on SQLite), Daemon `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingCloseIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingCloseCommandsTests`.

## Accounting financial reports and exports (NL-602 A3-T6; `Accounting/Reports/Financial/`, `Accounting/Export/Financial/`)
`AddAccountingReportServices` also registers `AccountingFinancialReportService : IAccountingFinancialReports` and `AccountingFinancialExportService : IAccountingFinancialExports` (Domain `Accounting/Financial/Reports/`, `Financial/Export/`; singletons, scopes of their own). `AccountingFinancialAccess.PrepareAsync`: books off → `AccountingBooksDisabledException`; the optional Domain `IFinancialBooksProjector` (one seam with A3-T5's close since the A3 integration: the projector A3-T4 implements, `IsEnabled` = `Profile=Financial`, `ProjectAsync`) off → `AccountingFinancialBooksDisabledException` before anything runs; else seal, project the operational book, then the financial one. `AddAccountingServices` registers the off `NullFinancialBooksProjector` until A3-T4 replaces it, so the daemon refuses the financial reports and exports ("financial book is off") before T4; only a host without any projector (tests) reads the financial book as stored. Reports: balance sheet and income statement from the new book-aware `IAccountingBooksDbRepository.SumAccountPostingsAsync(book, since, until, currency)` (msat grouped in SQL, fiat summed in memory: decimals are TEXT on SQLite), merged per financial account name (several roles may post to one), category from the name's root (`AccountingFiat.CategoryOf`, else the role's); assets/expenses debit-positive, the rest credit-positive; `UnvaluedPostings` per line (an msat posting without a value in the currency) and `IsFiatBalanced` (false while a book entry's lines do not sum to zero in fiat); `--price` adds the assets' market value. Realized gains from `IAccountingLotDbRepository.ListReliefsAsync(since, until, afterId, take)` (new, paged by id) and the lots (`GetLotAsync`, cached): per calendar month/quarter/year (UTC) or total, cost basis, proceeds, gain, short (held at most 365 days) and long term, pending valuation (a relief without cost or proceeds, or a lot in another currency: never a zero gain), the msat of estimated-basis lots. Lots: every open lot (`ListOpenLotsAsync`) by id, paged by lot id, remaining cost basis = cost × remaining / original (8 places), market value at the given price or (`UnrealizedGains`, `requirePrice`) the latest stored price at or before now (`AccountingFiat.StoredPriceLookback`, ten years: the report shows its time; none stored → `InvalidOperationException` "give one with --price"); totals over all open lots. Register and unclassified: `ListEntriesAsync` with `Book = Financial`, paged by (sequence, adjustment) (`NextAfterAdjustment`); unclassified = `WithFlags = Unclassified`, leaving out a closed entry that a reclassification adjustment moved out of every unclassified account (`AccountingReclassification.IsStillUnclassified`, NL-667; a page may then hold fewer entries than asked while `HasMore`). Unvalued: `ListUnvaluedPostingsAsync(Financial, take + 1)` (the oldest, no cursor). Risk-weighted capital: `INodeSnapshotSource` (books on or off) through the pure Domain `AccountingRiskCapital.Build` and `AccountingRiskWeights` (defaults: wallet 1 / unconfirmed 0.9, settled channel balance 1, our in-flight HTLCs 0.5, incoming with preimage 0.99, pending on chain 0.95, HTLC outputs 0.5; the peer's balance, incoming HTLCs without preimage and fulfilled outgoing ones count 0); `NodeSnapshotSource` now fills `ChannelBalanceBucket.LocalInFlightFulfilledMsat` and `RemoteInFlightPreimageMsat` (an HTLC's `KnownPreimage` or a fulfill `Removal`). Exports: `AccountingFinancialExportFormatter` (internal, pure, reusable: `WriteDocument(format, currency, entries, eventsBySeq, prices)` writes a whole export in one call; A3-T4's golden files and `JournalBalanceChecker` go through it): hledger (`commodity 1. msat`, `commodity 1.00 USD`, an `account` per account used, `P <date time> msat <price per msat> USD` for every stored price the valued lines used, `N msat @@ |fiat| USD` per valued line, `F USD` for a fiat-only line such as a realized gain), beancount (asset accounts opened with booking `"NONE"`, `N MSAT {{|fiat| USD}}` on asset lines and `@@` on the others, `price MSAT ...` directives, `!` for unvalued or unclassified entries), CSV (one row per posting with fiat amount, currency, price id, price per BTC, flags, classification, rule, closed period). An entry with a line not valued in the export's currency (or a value of the other sign) is written in msat only with its fiat in comments; a valued entry whose fiat does not sum to zero gets a residual line to `equity:fiat-rounding` (the journals balance; CSV keeps the book's rows). A3-T4 contract: a financial entry should balance in fiat (a gain/loss line with 0 msat carries the difference). Pages by (sequence, adjustment); the first page scans the period for the header (accounts, first date, price ids). Proofs: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Financial/` (`FinancialBooksFixture`: a hand-computed book on SQLite through the production repositories; report tests against the running balances; golden files `Golden/financial.{journal,beancount,csv}`, checked by `hledger check --strict` 1.52 and `bean-check` 3.2 by hand and by the `Explicit` `Given_TheRealTools_*` (`NLTG_HLEDGER`, `NLTG_BEAN_CHECK`); `JournalBalanceChecker`, the in-CI stand-in, balances each transaction per commodity at its `@@`/`{{ }}` costs and compares the account totals with the books), Daemon `AccountingFinancialIpcHandlerTests`/`AccountingFinancialCommandsTests`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingFinancialIpcRoundTripTests`.

## Adding an inbound channel message handler (the common task)
1. The message, payload and serializer must already exist. Check `src/NLightning.Domain/Protocol/Messages`, `src/NLightning.Infrastructure.Serialization/{Messages,Payloads,Tlv,Factories}` and `src/NLightning.Domain/Protocol/Constants/MessageTypes.cs`.
2. Create `Channels/Handlers/<Msg>MessageHandler.cs : IChannelMessageHandler<<Msg>Message>`. DI registers it automatically; don't add it by hand.
3. Add a `case MessageTypes.<X>:` to `ChannelManager.DispatchChannelMessageAsync`. It should call `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync` for a real channel id, then `GetChannelMessageHandler<T>(scope).HandleAsync(Cast<T>(message), ...)`. **If you skip this, the message falls into `default`, which throws a channel-scoped ChannelWarningException ("not supported yet"): the message is ignored, the peer gets a `warning` and stays connected.** That is the interim behavior for the dual-funding messages; we never fail the channel for a missing handler, since LND would force-close it. A message for a channel we don't know at all (memory, temporary channels of the peer, DB) gets an `error` for that unknown channel_id (BOLT 1 SHOULD; `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync`).
4. At the top of the handler, check the state and throw ChannelErrorException or ChannelWarningException (`NLightning.Domain.Exceptions`) with `(message, channelId?, peerMessage?)`. The peer receives `PeerMessage` when it is non-empty; otherwise the internal `message` is sent (`src/NLightning.Infrastructure/Node/Services/PeerCommunicationService.cs`), so always pass a `peerMessage` if the internal text shouldn't leak.
5. Persist through a scoped `IUnitOfWork` (`ChannelDbRepository` then `SaveChangesAsync`) and update `IChannelMemoryRepository`.
6. Build outbound messages only through `IMessageFactory`. Return the replies to the requester as a list, in wire order. To send to a peer other than the requester (e.g. when forwarding), raise an event that ChannelManager converts into `OnResponseMessageReady` (see `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler`), synchronously while the channel's lock is held, so wire order equals persist order. `ChannelManager` already holds the lock of the message's channel id around your handler: don't acquire it again (it is not reentrant), and never take a second channel's lock from inside a handler.
7. Add tests in `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/Handlers/` (xunit.v3 + Moq). Existing handler tests are named `HandleAsync_Condition_Outcome`; PeerManager tests use `Given_X_When_Y_Then_Z`.

## Conventions
- Use a file-scoped namespace. Put System and Microsoft usings above it, and relative `using Domain.X;` usings after it.
- Store injected dependencies in `_camelCase` readonly fields. Guard hot-path logs with `if (_logger.IsEnabled(...))`.
- Lifetimes: managers are singletons. They create an `IServiceScope` per message or event. Handlers and `IUnitOfWork` are Scoped. Never inject IUnitOfWork into a singleton. `IChannelMemoryRepository` and `IUtxoMemoryRepository` are singletons.
- `dotnet format --verify-no-changes --exclude "**/BlazorTests/**"` is a CI gate. `.editorconfig` treats IDE0005/0051/0060/0044 and the naming rules as errors.

## Dependency rules
- The intended direction is Domain only. In practice the csproj also references `NLightning.Infrastructure` and `NLightning.Infrastructure.Bitcoin`, because of IBlockchainMonitor and IBitcoinWalletService (`Infrastructure.Bitcoin/Wallet/Interfaces`), the funding/commitment tx builders (`Infrastructure.Bitcoin/Builders/Interfaces`), and ITcpService, NewPeerConnectedEventArgs and PeerAddress (`Infrastructure/Transport`, `Infrastructure/Protocol/Models`). Don't add more.
- `NLightning.Bolt11` is referenced (since ABCD W1-B) for invoice encode/decode. Keep its NBitcoin types (`uint256`, `PubKey`) inside `Payments/Invoices` and `Payments/Routing/PaymentTarget.cs` (`PaymentTarget.ToWireBytes`/`FromWireBytes` convert a Bolt11 `uint256` to wire bytes through its hex, which is the LND/wire order).
- MUST NOT reference: Infrastructure.Serialization, Infrastructure.Persistence(.*), Infrastructure.Repositories, Daemon, Client, Transport.Ipc. Depend on Domain interfaces instead, such as `IUnitOfWork`, `IMessageSerializer` and `IHopPayloadSerializer`.
- New abstractions belong in Domain as interfaces (as `ISphinxService`/`IOnionReplayStore` already do). Their implementations go in Infrastructure(.Bitcoin).

## Tests
- `dotnet test test/NLightning.Application.Tests` runs all the tests (CI included, since NL-167 added xunit.runner.visualstudio). The xunit v3 runner also works:
  `dotnet run --project test/NLightning.Application.Tests -f net10.0` (`-f` is required once SDK 11 is installed, because the tests then also target net11.0), or
  `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/NLightning.Application.Tests -class NLightning.Application.Tests.Node.Managers.PeerManagerTests`
- Existing coverage: OpenChannel1, AcceptChannel1 (upfront_shutdown_script, error cleanup, funding outpoint flow), FundingCreated, FundingSigned (rebuilt funding tx guard), FundingConfirmed, ChannelReady (first snapshot), ChannelManager (block events), ChannelManagerConcurrency (lock serialization, reply order, confirmation vs. message), ChannelManagerNormalOperation (dispatch, switch outside the lock, failed-channel persistence, funding_created double lock, startup revert), ChannelLockProvider, PeerOutbox and PeerManager (inbound order, outbox FIFO, error after queued replies). The N6-T1 handlers are tested with `Channels/Handlers/NormalOperationTestContext` (the pure engine with fake crypto ports `FakeCommitmentPorts`, mocked persistence that records the call order, mocked signer and shachain, real `MessageFactory`): `UpdateAddHtlc`, `CommitmentSigned` (incl. `Given_PersistFails_When_CommitmentSigned_Then_NoRevokeSentAndNoSecretReleased`), `RevokeAndAck`, `HtlcRemovalAndFee` handler tests. `SentCommitDiffCodecTests`. `Channels/Reestablish/` (N7): `ReestablishPlannerTests` (named §6.9 cases plus an exhaustive table against a spec-literal oracle), `ChannelReestablishMessageHandlerTests`, `ChannelManagerReconnectTests`, `StartupStateTests` (N7-T5, every stored state), `FunderRememberRuleTests` (N7-T6: channel, watches and the funding tx in one save before the publish, nothing stored on a bad signature, a refused publish keeps V1FundingSigned with the tx stored for rebroadcast; the rebroadcast itself is proven on SQLite in `Integration.Tests/Persistence/ChainMonitorPersistenceTests`), `ReestablishTrackerTests` and the I11 proof `ReestablishHarnessTests` (link drop at every message boundary, twice in a row, a crash of either node at every save with a restart from what was saved, an old-backup restore detecting data loss, an unacked add re-sent with the same id, a channel_ready lost with the link retransmitted by the reestablish for `ReadyForUs`/`ReadyForUs` and `ReadyForUs`/`Open`). The harness has `DisconnectAsync`/`ReconnectAsync` (what `PeerManager` tells the channel managers), `InMemoryChannelStateStore.CrashAtSave` (that save throws `SimulatedCrashException`, and every later one until the restart) with `PumpAsync` restarting a crashed node from its store (`RecoverAsync`), `DeliveryBudget`, `RestartFromBackupAsync`, channel states per side (`aliceState`/`bobState`: a state before Open registers the channel without a snapshot, `RegisterPending`), `Lost` (messages in flight when the link dropped), a real `IMessageSerializer`, and the production `ReestablishTracker`/`ReestablishGatedLivenessProbe`. `Channels/Harness/TwoNodeHarness` (N6-T4) joins two real `ChannelManager`s (production handlers, engine ports, `LocalLightningSigner`, builders, shachain; in-memory persistence committed per save; outboxes fed by `OnResponseMessageReady`, delivered alternately so messages cross) and records the txid of every commitment signed and verified; each node sends through the production `ChannelOperationsService` + `CommitScheduler` (no debounce; `PumpAsync` waits for the schedulers), with a recording switch that can wrap the real `LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch` (fake Sphinx peel and fake error onion, since this project has no failure-message serializer) and a `PeerAlive` flag: the probe answers it for channels marked up at `Open`, and while it is false every message the node raises is dropped into `Dropped` (as `PeerManager` drops them). `TwoNodeHarnessTests`: 30 HTLCs each way, fulfills/fails, a fee update, with and without anchors, I7 after every phase; crossing adds; local-only fail-back ending at commitment numbers 2/2 with the onion secret stored and the settled row pruned; offer, fail and fee all refused with nothing persisted or dropped while the peer is away, then the fail goes through once it is back. Send side: `Channels/Services/ChannelOperationsServiceTests` (persist-then-publish-then-schedule order, persist failure sends nothing, refusals: disabled, failed channel, peer away for offers, removals and fees, wrong preimage, default origin), `CommitSchedulerTests` (diff persisted before send, D7, liveness before and under the lock, debounce, save failure), `ConnectedPeerLivenessProbeTests` (link pinned per connection, down after reconnect until re-marked), `Channels/Switch/LocalOnlyHtlcSwitchTests`; startup replay and link marking (channel_ready opening marks, an Open channel's message or a reloaded channel does not) in `ChannelManagerNormalOperationTests`.
- Build: `dotnet build NLightning.sln -p:MSBuildWarningsAsMessages=MSB4121`.

## Gotchas
- Opening messages carry the **temporary** channel id. `currentState` only looks up real channels, so it is `None` for them, and the handlers must call `TryGetTemporaryChannelState(peer, id)` themselves. funding_created still carries the temp id. Only the initiator calls `UpgradeChannel`, which fires the `OnChannelUpgraded` event the Daemon's OpenChannelClientHandler waits on.
- `ForgetStaleChannels` only forgets fundee (non-initiator) channels still awaiting funding (`V1FundingSigned`/`ReadyForThem`) whose `FundingCreatedAtBlockHeight` is non-zero and at least 2016 blocks old. `FundingCreatedMessageHandler` sets that field to `IBlockchainMonitor.LastProcessedBlockHeight`; `HandleFundingConfirmationAsync` later overwrites it with the funding tx's first-seen height. Legacy rows with a zero height get it backfilled to the current block height on the next block, so their timeout starts then.
- `ConfirmUnconfirmedChannels` only re-drives `V1FundingSigned`/`ReadyForThem` channels whose watched funding tx is already `IsCompleted` (e.g. confirmed while offline). `HandleFundingConfirmationAsync` and `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler` ignore any other state, so channel_ready is sent once per confirmation. On reconnect, channel_ready is re-sent by the reestablish when both next_commitment_numbers are 1 (B2-RE-15).
- `AcceptChannel1MessageHandler` deliberately does not persist the initiator channel; `FundingSignedMessageHandler` saves it (V1FundingSigned, with its watches and the funding tx) before publishing the funding tx. On failure it removes the temporary channel by its temporary id (or the upgraded channel) and releases the locked UTXOs; failures in the validation checks before its `try` still rely on the Daemon's OpenChannelClientHandler to release them.
- Per-channel lock (NL-033): `ChannelManager` holds the lock for the message's channel id (the temporary id during the open flow, the real id after) around dispatch and the raising of the replies; block handlers (`ForgetStaleChannels`, the backfill, `HandleFundingConfirmationAsync` → `ConfirmFundingAsync`) take it per channel and re-check the channel's state under it. A channel is keyed by its temporary id until funding_created/funding_signed and by its real id afterwards; the per-peer inbound loop already serializes the open flow. The IPC open-channel operation takes it through `StartOpeningChannelAsync` (store the temporary channel + raise open_channel).
- scid aliases (NL-103): `FundingConfirmedMessageHandler` generates `LocalAliases` that avoid every real scid and alias in `IChannelMemoryRepository`, and reuses them if the channel already has some. `LocalAliases` is never persisted, so after a restart old aliases are forgotten: they are no longer recognized for incoming HTLCs and new ones are generated.
- Handler discovery uses reflection (`Assembly.GetTypes()`), which trimming or AOT may break.

## Payments (ABCD W1-B: ONION M4-T2/T3/T4/T6, invoices)
Registered by `Payments/PaymentsServiceCollectionExtensions.AddPaymentsServices()` (all singletons; called from `AddApplicationServices`). It needs `ISphinxService` (`AddBitcoinInfrastructure`), `IHopPayloadSerializer` (`AddSerializationInfrastructureServices`), `IOnionReplayStore` (`AddInfrastructureServices`: the persistent `PersistentOnionReplayStore`, which needs the Scoped `IUnitOfWork`; tests register `InMemoryOnionReplayStore`), `ISecureKeyManager` and `IOptions<NodeOptions>` (host), and a **Scoped `IInvoiceDbRepository` that shares the scope's DbContext with `IUnitOfWork`** (`AddRepositoriesInfrastructureServices` registers it, and `IPaymentDbRepository`/`IForwardCircuitDbRepository`, as the scope's `IUnitOfWork` properties).
- `Onion/IncomingOnionProcessor.ProcessAsync(onionRoutingPacket, paymentHash, replayOwner, updateAddPathKey = null)` (M4-T2): peel with the node key → replay set `IOnionReplayStore.TryAddAsync` (only after a successful peel; NL-078: the HMAC is persisted before the call returns, owned by `replayOwner` = `OnionReplayOwner(channelId, htlcId, cltvExpiry)`, so the same HTLC processed again after a restart or link-up is not a replay, another HTLC is; kept until the chain passes the owner's `cltv_expiry`; `replayOwner` is required (nullable, no default): null means no replay check and nothing recorded, so there are no ownerless rows that would never expire) → `IHopPayloadSerializer.DeserializeAsync` → `HopPayloadValidator`. Returns one of `IncomingOnionForward` (scid, `amt_to_forward`, `outgoing_cltv_value`, `NextPacket`), `IncomingOnionFinal` (payload), `IncomingOnionMalformed` (BADONION code + `sha256_of_onion` → `update_fail_malformed_htlc`) or `IncomingOnionFailed` (`FailureMessage` + shared secret → `update_fail_htlc` with `IFailureOnionService.CreateErrorPacket`). Never throws for a bad onion (only `ArgumentException` for a packet that is not 1366 bytes). Route blinding (M5, lane rf1-m5): blinded payloads are read only when `NodeOptions.Features.OptionRouteBlinding` is not `No` and an `IRouteBlindingService` is registered (`AddBitcoinInfrastructure` → `AddRouteBlindingServices`; `ReadsBlindedPayloads`); otherwise refused as before (an `update_add_htlc` `path_key` → malformed `invalid_onion_blinding`; a payload with `current_path_key` → `update_fail_htlc` `invalid_onion_blinding`, BOLT 2). When read, `ProcessAsync(..., incomingAmount, incomingCltvExpiry)` decrypts `encrypted_recipient_data` with the update_add path_key (reusing `PeeledOnion.PathKeySharedSecret`) or, at the introduction node, the payload's `current_path_key` (curve-checked, NL-077), checks it with `BlindedRecipientDataValidator` and returns `IncomingOnionForward`/`IncomingOnionFinal` with an `IncomingBlindedHop` (`IsIntroduction`, `RecipientData`, `NextPathKey`, and for a forward the `payment_relay` amount/expiry; null when the amounts were not passed, i.e. secret recovery). Any blinded failure: malformed `invalid_onion_blinding` with a path_key, else `update_fail_htlc` `invalid_onion_blinding`. `HtlcSwitch` forwards by the recipient's scid or `next_node_id` (any open channel to that peer), checks `payment_relay` against our fee with `ForwardingRequest.BlindedRelay` (the CLTV check is unchanged), offers with `BlindedPathTlv(NextPathKey)`, and fails every HTLC that came with a path_key with `update_fail_malformed_htlc` + `invalid_onion_blinding` and every introduction-node forward with our own `invalid_onion_blinding` (`SendFailureAsync`, `FailBlindedUpstreamAsync`; downstream errors are never relayed; the introduction role is re-derived by a re-peel, `IsBlindedIntroductionForwardAsync` → `Switch/BlindedHtlcFailures.IsIntroductionForwardAsync`, read from the onion whatever `OptionRouteBlinding` is set to now, so a restart with the feature off never relays a plain error for an earlier blinded forward; final-hop and processor-failure callers pass `blindedIntroduction: false` and skip the re-peel). An introduction node's `invalid_onion_blinding` in `update_fail_htlc` waits a random 0..`HtlcSwitchOptions.BlindedErrorMaxDelay` (`Node:Switch:BlindedErrorMaxDelay`, 250 ms, zero turns it off; real time, never the test clock; BOLT 2/4 SHOULD). A forward by `next_node_id` takes the first usable channel to that peer that can send the amount, else the usable one that can send the most (`HtlcSwitch.SelectOutgoingCandidate`). The failure paths outside the switch apply the same rules through `Switch/BlindedHtlcFailures` (`TryFailMalformedAsync` for an HTLC with a path_key, `FailAsync` with the introduction role): `DustExposureHtlcSwitch` and `HtlcExpiryMonitor.FailBackAsync` (M5 review). The blinded final hop is checked by `FinalHopProcessor` with `total_amount_msat` and our `path_id` (`Domain/Protocol/Onion/Models/BlindedPathId` = HMAC(preimage, "nltg_blinded_path_id")) instead of `payment_data`. Proof: `Payments/Switch/BlindedThreeNodeSwitchTests`. Sender side (M5 step 2): `IPaymentService.PayBlindedAsync(PayBlindedRequest(hash, amount, paths, invoice?), PayInvoiceOptions)` (`Domain/Protocol/Onion/Models/BlindedPaymentPath` = `BlindedPath` + `BlindedPayInfo`) runs the usual session with one part: each round `PaymentService.TryPlanBlinded` takes the cheapest path that is usable (`Routing/BlindedRouteComposer.CheckUsable`: HTLC limits at the introduction node, no unknown even feature bit, not excluded) and whose fee (`BlindedPayInfo.ComputeFeeMsat`, BOLT 12 rounding down) fits the fee limit, plans a route to its introduction node as payee (amount + fee, final delta = the path's CLTV delta), and `BlindedRouteComposer.Compose` replaces that last hop with the blinded hops (`RouteHop.EncryptedRecipientData`/`CurrentPathKey`/`IsBlindedRelay`; the introduction node by its real id; `PaymentRoute.BlindedStartIndex`/`BlindedPathIndex`/`PublicEdgeCount`); `PaymentOnionFactory` writes the BOLT 4 blinded payloads (only the final one has `amt_to_forward`, `outgoing_cltv_value`, `total_amount_msat`; no `payment_data`). A failure from inside a path (an erring index >= `BlindedStartIndex` that is not the final node, or `invalid_onion_blinding` from it) puts it in `RouteConstraints.ExcludedBlindedPaths` and retries with another; a recipient that is its own introduction node (one-hop path) returns normal errors, which go through the final-node rules (a permanent one stops the payment); `MissionControl` learns only the edges before the path. Since wave B12 (lane B12-E): `PayBlindedRequest.PayeeNodeId` is the stored payee (the stored route's last hop names it; without it the first path's last blinded id), `PayBlindedRequest.Bolt12` is kept on every row, `AllowMpp` lets a round split one part per path (cheapest proportional fee first, each capped by the path's `htlc_maximum_msat`, parts planned in the round counted against our channels' liquidity) when no single path carries the amount, and a path whose introduction node is this node is decrypted with the node key (`Send/Blinded/SelfIntroducedBlindedPath`, B12-PAY-02: our hops' `payment_relay`/`payment_constraints` turn the path's aggregate into our HTLC to the next node over a direct channel, the next path_key goes in `update_add_htlc` through `PaymentRoute.FirstHopPathKey`, and that peer's `update_fail_malformed_htlc` excludes the path; needs `IRouteBlindingService`, an optional `PaymentService` constructor argument). BOLT 11 invoices with bLIP 39 blinded paths (field 20, NL-440): `PayInvoiceAsync` decodes them and, when the invoice has any, hands them to `PayBlindedAsync` (`PayBlindedInvoiceAsync`: the invoice's amount and hash, `AllowMpp` from its `basic_mpp`, its `c` and signing key ignored; no payee id, so the stored payee is the first path's last blinded id); proofs `Payments/Send/Bolt11BlindedInvoiceTests` (in-process: our invoice paid, split over two paths only with `basic_mpp`, introduced by the payer) and Docker `Docker/Gossip/Bolt11BlindedPathFlowTests` (LND's field 20 decoded by us and paid). Not supported: a BOLT 12 `basic_mpp` compulsory bit forcing several paths; a path whose introduction node is us and that ends at us (paying ourselves) is refused. Byte-exact proof: `Integration.Tests/BOLT4/BlindedPaymentSendVectorTests` (the `generate` section of blinded-payment-onion-test.json: aggregated pay info, every payload, the onion). Receive side: `Invoices/BlindedPathBuilder.BuildAsync(BlindedPathRequest(preimage, amount?, minFinalCltvExpiryDelta, height, lifetime = 2016, maxPaths = 3, includePrivateChannels = false))` (singleton from `AddPaymentsServices`) makes a two-hop path per usable announced channel (link up, the peer's `channel_update` held and enabled; largest peer balance first): the peer as introduction node with `short_channel_id`, its policy as `payment_relay` and `payment_constraints`, our hop with `path_id` = `BlindedPathId.Compute(preimage)`, both padded to one length; pay info from `BlindedPayInfo.Aggregate` plus our final delta (pass at least the invoice's `MinFinalCltvExpiry`: `FinalHopProcessor` still checks it). Dummy hops (NL-440, BOLT 4 MAY): each path ends with `BlindedPathRequest.DummyHops` (default `Node:Invoices:BlindedPathDummyHops`, 1, at most 4; LND's default shape) hops of our own node that relay to us (`next_node_id` = our node id, the introduction node's `payment_relay`, `payment_constraints` chained), every hop padded to one length and the pay info aggregating them; `IncomingOnionProcessor` (optional `ISecureKeyManager` argument) peels a non-final blinded hop whose `next_node_id` is us and that has no `short_channel_id` by itself (next layer with the next path_key, the relay applied to the amount and expiry, at most `MaxSelfRelayHops` 20; every failure is the outer layer's blinded failure, the outer shared secret kept) and returns the last layer's result with `IncomingBlindedHop.DummyHops`/`ReceivedAmount`/`ReceivedCltvExpiry`; `HtlcSwitch.ReceiveAsync` gives `FinalHopProcessor` those received values (tests `Payments/Onion/DummyHopOnionProcessorTests`, `Payments/Send/BlindedSendThreeNodeTests`). Our onion-message paths have no dummy hops (NL-525). `InvoiceService` with `Node:Invoices:BlindedPaths` (off by default) puts these paths into our BOLT 11 invoices as bLIP 39 `b` fields instead of `r` hints and `s`, signed by an ephemeral key; no path refuses the invoice (`InvalidOperationException`); the builder and the chain height are resolved only then. Proofs: `Payments/Send/BlindedSendThreeNodeTests` (Carol's builder path paid by Alice's `PayBlindedAsync` through Bob), `Payments/Routing/BlindedRouteComposerTests`, Docker `Docker/Gossip/RouteBlindingFlowTests`. A replayed HMAC → `temporary_node_failure` (our choice; BOLT 4 only says "fail"). Pass `replayOwner: null` only when re-processing an HTLC this node already processed and acted on (secret recovery), and the incoming HTLC's owner otherwise (`HtlcSwitch` does; `ThreeNodeSwitchTests` proves a crash between the HMAC's save and the secret's save is not a replay after the restart). Keep the shared secret with the HTLC (`HtlcEntity.OnionSharedSecret`).
- `FinalHop/FinalHopProcessor` (M4-T3; `basic_mpp` since ABCD W6-B): `ProcessAsync(IInvoiceDbRepository, paymentHash, htlcAmount, htlcCltvExpiry, payload, height, acceptMultiPart = false, committedSetMember = false)` or the pure `Evaluate(InvoiceModel?, ..., acceptMultiPart = false, committedSetMember = false)`. First HTLC vs onion: `amount_msat < amt_to_forward` → 0x0013 (HTLC amount), `cltv_expiry < outgoing_cltv_value` → 0x0012 (HTLC cltv), both before the invoice lookup (as LND/CLN; they leak nothing about invoices). Then everything invoice-related → 0x400F with (HTLC amount, height): no payment_data, unknown hash, canceled/expired/already accepted or settled, secret mismatch, `total_msat != amt_to_forward` when `acceptMultiPart` is false, `total_msat` (the "amount paid", BOLT 4) below the amount or above twice it, `cltv_expiry < height + c`. An accepted result carries `PartAmount` (`amt_to_forward`), `TotalMsat` and `IsMultiPart`. `committedSetMember` (ABCD W7-B, NL-323): the HTLC's record carries the invoice's preimage (`HtlcRecord.KnownPreimage` of the incoming HTLC, persisted by the switch when it committed to the set): a **Settled** invoice is then accepted with `InvoiceAlreadySettled` (with or without `acceptMultiPart`; secret and `total_msat <= AmountReceived` still checked), and the checks that depend on the time of the replay (invoice expiry, `cltv_expiry` against the height) are skipped; for any other invoice status the flag is ignored (a mark left on a part of an `Open` invoice, by a set that became incomplete or a stop before the settle, commits to nothing and keeps every check); any other HTLC for a Settled invoice (a duplicate, a late part) is 0x400F as LND does. It never mutates the invoice. The check is a read, not a check-and-mark: the switch holds a per-payment-hash lock and settles the invoice in the first fulfill's unit of work, re-checking `Open` there.
- `Policy/HtlcForwardingPolicy : IForwardingPolicy` (M4-T4): the check order documented on `IForwardingPolicy`, over `NodeOptions.Routing` read on every call. `expiry_too_soon` when `outgoing_cltv_value <= height + ExpiryTooSoonBlocks` (inclusive, as LND); `expiry_too_far` when the incoming `cltv_expiry > height + MaxCltvExpiryDistance`; below `max(channel htlc_minimum, HtlcMinimumMsat)` → `amount_below_minimum`; above `HtlcMaximumMsat` or `AvailableToSend` → `temporary_channel_failure`.
- `Routing/` (M4-T6): `PaymentTarget.FromInvoice(Invoice)` (payee, hash, secret, amount, `c`, all `r` hints, metadata). `HintRouteBuilder.Build/TryBuild(target, amount, maxFee, height, ourNodeId, hasUsableChannelTo)`: the payee directly when it is a usable peer, else the first hint (in invoice order) whose first node is a usable peer; a hint containing our node starts after our entry. Per hop, from the payee back: final `outgoing_cltv_value` = height + `c` + `FinalCltvSafetyOffset` (3); each hint entry charges `ForwardingFee` on what it forwards and adds its `cltv_expiry_delta`. A route whose total CLTV exceeds `MaxCltvExpiryDistance`, or whose `Fee` exceeds the mandatory `maxFee` (hint fees are payee-chosen, so an unbounded invoice could charge ~4294x the amount), is skipped with its reason; the W2-C send path must pass the payer's fee limit (e.g. the RPC's `fee_limit_msat`). `PaymentRoute.Hops[0]` is our peer; `FirstHopAmount`/`FirstHopCltvExpiry` are our HTLC. `PaymentOnionFactory.CreateAsync(route)` builds the payloads (intermediate: amt, cltv, scid; final: amt, cltv, `payment_data` with total = amount, `payment_metadata`), draws a CSPRNG session key (re-drawn until it is a valid scalar, zeroed after) and returns `PaymentOnion` (packet + per-hop shared secrets for `DecryptErrorPacket`).
- `Invoices/InvoiceService : IInvoiceService`: CSPRNG preimage and secret, hash = SHA256(preimage), `Bolt11.Invoice` with the node `ISecureKeyManager` (W0-D encode path: `9` = bits 8 and 14 compulsory, plus `basic_mpp` (17) optional unless `Features:BasicMpp` is `No`, ABCD W6-B), `c` = `Routing.InvoiceMinFinalCltvExpiry`, `x` = the given expiry or `Routing.InvoiceExpirySeconds`, `CreatedAt` = the BOLT 11 timestamp; persisted in its own scope (`IInvoiceDbRepository.AddAsync` + `IUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync`) before it returns. Route hints (NL-245): one single-hop `r` field per `Open` channel whose link is up (`IPeerLivenessProbe`, as LND skips inactive channels; so no hints after a restart until N7 marks the links) and whose peer's `channel_update` we hold (`IChannelUpdateService.TryGetRemoteChannelUpdate`, not disabled), with the **peer's** fee and `cltv_expiry_delta` (BOLT 11: the entry is the channel from its `pubkey` towards the payee, which the peer charges for), the real scid or, for an `option_scid_alias` channel, `RemoteAlias` (unverified against LND); with an amount, a channel whose peer's spendable amount (`InvoiceService.GetPeerSpendable`: its gross balance minus its unsettled offered HTLCs, the reserve we require of it and, when it funded the channel, the commitment fee with one more HTLC) or the update's htlc min/max cannot carry it is skipped; at most `InvoiceService.MaxRouteHints` (3), largest spendable first. No peer update, no hint (as LND). `IChannelMemoryRepository`/`IChannelUpdateService`/`IPeerLivenessProbe` are optional constructor parameters: without the first two there are no hints, without the probe every `Open` channel counts as up. Cancel only moves Open → Canceled.
- `Send/PaymentService : IPaymentService, IPaymentOutcomeHandler` (ABCD W2-C, N8-T3 + M4-T6; retries and MPP since ABCD W6-C, NL-270; singleton, registered by `Send/PaymentSendServiceCollectionExtensions.AddPaymentSendServices()`, which `AddApplicationServices` calls right after `AddHtlcSwitchServices()`; it also adds `IOptions<PaymentSendOptions>` (the daemon binds `Node:Payments`: fee limit max(0.5 %, 5000 msat) as CLN, `MaxParts` 16, `MaxAttempts` 32, `MinPartMsat` 10,000, `ExpiryTooSoonExtraBlocks` 6), `Routing/PaymentRoutePlanner` and `Send/PaymentOutcomeSwitchHandler`, the `ILocalPaymentHtlcHandler` through which the switch reaches `IPaymentOutcomeHandler`; it needs `ILightningSigner` to verify the `channel_update` of UPDATE failures). `PayInvoiceAsync(bolt11, amount, PayInvoiceOptions { Timeout, MaxFee, MaxParts }, ct)` → `PayInvoiceResult(Payment, Attempts, Parts)`; the `TimeSpan` overload is the same with the node's defaults. Validation as before (a decode failure, expiry, our own invoice, a missing/zero/different amount, a timeout <= 0 or a part limit outside 1..`PaymentSendOptions.MaxPartsLimit` (128) → `ArgumentException`; no processed block yet → `InvalidOperationException`; nothing persisted). Under the per-payment-hash lock (refcounted, one per hash): reconcile a stored `InFlight` payment without an HTLC id, refuse an `InFlight`/`Succeeded` hash **or one a call of this process is still paying** (its `PaymentSession`), then run rounds. The **session** (`Send/PaymentSession`, memory only, mutated under the hash lock) holds the parts (`PaymentPart`: our channel, the `PaymentRoute`, the persisted hops with their Sphinx shared secrets, the HTLC id), `Attempts` (offers, refused ones included), the deadline (= the timeout: no HTLC is offered after it), the terminal reason and the `Routing/RouteConstraints` learnt so far. A **round** (`RunRoundsAsync`): `PaymentRoutePlanner.TryPlan` for what the parts in flight do not carry, within what is left of the fee limit and the part/attempt budget, over every usable channel (`Open`, snapshot, link up; what one can send is the engine's own dry run `Send/LocalLiquidityEstimator` = binary search on `ChannelCommitments.SendAdd`, never persisted), onions (`PaymentOnionFactory`, `total_msat` = `PaymentRoute.TotalAmount`), persist, offer each part with `HtlcOrigin.Local(hash)` (a `CommitmentRefusedException` for a rule a smaller HTLC may pass (B2-ADD-S01..S04 balance/reserve/fees, S09 in-flight value, B2-DUST-03/04) bounds that channel below the refused amount; B2-ADD-S06 (below the peer's `htlc_minimum_msat`, which the planner reads, so the channel's state moved since the plan) raises that channel's minimum above the refused amount (`RouteConstraints.RaiseLocalHtlcMinimum`, NL-924) instead of avoiding it; any other refusal (link down, HTLCs disabled, failed, shutting down, data loss, HTLC count) and a `KeyNotFoundException` avoid the channel for the rest of the payment; the round plans again; any other exception stops the payment unless channel memory shows the HTLC was added (`FindUnrecordedPartHtlc`)). **Persistence** (no schema change, one row per hash): the first round creates the row (`InFlight`, the amount, the round's fees, the first part's route and secrets; its HTLC id recorded in the next save); whenever every part failed, the row is saved `Failed` before a retry round replaces it (`AddAsync` over a failed row), so no crash leaves it `InFlight` without an HTLC; parts added while others are in flight live in memory only (their HTLCs carry the origin, so their outcomes still reach the payment after a restart, but their error onions can then not be decrypted). The row always records an offered part: when its part is refused by the engine or fails while others are in flight, `MovePrimaryPartAsync` rewrites it to a live part (route, secrets, HTLC id, the fees in flight); on success it holds the fulfilled part's route and HTLC and, as its fee, the fees of the parts in flight at the fulfill (the settled ones; failed parts cost nothing). The route and fee change only by replacing the row (`StageReplacementAsync`: the in-flight row failed, then `AddAsync`, one save). Outcomes while the session lives go to it: a fulfill with the right preimage succeeds the payment (the row gets the fulfilled HTLC's id if none is recorded); a part's failure is decrypted with that part's secrets (`DescribeFailure` + `FailureInterpreter`) and handed to `Send/PaymentRetryPolicy`, which learns (see its remarks: payee PERM or not understood → stop; `mpp_timeout` → retry; NODE failure of an intermediate hop → avoid the node; `fee_insufficient`/`incorrect_cltv_expiry`/`amount_below_minimum` → the hop's `channel_update` (our chain, the failed scid, the hop's direction, signed by the hop, not disabled, newer than one already used) becomes that channel's policy for this payment, else avoid the channel; `expiry_too_soon` → `ExpiryTooSoonExtraBlocks` more final CLTV; `temporary_channel_failure` → the channel may forward less than the failed HTLC; other channel failures → avoid the channel; unattributable, malformed from our peer or timed out on chain → avoid our channel); a retryable failure queues a round on the thread pool (`ScheduleRound`, even while other parts are in flight: partial-failure handling re-sends that part's amount), a permanent one stops new rounds. The payment is stored `Failed` (code, erring hop index and reason of the last failure, plus why it gave up: "Not retried: ..." or "Gave up after N HTLCs: ...") once no part is in flight and no round may run (stopped, no route left, attempts used, deadline, the caller's cancellation). The session ends (and the waiter returns) when the payment is `Succeeded` or finally `Failed`; a timeout returns the payment as stored while the session keeps handling its in-flight parts without new HTLCs. `WhenRoundsIdleAsync()` (internal) waits for queued rounds. **Without a session** (after a restart, or a hash this process never paid) the old rules apply (`MatchOutcomeAsync`): match by the recorded (channel, HTLC id), or, when none is recorded, the HTLC's stored origin must be `Local(hash)` or none and its record in channel memory must match the stored first hop; a failure is applied only when no other non-final outgoing HTLC carries the hash (`Pending`: another part of a split payment, or a retry, is live), and an HTLC whose stored origin is `Local(hash)` but that is not the recorded one (another part) fails the payment without a code once none is live. A fulfill with the right preimage that matches no in-flight attempt is logged at Error and still recorded as `Succeeded` (the preimage proves the payment; a stored origin other than `Local` makes it not ours). They save the outcome before returning (so call them before pruning on `OutgoingHtlcSettled`), and never take a channel lock. `IPaymentOutcomeHandler.ReconcileInFlightPaymentsAsync` (called at startup right after `PeerManager.StartAsync` registered the channels, by `NltgDaemonService` and the Docker `NLightningTestNode`): every `InFlight` payment without an HTLC id is attached to its HTLC when exactly one non-final outgoing HTLC in channel memory matches its first hop (or carries its `Local` origin), failed without a code ("never offered") when none does and no origin row sits on a channel that is not in memory, and otherwise left in flight; the same runs lazily when the hash is paid again.
- `Routing/PaymentRoutePlanner` (W6-C, pure; `TryPlan(PaymentPlanRequest, out parts, out reason)`): paths = every usable channel of ours to the payee, then to the first node of each route hint (`HintRouteBuilder.GetCandidates`, hint policies replaced by `RouteConstraints.PolicyOverrides`, avoided nodes/channels skipped, total CLTV within `MaxCltvExpiryDistance`); an amount fits a path when the fee is within the remaining limit, our HTLC is at most what the channel can send (given the parts already planned on it) and below its learnt local bound and at least the peer's `htlc_minimum_msat` on it (`LocalChannelCandidate.HtlcMinimumMsat` from `ChannelParams.Remote`, raised by `RouteConstraints.LocalHtlcMinimumsMsat` after an S06 refusal; the engine's B2-ADD-S06, NL-924), and each hint channel forwards at least its `htlc_minimum_msat`, at most its `htlc_maximum_msat` and, with the other planned parts and the payment's parts still in flight over it (`PaymentPlanRequest.HintForwardsInFlightMsat`, from `PaymentRoutePlanner.SumHintForwards`), less than its learnt liquidity bound. One part when a path (direct first, then hints in invoice order; each by sendable, largest first) fits the whole amount, except that a split over at least two of our direct channels to the payee (no fee, no stranger on the route; MPP payees only) is tried after the direct single parts and before any hint or graph path (NL-980); else, only with `PaymentTarget.SupportsMpp` (the invoice's `basic_mpp`) and at least two parts allowed, a greedy split (cheapest path first, each takes the largest amount that fits, parts of at least `MinPartMsat` unless that is the rest; a part whose rest no later path takes only because of an `htlc_minimum_msat` (exact `TryFit` checks with that part's usage counted) takes less and leaves the smallest such minimum, and the split without that shift is tried when the shifted one fails, NL-924; graph paths get the same minimum through `LocalChannelState.HtlcMinimumMsat`), every part with `total_msat` = the payment's amount (BOLT 4 writer: same hash and `payment_secret`, the set adds up to `total_msat`). The reason names every path's problem and, when it could not split, why (no `basic_mpp`, one part allowed, not enough parts).
- Graph routing (BOLT 7 G4-T2..T4 + G3-T5, gossip wave G-C lane C2): `PaymentPlanRequest.Graph` (a `Routing/GraphRoutingContext`: the `IGraphView` snapshot, a copy of mission control's `LiquidityEstimates`, its penalized nodes, the time, `StaleAfter`, `PathsPerAmount`, the payment's shadow CLTV) makes `PaymentRoutePlanner` a graph router too (plan D7, a third candidate source): when no direct or hint path carries the whole amount alone (none exists, the payment avoided them, or they are depleted, bounded by a `temporary_channel_failure`, too small or too dear; direct and hint paths are still tried first), `GraphPathfinder.FindPaths` runs from us to the payee with our usable channels as `LocalChannels` + first-hop `ExtraEdge`s (live sendable amount, local liquidity bound; their gossip state never applies), every route-hint entry as an `ExtraEdge` (policy from the hint or its `PolicyOverrides`), `RouteConstraints.ExcludedNodes` + penalized nodes, `ExcludedChannels` (mission control's penalties never exclude our own peers: their live state decides), `RouteConstraints.GraphPolicyOverrides` (the failure's verified `channel_update` by `DirectedChannel`, this payment only, D9), `MaxFeeMsat` and `MaxCltvExpiryDistance`; graph candidates keep each hop's `htlc_minimum_msat`/`htlc_maximum_msat`/capacity (`HopLimit`, capacity checked against every part planned over the channel) and add the shadow offset to the payee's CLTV (cut to the CLTV limit; the SCID-keyed `PolicyOverrides`, which have no direction, apply to hint paths only). A graph path is used alone when it carries the amount, else the split combines direct, hint and graph paths. `Docker/PaymentRetryFlowTests` runs with `Node:Payments:UseGraph=false` (its proofs are about hints; with the graph, (c)'s too-dear hint would bring in alice's cheaper public channel). With `basic_mpp` and more than one part the pathfinder is asked again for amount/2, /4, ... (4 levels, down to `MinPartMsat`) so the split sees smaller channels. `Routing/GraphPathSource` (singleton) builds the context from `IGraphStore.GetSnapshot()` and `MissionControl`; none (planner as before) without an `IGraphStore`, with `GossipGraphOptions.IsEnabledFor(network)` false or `PaymentSendOptions.UseGraph` false. `PaymentService` asks it every round and computes the shadow offset once per payment (`ShadowCltv.ComputeOffset`, `PaymentSendOptions.ShadowCltvMaxOffset`, default 144, 0 turns it off). `Routing/MissionControl` (singleton, memory only, D8; `MissionControlHalfLife` 1 h, `NodeFailurePenalty` 1 h): `RecordSuccess(route)` lower bounds on every channel after ours; `RecordFailure(route, erringIndex, code)` lower bounds up to the erring hop, then `temporary_channel_failure` → upper bound at the forwarded amount, the policy failures (fee/cltv/min/expiry_too_soon) nothing, other channel failures → bound 0 (unusable until it fades), a NODE failure → the node penalized (never hop 0, our own peer); the payee's failures only the lower bounds. `PaymentRetryPolicy` (optional `MissionControl` and `IGossipScidRefresher`) records every attributed failure and, for an intermediate hop's UPDATE-flag failure, calls `IGossipScidRefresher.RequestRefresh(scid)` (Domain `Gossip/Interfaces`, non-blocking; default `Send/NullGossipScidRefresher`, TryAdd'ed by `AddPaymentSendServices`, which `AddGossipSyncServices` replaces with `Gossip/Sync/GossipSyncScidRefresher`: `IGossipSyncManager.QueryScidAsync` on the thread pool, one query per SCID in flight, at most 5 min); a fulfill records `RecordSuccess`. The failure's `channel_update` never reaches the graph. `PaymentService` is also `Routing/Interfaces/IRouteQueryService` (`QuoteRouteAsync(payee, amount, maxFee?, finalCltvDelta?)` → `RouteQuote(Route, Channel, Probability, BlockHeight, Description)`: one-part planning over the same channels and graph, no shadow offset, nothing stored or sent; the probability is the product of mission control's estimates for the channels after ours), for `getroute`. Invoices: `Invoices/InvoiceOptions.RouteHints` (`Node:Invoices`): `Auto` (default) puts no `r` field at all once one of our announced channels (`ChannelAnnouncementService.IsAnnounced`) is `Open`, its link up, its peer can send the amount (`GetPeerSpendable`; any inbound without an amount) and it has been in our own `IGraphStore` with both policies (the peer's not disabled) for `Node:Invoices:PublicChannelGracePeriod` (10 min; `IGraphStore.TryGetChannelReceivedAt`; no graph store: hints kept), `Always` forces the NL-245 hints, `Never` drops them. Tests: `Payments/Routing/GraphRoutePlannerTests` (graph route with exact fees and CLTVs, fee limit, MPP over diverse paths, mission control and penalized nodes steering, exclusions, per-payment policy override with the graph unchanged, graph → hint for a private payee, shadow CLTV, direct wins), `MissionControlTests`, `SyntheticGraph` (test builder), `PaymentRetryPolicyTests` (refresh on UPDATE failures only, mission control recording), `Send/GraphPaymentHarnessTests` on `PaymentHarness` with `PaymentHarnessTopology(Erin: true, BobUsesGraph: true)` (a fourth node Erin with David–Erin, Bob's `GraphView` from `PaymentHarness.BuildGraph`, `GraphReads`, `MissionControl`, `RouteQuery`; no shadow CLTV): Bob → Carol → David → Erin with exact fees, a retry around Carol's failing channel with the graph unchanged, `getroute` equal to the paid route), `InvoiceRouteHintTests` (the public-channel cases).
- Tests (W6-C): `Payments/Routing/PaymentRoutePlannerTests` (one part, split with `total_msat`, no split without `basic_mpp` or with one part, part limit, fees within the limit, policy overrides, avoided channels and nodes, liquidity bounds, extra CLTV), `Payments/Send/PaymentRetryPolicyTests` (every failure class above, the update checks), `Payments/Send/PaymentRetryHarnessTests` on `PaymentHarness` (now with `PaymentHarnessTopology`: a second Bob–Carol and/or Carol–David channel and the Bob–Carol balances; `PaymentHarnessNode.SignedUpdate` and `CreateMppInvoiceAsync` (an invoice with `basic_mpp`); `HarnessForwardingSwitch.ForwardInterceptor` fails a forward with a chosen failure, `Forwards`/`Received` record what a node forwarded/received, and a final HTLC whose `total_msat` is above its amount is held until the set is complete (a stand-in `basic_mpp` payee)): retry after `fee_insufficient` with Carol's signed update, a new fee above the call's limit, a call raising the limit above the default, `temporary_channel_failure` then the other hint, `expiry_too_soon` then more CLTV, no retry after a payee PERM failure, a node failure leaving no route, a split over two channels, no split with one part allowed, a part failing while the other is held then re-sent, and the liquidity estimator against the engine.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/Send/` (W2-C): `Harness/PaymentHarness` joins three real nodes (Bob–Carol–David, production handlers, engine, signer, channel operations, scheduler, Sphinx, failure onions, payment core and `PaymentService`; per-scope staged persistence) with `Harness/HarnessForwardingSwitch`, a stand-in for the W2-B switch (forward by scid, final-hop accept, fulfill/wrapped fail upstream, our outcomes to `IPaymentOutcomeHandler`); `PaymentHarnessTests` (direct and hinted payments with balances and stored routes, 0x400F from hop 1 and unknown_next_peer from hop 0 decrypted and stored, refusal, timeout, fee limit, retry after failure; crash-safety regressions with the harness store holding no origins as in production: a failed HTLC-id save (`InMemoryPaymentDbRepository.FailNextUpdates`) still completes on fulfill and on failure, a failed add save (`HarnessStateStore.FailNextApply`) fails the payment and the hash pays again, startup reconciliation attaches a live unrecorded HTLC, a replayed failure of another HTLC does not fail a live unrecorded attempt), `PaymentServiceTests` (validation, hook rules, preimage recorded on a Failed/mismatched payment, reconciliation, per-hash lock), `InvoiceRouteHintTests` (NL-245, incl. the peer's spendable amount and down links). The Daemon side is `test/NLightning.Daemon.Tests/Ipc/Handlers/PaymentSendIpcTests` (the node composition plus `AddPaymentSendServices()` validates on build and `payinvoice`/`listpayments` reach `PaymentService` over IPC).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/` (`PaymentsTestNode` builds one node from the production registrations with a `TestNodeKeyManager`, real Sphinx and an in-memory invoice store; `ThreeHopPaymentTests` is the lane proof: Alice → Bob → Carol → David onion peeled at each hop, policies, final accept, and a 0x400F decrypted at Alice from hop 2).

## attribution_data (BOLT 4 attributable failures and hold times; ABCD W7-A, NL-326, NL-022, NL-325)
- Receive: `UpdateFailHtlcMessageHandler`/`UpdateFulfillHtlcMessageHandler` pass the TLV 1 `attribution_data` (and TLV 3 `fulfillment_payload`) to the engine, which keeps them on the persisted `HtlcRemoval`; `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` carries them, `OutgoingHtlcFailed.Removal` too. A `fulfillment_payload` longer than 32768 bytes fails the channel (`ChannelFailedException`, BOLT 2 MUST, NL-325), but a valid id and preimage are first applied without the payload and persisted (`KnownPreimage`, `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` queued; `ChannelManager` drains the queue even when the handler throws), so a forwarded HTLC is still fulfilled upstream; a wrong preimage persists nothing.
- Send: `IChannelOperations.FailHtlcAsync(channel, id, AttributedErrorPacket, ct)` and `FulfillHtlcAsync(channel, id, preimage, AttributedFulfillment, stageWithFulfill?, ct)` persist the TLVs with the removal (so `PendingLocalUpdates`/`ToWireMessage` and the reestablish retransmit them) and `IMessageFactory` writes them (empty = no TLV; a length other than 920 throws). An `AttributedFulfillment` whose payload is over 32768 bytes is an `ArgumentException`, nothing persisted. `GetHoldTimeAsync(channel, incomingHtlcId, ct)` = time since the add's receipt (`Htlcs.AddedAt`, stamped when the add's transition is saved) on `ChannelOperationsService`'s optional `TimeProvider`, in 100 ms units, 0 when unknown.
- Origin: `PaymentService` takes an optional `IAttributionDataService`; with one and a non-empty `attribution_data` a failure is decrypted by `IAttributionDataService.DecryptErrorPacket` (hops verified up to the erring hop): the source index is the erring hop, or, when no hop authenticated the return packet, the first hop whose attribution HMAC failed ("attribution_data blames hop i"); the verified hold times go on the stored route (`PaymentHop.HoldTime`, `PaymentHops.HoldTimeMs`) and into the failure reason. A fulfill's attribution is verified with `VerifyFulfillment` over the fulfilled part's secrets and its hold times recorded the same way, only when the stored route is that part's (`PaymentService.IsSameRoute`: same hops and shared secrets), else only logged. No attribution: exactly the old path.
- The switch seam (wired into `Payments/Switch/HtlcSwitch` at the W7 integration: it takes an optional `IAttributionDataService` and adds attribution only when `NodeOptions.Features.OptionAttributionData` is not `No` and the incoming add had no `path_key` (`AddsAttribution`); `SendFailureAsync` (erring/final node failures), `FulfillFinalAsync` (final-hop and set fulfills, with the invoice settle staged), `AttributedReturnPacket` (downstream failure, malformed or on-chain timeout) and `FulfillUpstreamAsync` (forwarded fulfill); without the feature every path is the legacy one. Not done: a blinded intermediate hop does not wrap a downstream `fulfillment_payload` (route blinding is M5)): erring node `CreateErrorPacket(secret, failure, await ops.GetHoldTimeAsync(in))` / `CreateErrorPacketFromMalformed(...)` then the attributed `FailHtlcAsync`; intermediate `WrapErrorPacket(inSecret, removal.Reason, removal.AttributionData, holdTime)`; final hop `CreateFulfillment(secret, holdTime)`; forwarded fulfill `WrapFulfillment(inSecret, fulfilled.AttributionData, fulfilled.FulfillmentPayload, holdTime)` then the attributed `FulfillHtlcAsync` (with `stageWithFulfill` for the invoice settle). The reference implementation is the test switch `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/Send/Harness/HarnessForwardingSwitch` (`UseAttribution`). `OptionAttributionData` is advertised Optional by default since NL-332 (LND 0.20 and 0.21 have no support and ignore the odd TLVs); a switch built without `NodeOptions` attributes too (the null default follows the option's default).
- Tests: `Payments/Send/AttributionHarnessTests` (the in-process proof over `PaymentHarness`: payee failure verified with both hops' hold times, fulfill hold times, a hop garbling the return packet blamed by attribution, partial support, legacy unchanged, an intermediate erring hop), `Channels/Handlers/AttributionHandlerTests`, `Channels/Services/ChannelOperationsAttributionTests`, `Protocol/Factories/MessageFactoryTests`.

## HTLC switch (ABCD W2-B: ONION M4-T2 wiring, M4-T4, M4-T5, M4-T7; BOLT2 N8-T2)
`Payments/Switch/HtlcSwitch : IHtlcSwitch` (singleton), registered by `HtlcSwitchServiceCollectionExtensions.AddHtlcSwitchServices()` (called by `AddApplicationServices` right after `AddPaymentsServices()`, so production decorates the reestablish-gated probe), which `services.Replace`s the `LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch` and decorates the registered `IPeerLivenessProbe` with `LinkUpReplayingPeerLivenessProbe` (call it after `AddChannelOperationsServices()`/`AddPaymentsServices()` and any other probe registration; it throws when no probe is registered; idempotent). It works outside every channel lock and changes channels only through `IChannelOperations`; locks are taken in the order incoming HTLC (`KeyedAsyncLock<(ChannelId, ulong)>`) → payment hash (`KeyedAsyncLock<Hash>`) → channel (only inside `IChannelOperations` or the prune), never two channel locks. A `CommitmentRefusedException`/`KeyNotFoundException` (peer away, channel not loaded) is logged: nothing was persisted and the HTLC is still pending. The replays: `ChannelManager` at startup (every link is down then, so it resolves nothing that needs the peer) and `LinkUpEventReplayer`, which the decorated probe schedules on every `MarkLinkUp` (on the thread pool, without the caller's context: the snapshot and the unpruned settled rows are read under the channel lock, then `DerivePending` goes to the switch outside it; `WhenIdleAsync` for tests). A link is marked when a channel opens and after each channel_reestablish (N7, `ChannelManager.CompleteReestablishAsync`), which queues the channel's pending events itself; the decorated probe therefore skips the replay when the `ReestablishTracker` says the channel is `Reestablished`, so each link-up hands the events to the switch once (NL-264; without a registered tracker every `MarkLinkUp` replays).
- `IncomingHtlcLockedIn`: skipped unless the HTLC is still `RcvdAddAckRevocation` in memory. An HTLC with a `ForwardCircuitModel` is resumed, not peeled again: `Pending` → the channel HTLC that carries `HtlcOrigin.Forwarded(in)` (`FindHtlcsByOriginAsync`) is recorded (`Offered`), or, when there is none (the offer never persisted), the circuit is failed and the upstream HTLC gets `temporary_channel_failure`; `Failed` without an outgoing HTLC (refused offer) → fail upstream again; any circuit with an outgoing HTLC → the outgoing record (live, else its archived row from `LoadAsync`) resolves the upstream HTLC (preimage → fulfill, final failure → wrapped fail; then the archived row is pruned when the forward is done), otherwise it waits. Otherwise `IncomingOnionProcessor.ProcessAsync` runs with `replayOwner: null` (no replay check) when the HTLC's shared secret is already stored, else with `OnionReplayOwner(channelId, htlcId, cltvExpiry)` (processed before a restart or reestablish), the secret is stored (`RecordOnionSecretAsync`), then: malformed → `FailMalformedHtlcAsync`; failed → `FailHtlcAsync(CreateErrorPacket)`; final → receive; forward → forward.
- Receive (NL-253; `basic_mpp`, ABCD W6-B): under the payment-hash lock, an HTLC that was resolved while waiting for the lock (a set's fulfill or timeout acts on other parts under the hash lock, not their incoming locks) is skipped; then re-read the invoice and `FinalHopProcessor.Evaluate(..., acceptMultiPart)`; `InvoiceAlreadySettled` → fulfill at once (even at height 0); otherwise height 0 → `temporary_node_failure` (`acceptMultiPart` = `NodeOptions.Features.BasicMpp != No`, the default Optional). Every accepted HTLC joins the payment's in-memory `HtlcSet` (`Switch/HtlcSet.cs`, by payment hash; parts no longer waiting are pruned first: not `RcvdAddAckRevocation`, or on a channel closing on chain at or past its `cltv_expiry`; a single-part payment is a set of one). A part whose `total_msat` differs from the set's fails the **whole** set with PERM|15 (BOLT 4 SHOULD). While the parts' `amt_to_forward` are below `total_msat` the set is held and a `TimeProvider` timer runs (`HtlcSwitchOptions.MppTimeout`, default 60 s from the first part, BOLT 4 SHOULD ≥ 60 s); when it fires (background round, `WhenIdleAsync` for tests) an incomplete set is failed with `mpp_timeout` (0x17); a part whose failure is refused (peer away) is remembered and failed with `mpp_timeout` on its replay. A complete set is **committed** then fulfilled (`FulfillSetAsync`, ABCD W7-B, NL-322/NL-323): first the preimage is persisted on the record of every part but one (`MarkPartAsync`: under that channel's lock, one `IChannelStateDbRepository.ApplyAsync` of the record with `KnownPreimage` over `ChannelCommitments.Restore`, one save, then `ChannelModel.UpdateCommitments`; the switch's only direct snapshot write besides the prune), then the remaining part settles the invoice in its own save: its `FulfillHtlcAsync(..., stageWithFulfill)`, or, when its channel is closing on chain or that fulfill is refused (peer away, `KeyNotFoundException`), its mark (`MarkPartAsync` with the settle staged); the staging re-reads the invoice, checks it is still `Open` (else `InvoiceNotOpenException`: nothing settled, the marks are taken back and the set fails with PERM|15) and stages `Accept(sum of the held parts' amount_msat)` + `Settle` + `UpdateAsync`. So `Settled` means "every part of the set is fulfilled or carries the preimage". The other parts are then fulfilled; a refused one stays locked in with its mark, and its replay (link-up, startup) is a committed member (`InvoiceAlreadySettled`) and is fulfilled, so the whole set is always fulfilled (BOLT 4 MUST). An HTLC for a Settled invoice without the mark is failed with PERM|15 (NL-323). The invoice's `Settled` save is the commit point: the switch counts a mark as a committed member only when the invoice is `Settled` with that preimage, and so do the resolvers. A set that is no longer complete before the settle (a part resolved elsewhere, or its channel unloaded) has its marks taken back and is held again; any other failure before the settle (e.g. the settle's save) takes the marks back and holds the set with its timer, and a timer that finds its set complete fulfills it again (with the invoice's preimage). `FailPartAsync` (total_msat mismatch, `mpp_timeout`, canceled invoice) takes a leftover mark back before it fails a part or leaves it to time out on chain. The set itself lives in memory and is rebuilt from the persisted incoming HTLCs by the startup and link-up replays after a restart (its timeout starts again). `AddHtlcSwitchServices` registers `HtlcSwitch` as its own singleton and `IHtlcSwitch` as a factory over it, so the container disposes it (timers stopped, timeout rounds canceled; `IDisposable` + `IAsyncDisposable`) also behind `DustExposureHtlcSwitch`. Without `basic_mpp` a second HTLC for a settled hash fails with PERM|15; the switch never writes `Accepted` on its own.
- Channels closing on chain (ABCD W7-B, NL-316; `IsOnchain` = the incoming channel is `Failed` or `OnchainResolving`): the lock-in event comes from the BOLT 5 resolvers (below, `Onchain/Resolvers/FinalHopClaims`) every round while the HTLC pays an `Open` invoice of ours or a hash with no invoice (a keysend the switch has not accepted yet), or from a replay. Only a final-hop onion is acted on: a forward is never forwarded and nothing is failed (the channel cannot carry an update; the HTLC times out on chain), a malformed/failed onion, a rejected `FinalHopProcessor` decision, height 0 and an `mpp_timeout` only log. An accepted part joins its set like any other (it counts until the tip reaches its `cltv_expiry`); when the set completes, its "fulfill" is the mark (with the settle when it is the settling part), from which the resolvers claim it on chain (`HtlcRecord.KnownPreimage` of an incoming HTLC means "accepted as our final hop", B5-LCL-RO-02). `FailPartAsync` never fails an on-chain part (it only takes its mark back). Known limits: a part of a set committed before this change (no mark) is failed as a duplicate on its replay (regtest-only HTLCs so far, accepted under the schema-reset policy; no reconciliation of older data); (`HtlcExpiryMonitor` reads "preimage known" for a final-hop HTLC from the record's mark and the Settled invoice since NL-337); the invoice expiry is checked at the on-chain decision, so an HTLC locked in just before its invoice expired and never fulfilled is not claimed.
- Forward: the onion's scid resolves to an `Open` channel by `LocalAliases`, `RemoteAlias`, or the real scid unless `UseScidAlias` is `Compulsory` (option_scid_alias in the channel type, BOLT 2; a public channel with the feature only negotiated has `Optional` and is announced by its real scid, NL-348; `ThreeNodeSwitchTests.Given_ScidAlias*` on `ThreeNodeHarness.CreateAsync(bobCarolScidAlias: ...)`); `IForwardingPolicy` gets an `OutgoingChannelInfo` (usable = open, snapshot, no data loss, link up; `AvailableToSend` = settled local balance − our pending offered HTLCs − the reserve the peer requires). A policy failure is returned with our signed `channel_update` (`IChannelUpdateService.TryGetLocalChannelUpdate`, only when its scid is the onion's; else `len = 0`) for the UPDATE codes. Then the circuit is saved `Pending`, `OfferHtlcAsync(..., HtlcOrigin.Forwarded(in))` (origin persisted with the add), and the circuit marked `Offered`; when the offer throws (a refusal, or anything else except a cancellation) the channel state decides: an HTLC carrying the origin → the circuit is marked `Offered`; none → the circuit is `Failed` and the upstream HTLC gets `temporary_channel_failure` with the update (a cancellation leaves it `Pending` for the replay).
- `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled` (origin `Forwarded`): fulfill upstream at once, then mark the circuit `Fulfilled` (also when the upstream fulfill was refused: the next replay of the upstream lock-in fulfills from the kept outgoing record). `OutgoingHtlcFailed` (only raised when irrevocable): `WrapErrorPacket` with the incoming shared secret (circuit, stored secret, or a re-peel), or `CreateErrorPacketFromMalformed` for `update_fail_malformed_htlc` (a bad hash length falls back to `temporary_channel_failure`), then `FailHtlcAsync` upstream and the circuit `Failed`. Origin `Local`: every registered `ILocalPaymentHtlcHandler` (`HandleFulfilledAsync`/`HandleFailedAsync`; the W2-C `PaymentService` hook). No origin: logged.
- `OutgoingHtlcSettled` prunes the archived outgoing row (NL-243) only when nothing needs its replay: for a forward, the upstream HTLC is no longer `RcvdAddAckRevocation` in a **loaded** channel (an upstream channel not registered yet keeps the row) and the circuit is resolved; for a local payment, no handler failed on it in this process. `IncomingHtlcSettled` prunes the incoming archived row at once (nothing reads it after our removal is final; the startup replay also prunes rows left by older builds).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/Switch/` (`HtlcSwitchTests`: pruning order, refusals, registration and probe decoration, `KeyedAsyncLock`; `ThreeNodeSwitchTests`: the lane proof on `Channels/Harness/ThreeNodeHarness`; `MppReceiveTests` (W6-B): two parts held then fulfilled with one settle, `mpp_timeout` at 60 s (not at 59 s) and a new attempt afterwards, a timeout failure refused while the link is down and sent on the replay, mismatched `total_msat` failing the whole set, a restart with a partial set (completed by the last part, or timed out again), a complete set not acted on before a restart, a settled set with one fulfill refused and fulfilled on the link-up replay (also past its final CLTV delta and at height 0, and for a part whose `amt_to_forward` alone equals `total_msat`), a replayed part waiting for the hash lock while its set is fulfilled, a single-part HTLC or a late part for a settled hash (failed with PERM|15, NL-323; another secret fails too), the parts left without a fulfill carrying the preimage, `basic_mpp` off; Carol's clock is `SteppedTimeProvider` through `SwitchNode.ConfigureServices`; `OnchainFinalHopSwitchTests` (W7-B, NL-316/NL-322): an unfulfilled HTLC of our invoice on a Failed/OnchainResolving channel accepted with the preimage on its record (memory and database) and the invoice settled, twice idempotent, nothing sent; two parts committed with one settle; a held part not failed off chain by `mpp_timeout` and held again; another secret neither accepted nor failed; a forward from such a channel neither forwarded, failed nor marked; `ThreeNodeSwitchTests.Given_CarolsLinkDownWhenSheWouldFulfill_...` now expects the set committed (Settled, preimage on the record) while the peer is away and one fulfill after). Docker proof: `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/MppFlowTests.cs` (LND david pays our invoice with `max_parts` over two channels).

## Trampoline relays: HTLC origin, consumers and accounting (NL-875, lane TR3-P)
- Storage (TR3-P1/P2): `HtlcOrigin.Trampoline(paymentHash)` (kind 3, `Htlcs.OriginKind` + `OriginPaymentHash`), tables `TrampolineRelays`, `TrampolineRelayParts` (PK incoming channel + HTLC id), `PaymentTrampolineHops` and `Payments.IsTrampolineRelay` (migration `AddTrampolineRelays`). `Payments/Trampoline/TrampolineRelayReads` (internal) is how the consumers read them: a unit of work without the repository (test doubles: the interface default throws `NotSupportedException`, a mock answers null) has no relays.
- `HtlcSwitch` (optional last constructor argument `ITrampolineHtlcHandler`, `Payments/Switch/`, implemented by the relay engine, singleton): origin-3 `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled`/`OutgoingHtlcFailed` go to the handler only, never to an `ILocalPaymentHtlcHandler`; the settled outgoing record is pruned only when `HandleSettledAsync` answers true, the relay is completed and no incoming part is still `RcvdAddAckRevocation` in a loaded channel; a lock-in of an incoming HTLC that is a relay part (no circuit, a `TrampolineRelayParts` row) goes to `HandleIncomingPartLockedInAsync`, never peeled again or failed. Without a handler nothing happens (one warning) and the records stay. A handler that throws keeps the record for the next replay.
- Safety: `HtlcExpiryMonitor` reads a relay part as `PreimageKnown` (its record's `KnownPreimage`, the relay's `Preimage`, or any outgoing HTLC of the relay that learnt it), `AwaitingDownstream` while an outgoing HTLC of the relay is unresolved (live without preimage, loaded without snapshot, or on an unloaded channel that is not Closed) or the relay is `Sending`, else `Unresolved` (failed back at the fail-back deadline). `DustExposureHtlcSwitch` never fails a relay part.
