- BOLT 5: `Onchain/Resolvers/TrampolineRelayClaims` (`GetRelayPreimageAsync`, used by both commitment resolvers after the forward lookup: a relay part is claimed with the preimage its own record, the relay or an outgoing HTLC of the relay holds, never after our fail; `IsUpstreamResolvedAsync`: the upstream of an origin-3 HTLC is every incoming part, resolved when each has our removal, is gone, or sits on a channel that is not Open while the relay is completed); `HtlcUpstreamOutcomeReader` reads origin 3 from the parts' removals, then the relay's status; `FinalHopClaims.GetFinalHopDecisionAsync` raises the lock-in of a relay part (to the handler) only while the relay is not completed, and never asks the invoice table for it. On-chain accounting books an origin-3 HTLC's value as `valueBookedBy=trampoline` (`OnchainAccounting.TrampolineValueOwner`), and so a claimed relay part.
- Accounting (TR3-P4): `AccountingEventKind.TrampolineRelaySettled` (7, key `AccountingEventKeys.TrampolineRelaySettled(hash)` = `tramp:{hash}:settled`): `AmountMsat` = the incoming parts' sum minus the outgoing payment's `TotalAmount` (amount + routing fees paid; the relay's `FeeEarned` without the payment); posting rule Dr Channels a / Cr Routing a, a negative one Cr Channels / Dr RoutingFees; the channels report counts it as a forward: out on the payment's channel, and in on each incoming part's channel with a share of the income proportional to what that channel brought (detail `incomingAmountsMsat` = `channelId:msat` pairs in `incomingChannelIds` order, `PaymentAccountingEvents.TrampolineIncomingAmountsDetail`; shares rounded toward zero, the rest to the largest, `AccountingReportService.SplitProportionally`; NL-899). An event sealed before that detail (no rewrite of sealed events) stays on its first part's channel. The engine stages it in the save that marks the relay `Fulfilled` with `PaymentAccountingEvents.StageTrampolineRelaySettledAsync(unitOfWork, relay, incomingChannel, height, logger)` (reads the parts and the payment from the unit of work; once per relay; never throws). `PaymentService` stages no `PaymentSucceeded`/`PaymentFailed` for a payment with `IsTrampolineRelay`, and the memo backfill skips those payments and writes fulfilled relays from before the cutover as memo `TrampolineRelaySettled` (counted with the forwards). A part of a booked relay (live `TrampolineRelaySettled` in the feed, relay `Fulfilled`) that the peer takes on chain by its timeout or that we give up is a `ForwardLostOnchain` (`PaymentAccountingEvents.TrampolinePartLostOnchain`, cause `upstreamOnchain`, key `ForwardLostOnchain(incoming channel, htlc)`), reversed by a reorg as a forward's.
- Tests: `Payments/Switch/TrampolineOriginSwitchTests`, `Onchain/Resolvers/TrampolineRelayConsumersTests`, the relay cases of `Channels/Safety/HtlcExpiryMonitorTests`, `Channels/Fees/DustExposureHtlcSwitchTests`, `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_ASettledTrampolineRelaysIncomingPart_*`, `Payments/Trampoline/TrampolineAccountingEventsTests`, `Payments/Send/PaymentServiceTests.Given_ATrampolineRelaysOutgoingPayment_*`; Domain `Accounting/Books/AccountingPostingRulesTests` (relay rules).

## Trampoline relay engine (NL-875 TR3, plan `docs/agents/TRAMPOLINE_PLAN.md`; `Payments/Trampoline/`)
- `TrampolineRelayService` (singleton, `AddTrampolineRelayServices()` called by `AddApplicationServices` after `AddPaymentSendServices()`): the switch's `ITrampolineRelayIngress` and `ITrampolineHtlcHandler` and the leg sender's `ITrampolineLegObserver`. It resolves `ITrampolineLegSender` (TR4's `PaymentService`) lazily, so the two may depend on each other; without one every new part fails with `temporary_trampoline_failure`. `Node:Trampoline` = `TrampolineOptions` (fee 1000 msat + 1000 ppm, delta 576, `MaxRelaysInFlight` 32, `LegTimeout` 60 s, `MinCltvMarginBlocks` 48); `TrampolineRelayPolicy` is the pure TR-R-09 check over a `TrampolineRelaySet` and `Node:Routing`: NODE|26 with our policy when the fee or the delta is short; `temporary_trampoline_failure` for the expiry bounds (NL-922, as `HtlcForwardingPolicy`: outgoing expiry within `ExpiryTooSoonBlocks` of the height, plus the recipient's path delta when we pay its blinded paths; an incoming expiry more than `MaxCltvExpiryDistance` above it; the lowest incoming one within `MinCltvMarginBlocks`).
- Per payment hash under one lock: the first part saves the relay row with its inner instructions, later parts must match its outer total, outer `payment_secret`, amount, expiry, next node, trampoline onion and recipient data (else `incorrect_or_unknown_payment_details` for that part only); a relay already Sending/Failed fails a late part with `temporary_trampoline_failure`, a Fulfilled one fulfills it. A Failed relay whose parts are all resolved and whose outgoing HTLCs are all resolved is removed (`ITrampolineRelayDbRepository.RemoveFailedAsync`) to let the payer's new attempt with the same hash start a new one (the NODE|26 retry, D-TR6). `mpp_timeout` runs from the relay's `CreatedAt` (`Node:Switch:MppTimeout`). At the total (parts still waiting only): policy, drain/chain-halt, `MaxRelaysInFlight` (Sending rows), then `MarkSending` in a save and `StartAsync` outside the hash lock (the leg may report from inside it). The leg gets the whole difference: `MaxFee` = sum in − amount out and `MaxFirstHopCltvExpiry` = lowest incoming expiry − `Node:Routing:CltvExpiryDelta` (at most our trampoline delta): our trampoline fee and delta pay for the route (D-TR6, as Eclair), so a payer that pays exactly the policy of our NODE|26 is routed past our direct peers (NL-875 TR5; keeping our fee and delta out of the budget left such a payment no route). Deadline = now + min(`LegTimeout`, 5 min per block left above `MinCltvMarginBlocks`); a watchdog at deadline + `LegTimeout` fails the relay only when no outgoing HTLC is unresolved (`TrampolineRelayReads.GetOutgoingStateAsync`), fulfills it when one learnt the preimage, else checks again later.
- Success (`OnLegSucceededAsync`, or the switch's origin-3 fulfill when the leg sender did not report it: fee = sum in − the leg payment's `TotalAmount`, else − `AmountOut`): one save under the first part's channel lock with `Fulfilled`, `StageTrampolineRelaySettledAsync` and `KnownPreimage` on that channel's parts (other channels' parts in their own saves after it; the relay's preimage already protects them), then every part fulfilled (attribution on the outer layer); a refused fulfill is redone by the part's replayed lock-in. Failure (`OnLegFailedAsync`): `Failed` only when no outgoing HTLC is unresolved (else kept for the watchdog), then each part failed with the downstream packet re-wrapped (`DownstreamTrampolineError`) or our own `unknown_next_trampoline` (`NoRoute`) / `temporary_trampoline_failure`, under the trampoline then outer secret; inside a blinded trampoline route past the introduction node `update_fail_malformed_htlc` + `invalid_onion_blinding`, at it our own `invalid_onion_blinding`. The failure keys come from the part's onion, kept in memory; after a restart the stored onion is peeled again (`IncomingOnionProcessor`, no replay owner). Replays (`HandleIncomingPartLockedInAsync`): Fulfilled → fulfill, Failed → fail again (the kept failure, else one rebuilt from the stored code), Collecting → check the set again (a crash between the last part and `Sending`), Sending → watchdog.
- Host: `StartAsync` after `ReconcileInFlightPaymentsAsync` (daemon `NltgDaemonService`, Docker `NLightningTestNode`): timers back for Collecting relays, watchdogs for Sending ones. `listforwards` lists relays (response key 2, kind `trampoline`).
- Blinded trampoline hops (NL-895; a BOLT 12 recipient with bit 57 made us a hop of its blinded path, `IncomingOnionTrampolineRelay.Blinded`): the next node is the recipient data's `next_node_id`, or the peer of the open channel its `short_channel_id` names (`IncomingOnionTrampolineRelay.NextShortChannelId`, resolved by `Payments/Switch/OutgoingChannelResolver`, the switch's own rule for blinded forwards: real scid unless `option_scid_alias` is Compulsory, aliases, `IRetiredScidMap` (optional engine argument)), saved as the relay's `NextNodeId`. Before a part joins, an unknown scid, an HTLC amount below the outer `amt_to_forward`, an expiry below the outer `outgoing_cltv_value` (BOLT 4 final-node rules; the next amount and expiry are derived from the outer total and expiry by `payment_relay`) or above `payment_constraints.max_cltv_expiry`, and a `payment_relay` below our policy for the hop (NL-922, D-NL922-1: `TrampolineRelayPolicy.CheckBlindedHopPrice`, the fee and the `cltv_expiry_delta` as `HtlcForwardingPolicy` checks a blinded forward, with its BOLT 7 grace helpers `PaymentRelayCoversFee`/`LenientCltvExpiryDelta`; the policy is the scid-named channel's from the optional `IChannelPolicyProvider` (its `setchannelpolicy` override, else `Node:Routing`; providers not loaded refuse), `Node:Routing` for a `next_node_id` hop, never `Node:Trampoline`) refuse that part with `invalid_onion_blinding` (our own at the introduction node, malformed past it, as every blinded failure); no relay row, no `MaxRelaysInFlight` slot. The price is the path's `payment_relay` (D-NL895-2), never NODE|26: at the total, `TrampolineRelayPolicy.EvaluateBlinded` checks sum in ≥ amount out, the outgoing expiry more than `ExpiryTooSoonBlocks` above the height, the hop's kept delta (the price check's: the channel's, the most lenient in grace, or `Node:Routing`'s) between the lowest incoming expiry and the outgoing one, no incoming expiry more than `MaxCltvExpiryDistance` above the height and the lowest above `MinCltvMarginBlocks`; it refuses with `invalid_onion_blinding` and gives the leg sum in − amount out and the lowest incoming expiry − the kept delta. The kept delta lives in memory per collecting relay and, since NL-923 (migration `AddTrampolineRelayBlindedDelta`), on the relay row (`BlindedKeptCltvExpiryDelta`, raised to a later part's larger one), so after a restart between the last part and `Sending` (the Collecting replay builds the leg from the stored `NextNodeId`/`NextPathKey`, never resolving the scid again) the row's value is used; only a row stored before the migration falls back to the largest of `Node:Routing`'s and the open channels to the stored node (an upper bound, safe over-refusal only). The leg carries the next path key in the next node's outer TLV 12. Tests: `Payments/Trampoline/BlindedTrampolineRelayTests` (ThreeNodeHarness), scenario 10(b) of `TrampolineRelayE2ETests`, the vector [1] payer and leg payloads in `TrampolineOnionFactoryTests`.
- `TrampolineRelayService` (singleton, `AddTrampolineRelayServices()` called by `AddApplicationServices` after `AddPaymentSendServices()`): the switch's `ITrampolineRelayIngress` and `ITrampolineHtlcHandler` and the leg sender's `ITrampolineLegObserver`. It resolves `ITrampolineLegSender` (TR4's `PaymentService`) lazily, so the two may depend on each other; without one every new part fails with `temporary_trampoline_failure`. `Node:Trampoline` = `TrampolineOptions` (fee 1000 msat + 1000 ppm, delta 576, `MaxRelaysInFlight` 32, `LegTimeout` 60 s, `MinCltvMarginBlocks` 48); `TrampolineRelayPolicy` is the pure TR-R-09 check (NODE|26 with our policy on every refusal).
- Per payment hash under one lock: the first part saves the relay row with its inner instructions, later parts must match its outer total, outer `payment_secret`, amount, expiry, next node, trampoline onion and recipient data (else `incorrect_or_unknown_payment_details` for that part only); a relay already Sending/Failed fails a late part with `temporary_trampoline_failure`, a Fulfilled one fulfills it. A Failed relay whose parts are all resolved and whose outgoing HTLCs are all resolved is removed (`ITrampolineRelayDbRepository.RemoveFailedAsync`) to let the payer's new attempt with the same hash start a new one (the NODE|26 retry, D-TR6); the same save keeps it as a `TrampolineRelayAttemptModel` (table `TrampolineRelayAttempts`, numbered per hash from 1; NL-899) that only the listings read (`ListReplacedAttemptsAsync`, `SummarizeAsync`), never the engine, the switch or the resolvers. `mpp_timeout` runs from the relay's `CreatedAt` (`Node:Switch:MppTimeout`). At the total (parts still waiting only): policy, drain/chain-halt, `MaxRelaysInFlight` (Sending rows), then `MarkSending` in a save and `StartAsync` outside the hash lock (the leg may report from inside it). The leg gets the whole difference: `MaxFee` = sum in − amount out and `MaxFirstHopCltvExpiry` = lowest incoming expiry − `Node:Routing:CltvExpiryDelta` (at most our trampoline delta): our trampoline fee and delta pay for the route (D-TR6, as Eclair), so a payer that pays exactly the policy of our NODE|26 is routed past our direct peers (NL-875 TR5; keeping our fee and delta out of the budget left such a payment no route). Deadline = now + min(`LegTimeout`, 5 min per block left above `MinCltvMarginBlocks`); a watchdog at deadline + `LegTimeout` fails the relay only when no outgoing HTLC is unresolved (`TrampolineRelayReads.GetOutgoingStateAsync`), fulfills it when one learnt the preimage, else checks again later.
- Success (`OnLegSucceededAsync`, or the switch's origin-3 fulfill when the leg sender did not report it: fee = sum in − the leg payment's `TotalAmount`, else − `AmountOut`): one save under the first part's channel lock with `Fulfilled`, `StageTrampolineRelaySettledAsync` and `KnownPreimage` on that channel's parts (other channels' parts in their own saves after it; the relay's preimage already protects them), then every part fulfilled (attribution on the outer layer); a refused fulfill is redone by the part's replayed lock-in. Failure (`OnLegFailedAsync`): `Failed` only when no outgoing HTLC is unresolved (else kept for the watchdog), then each part failed with the downstream packet re-wrapped (`DownstreamTrampolineError`) or our own `unknown_next_trampoline` (`NoRoute`) / `temporary_trampoline_failure`, under the trampoline then outer secret; inside a blinded trampoline route past the introduction node `update_fail_malformed_htlc` + `invalid_onion_blinding`, at it our own `invalid_onion_blinding`. The failure keys come from the part's onion, kept in memory; after a restart the stored onion is peeled again (`IncomingOnionProcessor`, no replay owner). Replays (`HandleIncomingPartLockedInAsync`): Fulfilled → fulfill, Failed → fail again (the kept failure, else one rebuilt from the stored code), Collecting → check the set again (a crash between the last part and `Sending`), Sending → watchdog.
- Host: `StartAsync` after `ReconcileInFlightPaymentsAsync` (daemon `NltgDaemonService`, Docker `NLightningTestNode`): timers back for Collecting relays, watchdogs for Sending ones. `listforwards` lists relays (response key 2, kind `trampoline`) and the failed attempts a retry replaced (relay key 14 `ReplacedAttempt`), merged newest first; its totals (summary keys 0-4) count the relays with the forwards (collecting = pending, sending = offered) and the fulfilled relays' `FeeEarned`, and summary keys 7-11 are the relays' share (NL-981). `listpayments` leaves the relays' outgoing legs (`Payments.IsTrampolineRelay`) out unless `--include-relay-legs` (request key 2; response key 1 counts the hidden ones; payment key 18 marks a listed leg) and shows a trampoline payment's node and last attempt's inner route from `PaymentTrampolineHops` (payment keys 19-21; NL-899).
- Blinded trampoline hops (NL-895; a BOLT 12 recipient with bit 57 made us a hop of its blinded path, `IncomingOnionTrampolineRelay.Blinded`): the next node is the recipient data's `next_node_id`, or the peer of the open channel its `short_channel_id` names (`IncomingOnionTrampolineRelay.NextShortChannelId`, resolved by `Payments/Switch/OutgoingChannelResolver`, the switch's own rule for blinded forwards: real scid unless `option_scid_alias` is Compulsory, aliases, `IRetiredScidMap` (optional engine argument)), saved as the relay's `NextNodeId`. Before a part joins, an unknown scid, an HTLC amount below the outer `amt_to_forward` or an expiry below the outer `outgoing_cltv_value` (BOLT 4 final-node rules; the next amount and expiry are derived from the outer total and expiry by `payment_relay`) refuse that part with `invalid_onion_blinding` (our own at the introduction node, malformed past it, as every blinded failure). The price is the path's `payment_relay`, not `Node:Trampoline`: at the total, `TrampolineRelayPolicy.EvaluateBlinded` keeps only our safety (sum in covers the amount out, outgoing expiry above the height, our forwarding delta and `MinCltvMarginBlocks` kept below the lowest incoming expiry), refuses with `invalid_onion_blinding`, never NODE|26, and gives the leg sum in − amount out and the lowest incoming expiry − our forwarding delta; the leg carries the next path key in the next node's outer TLV 12. Tests: `Payments/Trampoline/BlindedTrampolineRelayTests` (ThreeNodeHarness), scenario 10(b) of `TrampolineRelayE2ETests`, the vector [1] payer and leg payloads in `TrampolineOnionFactoryTests`.
- Tests: `Payments/Trampoline/TrampolineRelayPolicyTests`, `TrampolineRelayE2ETests` (TR5: the engine, the leg sender and the payer together on `Payments/Trampoline/Harness/TrampolineHarness`, A → T → X → C), `TrampolineRelayServiceTests` (ThreeNodeHarness with Carol as the relay and a fake leg sender; with `carolAlice` a real origin-3 HTLC Carol → Alice), Daemon `Extensions/TrampolineConfigTemplateTests`, `Handlers/ListForwardsClientHandlerTests`, `Client/ListForwardsPrinterTests`.

## Trampoline target and incoming classification (NL-875 TR2, plan `docs/agents/TRAMPOLINE_PLAN.md`)
- Feature switch (D-TR2): `Payments/Onion/TrampolineRoutingSupport.IsAdvertised(FeatureOptions)` (the node's own feature bits carry 57: configured and, while experimental, `AllowExperimentalFeatures`). Off: TLV 20 stays an unknown even type (`invalid_onion_payload`), as before.
- `IncomingOnionProcessor` (optional `ITrampolineOnionService` argument): the outer payload is validated with `allowTrampoline`; an outer final payload with TLV 20 is peeled (path key = the outer `current_path_key` past a blinded introduction node), its payload checked by `TrampolinePayloadValidator` (cross-onion rules) and, inside a blinded trampoline route, its recipient data decrypted (our own dummy trampoline hops peeled too, `MaxSelfRelayHops`). Results (base `IncomingOnionTrampolineResult`: outer/trampoline secrets and payloads, `Blinded`): `IncomingOnionTrampolineFinal` (`MergedPayload` = the inner records with the outer `amt_to_forward`, `ToFinal()`), `IncomingOnionTrampolineRelay` (a last trampoline layer naming `recipient_blinded_paths` is one too: we are the last trampoline node and `NextTrampolinePacket` is null, as in PR 836's trampoline-to-blinded-path vector; TR5 found it refused as a final payload; `NextTrampolinePacket`, `NextNodeId`, `NextPathKey`, `RecipientBlindedPaths`, `AmountToForward`/`OutgoingCltvValue` per part, `IncomingTotal`), `IncomingOnionTrampolineFailed` (create with both secrets). An unreadable trampoline onion (BADONION) is `IncomingOnionFailed` `invalid_onion_payload` on TLV 20 with the outer secret only (no trampoline secret exists); past a blinded introduction node every failure is `IncomingOnionMalformed` `invalid_onion_blinding` with the trampoline packet's sha256 (PR 836 blinded error vector); a blinded introduction node that is not the recipient answers `invalid_onion_blinding` double-wrapped. The replay check stays on the outer packet.
- `HtlcSwitch`: a trampoline final goes through `ReceiveAsync` with `ToFinal()` (the HTLC set counts the outer `amt_to_forward` of each part against the inner total, D-TR4) and a `TrampolineFailureKeys` that every failure path of that HTLC uses (`SendTrampolineFailureAsync`: `TrampolineErrorPackets.Create`/`CreateAttributed`, attribution on the outer layer; malformed past a blinded introduction node); `HtlcSetPart.Trampoline` carries it for `mpp_timeout` and set failures. Only the outer secret is stored (`RecordOnionSecretAsync`); a replay (startup, link-up) re-peels the stored onion with `replayOwner: null`, which yields the trampoline secret again, so no column is needed. A relay part goes to `ITrampolineRelayIngress.HandleNewPartAsync` (resolved from a scope per part, so no construction cycle; called on every replayed lock-in: idempotent), else fails with `temporary_trampoline_failure` double-wrapped (`invalid_onion_blinding` at a blinded introduction node). The failures sent outside the switch are double-wrapped too since NL-897 (next bullet).
- Failures outside the switch and the relay engine (NL-897): `Payments/Switch/TrampolineHtlcFailures` (internal) is the one helper. `ResolveKeysAsync(processor, unitOfWork, channel, htlc)` re-peels the stored onion (`replayOwner: null`, with the HTLC's amount and expiry) into a `TrampolineFailureKeys` (`KeysFrom`: a trampoline final, relay or failed result), else takes the secrets of the HTLC's `TrampolineRelayParts` row (no blinded information), else null (not a trampoline HTLC: the caller's old path, unchanged); `FailAsync` sends `update_fail_malformed_htlc` + `invalid_onion_blinding` past a blinded introduction node, our own `invalid_onion_blinding` at the introduction node of a relay (`TrampolineFailureKeys.IntroductionSha256`, which the switch's `SendTrampolineFailureAsync` now honors too), else the failure created with the trampoline secret then the outer one, with outer-layer attribution (`TrampolineErrorPackets.CreateAttributed`) when an `IAttributionDataService` is given. `HtlcExpiryMonitor.FailBackAsync` resolves the keys first (`temporary_node_failure`); `DustExposureHtlcSwitch` takes them from the onion it already peeled (`temporary_channel_failure`); both take an optional `IAttributionDataService` (last constructor argument) and use it only while `option_attribution_data` is advertised. Tests: `Channels/Safety/HtlcExpiryMonitorTests` and `Channels/Fees/DustExposureHtlcSwitchTests` (region/cases NL-897: a held final part, a relay part re-peeled or from its row, attribution, an ordinary HTLC unchanged; read by the payer with `DecryptTrampolineErrorPacket`), over `Payments/Switch/TrampolineFailureTestKit` (real onions built and read by a payer node). NL-921 (review follow-ups): `KeysFrom` keeps the processor's `invalid_onion_blinding` of an `IncomingOnionTrampolineFailed` (our recipient data refused at a blinded introduction node) as `IntroductionSha256`, as `BlindedHtlcFailures.IsIntroductionForward` does for ordinary HTLCs (the switch builds its keys with `KeysFrom` too); `FromStoredPart` (shared with the relay engine's `GetFailureKeysAsync`) keeps a re-peel's malformed `invalid_onion_blinding` (past a blinded introduction node with route blinding off since) as `BlindedMalformedSha256` when only the row has the secrets; a row whose outer secret is not 32 non-zero bytes gives no keys (the caller's ordinary path), and `FailAsync` never uses such a trampoline secret (outer-only failure, warning); `ResolveAsync` also returns the re-peel, which the monitor reuses for the secret and the introduction check (one peel); the dust switch peels with the HTLC's amount and expiry, so `payment_constraints` are checked. The BOLT 2/4 random delay before an introduction node's `invalid_onion_blinding` is not applied outside the switch (as `BlindedHtlcFailures.FailAsync`). Tests: region NL-921 of both test classes (blinded relay at and past the introduction node, a blinded final whose constraints only the HTLC breaks, route blinding off, zero row secrets, attribution off/on) over the kit's `BuildBlindedRelayAsync`/`BuildBlindedFinalAsync`, and `BlindedTrampolineRelayTests.Given_ARelayPartAtTheIntroductionNodeAddedAfterOurShutdown_*`.
- Invoices: `InvoiceService` sets bit 57 optional and `OfferInvoiceFactory.CreateInvoice(..., allowTrampoline)` (`InvoiceRequestHandler`) sets invoice_features bit 57 when advertised.
- Tests: `Payments/Onion/TrampolineOnionProcessorTests` (real Sphinx and trampoline onions, blinded and dummy trampoline hops), `Payments/Switch/TrampolineReceiveSwitchTests` (ThreeNodeHarness: single part, MPP with one shared trampoline onion and with two legs, errors read by the payer with `DecryptTrampolineErrorPacket` at the trampoline layer, `mpp_timeout` live, replayed after a refused failure and after a restart, the relay seam and its fallback, feature off), `Payments/Switch/TrampolineErrorPacketsTests`, invoice feature tests in `InvoiceServiceTests` and `InvoiceRequestHandlerTests`.

## Trampoline payer and outgoing legs (NL-875, lane TR4; `Payments/Send/PaymentService.Trampoline*.cs`, `Payments/Trampoline/TrampolineOnionFactory`)
- Onions: `PaymentOnionFactory.CreateAsync(route, keysend, TrampolineFinalHop?)` puts TLV 20 (and TLV 12 when given) in the route's last payload with `payment_data` = the route's secret and total (always sent); `TrampolineOnionFactory` builds the inner onion with its own CSPRNG session key, `TrampolineOnionSizePolicy.Auto(max)` where max comes from the planned routes (`GetIntermediateFramedLengthAsync`, `GetTrampolineFinalOtherTlvsLengthAsync`), and the inner payloads per recipient type (2/4/14; 2/4/8(/16); blinded hops 10(+12) and final 2/4/18; 2/4/22(+21)).
- Planner: `PaymentPlanRequest.AbsoluteFinalCltv` (replaces height + `c` + 3; `ExtraCltvDelta` still added) and `MaxFirstHopCltvExpiry` (paths, graph CLTV limit and shadow offset cut to it).
- Legs (`ITrampolineLegSender`, registered by `AddPaymentSendServices`): a session to `NextNodeId` (TLV 20/12, random outer secret, total = `Amount`) or along `RecipientBlindedPaths` (as `PayBlindedAsync`, intro expiry = absolute + path delta), `MaxFee`, MPP only with `AllowMpp`, origin `Trampoline(hash)`, row `IsTrampolineRelay`, no shadow offset. `StartAsync` per hash: a session or an `InFlight` relay row is left alone, a `Succeeded` relay row is reported again, a `Failed` relay row is replaced by a new leg, a row of our own payment is refused (`LocalFailure`). Failed parts of a leg to a trampoline are unwrapped (`UnwrapDownstreamErrorPacket`): an outer hop before the next trampoline retries as any failure; `MustRewrap` ends the leg `DownstreamTrampolineError` with the packet; the next trampoline's own outer error ends it `RouteFailure` + `ErringNodeIsNextTrampoline`. Other ends: deadline → `Timeout`, nothing offered → `NoRoute` (or `LocalFailure` after local refusals), else `RouteFailure`. The observer (`ITrampolineLegObserver`, resolved from DI on first use, `PaymentService.LegObserver` in tests) is called on the thread pool after the save, once per session; sessionless outcomes (restart) and replays of a completed leg's outcome report again, so do the legs `ReconcileInFlightPaymentsAsync` fails.
- Payer: `PayInvoiceOptions.TrampolineNode` or `Node:Payments:Trampoline` (`Never` default / `Auto` = no route of our own / `Always`) with `TrampolineFeeBaseMsat` 1000, `TrampolineFeeProportionalMillionths` 1000, `TrampolineCltvExpiryDelta` 576. A named node and an invoice without bit 56/57 → `ArgumentException`; bit 56 without a trampoline node → `ArgumentException`; payee = trampoline → plain payment; rebalances never. Picking: a peer with a usable channel whose `init` sets the bit (`IPeerManager`, resolved lazily), else a graph node announcing it. The session targets the trampoline node (no shadow; MPP unless the trampoline node is known without `basic_mpp`: the features it advertised in its `init` as a connected peer (`PeerModel.AdvertisedFeatures`, not the negotiated set), else its `node_announcement` in the graph, else assumed, as PR 836 has a trampoline node collect the outer parts; `TrampolineAcceptsMpp`, NL-924); `TrampolinePayerState` holds the attempt (one onion, outer secret and total for every part), the stored row names the payee (its last hop is stored under the payee too, like a blinded route, because `PaymentModel` requires its route to end at the payee; the trampoline node is `PaymentTrampolineHops` hop 0, and that last outer hop's hold time is the trampoline node's: failure reasons name the trampoline node for it, NL-924) and its fee = trampoline fee + routing fees (zero once the payment failed, as for every payment: `PaymentModel.Fail` clears it, NL-982; the last attempt's offer is only logged; a failed row fulfilled late takes the fee of the fulfilled HTLC only when it was the only paying part: a stored `PaymentParts` part whose siblings all failed, or, without stored parts, the row's recorded HTLC whose route delivered exactly the amount; else 0, NL-924). Failures: `DecryptTrampolineErrorPacket`; the outer layer's `attribution_data` is verified over the outer route as for any payment (NL-898: hold times recorded, the outer hop whose HMAC failed named in the reason and, when no hop of either route authenticated the error, blamed to the retry policy; the trampoline layer has none; the same branch of `HandleSessionFailureAsync` also runs for a relay's outgoing leg (`DecideLegFailure`): its leg row keeps the verified hold times and the reason, the leg's outcome is decided as without attribution, NL-924); outer layer → retry policy; the trampoline node's NODE|26 (trampoline or outer layer) caches its policy (`GetCachedTrampolinePolicy`) and starts a new attempt at it (at most `MaxTrampolinePolicyRetries` = 3, within `MaxFee`), NODE|25 one attempt at twice the fee; any other trampoline-layer error stops (`FailureSourceIndex` = outer last + inner index). Each attempt's inner hops are saved in `PaymentTrampolineHops` (next attempt number after the stored ones) before its first offer, and decrypt a failure without a session. BOLT 12: `PayBlindedRequest.RecipientFeatures` (offers: `VerifiedInvoice.Features`; bLIP 39: the invoice's) with bit 57 → blinded hops as trampoline hops (cheapest usable path not introduced by us or the trampoline), else TLV 22/21.
- Tests: `Payments/Trampoline/` (`TrampolineOnionFactoryTests` peel both onions with the real services, `TrampolinePlannerTests`, `TrampolinePaymentHarnessTests` on `PaymentHarness` with `HarnessTrampolineNode` stand-ins for the relay (TR3) and the target (TR2), through `HarnessForwardingSwitch.IncomingInterceptor`).

## HTLC interceptor settle accounting (NL-1182, NL-1205; `Payments/Switch/HtlcSwitch.Interception.cs`)
- An interceptor's SETTLE fulfills the held forward's incoming HTLC through the `stageWithFulfill` overload, which stages `AccountingEventKind.InterceptedHtlcSettled` (8, key `AccountingEventKeys.InterceptedHtlcSettled(channel, htlc)` = `icpt:{channel}:{htlc}:settled`, `PaymentAccountingEvents.StageInterceptedHtlcSettledAsync`: once per HTLC, never throws) in the fulfill's save: `AmountMsat` = the incoming HTLC's amount (no outgoing leg), details `kind=intercepted`, the onion's `outgoingScid`/`nextNodeId`/`amountToForwardMsat`; posted Dr Channels / Cr Received like an invoice, counted as a payment received by the channels report. FAIL books nothing; RESUME is a normal forward (`ForwardSettled`).
- `HtlcOutstandingReader` counts an incoming HTLC by that key (after `ForwardSettled`), so a settle not yet folded is outstanding, not drift. On chain: a claimed HTLC is `valueBookedBy=interceptor` (`OnchainAccounting.InterceptorValueOwner`); `OnchainResolutionExecutor.StageForwardLossAsync` (every call site: peer timeout, given up, trimmed at the close or after it) stages `PaymentAccountingEvents.InterceptedHtlcLostOnchain` for an incoming HTLC without a circuit whose live `InterceptedHtlcSettled` is in the feed and that was not failed: a `ForwardLostOnchain` with cause `upstreamOnchain` under `ForwardLostOnchain(channel, htlc)`, so a reorg or a replaced close reverses it as a forward's.
- Tests: `Payments/Switch/HtlcInterceptionSwitchTests` (settle, fail and resume on `ThreeNodeHarness`), `Payments/Interception/InterceptedHtlcAccountingEventsTests`, `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_AnHtlcTheInterceptorSettled_*`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingReconcileHtlcSettleTests.Given_AnHtlcTheInterceptorSettled*`/`Given_AnInterceptedSettleWithoutItsEvent_*`.

## Onion-routing (BOLT 4) hooks here
The onion library (M1+M2+M3) is used by `LocalOnlyHtlcSwitch` (peel + error onion, see above) and by `Payments/` (above: payload parsing, replay set, final hop, forwarding policy); wiring the payment core into the switch comes with ABCD W2-B. All of these are DI singletons:
- `ISphinxService` (`src/NLightning.Domain/Protocol/Onion/Interfaces/`, registered by `AddBitcoinInfrastructure`).
- `IHopPayloadSerializer` (`src/NLightning.Domain/Serialization/Interfaces/`, registered by `AddSerializationInfrastructureServices`).
- `IOnionReplayStore` (registered by `AddInfrastructureServices` as the persistent `PersistentOnionReplayStore`, NL-078; `IOnionReplayCache` was removed).
- `HopPayloadValidator` is a static Domain class.

`IHopPayloadSerializer` is declared in Domain (`src/NLightning.Domain/Serialization/Interfaces/`), so Application uses it without referencing Serialization.

The receive and send paths below are implemented by `IncomingOnionProcessor` and `PaymentOnionFactory`; they are kept as the reference call order.

Receive path (M4-T2, after the HTLC is irrevocably committed):
1. `var packet = new OnionPacket(add.Payload.OnionRoutingPacket.Span);`
2. `var peeled = sphinx.PeelAsLocalNode(packet, paymentHash, blindedPathTlv?.PathKey);`. Pass the update_add_htlc path_key only, never the payload's current_path_key. It throws `OnionException`:
   - A BADONION code (`ex.FailureCode.IsBadOnion()`) → `update_fail_malformed_htlc` with `ex.FailureData` (sha256_of_onion).
   - `InvalidOnionPayload` → `update_fail_htlc` encrypted with `ex.SharedSecret` (M3).
   - With a path_key, every failure comes back as `InvalidOnionBlinding`.
3. `if (!replayCache.TryAdd(packet.Hmac.Span))`: this is a replay, so fail it. Record the HMAC only after the peel succeeded.
4. `var payload = await hopPayloadSerializer.DeserializeAsync(peeled.Payload);`. Its offsets already count the stripped length prefix.
5. `HopPayloadValidator.Validate(payload, peeled.IsFinal, hasUpdateAddPathKey)`. When update_add_htlc carried a path_key, remap any `OnionException` from here on to `invalid_onion_blinding`.
6. If `peeled.IsFinal`, go to final-hop processing. Otherwise forward `peeled.NextPacket` (`.ToBytes()`) on the channel for `payload.ShortChannelId`.

Keep `peeled.SharedSecret` with the HTLC: failures need it later.

Send path (M4-T6):
- Serialize each `HopPayload` with `IHopPayloadSerializer.SerializeAsync`. That writes no length prefix, and `OnionHop` expects none.
- Call `sphinx.ConstructWithSharedSecrets(hops, sessionKey, paymentHash)` with a fresh CSPRNG session key.
- Keep the per-hop secrets for error decryption (M3).

Other hooks:
- The ChannelManager cases and handlers for UpdateAddHtlc, UpdateFulfillHtlc, UpdateFailHtlc, UpdateFailMalformedHtlc, CommitmentSigned, RevokeAndAck and UpdateFee exist (N6-T1); the onion is peeled only after `IncomingHtlcLockedIn` reaches the switch.
- Forwarding: resolve the next hop by ShortChannelId or its aliases through `IChannelMemoryRepository.FindChannels`. Then call `MessageFactory.CreateUpdateAddHtlcMessage`, which can't attach a BlindedPathTlv yet. Send the result through the `OnResponseMessageReady` event path.
- Failures: `CreateUpdateFailHtlcMessage` expects an already obfuscated `reason`, and no error-onion code exists yet (M3). `CreateUpdateFailMalformedHtlcMessage` takes sha256_of_onion plus a raw ushort failure code; cast from `FailureCode`.

## BOLT 5 remote-commitment resolution (plan O4, ABCD W5-C; `Onchain/Resolvers/`)
- `RemoteCommitResolver : IOutputResolver` (the shared port `Domain/Onchain/Interfaces/IOutputResolver.cs`; a stateless singleton registered by `Remote/RemoteCommitResolutionServiceCollectionExtensions.AddRemoteCommitResolutionServices()` together with `IRemoteSweepDestination` → `WalletRemoteSweepDestination` (an unused P2WPKH wallet address from a scope of its own, handed to the monitor), `IRemoteCommitmentSource` → `ChainRemoteCommitmentSource` (the commitment read from the block at `ChannelCloseModel.SpentAtHeight`, only for data loss or a rebuild whose txid differs) and `RemoteResolutionOptions` (`ReasonableDepth` 6, `IrrevocableDepth` 100); called from `AddApplicationServices` after `AddOnchainServices()`, options bound from `Node:Onchain` by the daemon) handles `ChannelCloseKind.RemoteCommitment`, `RemoteNextCommitment` (the snapshot's `RemoteNextCommit`, B5-RMT-01), `FutureCommitment` (data loss, B5-RMT-03) and, since NL-320, `Unknown` (a funding spend that is no known commitment, handled exactly like data loss with a B5-GEN-06 alert). It reads the channel, HTLC origins, circuits and payments through its own scope, never saves, never publishes and keeps no state: `ResolveAsync(close, outputs, height)` and `OnOutputSpentAsync(close, output, spender, height)` return `OutputResolverAction`s (row upserts, `ResolutionOutput` watches, `BroadcastAction`s, `StageWriteAction`s, `RaiseChannelEventAction`s, `AlertAction`s) that the executor applies in one save. Rows are created on the first round when the watcher did not write them (`OutputDescriptorData` with the peer's per-commitment point of that commitment; keys are re-derived by the signer). The resolver moves rows only between Pending, Waiting, Broadcast and Ignored; the executor marks a spent output Resolved and, 100 blocks on, Irrevocable. Alerts come from the spending witness (at spend time) and from the first data-loss round only.
- Final-hop decision (both resolvers, ABCD W7-B, NL-316/NL-322; `Resolvers/FinalHopClaims.GetFinalHopDecisionAsync`): while an unspent received HTLC output (ours: `LocalReceivedHtlc`; theirs: `RemoteOfferedHtlc`) belongs to an HTLC that is `RcvdAddAckRevocation` without our removal, below its `cltv_expiry`, not forwarded (no origin row, no circuit) and whose hash is an `Open` invoice of ours or has no invoice (keysend: the switch makes the record when it accepts; lane lh1-l3 review), the round raises `IncomingHtlcLockedIn` for it (every round, the switch is idempotent): the switch decides as final hop and persists the preimage on the record, and the next round claims with it. The resolvers read `IInvoiceDbRepository` and `IForwardCircuitDbRepository` for it (test stores: `InMemoryOnchainStore.Invoices`, `LocalCommitResolutionHarness.Invoices`).
- Rules: `to_remote` swept at once (D5; also after data loss through `ICommitmentOutputMapper.FindPaymentToRemote`); our offered HTLCs claimed with `<sig> <>` at `nLockTime = cltv_expiry` once the tip reaches it; the peer's offered HTLCs claimed with `<sig> <preimage>` before `cltv_expiry`, only when irrevocably committed (state >= 34) and only with an allowed preimage: our own fulfill of that HTLC (`HtlcRecord.Removal`), the preimage the switch persisted on it when it accepted it as our final hop (its own `KnownPreimage`, above), or the preimage its forward learnt downstream (the outgoing record, found through `FindHtlcsByOriginAsync(Forwarded(in))` or the circuit, live or archived: `KnownPreimage` or its fulfill). The switch cannot fulfill upstream once the channel is closed, so that downstream record is where the preimage is; never an invoice preimage alone (B5-LCL-RO-02; a final-hop HTLC whose fulfill was refused before the close is claimed once the switch accepted it, above). One transaction per output (fee from `IFeeService` through `SweepFeePolicy.Decide`; an output that does not pay its own fee is `Ignored`, but an ignored HTLC of ours still gets its upstream fulfill (a later preimage spend) or fail (as an HTLC without output)). The planner (`OutputResolutionPlanner`) decides every step.
- Upstream: a preimage of our offered HTLC seen on chain is staged into the HTLC's record (`HtlcRecord.KnownPreimage`, through `IChannelStateDbRepository.ApplyAsync`, and into the loaded channel's snapshot) in the round that raises `OutgoingHtlcFulfilled`, so the switch's own replays (`DerivePending` at link-up/startup) derive the fulfill too; our confirmed timeout claim (or an HTLC without output, B5-RMT-LO-03: at once when no other valid commitment has an output for it, else at `ReasonableDepth`) raises `OutgoingHtlcFailed` with `RemoteHtlcSwitchEvents.OnchainTimeoutKind` (= `HtlcRemovalKind.OnchainTimeout`), or with the peer's own failure when it had sent one. Both are raised again **every round** until the upstream actually has its removal: the incoming HTLC (origin `Forwarded`) has a removal, is final or gone, or its channel is not Open and the circuit is resolved; the payment (origin `Local`) is no longer in flight; an HTLC without a stored origin is raised until its row is irrevocable (the switch only logs a removal it cannot send).
- Data loss: every output of the commitment gets a row and a watch; `to_remote` is swept, the others are `OutputDescriptorKind.Unknown`, and any spend of them is searched (`HtlcWitnessParser.TryExtractPreimage`) for the payment hashes of our still-open offered HTLCs of the snapshot (fulfilled as above). Unknown rows become Ignored only once the commitment is irrevocable and every such HTLC is resolved upstream or expired `IrrevocableDepth` blocks ago. NL-320: every round, each such HTLC whose upstream is not resolved is fulfilled upstream when its record has a preimage, else failed upstream (`OnchainTimeout`, our `permanent_channel_failure`) once the tip is `cltv_expiry + ReasonableDepth` and the close is `ReasonableDepth` deep, so the upstream HTLC is failed well before `Closed` (the old path left it to the switch after `Closed`, past the upstream deadline). A preimage revealed by a later spend is then a loss for a forward (the upstream was failed, as it would have been timed out on chain anyway). Our own payment (origin `Local`) has no upstream deadline: it is failed only at `cltv_expiry + IrrevocableDepth`, and its Unknown rows stay watched until then unless the payment succeeded (a failed payment does not release them), so a preimage the peer reveals meanwhile still makes it succeed.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Onchain/Resolvers/Remote/` (`RemoteResolutionTestContext`: `RealSigningCommitmentPair` engines with real signers, the production mapper/sweep builder, `InMemoryOnchainStore` with EF visibility rules (staged until save; list reads see saved rows only) plus channels, origins, circuits and payments, and a minimal executor that applies each round's actions in one save; every sweep and claim is script-executed against the commitment output). Docker proofs O4 (a)-(e): `test/NLightning.Integration.Tests/Docker/Onchain/OnchainO4Tests.cs` (need the watcher and executor wiring). Final hop (W7-B): `Remote/RemoteFinalHopClaimTests` (decision raised every round for an Open invoice, never claimed before it; claimed with the record's preimage, script-valid, confirmed; accepted between two rounds; a record preimage of another hash never used; no decision for an unknown/settled/canceled invoice, a forward, an expired HTLC), `Local/LocalFinalHopClaimTests` (the same on our commitment with the HTLC-success); Docker `Docker/Onchain/OnchainFinalHopTests` (david pays our invoice in two parts over two channels, the first held, david force-closes its channel, the second completes the set: the part on chain is accepted with the preimage on its record, the invoice settles, our `<sig> <preimage>` claim confirms before `cltv_expiry` and david's first attempt succeeds).

## BOLT 12 offers, receive side (wave B12 lane D, NL-447, plan `docs/agents/BOLT12_PLAN.md` B3; `Offers/`)
- `Offers/OffersServiceCollectionExtensions.AddOffersServices()` (TryAdd, idempotent; `AddApplicationServices` calls it after `AddPaymentsServices()`, whose `BlindedPathBuilder` it uses, and after `AddOnionMessageServices()`; the daemon binds `OfferOptions` from `Offers`): `OfferService` (itself and `IOfferService`), `InvoiceRequestHandler` (an `IOnionMessageHandler` for type 64, built by a factory so a missing `IBolt12Signer` only turns it off), `OfferPathIds`, `InvoiceRequestRateLimiter`, `IBlindedPaymentPathSource` → `BlindedPaymentPathFactory`. Invalid `OfferOptions` fall back to the defaults with an error log.
- `OfferService`: offers are available only with an `IBolt12Signer` registered, the onion-message service on and `option_route_blinding` advertised. `CreateOfferAsync` writes `offer_chains` off mainnet, 16 random bytes of `offer_metadata`, amount (msat only), description, expiry, issuer, quantity max and `offer_issuer_id` = our node id (plan D2); `offer_paths` when no `Open` channel is announced (`ChannelAnnouncementService.IsAnnounced`) or `ForcePaths`: two-hop message paths `peer → us` over the connected onion-message peers (`OnionMessagePathFinder.ListOnionMessagePeers` through `OfferService.SelectIntroductionNodes`: only peers with an `Open` channel, others only when none has one, with a warning log (a path through a peer without a channel dies with its connection: we do not reconnect to it); `Offers:MaxOfferPaths` 2), our hop's `path_id` = `OfferPathIds.Compute(offer_metadata)` (HMAC of a node-key secret; no row per path). No peer when paths are needed → `InvalidOperationException`, nothing stored. `offer_id` = SHA256(offer bytes); saved through `IUnitOfWork.OfferDbRepository` before it returns.
- `InvoiceRequestHandler.ProcessAsync` (see its remarks for the order): nothing is answered before `invreq_payer_id`'s signature verifies (`InvoiceRequestReader`, plan D10); the offer is found by the exact bytes of the request's offer fields (1-79, 1e9-1,999,999,999; `GetByOfferBytesAsync`), unknown → ignored (an answer would reveal which offers are ours, BOLT 12 rationale); the arrival rule (offer with paths: our `path_id`; without: no `path_id`) and the rate limit (`Offers:InvoiceRequestsPerSecondPerOffer` 5 after the offer lookup, `InvoiceRequestsPerSecond` 20 taken by every invoice_request before parsing, `TryTakeGlobal`) are silent; `OfferInvoiceRequestRules` (chain, quantity, amount; a tip above the offer amount is accepted), a disabled or expired offer, the unpaid caps (`MaxUnpaidInvoicesPerOffer` 1,000, `MaxUnpaidInvoices` 10,000; plan D11) and "no payment path" answer `invoice_error` (`erroneous_field` when one field is at fault). The invoice (`OfferInvoiceFactory`): the request's non-signature records copied raw, `BlindedPathBuilder` paths (announced and private channels, lifetime = relative expiry in blocks + `PathLifetimeMarginBlocks` (1,008 since NL-719: Eclair adds 150-350 blocks of random final-expiry delta as payer; 144 refused its payments as above `max_cltv_expiry`), `Routing.InvoiceMinFinalCltvExpiry` in the payinfo) with one `blinded_payinfo` each, `invoice_features` bit 17 with `basic_mpp`, `invoice_node_id` = our node id, one BIP-340 signature by the node key. The `InvoiceModel` (`Bolt12` details, no BOLT 11 string) is saved before the reply goes out through the request's `reply_path` (`IOnionMessageService`, resolved lazily: the service takes every handler).
- Offer invoice paths' `max_cltv_expiry` (NL-723, lane b10-ldk): `BlindedPaymentPathFactory` sets our hop's `max_cltv_expiry` to height + the invoice lifetime in blocks + `Offers:PathLifetimeMarginBlocks` + our final delta. The margin defaults to 1008 (was 144): a payer may add a random shadow CLTV offset to a blinded path's final CLTV, and LDK adds up to 432 blocks (`MAX_SHADOW_CLTV_EXPIRY_DELTA_OFFSET`, a random walk over its graph's deltas), which the old margin refused with `invalid_onion_blinding` once LDK knew a public graph. Existing nodes keep the value their `appsettings.json` was written with. Test: `BlindedPaymentPathFactoryTests.Given_ThePathsOfAnInvoice_When_APayerAddsLdksLargestShadowOffset_*` (walks every hop's constraints); Docker `Interop/Ldk/LdkOfferTests`. A bound value of 144 (the former default the `appsettings.json` template wrote into every node's file) is taken as that old default and raised to 1,008 with one warning (`OfferOptions.UpgradeFormerDefaults` in `AddOffersServices`' `PostConfigure`, NL-743); another value pins a margin. Blinded BOLT 11 invoices (`InvoiceService.BlindedPathLifetimeMarginBlocks`) use the same 1,008. Unannounced channels are named by the peer's `channel_ready` alias whenever it sent one in blinded paths, route hints and a circular payment's incoming candidates alike (`InvoiceService.GetInboundShortChannelId`, NL-717, NL-742). Tests: `Offers/Receive/OfferOptionsUpgradeTests`, `Payments/Send/InvoiceRouteHintTests.Given_APrivateChannelWithoutScidAlias_*`.
- `FinalHopProcessor`: an HTLC for a `Kind = Bolt12` invoice that is not at the end of a blinded route is `incorrect_or_unknown_payment_details` (B3-T4); the blinded path's `path_id` check is M5's.
- The receive side calls the Domain codecs directly (`Bolt12TlvStream.TryParse/Encode/EncodeRecord`, `Bolt12MerkleTree.ComputeRoot`, `Bolt12Bech32.Encode`, `Bolt12FieldCodec.EncodePayInfos/FindUnknownEvenBit`, `Bolt12TlvRanges.IsSignatureField`); the `Bolt12Wire` seam was removed (NL-453). `Offers/Receive/Bolt12CodecTests` pins those calls to `bolt12/signature-test.json`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Offers/Receive/` (`OfferTestKit`: a BIP-340 stand-in `IBolt12Signer` over NBitcoin, an in-memory offer store with the D11 counts, a recording onion-message service, `InvoiceRequestBuilder` that signs like a payer, `ManualClock`).
- Expired invoice pruning (NL-448): `Offers/Receive/ExpiredBolt12InvoicePruner` (singleton from `AddOffersServices()`, `Start()`/`StopAsync()`; the daemon runs it through `ExpiredBolt12InvoicePruneHostedService`, `AddExpiredBolt12InvoicePruning()` in `Daemon/Extensions/OfferIpcServiceExtensions`) calls `IInvoiceDbRepository.PruneExpiredBolt12InvoicesAsync` once at start and every `Offers:ExpiredInvoicePruneInterval` (10 min; zero = off), in batches of `Offers:ExpiredInvoicePruneBatchSize` (500) with one scope and save each, until a batch is short or `MaxBatchesPerRound` (100) ran; a failed round is logged and retried at the next interval. Only `Open` BOLT 12 rows expired by at least `Offers:ExpiredInvoicePruneGrace` (1 h, never less than `Node:Switch:MppTimeout`; `EffectiveGrace`) go: the final hop checks the expiry per HTLC arrival and the settle does not, so an HTLC set held across the expiry must still find its Open invoice. A late HTLC (after the expiry) is refused with `incorrect_or_unknown_payment_details` before and after the prune alike (`ExpiredBolt12InvoicePrunerTests`). `NLightningTestNode` does not run it.

## BOLT 12 offers, payer side (wave B12, lane B12-E; plan `docs/agents/BOLT12_PLAN.md` B4; `Offers/Send/`)
- `OfferSendServiceCollectionExtensions.AddOfferSendServices()` (TryAdd; `AddApplicationServices` calls it after `AddOffersServices()`, and the Daemon's `AddOfferSendIpcServices()` calls it too): `OfferPaymentService` as `IOfferPaymentService`, built by a factory with the optional `IBolt12Signer` (without one `IsAvailable` is false and every fetch is `Unreachable`) and an `OnionMessagePathFinder` (from `IPeerManager`, `IChannelMemoryRepository`, `ISecureKeyManager`, `IGraphStore`) that names the `sciddir_or_pubkey` introduction node of an invoice path.
- `OfferToPay.Parse(text, chain, now)`: the BOLT 12 "Offers" reader (B12-OFR-03), `ArgumentException` whose message starts with the requirement id. `InvoiceRequestFactory.Create(offer, request, chain, signer, metadata?)`: B12-IRQ-01 (every offer record copied raw, unknown ones included; 32 random bytes of `invreq_metadata`; `invreq_chain` only off bitcoin; `invreq_amount` only when the caller gives one, required without `offer_amount` or with `offer_currency` (never converted); `invreq_quantity` exactly with `offer_quantity_max`; `invreq_payer_id` = `IBolt12Signer.DerivePayerId(metadata)`; the note; signed with `SignAsPayer` over the Merkle root) → `BuiltInvoiceRequest` (bytes, metadata, expected invoice amount). `InvoiceVerifier.TryVerify(...)`: B12-INV-03/04/05 (strict stream, ranges, exactly one `signature`, no unknown even type, required fields, chain, `invoice_features` with `basic_mpp` 16/17 the only known even bit, expiry, paths and one pay info per path (paths with an unknown even pay-info bit or an unnamed introduction node dropped, at least one left), exact byte match of fields 0-159 and 1e9-2,999,999,999 with our request, `invoice_node_id` = `offer_issuer_id` else the final blinded id of the offer path we sent to, amount = `invreq_amount` else offer amount x quantity, signature) → `VerifiedInvoice`.
- `OfferPaymentService`: up to `PayOfferOptions.MaxFetchAttempts` (1-10) sends of the same invoice_request (field 64) through `IOnionMessageService.SendAndWaitForReplyAsync` expecting 66/68 within `FetchTimeout`, over the offer's paths in turn (B12-OFR-04), else to `offer_issuer_id`; a timeout, `NoPath`/`Dropped`/`TooLarge` or an invalid invoice moves on to the next attempt; `invoice_error` ends the fetch (`ReadInvoiceError`: `error` and `erroneous_field`); `NotAvailable` is `Unreachable`. `PayOfferAsync` then calls `IPaymentService.PayBlindedAsync` with `PayeeNodeId` = `invoice_node_id`, `AllowMpp` from `basic_mpp`, and `Bolt12PaymentDetails(offer string, invoice bytes, invreq metadata, note)`. No invoice handlers are registered for 66/68: replies reach the waiting caller through the M6 reply registry.
- The payer calls the Domain codecs directly (`Bolt12Bech32.Decode(text, expectedHrp)`/`Encode`, `Bolt12TlvStream.Parse/Encode/EncodeRecord`, `Bolt12MerkleTree.ComputeRoot/GetSignatureDigest`, `Bolt12TlvRanges.IsSignatureField`); the `Bolt12Wire` seam was removed (NL-453). `Offers/Send/Bolt12CodecTests` pins them to `bolt12/format-string-test.json` and `signature-test.json`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Offers/Send/` (`TestBolt12Signer` (BIP-340 over NBitcoin, for the unit tests), `TestOfferIssuer` (writes offers, invoices and invoice_errors like an issuer), `Bolt12CodecTests`, `OfferToPayTests`, `InvoiceRequestFactoryTests`, `InvoiceVerifierTests`, `OfferPaymentServiceTests` (mocked onion messages and payments), `PayBlindedTests` (the `PayBlindedAsync` changes on `ThreeNodeHarness`, including the payment row's BOLT 12 columns through SQLite)), `Offers/OfferHarnessTests` (Proof B4-T4 between two nltg nodes, everything production except the connections: on `ThreeNodeHarness` each node runs `AddOnionMessageServices` over the test's `OnionMessageLinks` (a mocked `IPeerManager` plus an `IPeerOnionMessageOutbox` that hands each message to the receiver's `IOnionMessageService.HandleIncoming`), Carol `AddOffersServices` (lane B12-D's `OfferService` and `InvoiceRequestHandler`), payers `AddOfferSendServices`, every node the real `Bolt12Signer`; Alice pays Carol's offer through Bob (offer path and reply path introduced by Bob), Bob pays as introduction node (B12-PAY-02), an amountless offer with a quantity, Carol restarted after an invoice, a disabled offer's `invoice_error`; Carol needs Bob's `channel_update` handed over by hand, again after a restart, and Bob lists Carol first because a reply path is introduced by the first listed peer, not the one the request leaves through); Docker `Integration.Tests/Docker/Interop/Cln/ClnOfferPayTests` (we pay CLN's offers with the production signer).

## Dual-funded open (BOLT 2 "Channel Establishment v2"; splicing plan wave DF, wave sp1 lane SP1-F; `Channels/DualFunding/`)
- `DualFundingServiceCollectionExtensions.AddDualFundingServices()` (TryAdd; **the integrator adds the call to `AddApplicationServices`** after `AddInteractiveTxServices()`, and the daemon binds `DualFundingOptions` from `Node:DualFund`): `DualFundedOpenService` (singleton, itself and `IDualFundedOpenService`) and `DualFundReestablish` (scoped). `OpenChannel2MessageHandler`/`AcceptChannel2MessageHandler` are on the explicit handler list in `DependencyInjection.cs` (NL-055); `ChannelManager` has the 64/65 cases (65 after `ThrowIfUnknownChannelAsync`: the peer's temporary channel). Without the service both answer an `error` for the (temporary) channel. `option_dual_fund` (`Node:Features:DualFund`) stays experimental until Proof DF is accepted (plan DF3).
- Opener (`OpenAsync`, `openchannel --dual-fund`): the peer's negotiated features (our own without a peer manager, in-process harnesses), keys from the signer, `open_channel2` with `DeriveTemporary` as temporary id, our `ChannelParty`, the channel type as for v1 (anchors when negotiated, `option_scid_alias` for a private channel), an empty `upfront_shutdown_script`, locktime = the tip; a placeholder `ChannelModel` (V1Opening, `ChannelVersion.V2`) is the temporary channel (`IChannelManager.StartOpeningChannelAsync`). `accept_channel2` (`HandleAcceptChannel2Async`, under the temporary lock): the echoed channel type (else `error`), the v1 mandatory checks, the reserve `DualFundingRules.GetChannelReserve(total, max dust)` on both sides, the real `ChannelModel` under `ChannelIdV2.Derive` (`IChannelMemoryRepository.UpgradeChannel`), then the driver starts as initiator with an `InteractiveTxContributionRequest` (purpose `DualFund`, our share, `GetOpenerExtraWeight`). `OpenAsync` completes once both `tx_signatures` were exchanged and the funding was published, or with a `FailureReason` (the peer's `error` on either id through `IPeerService.OnAttentionMessageReceived`, an abort, `DualFundingOptions.OpenTimeout` 2 min). A failed open is aborted through the driver (`tx_abort`, the reservation released, also after our `commitment_signed`) and forgotten (a stored channel persisted `Stale`) only while no attempt is fully signed and our `tx_signatures` were not sent (`CanForget`); after them the channel is kept in `V1FundingSigned` with its funding watched, the result's reason says so, and the peer's later `tx_signatures` still complete and publish it (wave sp1 review: the peer holds our witnesses). The peer's warnings reach the same event as its errors (`AttentionMessageEventArgs` does not tell them apart), so a warning also ends an unsigned open. Nothing fails the channel on a peer `error` yet (no general error handling for channels).
- Accepter (`AcceptAsync`; contribution `DualFundingOptions.AcceptContributionSat`, 0 by default, capped at the opener's with `MatchOpenerContribution`): `option_dual_fund` negotiated, not halted (NL-216), the public-channel gates of v1, `DualFundingRules.CheckOpenChannel2`, the v1 mandatory checks, the NL-379 anchors reserve (`EnsureCanAcceptAnchorsChannelAsync`, released when the open ends or the funding is published); the driver starts as non-initiator with our share, or with none when the wallet cannot fund it (BOLT 2 recommends going on); `accept_channel2` echoes the temporary id and channel type and carries `second_per_commitment_point`; when our share dropped to 0 the reserve and our in-flight limit are recomputed from the announced total (BOLT 2: 1% of both `funding_satoshis`). The accepter's negotiation must reach our `commitment_signed` within `OpenTimeout` (`ScheduleAccepterTimeout`, on the service's optional `TimeProvider`; cancelled by `Dispose`): otherwise it is aborted with our `tx_abort` and forgotten, releasing the anchors reserve.
- `DualFundHost : IInteractiveTxHost` (one per channel, kept across RBF attempts so the driver keeps the completed ones; purpose `DualFund`, `DualFundRbf` once an attempt is signed): shared spec = no shared input, the P2WSH 2-of-2 funding output of both shares (`GetFundingScript`, Infrastructure.Bitcoin's `FundingOutput`); commitment step = the constructed transaction's funding outpoint on the channel, the signer registration (an RBF keeps the first one: the signer refuses another outpoint until SP1-C's per-funding API; the BIP 143 sighash commits to the outpoint, and the signatures are right only because the signer takes the prevout from the transaction it is given), our signature of the peer's commitment 0 (zero HTLCs, `funding_txid` TLV), and the channel staged (`AddAsync` in `V1FundingSigned`, `UpdateAsync` for an RBF) with the funding watch and the `FundingOutput` outpoint watch of that attempt in the driver's save before our `commitment_signed` ("MUST remember"; tracked in the monitor by a background task once that save is done), so a funding the peer broadcasts after our `tx_signatures` is seen even when its own `tx_signatures` never arrive; completion = the `Funding` `BroadcastTransactions` row (and the watches when a row stored by an older build lacks them), earlier attempts' rows `MarkReplacedAsync`, and after the save (a background task that takes the channel lock once the handler released it and checks the driver kept the completion) `TrackWatched*`, `IChainBroadcaster.PublishAsync`, `UpdateChannel` and the open's completion (`WhenIdleAsync` for tests). From there the v1 path runs unchanged: the funding confirmation sends `channel_ready` and the first `channel_ready` builds the snapshot from `LastReceivedSignature`. An abort before our `tx_signatures` forgets the channel (a stored one is persisted `Stale`); after them IT-ABT-01 keeps it.
- The peer's first `commitment_signed`: `ChannelManager`'s 132 case asks `DualFundedOpenService.TryHandleCommitmentSignedAsync` first (null = normal operation): DF-CS-01, the `funding_txid` TLV, `ValidateSignature` against our commitment 0 (a failure is our `tx_abort`, never a channel failure), `LastReceivedSignature` staged, then `IInteractiveTxDriver.OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync` (its save, then our `tx_signatures` in the IT-SIG-01 order); a repeat is ignored.
- RBF (`BumpAsync`, either role since lane accrbf (NL-530; BOLT 2 "Fee bumping": the sender "MAY be either the initiator or the accepter" and is the new attempt's interactive-tx initiator: it adds the funding output and pays the common fields, while the opener still pays the commitment fee), before `channel_ready`; a peer's `tx_init_rbf` is followed in either role), **on by default** since lane dfrbf (`Node:DualFund:AllowRbf`, owner decision 2026-09-28, after NL-528): with it false `BumpAsync` throws and the peer's `tx_init_rbf` gets `tx_abort`. Refused anyway once an attempt's funding watch has a confirmation (BOLT 2: abandon the RBF when the previous transaction confirms) or `channel_ready` was sent or received (a deferred one included). Our `tx_init_rbf` that ends before an attempt exists (the peer's `tx_abort`, a simultaneous `tx_init_rbf`, our attempt that cannot be built after `tx_ack_rbf`, a disconnection) reaches the host through `IInteractiveTxHost.OnRbfRequestEndedAsync`, so `BumpAsync` returns the reason at once (NL-527; the splice host keeps the no-op default: its bump ends with the quiescence). Otherwise: the IT-RBF-01 floor (`InteractiveTxDriver.GetMinimumRbfFeeratePerKw`), our contribution as initiator (`CreateInitiatorRbfContributionAsync`: `DualFundingRules.RebuildContributionForFeerate(..., isInitiator: true, ...)` over the same inputs and reservation, or, for an accepter none of whose inputs is in the earlier attempts, fresh wallet inputs for its share plus `GetOpenerExtraWeight`, a share of 0 included (`InteractiveTxContributionRequest.FundWeightWithoutAmount`); that fresh reservation, `DualFundNegotiation.FreshRbfContribution`, is released when the request ends before an attempt exists), `RequestRbfAsync` published under the lock. The opener must contribute; the accepter may pass 0. CLN v26.06.8 answers an accepter's `tx_init_rbf` with `tx_abort` "Only the channel initiator is allowed to initiate RBF". The peer's `tx_init_rbf` (`DecideRbfAsync`) is accepted with our rebuilt contribution (or, when we contributed nothing before and `AcceptContributionSat` wants a share, a fresh one from the wallet, still 0 when it cannot fund it). Either contribution may change in an RBF (BOLT 2, NL-521): the opener's in `tx_init_rbf` (`BumpAsync(channelId, feerate, localContribution)` sends a new one of ours from the same inputs) and the accepter's in `tx_ack_rbf`, which the driver hands to the host before the attempt is built (`IInteractiveTxHost.OnRbfAcknowledgedAsync`, default: nothing; a refusal is our `tx_abort` and `OnAbortedAsync`). A new contribution is checked by `GetRbfShareViolation` (not negative, not above 21M BTC, a funding output above the dust limit, the opener's share paying the first commitment's fee and anchors, `option_support_large_channel` for a large one; `tx_abort` otherwise) and kept in `DualFundNegotiation.ChangeShares` (the signed shares in `SharesBeforeRbf`, put back when the attempt ends unsigned). At the commitment step an RBF attempt moves the channel's funding output, capacity, both balances, the reserve (1% of the new capacity, at least the dust limit) and our in-flight limit (`ChannelModel.ReplaceUnconfirmedFunding`, before the first snapshot only; `WithCapacity`), all restored with the outpoint and signatures when the attempt ends unsigned (`LastSignedFunding`, rebuilt from the latest signed row after a restart mid-RBF, NL-528; `RestoreLastSignedFundingAsync` also moves the signer back). The signer signs an RBF attempt as a pending funding (`RegisterFunding`, `SignChannelTransaction`/`ValidateSignature` by funding txid; the BIP 143 sighash commits to the funding amount), and a completed one becomes its current funding (`LockFunding`); the `Initial` `ChannelFundings` row follows the channel (`ChannelDbRepository.EnsureInitialFundingAsync`). Any signed attempt may confirm (NL-528): each `InteractiveTxSessions` row stores our share (`LocalFundingSatoshis`, from the `SharedFundingSpec`) and the peer's signature of our first commitment (`TheirCommitmentSignature`, passed to `OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync`); `OnFundingConfirmedAsync` (from `ChannelManager.ConfirmFundingAsync`, before the confirmation is applied) abandons a running attempt and moves the channel to the attempt that confirmed (outpoint, capacity, balances, reserve, stored signature, our signature again, the signer through `MoveSignerToFunding` (`LocalLightningSigner.LockFunding` accepts a replaced initial funding of an unconfirmed open), its broadcast row pending and the others replaced), or ignores a confirmation it cannot follow (logged critical); the peer's `channel_ready` of an open with several signed attempts waits in memory (`TryDeferChannelReadyAsync`) until our confirmation, which applies it (`TakeDeferredChannelReady`) so the first commitment state is built on the followed attempt; `ConfirmUnconfirmedChannels` also looks at the other attempts' watches; the losing attempts' own wallet inputs are released by `DiscardedSpliceReservations` once the confirmed funding is irrevocable. A public dual-funded open may be bumped (its announcement comes from the followed funding).
- An earlier attempt confirming during an RBF (NL-867; BOLT 2 "If the previous transaction confirms in the middle of an RBF attempt, the attempt MUST be abandoned"): abandoned at the attempt's first confirmation, not at the funding depth. `ChannelManager`'s block handler calls `DualFundedOpenService.ScheduleConfirmedAttemptRound` and `SpliceService.ScheduleConfirmedAttemptRound`: under each channel's lock, a running attempt (or our unanswered `tx_init_rbf`) whose signed sibling's `WatchedTransactions` row has a first-seen height (a splice: also a pending funding's `ConfirmedHeight`) gets our `tx_abort` through `IInteractiveTxDriver.AbortAsync` (which now also tells the host that our `tx_init_rbf` ended), so the reservation and the sale slot go back, the attempt's stored purchase is replaced (`ReplaceAbandonedPurchaseAsync`) and the waiting `bumpopen`/`bumpsplice` returns "an earlier attempt <txid> confirmed"; nothing after our `tx_signatures` (IT-ABT-01). Before our `tx_signatures` the driver asks the host (`IInteractiveTxHost.GetTxSignaturesRefusalAsync`, both hosts check the siblings) and turns the contributor's `InteractiveTxInputsSpentException` (Domain; an input still reserved but gone from the wallet) into `tx_abort`, never an exception that fails the connection. `channel_reestablish` abandons such an attempt before planning (`DualFundReestablish.AbandonConfirmedRbfAttemptAsync`, `SpliceService.AbandonConfirmedRbfAttemptAsync`), so the peer's `next_funding` gets our `tx_abort` instead of the `commitment_signed` again. A splice RBF is refused (ours) or answered with `tx_abort` (the peer's) once an attempt has a confirmation (`SpliceRbfConditions.AttemptConfirmed`). Tests: `Channels/DualFunding/DualFundRbfConfirmedAttemptTests`, `Channels/Splicing/SpliceRbfConfirmedAttemptTests`.
- Restart and `channel_reestablish` (DF2; one path with splices since SP2-A): `GetOrLoadAsync` rebuilds the negotiation of a V2 `V1FundingSigned` channel from its stored `InteractiveTxSessions` rows and `ResumeAsync`s the driver. The reestablish planner decides `next_funding` from the latest session row for a dual-funded open as for a splice (ours: sent `commitment_signed` and no `tx_signatures` received, bit 0 when the peer's `commitment_signed` is missing; the peer's: our `commitment_signed` again when bit 0 is set, our stored `tx_signatures` (`CreateTxSignaturesRetransmission`), `tx_abort` for another txid, and when we set `next_funding` too and the txids differ `ChannelFailedException` without broadcast, `SP-RE-03`). `DualFundReestablish` (scoped, optional handler argument) only loads the negotiation (`EnsureLoadedAsync`) and rebuilds the open's `commitment_signed` (`CreateCommitmentSignedRetransmissionAsync`, RFC 6979: the same signature, only for the pending txid).
- Simple taproot opens (taproot wave t02 lane V2, BOLTs PR #1324 as Eclair 0.14.3 speaks it): `DualFundedOpenRequest.SimpleTaproot` (the seam `openchannel --channel-type taproot` sets on a v2 open) sends channel type {80} (+46 private), refused unless `option_simple_taproot` is negotiated and for `--public`; liquidity ads go with it in both roles and its RBF (t03 lane RBF, NL-971: `CheckWillFund` over the MuSig2 P2TR script of both funding keys, the first commitment's fee in the taproot format); the accepter takes a taproot `open_channel2` when `ChannelOpenValidator` accepts it (negotiated, not announced). The funding output is the MuSig2 P2TR (`FundingScriptOf`). The driver asks the host for `commit_nonces` on every `tx_complete` it sends (`IInteractiveTxHost.GetLocalCommitNonces` over the transaction negotiated so far; none while it has no input or output) and hands the peer's last ones to `AcceptRemoteCommitNonces` before the commitment step (missing: `tx_abort` `MissingCommitNonce`). Our first `commitment_signed` is a MuSig2 partial signature against the peer's current nonce; the peer's is checked against our nonce and stored as `ChannelModel.LastReceivedPartialSignature` (the session row keeps no signature); the first commitment state takes the peer's next nonce from its `channel_ready`, which must carry it as in a v1 open (TAPROOT-CR-R01; the `tx_complete` next nonce is not kept, NL-969). `channel_reestablish` type 24: `ReestablishService.CreateOwnAsync` sends ours while the peer's `commitment_signed` is missing, `DualFundReestablish.ReceiveCurrentCommitNonceAsync` keeps the peer's (one that does not parse as two points counts as missing), and the retransmitted `commitment_signed` is re-signed against it with a fresh nonce; without a usable one our `tx_abort` answers while our `tx_signatures` are not sent (`AbortForMissingCommitNonceAsync`, NL-969). RBF of a taproot open works both ways (t03 lane RBF, NL-970): every attempt's `tx_complete` nonces and our partial signature are bound to its txid, the peer's partial signature of our commitment 0 is stored with the attempt (`InteractiveTxSessions.TheirCommitmentPartialSignature`, migration `AddDualFundTaprootAttempts`; `IInteractiveTxDriver.OnCommitmentSignedReceivedAsync`'s `theirCommitmentPartialSignature`), and `OnFundingConfirmedAsync`/`RestoreLastSignedFundingAsync` put it back on the channel (`LastReceivedPartialSignature`, our `LastSentSignature` the zero signature) for whichever attempt the channel follows, so the force close aggregates it with a fresh signing of ours; an RBF is refused when a signed attempt's row has no partial signature (an older build's). A pending open's `channel_reestablish` `next_local_nonces` carry every signed attempt plus the funding output (`TaprootChannelNonces.CreatePendingOpenNonces`, `ReestablishService.GetSignedOpenAttemptsAsync`), and the peer's must cover every signed attempt and the pending one only when its `next_funding` names it (Eclair's `checkCommitNonces`). Tests: `DualFundTaprootRbfTests`. The signer binds a dual-funded channel's commitment-0 nonce to its funding txid (`ChannelSigningInfo.IsDualFunded`, NL-972; before registration the key index overload `GetLocalVerificationNonce(keyIndex, txid, n)` gives the same nonce). Tests: `DualFundTaprootTests`.
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/DualFunding/` (`DualFundHarness`: two production `ChannelManager`s with the dual-funding service, the Domain interactive-tx engine, real signers, the HTLC switch and invoices, each on its own SQLite database, over the test wallet/builder/inspector of `InteractiveTx/TestDoubles`; `DualFundHarnessTests` is the proof: both contribute, `commitment_signed` both ways with zero HTLCs, `tx_signatures`, the channel persisted `V1FundingSigned` with a Signed session and the funding published on both sides, confirmation, `channel_ready`, payments both ways; RBF with the floor refused and the replacement confirmed; restarts before the second `commitment_signed` (next_funding bit 0) and before the first `tx_signatures` (retransmitted); `DualFundRefusalTests`: no `option_dual_fund`, an accepter without funds, an HTLC signature in the first `commitment_signed`, the reserve of an accepter without funds; `DualFundRbfContributionTests` (NL-521): the accepter's new contribution in `tx_ack_rbf` and the opener's in `tx_init_rbf` followed end to end (capacity, balances, reserve, the `Initial` funding row, payments both ways), a negative `tx_ack_rbf` contribution and an opener share below the commitment fee refused with `tx_abort`, an attempt with a new contribution aborted after both `commitment_signed` restored to the old capacity; `DualFundSafetyTests`: the open kept after our `tx_signatures` on a timeout or a peer error (and completed by the late `tx_signatures`), aborted through the driver before them, RBF on by default (off refuses both ways) and allowed for a public channel, an RBF aborted after both `commitment_signed` restored, the accepter's timeout, mismatched `next_funding`; `DualFundRbfEndTests` (NL-527: the peer's `tx_abort` or a disconnection ends `BumpAsync` at once); `DualFundRbfFollowTests` (NL-528: an earlier attempt confirming followed in both roles, the peer's early `channel_ready` deferred, a restart during an RBF attempt the peer forgot, three attempts through restarts of both nodes); `DualFundAccepterRbfTests` (NL-530: the accepter bumps as interactive-tx initiator with both sides' inputs re-added, with a fresh contribution, fee-only with share 0, followed when the first attempt confirms, after its restart, a restart during its attempt, and the refusals; `DualFundNode.AllowRbfOverride` sets one node's `AllowRbf` from its next start); the harness takes `allowRbf` (default true, as in production) and `withPeerServices` (a mocked peer manager and `IPeerService` per node to raise the peer's error); `DualFundHandlerTests`); Docker Proof DF `Integration.Tests/Docker/Interop/Cln/ClnDualFundTests`.

## Interactive-tx wallet contributor (splicing plan IT2-T3, wave IT lane IT-B; `InteractiveTx/WalletInteractiveTxContributor.cs`)
- `WalletInteractiveTxContributor : IInteractiveTxContributor` (**singleton**, `InteractiveTxContributorServiceCollectionExtensions.AddInteractiveTxContributorServices()`, TryAdd, as itself and as the interface; the integrator calls it after `AddInteractiveTxBitcoinServices()`). It needs `IFeeInputSelector`, `ILightningSigner`, `IUtxoMemoryRepository`, the Domain ports `IWalletPrevTxSource`, `IPrevTxInspector` and `IInteractiveTxTransactionParser` (all from Infrastructure.Bitcoin's `AddInteractiveTxBitcoinServices`), and optionally `IServiceScopeFactory` (the stored negotiations, `IUnitOfWork.InteractiveTxSessionDbRepository`) and `IAnchorReserveService`. It no longer uses any Infrastructure.Bitcoin type. `ContributeAsync`: no wallet input without a positive `WalletAmount` (the request's outputs are returned alone: a splice-out paid from the channel balance, plan D16); otherwise one `IFeeInputSelector` reservation (purpose `itx:<purpose>:<channel id>`; mined P2WPKH/P2TR outputs only, so `require_confirmed_inputs` always holds, re-checked against the UTXO set when asked) for wallet amount + outputs, charged `ExtraWeight` + our outputs + our inputs each at least the minimum witness weight 107 (BOLT 3 Appendix G "Expected Fee Calculation"; a P2TR input weighs 231 but the peer charges 271; a short reservation is released and retried with more weight, 3 attempts) + a P2WPKH change, which is kept only at 294 sat or more (else fee). Each input's `prevtx` comes from `IWalletPrevTxSource` and must inspect to the same txid, amount and script; a `prevtx` longer than `MaxPrevTxLength` (65,483 = 65,535 - the other `tx_add_input` fields) cannot go on the wire (u16 `prevtx_len`), so its reservation is held while the selector picks again (at most 3 times) and released at the end; none left → `InvalidOperationException`. The anchors reserve is checked after reserving (held reservations released first), as for a withdraw. Any failure releases the reservation.
- `SignAsync`: the constructed transaction's bytes must be what its metadata says (parsed with `IInteractiveTxTransactionParser`: txid, version 2, locktime, each input's outpoint and sequence, each output's amount and script, in ascending `serial_id` order, no witness), and it must contain each of our inputs once, as ours, with our sequence, and each of our outputs (amount and script) as ours, else `InvalidOperationException` and nothing signed; then `SignWalletTransaction(tx, reservationId, otherSpentOutputs)` (only that reservation's inputs; P2TR needs the other spent outputs) and our witnesses in ascending `serial_id` order.
- IT-ABT-01, durable: with a session store, `SignAsync` refuses a reservation that no stored, unresolved, not `Aborted` negotiation holds (`LocalContribution.ReservationId`): the driver must store the negotiation (from our `commitment_signed`, BOLT 2) before it asks for our witnesses. `ReleaseAsync` refuses (logged) while such a stored negotiation holds the reservation, also after a restart, and while this process signed it; the driver marks the negotiation `Aborted` (saved) before releasing. `ConfirmAsync` ends it once the selector sees an input spent. Without a store (no scope factory, or a unit of work whose `InteractiveTxSessionDbRepository` throws `NotSupportedException`) only the in-process guard applies (warning logged at signing). Release, sign and confirm run one at a time (one gate), so a release never frees a reservation a concurrent signature is using. `ReleaseOrphanedReservationsAsync` (host, once at startup, before negotiations): releases every `itx:` reservation that no stored negotiation holds and this process did not hand out; without a store it releases nothing.
