Not done (ledger follow-up): a signed negotiation that can never confirm (the peer double-spent its input) keeps its reservation until an input is spent; there is no "abandon by double-spending our own inputs" path yet. `ReleaseDiscardedAsync(discarded, keptOutpoints)` (NL-492, wave spr): releases, without the stored-negotiation guard, every reservation that holds one of our non-shared inputs of the discarded transaction, whole (reservations are all or nothing), and only when each of its other inputs is also ours in that transaction or a kept outpoint the wallet no longer holds; a reservation with any other input, or a kept outpoint still in the wallet, is kept (logged); so is a reservation that is not ours (`itx:` purpose), that a stored unresolved negotiation of another transaction holds, or that this process handed out to a negotiation that has not signed, so a repeated call never frees another spend that reserved the outputs a first call gave back; returns the outputs given back (still in the wallet); idempotent; call it only once the conflict is irrevocable (the executor's `DiscardedSpliceReservations` does).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/InteractiveTx/WalletInteractiveTxContributorTests` (real `FeeInputSelector`, `LocalLightningSigner`, `UtxoMemoryRepository`, `PrevTxInspector`, `InteractiveTxTransactionParser` and `InteractiveTxBuilder`, a mocked session store; every signature script-checked; P2WPKH and P2TR; prevtx at and over the limit; the stored-session guard across a restart; the startup sweep; release racing a signature; bytes that are not the described transaction).

## Keysend (spontaneous payments, wave lh1 lane l3; `Payments/Keysend/`)
- No feature bit (keysend has none; LND's `--accept-keysend` changes no bit). Options `Node:Keysend` (`NodeOptions.Keysend`, Domain `KeysendOptions`, validated with `NodeOptions`): `Accept` (default **true**, as CLN; LND defaults off: accepting only adds money to our side and reveals the payer's own preimage), `MinFinalCltvExpiryDelta` (18, at least 12: the record's `min_final_cltv_expiry_delta`), `FinalCltvExpiryDelta` (40, our sends), `RecordExpirySeconds` (86,400).
- Send: `IPaymentService.PayKeysendAsync(PayKeysendRequest(destination, amount) { CustomRecords }, PayInvoiceOptions)`: CSPRNG preimage, hash = SHA256; a `PaymentTarget` with no hints or secret, `SupportsMpp` false, max parts 1 (no split: keysend has no standard multi-part form and LND refuses keysend with `payment_data` total above the amount); routed like an invoice (direct channel, else the graph). `PaymentSession.Keysend` (`KeysendFinalRecords`: preimage + custom records) goes to `PaymentOnionFactory.CreateAsync(route, keysend)`: the payee's payload is `amt_to_forward`, `outgoing_cltv_value`, `keysend_preimage` (5482373484) and the custom records, **no** `payment_data`. The row is stored with `PaymentModel.Keysend` (its custom records); `Bolt11`/`Bolt12` null.
- Receive: `HtlcSwitch.ReceiveAsync`, under the payment hash lock, when no invoice exists for the hash and the final payload is keysend (`KeysendReceiver.IsKeysend`: `keysend_preimage`, not blinded), asks `KeysendReceiver.TryCreateInvoice` (refused: `Accept` off, a preimage that is not 32 bytes or does not hash to the payment hash; logged, and the HTLC then fails as an unknown hash) for an unsaved `InvoiceKind.Keysend` record (any amount, the payer's preimage, the payload's custom records (types >= 65536 other than the preimage)); `FinalHopProcessor` checks the HTLC against it (keysend branch: `payment_data` optional and its secret not checked, the onion's preimage must equal the record's, single part, the record's CLTV delta); only an accepted HTLC saves the record (`SaveKeysendRecordAsync`, own save, before the set is fulfilled), then it settles like any invoice. A keysend HTLC on a channel closing on chain before it was accepted has no record yet: `FinalHopClaims.GetFinalHopDecisionAsync` raises the decision for a hash with no invoice too, so the switch accepts it (record saved and settled, preimage on the HTLC's record) and the resolvers claim it; a refusal persists nothing and the HTLC times out on chain. An existing record (a retry with the same preimage after a failed attempt, or a replay) is reused; a Settled one refuses a duplicate as NL-323.
- Custom records are stored without a schema change in the `Bolt12InvoiceBytes` column of `Invoices`/`Payments` (see the repositories' remarks; seam for a dedicated column).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Payments/Keysend/` (`KeysendReceiveTests`: receiver + processor rules; `KeysendOnionTests`: the onion peeled by the production processor; `KeysendHarnessTests`: Alice and Bob keysend each other on `ThreeNodeHarness` (SQLite), a refusing payee, invalid records). Docker: `Integration.Tests/Docker/KeysendFlowTests` (LND 0.21 alice with `--accept-keysend`, both directions with an even and an odd custom record, and a wrong preimage refused).

## Rebalances: paying our own invoice (NL-609)
- `PaymentService.PayInvoiceAsync` pays an invoice signed with our node key over a **circular route** instead of refusing it: the invoice must be in our table and `Open` (else `ArgumentException`), its route hints are dropped (`PaymentTarget with { RouteHints = [] }`), and the session (`PaymentSession.IsCircular`) plans each round with `PaymentPlanRequest.IncomingChannels`: the usable channels (`Open`, snapshot, link up) whose peer's `channel_update` we hold through the optional `IChannelUpdateService` (not disabled), under the scid the peer forwards over (its alias for an alias channel, as in our route hints), with its fee, CLTV delta, htlc limits and `InvoiceService.GetPeerSpendable` as `ReceivableMsat` (`Routing/IncomingChannelCandidate`). `PayInvoiceOptions.OutgoingChannelId` (any payment) and `IncomingChannelId` (only our own invoice) pin the channels; the same channel both ways, a pin that is not our channel, an incoming pin for another node's invoice and pins on a bLIP 39 blinded invoice are refused (`ArgumentException`, nothing persisted). No circular route in the first round: stored `Failed` (reason "No circular route back to us: ...") as any unroutable payment. Keysend and `PayBlindedAsync` to ourselves stay refused.
- `PaymentRoutePlanner` (circular when the payee is `OurNodeId`; without `IncomingChannels` it throws): `BuildCircularPaths` (another usable channel of ours to the incoming channel's peer, then the last hop [peer, scid, peer policy] with a `HopLimit(htlcMin, htlcMax, receivable)`; never out over the incoming channel) and, with a graph, `BuildCircularGraphPaths` (the pathfinder from us to the incoming peer for the amount plus the last hop's fee and with its CLTV delta added to the final delta, our incoming channel left out of `LocalChannels` and excluded, then the last hop appended). Failure-learnt constraints (`ExcludedNodes`/`ExcludedChannels`/`PolicyOverrides` by scid) apply to the last hop; single part, graph and `basic_mpp` split rules are unchanged. Shared helpers `BuildGraphLocals`, `OwnFirstHopEdges`, `GraphHops`.
- The incoming side is the ordinary final hop: `HtlcSwitch.SettleInvoiceAsync` flags its `InvoiceSettled` with `selfPayment=true` when a payment row for the hash is `InFlight` or `Succeeded` (`IsOurOwnPaymentAsync`, a read failure only logged); `PaymentService` flags `PaymentSucceeded` as before (`IsOurInvoiceAsync`). The memo backfill flags a settled invoice whose payment `Succeeded`. The operational books (Domain `AccountingPostingRules`) then post the rebalance as: `PaymentSucceeded` self: Cr Channels (a + fee), Dr Rebalance (a + fee); `InvoiceSettled` self: Dr Channels a, Cr Rebalance a: net Rebalance = the route fee, no `Received`/`Sent`/`RoutingFees`. The channels report does not count a self `InvoiceSettled` as a payment received.
- **One self-payment rule for both halves (NL-670):** `Payments/SelfPaymentRule` (`IsSelfPayment` for the paying side and the backfill, `IsSettledBySelfPayment` (payment `InFlight`/`Succeeded`) for the receiving side): same hash, the invoice is a BOLT 11 invoice of ours (never a keysend record or a BOLT 12 invoice), the payment is no keysend, and **its payee is our node id**. A hash match alone is not enough: a peer that learned an invoice's preimage can bill us on the same hash, and a keysend received on the hash of a payment we made is income. `PaymentService` reads our id from `ISecureKeyManager`, `HtlcSwitch` takes an optional `ISecureKeyManager` (without one nothing is flagged), the backfill resolves it from its scope. Tests: `Payments/SelfPaymentRuleTests`, `PaymentServiceTests.Given_AnotherNodesInvoiceOnTheHashOfOurInvoice_*`.
- Tests: `Payments/Routing/CircularRoutePlannerTests` (same-peer route, never the same channel, no incoming channel, triangle through the graph, excluded incoming channel, htlc max and receivable limits, fee limit, split over two incoming channels), `Payments/Send/RebalanceHarnessTests` (`ThreeNodeHarness.CreateAsync(carolAlice: true)` adds the Carol-Alice channel; Bob with a mocked `IGraphStore` of the triangle pays his own invoice out over Bob-Carol and back over Alice-Bob, pinned the other way, fails with no way back, refuses a settled invoice and bad pins; balances, feed events and the books checked with `BooksSimulator`).

## Labels and tags at the source (NL-602 A3-T1)
The services store the checked `SourceLabels` (Domain CLAUDE.md "Labels and tags at the source") on the row in the row's own first save: `InvoiceService` (labelled `CreateInvoiceAsync` overload), `PaymentService` (`PaymentSession.Labels` from `PayInvoiceOptions.Labels`, set on every `PaymentModel` it creates: a round's row, a retry's replacement row and a row failed before any HTLC; bolt11, keysend, blinded and BOLT 12 payments alike), `OfferService` (the offer row; never in the offer's bytes), `InvoiceRequestHandler` (a BOLT 12 invoice copies its offer's `Label`/`Tags` when it is issued, so the plan's "a BOLT 12 invoice from its offer" is read from the invoice row: offers created before A3-T1 have no labels, so no fallback lookup is needed at settle), `DualFundedOpenService` (the placeholder channel takes them and the channel built at `accept_channel2` copies them before its first `AddAsync`); the v1 open sets them in the Daemon's `OpenChannelClientHandler` on the factory's model, before `funding_signed`'s first save. `Channels.Label/Tags` are never written by `ChannelDbRepository.UpdateAsync`, so a channel keeps what its first save stored. The writers copy them into the event details with `SourceLabels.FromStored(row.Label, row.Tags).ToDetailPairs()` spliced into `AccountingDetailsCodec.Create([...])`: `PaymentAccountingEvents.InvoiceSettled` (from the invoice), `PaymentSucceeded`/`PaymentFailed` (from the payment), `ChannelAccountingEvents.BuildChannelFundedAsync` (from the channel; the push event carries none) and, in Infrastructure.Bitcoin, `BlockchainMonitorService.CollectWalletSent` (from the `WalletSend` broadcast row; the wallet movements of the same transaction carry none). The backfill's memo events use the same builders, so they carry the rows' labels too. Readers (A3-T3 classification) use `SourceLabels.FromDetails`. Tests: `Payments/Send/PaymentHarnessTests` (labelled pay), `Offers/OfferHarnessTests` (labelled offer and payoffer over SQLite), `Channels/DualFunding/DualFundHarnessTests` (labelled v2 open), `Payments/Invoices/InvoiceServiceTests`; the test double `InMemoryPaymentDbRepository` copies `Label`/`Tags` like the EF repository.

## Accounting financial profile: prices and back-valuation (NL-602 A3-T2; `Accounting/Prices/`)
`AccountingServiceCollectionExtensions.AddAccountingPriceValuation()` (called by `AddAccountingServices`, TryAdd) registers `PriceValuationService` as itself and as the Domain `IAccountingPrices` (one instance) and the period lock's seam `IAccountingAdjustmentSink` (default `NullAccountingAdjustmentSink`, which takes nothing: A3-T5 registers its rule before this call or replaces it). The price sources (`IPriceSource`) come from the host's `AddAccountingPriceSources()` (Infrastructure.Bitcoin, next to `AddFeeServices` in `AddNltgNodeServices`); options `AccountingPriceOptions` (Domain) bound from `Accounting:Prices` (`Currency` USD, `Source` `None`/`Csv`/`Http`/`Both` (default `Both`, the file first), `Url` mempool.space's `/api/v1/historical-price`, `CsvFile` `prices.csv` anchored to the config directory, `MaxAge` 26 h, `FetchInterval` 10 min, `MaxFetchesPerRound` 24). The service is a timer loop like the sealer, never on a hot path: `NltgDaemonService` and `NLightningTestNode` call `Start()` after the books and `StopAsync()` before them; off while the books are off or the options are invalid (logged). A round (serialized with `ValueNowAsync`, `ImportAsync`, `FetchAsync` on one gate) pages the financial book's unvalued postings with the keyset overload `ListUnvaluedPostingsAsync(book, AccountingUnvaluedPostingCursor?, take)` (pages of `PageSize` 500, one save each, at most 100 pages), resolves each posting's price with D-A11's rule (`AccountingValuation.NearestAtOrBefore` over one window query per page, or `GetAtOrBeforeAsync` per posting past 20,000 prices), and with a source configured makes a posting whose own UTC hour has no stored price wait for it (`Deferred`): the waiting hours are asked once each (at their earliest posting's time) within the round's budget, the answers stored with `TryAddAsync` and saved before any posting uses them (ids exist only after the save), then the page's `SetPostingValueAsync` calls (fiat = `AccountingValuation.FiatValue`, msat → BTC × price, 8 places) are saved together. An hour asked without an own-hour answer is not asked again for `RetryDelay` (max(FetchInterval, 1 h)) and its postings take the nearest price within `MaxAge` once the hour is `RecentHourWait` (2 h) old. `Source=None` (or `MaxFetchesPerRound` 0) asks nothing (no HTTP client is even built) and values with stored prices at once. A posting of a closed period (its entry's `ClosedPeriodId`, or a closed `AccountingPeriod` holding its time) is never filled: with a price it goes to `IAccountingAdjustmentSink.AdjustLateValuationAsync(unitOfWork, AccountingLateValuation)` on the page's unit of work, each page holding the sink's write lock `EnterAsync()` from its closed-period check (periods re-read inside it) to its save, never across the source's asks (remembered per process once taken; the sink must be idempotent per posting); routine rounds start at the last closed period's end, and a full pass from the start runs at the first round, every 6 h, after an import or fetch and when the closed periods change. `ImportAsync` validates every row first (nothing stored on a bad row), stores `Import` prices in saves of 1,000 (a stored time keeps its price) and runs a valuation round without asks; `FetchAsync(since, until)` asks every hour of the range without a stored price (at most 744 hours; `InvalidOperationException` with `Source=None`) and runs one too. Meter `NLightning.Accounting`: `prices.fetched`, `prices.stored`, `postings.valued`, `valuation.late`, `valuation.failures`. Sanity bound (NL-678): a price the HTTP source returns (`AccountingPriceSource.Http`) that is more than `Accounting:Prices:MaxPriceJumpFactor` (default 3, 0 = off, at most 10^6) away from the nearest saved price within `MaxAge` before or after its time is refused before `TryAddAsync` (`IsPlausibleAsync`; logged, meter `prices.rejected`, the hour treated as unanswered), in rounds and in `FetchAsync`; imports and the price file are not checked. Lots of an acquisition valued late keep a null cost: the financial projector (A3-T4) owns lots and must revalue them (NL-640). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Prices/PriceValuationServiceTests` (SQLite, seeded Book 1 entries: the `MaxAge` boundary, one ask per hour, the budget, `Source=None` with no request at all through the production registration, closed periods and the sink, the retry delay, the recent-hour wait, paging past unvaluable postings, import, fetch, books off, the loop).

Batch10 integration fixes of the price valuation: the `MaxPriceJumpFactor` sanity bound also compares a fetched price with the prices the same `prices fetch` or valuation page accepted and staged but has not saved yet (NL-733: a fresh range's 10x price between good ones was stored), and `IsCatchingUp` is also set when a page still waits for its hours after an earlier page used the round's fetch budget exactly (NL-734; a round that may not fetch, `maxFetches` 0, never sets it). Tests: `PriceValuationServiceTests` (`Given_NoStoredPrice_*`, `Given_APageThatUsesTheFetchBudgetExactly_*`).

**Replacing a wrong stored price (NL-693).** `PriceValuationService.ReplaceAsync` (`IAccountingPrices`, IPC 45 action 14, `nltg accounting prices replace <time> <price> [--currency] [--source <text>] [--note <text>]`): under the round gate, the stored price of that second (`ListAsync` over [t, t+1 s); none or two is an `ArgumentException`) is replaced in place (`IAccountingPriceDbRepository.ReplaceAsync`: id and time kept, source `AccountingPriceSource.Manual` = 4, `FetchedAt` = now; an equal price changes nothing). Under the sink's write lock, in the same save, every financial entry dated at or after the price's time with a line of that `PriceId` is re-valued: an open entry the projector projected (adjustment 0 or a late fact) lowers the financial cursor to just before the earliest (the projector's replay values it again, lots, reliefs and gains included); an entry of a closed period, or an adjustment of one (a late valuation, tagged `[price:{seq}:{adj}:{index}]`, is revalued from the posting it valued; a reclassification's two lines cancel), gets one `Price` adjustment per entry (`StageAdjustmentAsync`, dedupe `reprice:{priceId}:{ticks}:{old price}:{seq}:{adj}`, note = the audit text: old -> new price, the old source, `--source` and `--note`) with the lines of `Domain/Accounting/Prices/AccountingRepricing` (each line's `FiatValue(msat, new) - FiatValue(msat, old)` and the balancing line: the realized gain for what left the node, `assets:cost-basis` for what came in, the rounding only when the asset lines were at market). A fact a reorg reversed after its close: left alone when the reversal is closed too; an open reversal is projected again, and `FinancialBooksProjector.ClosedFactLinesAsync` negates the fact's `[reprice:` corrections in a closed fact's reversal (either order). Logged as a warning, meter `nlightning.accounting.prices.replaced`. Closed lots keep their cost (D-A8), so a later disposal of them realizes against the old cost, and a closed period's export prints the stored (corrected) price in its price directives (NL-759); the old price and the note live only in the log and the adjustments' notes (NL-758). Tests: `Accounting/Financial/FinancialPriceReplaceTests` (open period equal to a book valued right from the start; closed period adjustments, verify, rebuild = incremental, a second replacement adding up; late valuations; a reversed closed fact in both orders; refusals), Domain `Accounting/Prices/AccountingRepricingTests`, Daemon `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingPricesIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingPricesCommandsTests`.

## Accounting financial projector, lots and gains (NL-602 A3-T4; `Accounting/Financial/FinancialBooksProjector.cs`)
`AddFinancialBooksProjector()` (called by `AddAccountingServices` before `AddAccountingPeriodServices`, TryAdd) registers `FinancialBooksProjector` as itself, as the Domain `IFinancialBooksProjector` (A3-T5's close and rebuild, A3-T6's reports and exports) and as `IAccountingLots` (the lot import), one instance; `IsEnabled` = books on, `Accounting:Profile=Financial` and a valid `Accounting:Prices:Currency`. The period lock (`IAccountingAdjustmentSink`, the period service) depends on the projector, so the projector takes it as a `Func<>` resolved at its first round (tests pass `() => periods`). A timer loop like the books (`SealInterval`; `Start()` after the operational books, `StopAsync()` before them: `NltgDaemonService` and `NLightningTestNode`); `ProjectAsync` and `RunExclusiveAsync` share one round gate. **A page** (`SealBatchSize` operational entries after the financial cursor, one scope and one save: entries, postings, lots, reliefs, cursor) holds the sink's `EnterAsync` from its checks to its save; it reads the operational entries (never the raw feed) and their events by ledger sequence, classifies each with a `ClassificationEngine` built per page from the enabled rules and the page's overrides (`MapPostings`; `Unclassified`, `Classification`, `RuleId` on the entry), values it and plans its lots with the pure Domain `FinancialEntryPlanner` over the round's `FinancialLotPool` (Domain CLAUDE.md "Accounting financial lots"), writes the entry (`Book = Financial`, the operational sequence, adjustment 0) and stages the reliefs (`UpdateLotAsync` + `AddRelief`) and the new lot (`AddLotAsync`). An operational entry dated in a locked period is staged through `StageAdjustmentAsync` as a `LateFact` (valued at any usable price, never `PendingValuation`; its lots and reliefs dated as the adjustment, `SourceAdjustment` = its number; nothing when the fact has no lines or the sink skips it). The round state (`FinancialProjectionRound`: the pool from `ListOpenLotsAsync`, the imported lots, the reversals after the cursor) is loaded from the database at the start of a round and after every rollback, never kept across rounds. **Valuation** (D-A11): the stored price nearest at or before the entry's time within `Accounting:Prices:MaxAge` (`FinancialPriceWindow`, one window query per page, a lookup per time past 20,000 prices); with a fetching source (`Source` not `None`, `MaxFetchesPerRound` > 0 and an `IPriceSource` registered) a price older than the entry's own hour only once the hour is `PriceValuationService.RecentHourWait` old, as the back-valuation does. Without a usable price the entry is projected unvalued with `PendingValuation`. **Replay** (the open period's lots rebuilt from a point): an open financial entry (adjustment 0, no closed period) above the cursor (`GetLastOpenEntrySeqAsync`) means a writer lowered the cursor; the page then rolls back to the cursor (`RollbackOpenEntriesAsync(Financial, cursor + 1)`, at once, one transaction) and projects again. Writers that lower it in their own save under the sink's write lock: `PriceValuationService` (it valued a `PendingValuation` entry's line: just before the earliest such entry, so lots, reliefs and gains are those of a rebuild; NL-640) and `AccountingClassificationService` (`classify set|unset` of an event the book holds in the open period: just before it; a rule added, removed, enabled or disabled: the open period from the last close's `ReplayAfterLedgerSeq`). **Reversals**: a fact and its reversal both after the cursor are cancelled (the fact valued, no lot, note `reversed in the open period by <key>`; the reversal its exact negation); a reversal of a fact already projected in the open period rolls back to the fact (`FinancialProjectionRound.RollbackTo`, or a `RestartException` from a page that meets it); a reversal of a closed period's fact posts the negation of the fact's financial lines at their values (or at the reversal's price when they were unvalued), flagged `Adjustment`, with the usual lot rules. At most `MaxRollbacksPerCall` (20) rollbacks per call. **Lot import** (D-A9, `ImportAsync`, IPC 45 `lots import`): refused with the book off, after any close, without opening balances, or when the lots' msat differ from the operational opening balances' by a sat or more (the last lot takes the msat difference); under the round gate and the sink's lock: `ResetToCloseAsync(Financial, (0, []))`, `DeleteLotsByOriginAsync(Import)`, the new `Import` lots, one save; then `ProjectAsync` replays the book: opening balances open no lot while imported lots exist, and the cutover marker's entry (`open:cutover`) carries `assets:cost-basis` against `equity:opening-balances` by the imported cost less the opening balances' market value (`GainPending` while one is unvalued). A failure at an entry stops the round before the page is saved (critical log once, `ProjectionError`, metered); the next round retries; it never skips. Meter `nlightning.accounting.financial.projected`, `.financial.rollbacks`, `.financial.failures`, `nlightning.accounting.lots.shortfall` (msat no lot covered: its cost is taken as its proceeds, warned). The operational book is never written (proof below). `AccountingOptions.CostBasis` (`Accounting:CostBasis`, `Fifo` default / `Lifo` / `Hifo`) applies to disposals projected after a change; `rebuild --book financial` applies it to the open period. Reading of the plan: the asset lines carry the market value of their flows and the cost-basis line brings the assets' total to the open lots' cost (lots are pooled node-wide, not per bucket; NL-657). Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Accounting/Financial/FinancialBooksProjectorTests` (`FinancialProjectorTestKit`: SQLite, real sealer, operational books with the production rules, the projector, the period service and signer, the back-valuation with `Source=None`, the classification service): the story (opening balance, deposit, channel open, invoice, payment, forward, rebalance, mutual close, withdrawal) under FIFO, LIFO and HIFO with the gains computed by hand and only the fees and payments disposing, the assets' fiat equal to the open lots' cost; golden exports `Golden/projector-fifo.{journal,beancount,csv}` through A3-T6's formatter, checked by `JournalBalanceChecker` (and by hand with `hledger check --strict` 1.52.4 and `bean-check` 3.2); the late valuation equal to a book valued from the start; a reorg in the open period (rollback and the cancelled pair); rebuild equal to incremental (several pages and rounds; after closes with a late fact); closes verified; the operational book byte-identical with and without the financial profile; the lot import and its refusals; an override re-projected; a reversal of a closed fact. Also `AccountingPeriodServiceTests` (DI), Domain `Accounting/Financial/Lots/`, Integration `Persistence/AccountingFinancialRollbackTests`, Daemon `Ipc/Handlers/AccountingLotsIpcHandlerTests`, `Client/AccountingLotsCommandsTests`, `Extensions/NodeServiceExtensionsTests.Given_TheFinancialProfile_*`.

**Review fixes of the A3 final integration (NL-671..NL-673).** A late fact is staged with `AccountingEntryFlags.LateFact` (and keeps `PendingValuation` when no price of the fact's time is usable); a rollback or a financial rebuild from before it deletes it with its lots and reliefs and the replay stages it again (dated at the replay, adjustment 1 again), so its reliefs survive a lot the replay re-opens (NL-671). `PriceValuationService` never values a late fact's lines in place: it looks them up at the fact's time (the operational entry's `OccurredAt`, `LateFactTimesAsync`), asks the source for that hour, and when a price is usable lowers the financial cursor to the fact, so the projector stages it again at the fact's price (NL-672). The reversal of a wallet fact from before the feed (`Wallet` against `Opening`) relieves the opening/imported lots at their cost and realizes nothing (Domain `FinancialLotRules.IsOpeningCorrection`, NL-673). Tests: `Accounting/Financial/FinancialBooksProjectorLateFactTests` (each case failed before the fixes).

**Lots per bucket and the held-outside pool (batch10 lane b10-lots, NL-657, NL-674, NL-675, NL-680, NL-681, NL-658).** The projector stages the planner's reliefs with their `Kind` and every new lot (acquisitions, moved parts with `ParentLotId`/`HeldSince`, debts with `Lender`), dated as the entry (Domain CLAUDE.md "Lots per bucket"). Each asset account now carries its own bucket's cost basis; the cutover marker posts nothing after a lot import (the opening balances take the imported lots into their buckets at cost, in ledger order). A closed fact's reversal passes `CorrectionOf` (the fact's sequence) so it takes the fact's own lot back at cost (NL-675). A withdrawal or deposit a rule or an override classifies to an equity account other than `equity:transfers:out`/`in` moves its lots out of or back into the node at cost (NL-674, D-A12 as amended); in the open period a reclassification replays as before, so its lots follow, while a reclassification of a closed entry still moves its lines only (NL-683). The financial reports skip non-disposal reliefs in the realized gains (the holding period from `HeldSinceOrAcquired`), leave debts out of `report lots`, and the lots IPC answer carries the bucket name in key 4 (`Account`), `HeldSinceUnixMilliseconds` (14) and `ParentLotId` (15); the client prints `in <bucket>` and `(held since ...)`. **Forced closes (NL-680, NL-681):** `PriceValuationService` looks a late fact's lines up at the fact's time also when a forced close closed it unvalued (its price adjustments carry the fact's price); `AccountingClassificationService` (new optional `IOptions<AccountingPriceOptions>`, its `Currency`) moves an unvalued closed line at 0 in the book's currency, and `AccountingPeriodService.AdjustLateValuationAsync` posts a classifiable line's price adjustment on the account the reclassifications hold it in then. The lots of a fact a forced close left unvalued keep no cost (their disposals stay `GainPending`), as with any forced close (NL-685). **Scale (NL-658):** the projector keeps its `FinancialLotPool` across rounds and reads every open lot again only when `GetOpenLotsFingerprintAsync` (one aggregate query: count, highest id, msat, closed count) no longer matches the pool's `Fingerprint`, after a rollback or inside `RunExclusiveAsync` (close, rebuild, import) (`OpenLotLoads` counts the reads, internal); its background rounds (`RunRoundAsync`) wait with a replay while `PriceValuationService.IsCatchingUp` (its last round used the whole fetch budget with hours left), at most `FinancialBooksProjector.MaxReplayDeferral` (6 h); `ProjectAsync` never waits. Tests: `Accounting/Financial/FinancialHeldOutsideTests` (a withdrawal tagged `dest=cold` and the deposit back labelled `from cold` against the unclassified story, rebuild and close), `FinancialForcedCloseValuationTests`, `FinancialProjectorScaleTests`, and the A3-T4 story's per-account cost basis check under FIFO, LIFO and HIFO in `FinancialBooksProjectorTests` (golden `projector-fifo.*` regenerated: the asset lines at cost, no `assets:cost-basis`). **NL-749** (the planner's debts, Domain CLAUDE.md "Lots per bucket"): `FinancialBooksProjectorTests.Given_TheStoryInAnyOrder_*` projects the story entry by entry, also with every half before the half that pays it, and checks after each that every asset account's balance and fiat are its bucket's lots plus what it is owed less what it owes; the `Explicit` `FinancialNodeReplayTests` replays a node: `NLTG_REPLAY_DB` a copy of its database (copied again, migrated, the financial book rebuilt), `NLTG_REPLAY_NODE_ID` its node id (so the stored closes' digests and signatures verify), `NLTG_REPLAY_OUT` a report path, `NLTG_REPLAY_TRACE=1` every entry and lot.

## Accounting A3: running the financial profile (NL-602 A3-T7)
How the A3 pieces fit at run time (each has its own section above). **Turning it on:** `Accounting:Profile=Financial` (the default `Operational` keeps only the operational book; `Accounting:Enabled=false` stops both, the feed stays on). The daemon's template (`NodeConfigurationExtensions.CreateDefaultConfigJson`) writes `Profile`, `CostBasis` (`Fifo`) and the whole `Accounting:Prices` section at their code defaults (test `Daemon.Tests/Extensions/AccountingConfigTemplateTests`), so changing a value in `appsettings.json` is the operator's only step. **Order in the host** (`NltgDaemonService`, mirrored by `NLightningTestNode`): start sealer, operational books, `FinancialBooksProjector`, `PriceValuationService`, then the memo backfill; stop the back-valuation, then the financial projector, then the operational books, then the sealer. The financial projector reads only operational entries (D-A7), so it is never ahead of the operational cursor; the back-valuation and `classify set|unset`/rule changes lower the financial cursor in their own saves and the projector replays the open period. **Prices:** with `Source` `Both` (default) the file `<configPath>/prices.csv` is asked first, then mempool.space (through Tor only in `TorOnly`; privacy notes SR-21/SR-22 in `docs/agents/SECURITY_REVIEW.md`); `Source=None` plus `accounting prices import` keeps the node off the network. The Docker test node sets `Accounting:Prices:Source=None` (NL-641): a Docker test that runs `Profile=Financial` imports its prices (`IAccountingPrices.ImportAsync` or the IPC) or overrides the source through `ExtraConfiguration`. **Operator flow:** `accounting lots import` (optional, before the first close), `classify rule add`/`classify list --unclassified`, `report unvalued`/`unclassified --book financial`, then `accounting close <YYYY-MM>` (refused with unvalued or unclassified rows unless `--force`) and `accounting verify`. A3's Docker smoke (`Profile=Financial` on the ABCD or LND suite: reconcile clean, financial book balanced in fiat) was not run (owner decision 2026-10-02); plan "A3 record" lists the open follow-ups.

## Accounting feed edge cases (batch10: NL-606..NL-613, NL-645)
- One key scheme for an on-chain fact recorded again after a reorg (NL-613): the resolution writers (`OnchainAccounting.NewKeyAsync`/`FindAsync`, used by `OnchainChannelWatcher` and `OnchainResolutionExecutor`) key like the chain monitor's wallet writers, through `AccountingConfirmations.NextConfirmationKey` (base key, then `{key}:c2`, `:c3`, ...; null while a confirmation stands) over `GetByKeyPrefixAsync`; `AccountingConfirmations.FindStanding`/`FindAt` find the confirmation a rewind reverses. `AccountingEventKeys.Reemitted` (`{key}:re:{height}`, which collided when a fact was reorged back to the same height twice) is gone; a former re-emission still in a feed counts as a confirmation of its fact (`IsConfirmationKey`). `OnchainAccounting.StageReversalAsync` keys the reversal by the original's own block (`original.BlockHeight`), as `IsReversed` reads it.
- Offer ids on our payments (NL-645): `PaymentAccountingEvents.PaymentSucceeded`/`PaymentFailed` (and the backfill's memo copies) write detail `offerId` (`ClassificationEngine.OfferIdDetail`) next to `offer`, computed by `PaymentAccountingEvents.OfferIdOf` from the `lno1...` string as `OfferService` computes ours (SHA-256 of the offer's TLV bytes; null when it does not decode or is not an offer), so an `AccountingRule.OfferId` matches the offers we pay as well as the ones we are paid through. Tests: `Payments/PaymentOfferIdDetailTests`, `Offers/OfferHarnessTests`.
- Our anchor sweep (NL-611): `AnchorCpfpService` stores the sweep as an `AnchorSweep` row with its fee before publishing it (see "BOLT 5 anchor CPFP"), so `OnchainResolutionExecutor` books our anchor's resolution as ours. The sweep also spends the peer's anchor, which has no row: for a stored `Sweep` or `AnchorSweep` with a known fee `OnchainAccounting.Ours(..., externalInputsFeeMsat)` values the inputs that are not rows as outputs + fee - rows and books them with the first row's event (note `AccountingDetailKeys.ExternalInputsNote`: the whole wallet output and fee, the peer's anchor a gain), instead of the CPFP child's `merged` rule, so the clearing account nets with the sweep's `WalletReceived`. Tests: `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_OurStoredAnchorSweepOfBothAnchors_*`, `Onchain/Anchors/AnchorCpfpServiceTests` (`Given_CommitmentConfirmed16BlocksAgo_*`, `Given_ARefusedAnchorSweep_*`).
- The peer's spend of our offered HTLC output (NL-612): `OnchainResolutionExecutor.ClaimPathOf` reads the spending input's witness (`HtlcWitnessParser`): detail `claimPath` = `preimage` (the HTLC's preimage, checked against its payment hash), `revocation` (the revocation path of our own revoked commitment) or `unknown`. Only `preimage` keeps `valueBookedBy` = payment or forward (rule (b)); any other path is booked as a loss (`LossOnchain`), since no payment or forward was paid by it. Tests: `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_OurRevokedCommitment_When_ThePeerTakesOurHtlcByRevocation_*` and the preimage case in `Given_PeersCommitmentResolved_*`.
- A settled forward whose incoming HTLC we lose on chain (NL-608): when the peer takes an incoming HTLC output of the close's commitment (`LocalReceivedHtlc` or `RemoteOfferedHtlc`, not revoked) or we give it up (`Ignored`) and its circuit is `Fulfilled` (the forward was booked by `ForwardSettled`; the upstream fulfill never got through), `OnchainResolutionExecutor.StageUpstreamForwardLossAsync` stages a `ForwardLostOnchain` (`PaymentAccountingEvents.ForwardUpstreamLostOnchain`: -incoming amount, channel = incoming, detail `cause=upstreamOnchain`, `closeTxId`, `spenderTxId`, the spend's block) in the resolution's save, keyed `AccountingEventKeys.ForwardLostOnchain(incoming channel, htlc)` by generation; a reorg that unresolves the row, or a close replaced by another, reverses it (`StageUpstreamForwardLossReversalAsync`). The books post Cr Channels / Dr LossOnchain, taking out the incoming amount the forward's settlement left in the channels. The same gap for our own invoice settled before its HTLC timed out to the peer is NL-688. Tests: `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_ASettledForwardsIncomingHtlc_*`, `Given_AFailedForwardsIncomingHtlc_*`.
- A settled invoice whose incoming HTLC we lose on chain (NL-688): the same two places stage `InvoiceLostOnchain` (`AccountingEventKind` 6, `PaymentAccountingEvents.InvoiceLostOnchain`: −the HTLC's amount, channel = the HTLC's, payment hash, detail `cause=invoiceOnchain`, `htlcId`, `closeTxId`, `spenderTxId`, `settledKey`, the invoice's description, label and tags) when the HTLC has no circuit, its payment hash is one of our invoices `Settled`, and its record (`channel.Commitments`) carries the invoice's preimage (`KnownPreimage` or a fulfill) and no failure, i.e. it was a part the settle booked; keyed `AccountingEventKeys.InvoiceLostOnchain(hash, channel, htlc)` by generation and reversed by a reorg or a replaced close (`StageInvoiceLossReversalAsync`). The books post Cr Channels / Dr LossOnchain (the forward's rule): the sale stays income (the payer holds the preimage), and the amount `InvoiceSettled` put in the channels, which the close never took out (an incoming HTLC is not in our balance at the close), leaves them. The channel report counts it in `OnchainLossMsat`, and a reversal of either loss kind takes it off again. Tests: `Onchain/OnchainAccountingTests.Given_ASettledInvoicesIncomingHtlc_*` (FIFO/LIFO/HIFO, the NL-749 lots invariant, reorg and reconfirmation), `Given_AnIncomingHtlcNotBookedByASettledInvoice_*`; Domain `AccountingPostingRulesTests.Given_AnInvoiceLostOnchain_*`.
- Funding and mutual-close events across reorgs (NL-607): when a funding confirms again at another position, `FundingReconfirmationHandler` reverses the standing `ChannelFunded` and push (or the `SpliceLocked` of a spliced channel's current funding; memo events left alone) in the move's save and records them again at the new block and short channel id under their next confirmation key (`AccountingConfirmations.CreateReconfirmation`, detail `reconfirms`). A mutual close confirmed above a reorg's fork is reversed by the chain monitor in the rewind's save (`ChannelClosedMutual` is one of its reversible kinds; its closing watch is pending again), and when the closing transaction of a Closed channel confirms again `ChannelManager.ConfirmFundingAsync` records it again (`ChannelAccountingEvents.RecordMutualCloseAgainAsync`, own save, nothing while a confirmation stands, never for a memo close of the backfill, NL-737) without bringing the channel back into memory. The funding events also move when the funding confirms again at the same height and another index (a stale short channel id, NL-738). Tests: `Onchain/Reorg/FundingReconfirmationHandlerTests.Given_TheFundingsAccountingEvents_*`, `Channels/Close/ClosingLifecycleTests.Given_AClosedChannel_When_ItsClosingTransactionConfirmsAgain_*`, `Integration.Tests/Persistence/ChainMonitorAccountingTests.Given_AMutualCloseConfirmedAboveTheFork_*`.
- Who paid a mutual close's fee (NL-610): the channel records how its closing transaction was agreed (`ChannelModel.CloseProtocol` `MutualCloseProtocol.Legacy`/`Simple`, `LocalIsCloser` for a simple close; `SetCloseTerms`, migration `AddMutualCloseTerms`): `ChannelCloseCoordinator.FinalizeAsync` (legacy: the funder pays), `SimpleCloseCoordinator` (our `closing_complete` answered: we are the closer; the peer's signed: it is), and `ChannelManager.CloseTermsOf` for a mutual close found on chain that we did not record (protocol from the input's sequence, the closer from our output: below our balance in whole satoshis means we paid). `ChannelAccountingEvents.BuildMutualClose` takes `feePaidByUs` from those terms (details `closeProtocol`, `closer`); only a close agreed before they were recorded infers it from a loss of 1,000 msat or more (`feePayerInferred=true`). Tests: `Channels/Close/ClosingLifecycleTests.Given_ASimpleCloseThePeerClosedWithoutOurDustOutput_*`, `Given_MutualClosesFoundOnChain_*`, the harness assertions in `CooperativeCloseHarnessTests`/`SimpleCloseHarnessTests`, `Integration.Tests/Persistence/ChannelRoundTripTests`.

## Liquidity ads (NL-850; `LiquidityAds/`, `Channels/DualFunding/DualFundLiquidity*`, `Channels/Splicing/SpliceLiquidity*`)
- BOLT PR #1153 as Eclair 0.14.3 speaks it (plan `docs/agents/LIQUIDITY_ADS_PLAN.md`, Record): TLV 1339 on `init`, `open_channel2`/`accept_channel2`, `tx_init_rbf`/`tx_ack_rbf`, `splice_init`/`splice_ack`, `node_announcement`; no feature bit, the peer's `init` rates are `IPeerService.LiquidityRates`. Options `Node:LiquidityAds` (Domain `LiquidityAdsOptions`, validated with `NodeOptions`; the daemon template writes the section without rates): selling is off until `FundingRates` lists a rate (D-L3), only `from_channel_balance` (D-L2), `MaxConcurrentSales` 4, `MaxSalesPerPeer` 1 (D-L5), `LeaseBlocks` 4032 (D-L4), `MaxFeeSat` (the buyer's default fee limit, null = none).
- `LiquidityAdsService` (singleton, `TryAddSingleton` in `AddApplicationServices`; flows resolve it optionally, without it a request is refused): seller `TryStartSale(buyer, request, out sale)` (`LiquidityAdsRules.ValidateRequest` against our rates, then a sale slot under the D-L5 caps, in memory; dispose the `LiquiditySale` when the negotiation ends, signed or aborted) and `CreateWillFund(rate, fundingScript)` (`ILightningSigner.SignNodeMessage` over `LiquidityAdsRules.SignedData`); buyer `GetSellerRates` (the peer's `init` on the current connection, else its `node_announcement` in the graph via `NodeAnnouncementRates`), `CreateRequest` (the given rate when the seller offers it, else its cheapest compatible one for the amount; always `FromChannelBalance`) and `ValidateWillFund` (Eclair `validateRemoteFunding` + our fee limit: the request's `MaxFeeSat`, else `Node:LiquidityAds:MaxFeeSat`); `CreatePurchase` builds the Pending `LiquidityPurchaseModel` for the attempt's save.
- Seller rules: contribute exactly the requested amount from the wallet; refuse an open with an `error` (`ChannelErrorException`, Eclair's behavior) and a splice or RBF with `tx_abort`; a wallet that cannot fund the sale is a refusal, not a 0 contribution. Buyer rules: check the answer before anything is funded; refuse with an `error` before the interactive tx (a missing or bad `provide_funding` in `accept_channel2`) and with `tx_abort` after it. An RBF after a purchase must request again (#1153): our bump repeats the latest attempt's purchase (same amount and rate, the fee at the new feerate) unless a new `LiquidityRequest` is given; a peer's `tx_init_rbf` without `request_funding` after a purchase gets `tx_abort` (`InvalidRbfMissingLiquidityPurchase`); every RBF attempt is re-validated and re-signed by the seller. Only the buyer bumps a sale: our bump of an attempt we sold in is refused (`[LA-RBF-01]` for a splice), and `Onchain/Fees/SpliceAutoBumper` skips a splice whose latest attempt we sold in (`IsSaleAsync`). LA-RES-01 (Domain `SpliceRules`): the splice buyer keeps its reserve of the new capacity after the fee (`CheckBuyerCanPay` before we ask, the seller on `splice_init`, `SpliceTxCompleteFacts.LiquidityFeeMsat` at `tx_complete`); the dual-fund equivalent is `DualFundLiquidity.GetBalanceViolation` (the buyer's share pays the fee and the opener's balance still pays the first commitment's fee and anchors).
- Where the fee lives. Dual-funded open: the first commitment's balances are the attempt's shares with the fee moved (`DualFundLiquidity.ApplyFee(localShare, remoteShare, localFeeMsat)`, + we buy, − we sell); the shares and contributions themselves are unchanged. On restart (`GetOrLoadAsync`, `TryGetSignedAttempt`) and when another attempt confirms (`ApplyAttemptFunding`) the shares are recovered with `RemoveFee` and the fee re-applied from that attempt's purchase row (`GetOpenPurchasesAsync`), so the row is the source of truth for the fee. Splice: in the `ChannelFunding` balance deltas (the buyer's delta is its contribution less the fee, the seller's its contribution plus the fee), so both commitments on the new funding, the lock and BOLT 5 carry it; `SpliceService.GetContributions(funding, purchase)` takes it back out where the contributions are needed (a resumed negotiation, an RBF rebuilt from the latest attempt). `SpliceLiquidity`/`DualFundLiquidity` are memory only, on the negotiation.
- Purchase records (`IUnitOfWork.LiquidityPurchaseDbRepository`, table `LiquidityPurchases`, one row per channel and funding attempt, no FK so a record outlives its channel): staged Pending in the save of our `commitment_signed` (dual-fund `RecordPurchase`, kind `ChannelOpen`/`OpenRbf`; splice `StagePurchaseAsync`, kind `Splice`/`SpliceRbf`). Status moves: at the dual-funded funding confirmation `DualFundLiquidityAccounting.StageFundingConfirmedAsync` marks the confirmed attempt's row Active (lease from that height) and the other attempts' Pending rows Replaced (until then they stay Pending, NL-852); an abandoned never-signed first attempt marks its row Closed; the splice lock's save (`StageLiquidityAtLockAsync`) marks the locked attempt Active and its siblings Replaced; `LiquidityLeases.StageChannelClosedAsync` marks Pending/Active rows Closed (`ClosedEarly` inside the lease) in the save that closes the channel (`ChannelManager` mutual close, `OnchainResolutionExecutor`). A unit of work without the table (test doubles: the interface default throws `NotSupportedException`, a mock answers null) has no purchases; every reader tolerates both.
- Accounting: the fee is staged as `LiquidityFeePaid`/`LiquidityFeeEarned` (`ChannelAccountingEvents.RecordLiquidityPurchaseAsync`, key per channel and funding txid) in the save of `ChannelFunded` (open: at the funding confirmation, including the late `RecordLateChannelFundedAsync` path of a Failed/OnchainResolving channel, untested, NL-851) or of `SpliceLocked` (at the lock), never at negotiation; both carry `liquidityFeeMsat` so their own balance change leaves the fee out and the reconcile never sees the channels account drift. A replaced attempt never booked one.
- Lease guard (D-L4): `ChannelCloseService` refuses our cooperative `closechannel` while `LiquidityLeases.GetActiveSaleLeaseAsync` finds a seller purchase in force at the chain monitor's height (Pending, or Active before `LeaseEndHeight`), with `DescribeCloseRefusal`'s text naming every lease in force on the channel (`LiquidityLeases.GetLeasesInForceAsync`: both roles, each Active one before its end and the newest Pending attempt, with amount, role, kind and end; NL-882; the service no longer logs the refusal, the IPC handler does in one line), unless `ChannelCloseRequest.Force` (`closechannel --force`); the peer's `shutdown`/`closing_complete` and `forceclosechannel` are never blocked. Nothing else enforces the lease.
- Operator surface (Daemon `LiquidityAdsClientHandler`, `OpenChannelClientHandler`, `SpliceClientHandlers`, `BumpOpenClientHandler`; Client `LiquidityAdsCommands`, `LiquidityOptions`, `LiquidityAdsPrinter`): `openchannel --request-inbound` (v2 only), `splicein --request-inbound`, `bumpopen --request-inbound`, `closechannel --force`, `liquidityads rates|sellers|purchases` (`ClientCommand.LiquidityAds = 46`).
- Tests: `test/NLightning.Application.Tests/Channels/DualFunding/` (`LiquidityAdsKit` on `DualFundHarness`: Bob sells at `Rate()`, Alice buys, each node seeing the other's rates as its `init` would; `DualFundLiquidityAdsTests` (open, RBF repeat and new amount, first attempt confirming after a bump, restarts), `DualFundLiquidityAdsRefusalTests`, `DualFundLiquidityTests`), `Channels/Splicing/` (`SpliceLiquidityTestKit` on `SpliceHarness`: `Sell(options, rates)`, `AddLiquidityAds(services, peerRates)`, purchases in `InMemorySpliceLiquidityPurchases` committed with the node's saves; `SpliceLiquidityAdsTests`), `LiquidityAds/LiquidityAdsServiceTests`, `LiquidityLeasesTests`, `Channels/Accounting/LiquidityAccountingEventsTests`, `Accounting/Reports/LiquidityFeeReportTests`, `Protocol/Factories/MessageFactoryLiquidityAdsTests`. Docker namespace: `Integration.Tests/Docker/Interop/Eclair/EclairLiquidityAdsTests` (buyer role against Eclair 0.14.3; on the cluster harness only since NL-866, `scripts/run-cluster.sh -n 1 --suite eclair --class NLightning.Integration.Tests.Docker.Interop.Eclair.EclairLiquidityAdsTests`; see `test/CLAUDE.md`).


## Recovery key state and interceptor reliability (NL-1215, NL-1182)
Recovery may know a taproot funding outpoint without knowing its key aggregate. `FundingKeysUnknown` survives `ChannelFundings`, signer/model views and SCB flag bit 5; the stored key values are historical hints. Unknown fundings are not registered or locked for signing, and the signer refuses unknown-key registrations outside data-loss recovery. Recovery still uses the outpoint and payment basepoint.

An intercepted forward stays in the hub until its callback succeeds. A failed callback returns `Failed`, leaves expiry protection active and may be retried; `InProgress` prevents concurrent resolution of one circuit. Failed disconnect resumes retry on block notifications. gRPC monitors both directions and cancels/disconnects when either ends.

## HTLC interceptor LND parity (NL-1182; `Payments/Interception/`, `Payments/Switch/HtlcSwitch.Interception.cs`, `Onchain/Resolvers/InterceptorClaims.cs`)
LND v0.21.4's `InterceptableSwitch`/`heldHtlcSet`/`forward_interceptor.go` semantics:
- `RESUME_MODIFIED` (`ForwardInterceptResolution.Modified(in, out, records)`, zero = unchanged, records of type 65536+ validated with LND's text): the switch's `ForwardAsync(..., modification)` checks the forwarding policy against the interceptor's incoming amount and the outgoing amount, offers `out` with exactly the interceptor's records in the `update_add_htlc` extension (the forwarded add starts without any, as in LND; at most `WireCustomRecordCodec.MaxEncodedLength` encoded bytes so the add fits BOLT 8's 65,535, checked by `Modified` and again by `OfferHtlcAsync` before anything is staged; even types are sent like LND's but logged, NL-1283) (`IChannelOperations.OfferHtlcAsync(..., wireCustomRecords, ct)`, persisted on the outgoing `HtlcRecord.WireCustomRecords` so a retransmission carries them; migration `AddHtlcWireCustomRecords`). The circuit keeps the HTLC's real incoming amount (the books follow the channel; LND records the override), and a forward that would send more than the incoming HTLC brings in is failed back with `temporary_channel_failure` (no custom channels here). Incoming `update_add_htlc` records (odd types only, BOLT 1) are kept on the incoming record and offered as `in_wire_custom_records`.
- `requireinterceptor` (`HtlcInterceptorSettings.RequireInterceptor`, from `LndGrpc:RequireInterceptor` through `LndGrpcOptions.ToInterceptorSettings`, only while the server runs; the hub reads it from `IOptions<HtlcInterceptorSettings>` at construction): without a client a new forward is failed with `temporary_channel_failure` (`ForwardInterceptOutcome.InterceptorRequired`) and a replay (`Intercept(..., isReplay: !firstHandling)`: the switch handled the HTLC before, its onion secret stored) is held; a disconnect keeps every hold; the expiry checks and the auto-fail still apply. LND's `expiry_too_far` (auto-fail height above `int32`) is `ForwardInterceptOutcome.ExpiryTooFar`.
- On-chain interception: a forward whose incoming channel is closing on chain (`IsOnchain`, resolver-raised `IncomingHtlcLockedIn` of an HTLC without an outgoing leg) is held on chain (`IHtlcForwardInterceptor.InterceptOnChain`: only with a client connected, an interceptor required or an off-chain hold to promote; offered with `AutoFailHeight` = the incoming expiry and `IsOnChain`; kept across disconnects; dropped at that height without a callback or by `Release` on `IncomingHtlcSettled`). Only a settle is accepted (`InterceptResolveResult.NotAllowedOnChain`, the RPC ends the stream with LND's "cannot fail/resume held htlc in the on-chain flow"); an off-chain hold whose callback finds the channel on chain throws `ForwardHeldOnChainException` and is promoted. The settle persists the preimage on the incoming record (`MarkPartAsync`) with `InterceptedHtlcSettled` in the same save; `InterceptorClaims.GetSettledPreimageAsync` lets both commitment resolvers claim it.
- Tests: `Payments/Interception/HtlcInterceptorHubParityTests`, `Payments/Switch/HtlcInterceptionParitySwitchTests` (ThreeNodeHarness), Domain `Payments/Keysend/WireCustomRecordCodecTests`, Serialization `UpdateAddHtlcMessageTests` (custom records region), LndGrpc `Wave3/InterceptorParityMappingTests`, `Wave3/LndGrpcWave3HostTests.InterceptorParity`, `LndGrpcHostTests.InterceptorParity`. Remaining differences: NL-1283.


## VLS channel semantics

`CommitmentSigningService` routes a signer implementing `IVlsChannelSigner` through policy-aware commitment sign/validate operations. VLS channels support the explicitly negotiated single-funded ECDSA subset; there is no native fallback. Initial opening handlers set up the semantic channel, validate commitment zero, persist the initial peer signature, then activate it. Ordinary holder validation and peer revocation use durable workflow scopes; local revocation secret release follows the durable channel transition. Legacy cooperative-close signing sends the exact dust-trimmed output values and scripts to VLS rather than requesting an arbitrary transaction signature. Funding, mutual-close and force-close signatures are durable workflows (NL-1330): `FundingSignedMessageHandler` takes the native funding path (the VLS coordinator implements `INativeFundingSigningRecovery`), `ChannelCloseCoordinator` consumes each `closing_signed` signature before sending it (the agreed one in the `Closing` save) and answers a restarted negotiation in `Closing` from the stored transaction's witness (`VlsClosingSignatures`), `ChannelFailureService` consumes the force close in the `Failed` save and republishes a saved `LocalCommitment` row as is, and `ChannelStateTransitionService.ResumeSigningWorkflowsAsync` hands interrupted close workflows to `Services/VlsCloseSigningWorkflows` (replay and retire a mutual close, complete a force close).
